# Changelog

User-facing changes, newest first. Dates are release dates.

## 2026-10-20

### Improvements
- An install that cannot reach the European Central Bank can read exchange rates from a file, and a failed rate download is retried within the hour

### Fixes
- Ask Cobb keeps recovered commands from repeating changes during an update.
- Ask Cobb now recovers completed commands and their Undo controls after a lost reply or reload.

## 2026-10-19

### Fixes
- A suggested photo for a record is no longer added to your files before you choose Use this photo, and looking up photos for records is off until a workspace turns it on in the AI page's Scan photos section.

## 2026-10-18

### Improvements
- The wire composer no longer offers a field you are not allowed to read, and says so when a wire already uses one
- Embroidery, kilns and electronics production can each be turned off in a workspace, under Digital Fabrication in Configuration, Modules; they stay on unless you do.
- While main waits on one pull request's re-check, other pull requests that change none of its files now merge instead of waiting with it.

### Fixes
- A record whose photo is its cover shows that photo in its Photos panel instead of saying it has none (#7335).
- A comment that finishes sending no longer erases the next one you started typing, or who it replies to
- On a phone, the Files page's pictures show their names again and each Move to menu stays inside its own picture, with no sideways scroll
- On a phone, a scan row's "+1 more" is as wide as its words again instead of drawing "more" past its button (#7338).
- On an iPhone, the station's Set total keypad keeps 0 and the delete key clear of the home bar, so a tap on them no longer swipes you out of the station
- A narrow table column keeps each word whole ("Acrylic", not "Acryli c"); a cell breaks only between words (#7334).

## 2026-10-17

### Features
- An update to a bundle no longer undoes the changes you made to its views. Change the columns, sort or filter of a view a bundle brought, or rename it, and the update keeps what you changed and still brings in what the bundle changed that you did not touch. The view stays the same view, so a dashboard pin or an app page that shows it keeps showing it. When you and the update both changed the same setting, you are asked, with your version beside the update's. A view you changed has a button under Edit to put it back as the bundle made it, and the assistant can do the same.
- The wire composer now says how many existing records a wire fits right now ("2 parts of 3 in that state now"), updating as you change it, and says what the number means for the trigger. When a wire runs on linked records, the relationship is picked from the links in use in your workspace, with a way to type a new one. The assistant reads the same count and the same list before it sets an automation up.
- A form in a worker app can now change an existing record, not only add one: pick a part, an asset or a project, correct its name, place or other fields, and save. The form offers only the fields you can see and may change, the record's history shows the change, and the assistant can make the same change by asking.
- A build takes sub-assemblies from stock before making more
- A storage tote scanned in the camera can become a bin in one tap, and the next scans land inside it without leaving the camera; undoing a scan put onto a bin you already had no longer deletes that bin.
- Food moved into the fridge or the freezer is now re-dated by how long it keeps there, and by its warm shelf life when it comes out, with a line in its history saying why. An item can state a fridge and a freezer number beside the existing two, and an item with no number for the place, or a date you typed yourself, is left alone.
- A food scanned from Open Food Facts now shows what it contains: its allergens and traces appear as warning pills on the scan card and on the pantry item, a view can keep only the foods that list a word such as nuts, and Cobb can answer which of your snacks have them. A food the catalog lists nothing for shows no pill.
- A task left undone is now told again, louder each step: its assignee hears on the day it is due and again when it is late (the task is tagged late), and the whole household hears only when it has gone stale. A task nobody has goes to everyone at once, and marking it done stops the next step.
- Every finished print knows what it cost, from the spool it used, with cost per day in Job history
- Klipper, OctoPrint and PrusaLink printers show their camera on the fleet card with no address typed
- ABB IRC5 arms connect over Robot Web Services: programs load, start once or forever and stop from Cobblr, with the controller's write lock given back after every command.
- Robot arms get guided software updates read back from the controller, licences on the machine page, and a written safety boundary linked from every arm and cell page.
- Cells: a CNC and the arm that unloads it run as one job on the shop bridge, started at the cell, with every step recorded, its parts counted into stock and its operators told when it pauses.
- Universal Robots, Elite and Dobot arms connect through the shop's bridge: their programs are listed, loaded, started, paused and stopped, and their state and faults watched.
- Machines show their alarms with escalation, observed use with how much was seen, the tools installed in them with their wear, and their spare parts.
- KUKA, FANUC, ABB and PLC-wired arms connect through an I/O board: a program is chosen by number and started over wires, with the wiring explained in plain words and safety left hardwired.
- ABB OmniCore arms connect over Robot Web Services 2.0, with the controller's certificate pinned.
- Robot arm programs are versioned and compared before release, copies to another arm are a re-teach checklist, and backups are proven by a restore test.
- A scan of a thing you already have can be marked broken: the thing gets a repair entry in its service log instead of a second one being filed.
- Scanning within one product opens its own unit when another product shares the serial, with no question, and each scan rule shows how often its key matched more than one thing in the last 30 days.

### Improvements
- Ask Cobb to rename, re-lay or remove a saved view, change how units show or remove one, save or delete a template, and attach a file to a record or take it off
- Ask Cobb to make or remove a printer pool, take a printer out of one, pause or cancel a production run, or arrange the machine floor
- Ask Cobb to move a print job up the queue, refresh or clear a job, and rename or delete a file in the print library; none of it starts a machine
- Ask Cobb to turn print failure detection on and tune it, check a print for failure now, or test a failure detector
- Ask Cobb whether a printer manager connection is still working; it checks without sending anything to a machine
- Ask Cobb to add or remove a build's components, add, change or remove its steps, log time or scrap at a step, and plan, change or remove a build on the schedule
- Ask Cobb to change or remove a queued label, save or remove a label size, pull spools in from Spoolman, add a numbered unit, change or remove a sales order line, remove a mistaken reading, or read a pattern's materials
- A device post sent again under its delivery id is applied once, so a lost answer no longer counts pulses or parts twice
- A new workspace's home leads with one box to add things, then three clear rows and a section of ready-made setups, with a one-line AI notice you can close
- Turning on notifications on a phone or computer asks which of your other channels to turn off, and whether normal updates should push too
- Photos uploaded together are offered as one thing more readily: a listing screenshot and a photo of the printer it sells, sent in one go, now get the Combine offer, while different things in one upload stay apart.
- Ask Cobb or an MCP assistant to tidy the scan inbox: rename a scan, bin or restore it, send a filed scan back, look it up again, combine duplicates, tag a batch, or fix a photo

### Fixes
- A bundle update that stopped or failed part-way used to leave the workspace without the bundle, and the hourly update pass never found it again. The old version now stays until its replacement is in, and an install that did not finish is finished on the next pass, with no reinstall.
- When two collections each have a date field with the same name, the calendar now gives each record the label and the meaning of its own collection's field. Before, a past purchase could be shown as "Return by" and counted overdue, or an overdue return filed as a past event.
- Turning a module off now takes its dates off the calendar and out of the subscribed feed as well, and turning it back on brings the same records back. Before, a task due tomorrow stayed on the calendar after Projects was off.
- A module part that is turned off now refuses its routes whatever capitals they are written with. Before, `/ARMS/1` still answered after Robot arms was off, because the gate matched the exact text while the router did not.
- On a small phone, a part's Duplicate button keeps its word whole beside Move and Delete
- On a phone, the Locations page fits the screen again: Export and Import sit beside Recently deleted instead of pushing the page sideways
- On a phone, a place's name on the Locations list stays readable: Add inside and Edit share one More menu on each row
- On an iPhone, a full-screen picker and the floor plan's edit panel keep clear of the status bar and home bar, a long machine name shows whole, and Ctrl+K pressed as a page loads opens the palette
- A read notification keeps its buttons at full strength, and a one-item notification says its line once
- On a phone, a list counted in a unit other than each keeps each name readable: the amount control moves under the name instead of squeezing it
- A public page's photo stops being served once its record leaves the page, its photo is replaced, or the page ends
- A public link to a saved view, board or app shows only the records the view's conditions keep, never ones its comparisons exclude
- A public record with a custom look opens again after you go back to the list
- A discussion comment sent again while it is posting is posted once, not twice
- Signing in as another account in the same tab no longer shows anything the previous account had open, such as its connections
- Sharing several photos or links to Cobblr says which ones failed to save, keeps them, and offers Try again for only those.

## 2026-10-16

### Features
- When a member offers to share their AI with your workspace, you can approve or decline it with one tap from the notification, in Cobblr or in your Discord DM.
- Machines now say what firmware and software they run: a printer on OctoPrint, Klipper, PrusaLink, Duet, a GRBL or FluidNC controller or the shop bridge reports it every hour, and any other machine's is typed on its page. The new Versions tab groups machines by model and firmware family and counts them against the version an owner or admin sets, such as "15 of 20 current", with how many are behind, unknown or down for repair, and a place filter. A version nobody has seen for a week is unknown, never current, and each new version is a line in the machine's History.
- A message to Slack, Discord or a webhook that meets a busy or briefly-down service is tried again, sent at the service's pace, and kept with a Send again button if it still fails
- A Part-DB part kept in several storage locations imports as one card with a place for each, with the right amount in each
- Robot arms are machines: an arm's page shows its controller's connection, mode, program and safety state, keeps its programs by revision and family and what it is known to work with, and its station loads, starts, pauses and stops programs. Cobblr never moves an arm or clears a stop. Robot arms are a part of Digital Fabrication you can switch off in Configuration, Modules: features can now have parts, and an off part's screens, actions and assistant tools disappear while everything it recorded is kept.
- Robot arm runs are safe to start: one station has control at a time and the pendant always wins, a run starts only from a person's approval for that program revision and tooling, Start is sent once and never resent, and a run completes only when the arm reports it finished.
- Confirming a scan offers the catalog entry it matches, such as a Lego brick's Rebrickable part, by its part number first, and tapping it files the thing bound to that entry.
- Assets and locations from a Snipe-IT instance import with their serial numbers, asset tags, prices and pictures, and can be kept in sync

### Improvements
- A notification channel can no longer be flooded: each person's channel sends at most so many notices an hour, holds the rest for its next summary and says so once in the bell; sign-in links are never delayed
- Ask Cobb to sync a connected shop now, turn a section's sync on or off, archive or bring back a connection, or test whether it still works
- Ask Cobb whether a label printer is online; it checks without printing anything
- A Discord digest is one card per workspace, naming it and linking back into it; email still gathers every workspace into one message
- Every phone, every shape. On a phone or tablet every button and link is now a finger-sized 44 pixels, so a tap lands where you aim. The top bar and the scan inbox's header keep their height and their words. Turn an iPhone on its side and the header, the page, the Feedback button and open records keep clear of the Dynamic Island and the rounded corners. The phone menu no longer scrolls the page behind it, and a record's Duplicate, Move and Delete row sits at the very bottom, with nothing scrolling beneath it.
- Recount from a tag's page. A tag's page now has Recount these… beside each collection's group of things carrying the tag: it starts a recount of just those things and opens it, the same recount Cobb starts when you ask it to recount everything tagged garage.
- You can sign in with a username as well as your email (set it under Your account, Identity & password), and a kiosk install can make accounts that have no email at all.
- A saved view can look for words. In a view's filters, a text field now offers contains and does not contain: *notes contain warranty* finds "Warranty until 2027" and "kept the WARRANTY card", ignoring case, and what you type is matched exactly, a `%` or `_` included. Does not contain also keeps the records with nothing written there. Cobb can filter the same way when you ask ("which tools mention a warranty?"), and a collection that cannot filter by text says so instead of showing everything. On a phone, a filter's value box now wraps onto its own line instead of running off the screen.
- A saved view can ask for a value, like a recipe's Servings, and its computed fields scale by it
- An owner or admin can choose how members hear a workspace's urgent and high priority events (email, Discord or phone notifications) until each member chooses for themselves

### Fixes
- An operator can now send a job from the machines page, queue it, retry it and print it again. Any member can still stop or pause a machine, but resuming, framing or starting one, or starting a production test, now needs an operator or an admin. A refusal says which permission to ask for.

## 2026-10-15

### Features
- A model on your own computer, reached through an updated edge bridge, can now read personal documents. The read never follows a redirect off that computer and is never written to Cobblr's database or the bridge's disk; an older bridge is still refused.
- Machines now track run hours from completed jobs, so service can come due after a set number of hours.
- Find Klipper, OctoPrint, PrusaLink, Duet and Bambu printers on your network and add them with their address and connection type already filled in.
- A recount can cover a shared saved view (Recount this view…) or everything carrying a tag, and expects only those things, so nothing outside them is marked gone.
- Sales orders gained quotes and numbered invoice PDFs with shop branding, tax, payment dates and email sending by the workspace owner.
- A laser on the network (FluidNC, grblHAL or GRBL in laser mode) is recognised from its address, and a laser connected directly now opens its station page with its sheets instead of showing it unavailable.
- Templates work for your own collections: a Bookshelf, or any collection built on Records, can have templates and offers New from template, the Kind of thing list is the one your workspace has, and the starting values are filled in as fields, not typed as JSON.
- Added controls to delete lists, revoke signup invites, test notification delivery and uninstall file previews.
- Added editing for saved sync connections and print notification channels, plus demo creation, account notices and selected feedback resolution in the operator console.

### Improvements
- A printer's camera shows on its page from its snapshot or stream address, fetched by the server, so a camera on the shop network is seen from anywhere the shop's site opens.
- A project can be started again from another, with its tasks in order and who has each
- A workspace can give the people who run its machines the operator role: they start, send and resume jobs, confirm the stock at a station and record outcomes, without setting machines up
- An order keeps the date the customer wants it, the order list sorts by it, and each order in the list shows what it comes to
- An order due tomorrow or late tells the shop once, and the work that makes an order is linked to it
- The order list can show one customer's orders, and only the paid or unpaid ones
- An admin can sign a worker's phone in by showing a QR, with no password to hand over
- Printers connected by address (Klipper, PrusaLink, OctoPrint, Duet) now show their nozzle and bed temperatures and how far a print has got, live, instead of only every half minute.
- A server can name its product, so a second product's emails and notifications say its own name
- The wall QR sheet says what it lets someone do. Print QR beside a join link now prints one Letter page that names the workspace, the role a person joins as and what that role can do, and the pace, and says to treat the code and link like a password: anyone who has it can join with those permissions. If it gets out, revoke it or make a new code on the Members page.
- The dashboard's "What do you want to do?" panel lists only what you typed into it, and always folds. Things an assistant added over MCP, a share, an offer or an import wait in the scan inbox, and a folded panel says when things you typed are still waiting.
- When a task waiting on another is freed, the person it is given to hears it is their turn, and the rest of the crew is not pinged

### Fixes
- The Groceries board (What's on hand) and the other card views now keep an "Add an item" button once they hold something. Before, the button only showed while the board was empty, so adding by hand meant finding the Table tab.
- A collection of more than ten part types, such as Component Parts or filament types, now has a Find box and lists names in number order, so R10 comes after R2.
- Quotes, invoices and everything dated for a person use their own day, learned from their browser's time zone
- A shopping line you type that names one of your items, "milk" when you keep Milk, now restocks it when you tick it, and the line says so before you do. A title that names nothing, or two things, stays plain words.
- Orders marked arrived no longer miss their stock update when a background receipt check runs at the same time.
- The Going off list now has Used up and Threw out on every row, so an expired or expiring lot can be dealt with where it is listed instead of by opening each item. Each tap takes one off and offers Undo.
- Emails and workspace screens now use the product name configured by the server.
- Recent activity names a scan by what it became and a sync by its source, says a burst once, shows barcode scans, and keeps times out of the code font
- The assistant prints a label for the thing you named even when a photo of it is in the workspace, and never says a change was made after it was refused. "Do I have any delta printers?" is counted without asking a model, "rename the Buying heading" needs no kind of record named when only one has that heading, and a red nightly answer-quality bench now files an issue the same day.
- An assistant adding a thing to a place that does not exist yet no longer names the thing after the place ("Cordless drill in Garage"); it is told there is no such place, and its answer repeats how many it added. A search that finds nothing now says how much of the workspace it looked through, and an assistant asked to do something only a person may do offers to open the screen only where it can.
- Ticking a shopping line, unticking it and ticking it again no longer adds the stock twice. Unticking takes the restock and the purchase back, so a slip of the finger leaves the shelf as it was.
- A task given to you, and your turn on one, now reach a phone left at the default notification level

## 2026-10-14

### Features
- Cobb can change a list's icon, navigation visibility and order, grouping, and the word used for its records, with Undo to restore the previous settings.
- Ran out on a scan keeps a thing at none on hand and puts it on the shopping list, whether Cobblr knew it or not
- Several things in one photo can now be added by name. On a photo in the scan inbox, Split by names… takes one thing per line; the assistant and connected AI tools do the same with a photo and a list. Each thing gets its own row with its own crop, the photo stays as their group shot, and Undo split puts it back as one. When the photo shows a different number of things than you named, it says what it sees and adds nothing.
- A pack split into parts of one kind shows each part as a short row under a parent that says the table and shared values once, and the parent's picture loads on a phone
- You can reorganise lists, choose where unmatched scans land, add several items together, recheck scan matches and undo a past inbox import from the app.

### Improvements
- The assistant no longer invites people, hands one person's things to another, approves requests or shares with a new email address: those grant access or decide what someone may do, so you do them yourself, and the assistant opens the screen for you. An automation set up through the assistant cannot do them either, and its card says who it would message.
- A workspace now sends at most 20 emailed invites, 20 hand-offs and 5 test emails an hour, through every screen and the assistant alike. One more is refused with how long until the next can go. Self-hosted servers can change the numbers.
- The assistant can arrange the dashboard (with Undo), install a pasted blueprint after a card that lists every public page it would publish, and copy an app's records into a full workspace you run. Copying an app's records now needs you to be an owner or admin of the app, as its design always said.
- The assistant can install a bundle from the catalog, turn its optional parts on or off, and put it back to an earlier version, each as a card to confirm. The assistant can also choose which list scans that fit no list land in.
- An owner or admin can ask the assistant to send a test email, as a card to confirm. Inviting someone, handing on their things and sharing with a new email address stay your own steps: the assistant opens the screen for you.
- The assistant can link two records, change a link's note and unlink them, the same way the Links panel does, and each can be undone. A move it makes between lists now brings the custom fields you choose along, as the move on the screen does.
- The assistant can set up, change and remove an automation, not only switch one on or off. Each is a card you confirm, held to the same checks as the Automations screen, and each can be undone.
- An open record shows a change made elsewhere (by the assistant, another device or another person) within a moment, without a reload
- Hide or reorder the panels on each kind of record, under Configuration, Record actions and panels: a 3D printer and a kiln are arranged apart, with Undo, and Cobb can do it too
- On an iPhone the update notice and every bar at the top or bottom of the screen now sit clear of the clock, the Dynamic Island and the home bar.
- The scan camera has one Mode button (Add, Move, Count, Sort) with a menu that explains each, and its area chip shows the whole name with nothing scrolling sideways.
- Undo split now offers Split again, and a pack of different things that is one row has a Split into button that brings back the same parts. The scan inbox's table pickers are one list in one order, and a long list of tables scrolls on a phone instead of closing. On a phone, a split pack's controls fit one row, its parts read in one case, a count button never breaks a word, and "You already have this · 3 on hand" opens the record.
- A scan row's quantity stepper counts every tap on a phone, even a quick run while the last tap is still saving, and the number no longer has a × in front of it.

### Fixes
- Opening the Location picker on a record that already has a place no longer clears that place. The picker's Clear button sat inside its own clickable area, so a click on the field could press Clear; they are now separate buttons.
- Going offline with a form open no longer reloads the page and loses the form. On a narrow phone, a scanned item's destination keeps its name beside a long action, and a refused notification sign-up ends the browser's words with a full stop.

## 2026-10-13

### Features
- A thing you lend out keeps its place and its state. Lend… on its page asks who has it, why (repair, borrowed, or your own words), since when and when it is due back, and the page then reads "With Sam Lee · repair · since Oct 13, 2026 (due Oct 27, 2026)". While it is out it cannot be moved or labelled (each says "Lent to Sam Lee."), a lent printer gets no print-farm jobs, and its service reminders wait. A due day comes up in Needs you and on the calendar. It's back ends the loan with nothing to put back by hand, and back from a repair it asks "Was it repaired?" and logs the repair. History keeps both, and you can tell the assistant ("I dropped the Dremel off with Sam to fix it").
- Choose a nesting layout, pin parts, avoid defects, move copies between sheets, and bring edited layouts back without losing job counts.
- Recount with the phone camera, read a label off a photo for things with no barcode, and print Cobblr labels so the next recount is a scan
- Finish a recount with a preview of what changes, choose what happens to things not seen, and undo the whole recount in one press
- Recount a whole collection across every place: scan barcodes, type a label for things with none, or tap them in the list, and pick it up another day
- Set a scan station to a recount so several tablets and phones count one collection at once, never counting a thing twice
- A recount adds a new spool under its filament type, and asks which type when two read alike
- A shared pattern brings the needs you typed for it, and a change to them reaches the friend's copy.

### Improvements
- The assistant can turn a list's sections on and off ("track lending on my tools"), the same way + Lending does on a record, and Undo puts them back. Asking what can be done with one kind of record now answers with that kind's own actions, a sentence each, instead of every action in the workspace.
- An empty panel on a record is one line: a printer with no service schedule, no spool loaded or no print manager shows a single line and one control, and the form opens when you tap it
- Your workspace can send its email from a Gmail account: connect it on Configuration, Email, and Google lets Cobblr send as that address and nothing else.
- Section headings and field labels read in the app's own face: no more code font or // in front of Tags, History or Service schedule
- A message an automation sends to someone outside your workspace can carry a reply link: they answer Got it or Bought it, and Bought it puts the thing on your shopping list.
- An automation can text someone outside your workspace through your own Twilio account, and the People list can hold a phone number for them.
- A live sync keeps what you changed in Cobblr: each section has an If they differ rule (Cobblr wins by default), a record that could be one you already have is asked about instead of copied, and Preview & sync is a dry run that shows each field before anything is written.

### Fixes
- A Count scan waiting for review offers the count's own actions: Set the total, or The count is right when nothing changed, which finishes the check. It no longer offers Name it for something already named, or Add to Inventory for a count.
- A tool added from the Cataloging Bench, or anything an app or the assistant adds, is named from its real values. Its name no longer carries labels and empty values like "SKU: None Brand: Unbranded", and names saved that way before are cleaned up.
- Something marked gone (lost, sold, cleared by a stocktake) no longer looks like stock you have. Its page says "Not on hand" and what it had, and offers no Use one, Stock arrived or stock use until you bring it back. Its count is kept for Undo.
- A title with a word like None or Null in it is kept whole ("None of the Above" is no longer saved as "of the Above"). Only empty values after a label such as "SKU: None" are cleaned out of a name. Titles the earlier cleanup cut are put back from the name they were created with.
- A yes-or-no setting on an automation is kept as a yes or a no, however it was entered. An automation that adds something to a list "to restock it when checked" now makes a line that restocks, and automations saved before this are corrected when the app updates.
- A Bookshelf copied to another of your workspaces keeps calling its records books, and a scanned ISBN goes to it.
- A Bookshelf copied to another of your workspaces before the matching-collection fix calls its books books again after the next update, unless you renamed it yourself.
- Confirming a count now leaves offered photos off your record until you choose one.
- A guest's app in the portal starts instead of waiting behind a read-only notice.
- A label's short code now always starts with its own list's letter, the one the Codes page shows: a tool in Tooling is t1, not the catch-all n1. A code already given out but never printed moves to the right list the next time you label the thing; one already printed stays as it is and still opens its item.
- A scanned book whose Bookshelf is not set up says Inventory everywhere, and Details still offers Bookshelf as a choice.
- A record page shows only what belongs to it: a 3D printer no longer shows a kiln's firing logs, a bench instrument or a licence's renewal terms. A kiln keeps its firing logs wherever it is filed, and anything with a renewal or an expiry date keeps its Renewals.
- The phone menu stacks from the top again: the community links no longer stretch to fill the screen, an empty group draws no divider, and a menu with every row hidden lists the apps.
- On a phone, the list view of Machines, Assets, Purchases, your own collections and saved views shows each record as a stacked row with its name and its whole state together, and nothing to scroll sideways.
- Members can now undo their own stock taps from a record page.
- A friend's copy of a shared Pattern shows the pattern's fields, what it needs and its PDF.
- "A new version is available" now shows as a bar across the top that makes room for itself, so on a phone it no longer sits over the scanner's buttons or anything else you might tap.

## 2026-10-12

### Improvements
- The admin console's Health tab shows what each kind of request costs the deployment: calls, CPU, time and memory per route, and how often one workspace calls it a day
- A forwarded store order splits its known packs into their spools, names its store from the email, and its cards name tables and shops in words
- A wire can message someone in your workspace when something runs out, through their own notification channels, with words that name the thing.
- Automations can email someone outside your workspace when something runs out, only from your workspace's own email sender, only after an owner turns it on, within daily limits, with a link that stops it.
- Two printers of the same model waiting in your Scan Inbox both get the model's facts, such as its bed size, while each keeps its own seller and upgrades.
- A line split into its parts stays in the inbox as their parent, saying how it was split and whether the parts add up
- A wire can be about one record, a category, a tag or a saved view, and on a stock count about it being used, running out or falling below a number, firing once each time it crosses the line. A stock record's When this runs out… sets one up.

### Fixes
- A bill of sale's preview now reads it as a sale. It shows the VIN, year, make and model and asks only about those, never about a plate, an expiry or a confirmation number a sale doesn't have, and it no longer suggests connecting an AI to find them. A row printing several labels ("Year: 2021 Make: Toyota Model: Tacoma") now gives each its own value, the documentary fee is no longer shown as the price, and after you file the purchase the card no longer also says the original has not been saved.
- An AI connection is marked Verified only once it has answered: saving or testing it now asks one tiny question with the model it will use, not just whether the key can list models. A model your account cannot use, such as an older Gemini model for a new Google account, reads Model not available in the provider's own words, and names the default model that does answer.
- A document in your library that is plainly about a different vehicle or device is no longer offered as a match. When its VIN or serial number isn't this record's, the suggestion says so ("Not this record's: VIN … on it is not this record's") and offers only "Link anyway", never "matched by model". A year that disagrees is shown as a warning beside the match. Existing suggestions are checked again on their own.
- A guest opening a record or scanning a label no longer sets off requests the workspace turns down: the page asks only for what the portal shares with them.
- A long workspace name is cut short in the member portal's header instead of pushing the page wider than a phone
- Saving an AI connection with a model your account cannot use no longer says "The provider rejected this key". The dialog now reads "This model is not available on this key": the key works, and it says to clear the Model field to use the default that answers, or to choose another model. The provider's own words follow, for reference.
- When a lookup finds a name for an item and your photo clearly shows something else, the found name is no longer kept: the item keeps the name it had (or stays unnamed for you to name), the card says what was found and why it was not used, and that barcode's answer counts against it in the shared barcode database.
- A page that fails to load on a patchy connection tries again on its own, then says "Couldn't load this page" with a Retry button, instead of staying blank. A page left open across an update gets the new version the same way.
- Bringing a copy of a personal document, such as a bill of sale, from a connected library like Paperless no longer ends at a refusal. The record says it's a personal document, so it can't be a shared file, and offers "Keep it in your Vault instead": the copy goes into your own Vault, in Personal documents, encrypted and listed on the record. Shared Files still never hold it.
- A thing's Price history lists only orders you actually placed. A draft order made automatically when stock ran low no longer shows as a purchase, and the next draft takes its usual amount and vendor from what you last bought, never from a cancelled order or another draft.
- A document Paperless sends through its workflow now shows on a record you have open without a reload, and the setup says when Cobblr last heard from Paperless
- On a phone, a list line's restock note ("+1 to stock when checked, dated today") sits on its own line under the item and reads as a sentence, instead of being squeezed into a narrow column under I'll get it.
- An inbox item whose short barcode is right but whose name may be another product's now says so: "A shop's own barcode: names found online may be other products", asks "Is this the right name?" with the name read off your photo as one tap, and offers Edit name. "Fix the barcode" appears only when the barcode itself looks misread. The confidence beside Source data is said in words.
- A connection's Off / Just me / Share choice, and "Use this one first", now mark the picked option with a check as well as its colour, and a screen reader announces each workspace's choice as a named group with the pick selected. Every pick-of-a-few control in the app gets the same check mark, and slightly larger tap targets.
- A bill of sale can no longer be filed as the wrong vehicle. A VIN now counts only when its check digit is right; when the VIN on the page won't read, Cobblr asks you to type it instead of naming the car from the year and model printed beside it, and nothing is filed until you do. A car found only by its year, make and model, with no VIN read, is offered as a question ("Is this your Civic? Matched by name; the VIN didn't read; check it's this car"), and nothing is filled until you say it is. If the page reader stops partway, you're told so and can read the page again; it's never treated as a blank page.
- Scanning something you keep by measure, like a plank in metres, now says the unit: "You already have Red cedar timber plank 8.4 m", "You have 8.4 m → 9.4 m" and "+1 m". Counted things keep their plain numbers, so the two never read alike.
- A shop's own short barcode (an 8-digit code starting with 2, like a store's own jar of spices) is no longer looked up in global catalogs, which answered with some other shop's product: a jar of ground cumin was renamed a wool skirt. It is named from your photo and its label, or from how you filed the same code before. Items already named that way are re-checked from their photos, and Revert puts back what they said.
- A stocktake's history says what its counts are in, so a ribbon counted from 8.4 m to 7.9 m reads "7.9 m left, was 8.4 m". What's still here? shows each amount in its unit too, never "8.4 ms" or "3 eaches".
- A pattern's yarn requirement is named for what it is: a yarn you set to blue now reads "Worsted yarn, blue", not just "blue".
- An Inventory CSV import keeps a Barcode column (or UPC, EAN, GTIN), so scanning an imported thing finds it straight away instead of looking it up as unknown. Export writes the barcode back out.
- A thing you put on a list with Add to list, because it was running low, now goes back into stock when you check it off, and the line says how much before you check it. This works with Inventory and Lists alone, without the Groceries bundle, and checking the same line again does not add it twice.
- The Cataloging Bench keeps the photo it captures: the saved tool opens with it. A tool the bench commits, or a record a spreadsheet import makes, now starts its History with who made it.
- A thing said to be a book goes to the installed Bookshelf from the assistant and over MCP, as a scanned book does.
- Bring back on a thing a friend took now says who took it and when, and to ask them to give it back, from every place the button is.
- The Calendar is in All apps and search, and Menu & surfaces can now add any app to your menu, including on a phone.
- On a phone, the OpenAPI page's Copy and Download buttons and the Actions page's long descriptions stay on the screen instead of pushing the page sideways
- The dashboard's Add all offers only what the Scan Inbox would add: a scan held for a look stays out and is named as waiting.
- Workshop stock follows every change to a record's stock straight away: Stock arrived, restocking from another place, opening a unit and the rest, not only Use some. It no longer shows the old remaining amount until a reload.
- A guest lands in an app they can open, and Back from an app opens All apps instead of the same app again.
- Every record's own page shows its History, Links, Photos and files and Tags on a phone too, including a vendor and a customer.
- Every icon button now shows a tooltip that says what it does, and a project's Status, Priority and task size are named for screen readers and voice control.
- Every record an Inventory CSV import makes says so in its own History; the activity feed still shows the import as one line.
- Another editor on a record is told whose Vault holds a private copy, and a teammate's working library is no longer called disconnected.
- On a phone, a list line's edit, fields and remove buttons are each big enough to tap without hitting the one beside it, and easier to see. The icons stay small.
- Members who don't run a workspace can now see which AI answers each kind of work there: Configuration → AI shows them the workspace's order, read-only, under the note that the page is for the workspace's admins. Before, they saw only the note, even when Your account's "Change the order" sent them there. A step that names another member's own "Just me" connection now reads as that person's own connection instead of "A connection no longer here".
- Reminder buttons in Your notifications and the bell now wrap to fit a phone.
- A pattern's materials list keeps the amounts it prints: "100 m of worsted weight wool" now reads as 100 m, and "2 black safety eyes" as two, with no AI. Read again no longer brings back a second copy of a requirement you corrected: your corrected card stays as it is, once, and a card you picked a yarn for gains only what it was missing.
- Changing a thing's Minimum updates its Workshop stock panel at once: the low-stock threshold, Low stock and the Add to list button follow the new Minimum, and History shows the change, without a reload.
- Typed filament scans respect different colours, and match choices distinguish a type from a spool and let you keep a new item.
- A lookup that renames a scanned item wrongly can now be undone. The "Lookup updated" toast has Undo, and every step in the item's History that changed it has Put back, which restores the name, brand, barcode, pictures and kind together. History says what each step changed in plain words ("Looked up again: name changed from … to …") and lists a step that changed nothing once. The assistant can do the same with put_back_scan_item.
- Scanning a barcode you already have shows its card at once: your own records are checked before any lookup goes out.
- On the camera scanner, a message never covers the barcode field, Scan or the buttons under them.
- On the scanner, Used it up lets you choose how many before you confirm, and the card stays until you do.
- A shared Pattern arrives as a Pattern on the friend's Patterns page, with its PDF.
- A shared Pattern now arrives in the friend's share space, and Share is no longer offered for what a share cannot carry.
- Lists of sizes in a picker run smallest to largest: the hook gauges read 1.0, 1.5, 2.0 up to 10.0 mm instead of putting 10.0 mm after 1.5 mm. Fractions such as 3/8 and 1 1/2 in sort by size too.
- Small tool labels on drawer plans no longer clip, and zoom controls make packed tools easier to inspect.
- A scan station in Open or Print mode says what its scans do, instead of File's steps for places and holders.
- The quantity stepper's − and + are easier to hit on a phone, and every quick tap counts, even while the last one is still saving.
- The scan inbox no longer opens with a "Personal documents" box above everything. A registration, bill of sale or insurance card dropped, pasted or uploaded in the inbox opens on its private card by itself, with no failed card or error beside it. To read one on purpose, choose Read a personal document in the inbox's ⋯ menu or at the top of the Vault.
- A yarn's Total length in By weight counts what is left in its opened skeins: two full skeins of 200 m and one opened at 150 m read 550 m, not 400.
- Exit on a View as session returns to the operator console instead of your own dashboard, the announcements page fits a phone, and a person who does not run the instance sees why the console is not for them
- An owner whose friend only opened a shared record no longer has a Stock edit to decide.

## 2026-10-11

### Features
- A barcode someone has filed is filed the next time into the same kind of table, with its product details filled in, without asking the AI.
- A vehicle's bill of sale read under Personal documents can be filed again: File this vehicle purchase puts the car in the scan inbox, offered against the car you already have by its VIN, and records its purchase with the fees and tax kept as charges. The page is read by code first and is never stored.
- A photo of a filament box's label now names the spool and counts it toward its order with no AI, and a mixed pack whose contents are known splits into them with no AI.
- A forwarded order is read on the store's own page: each line takes its variant's photo, and a mixed pack like "10KG / Multicolor" becomes its ten spools, one per colour, each matched on its own against what you already have.
- A mixed pack someone has filed is known to the next person who orders it, so their order splits into its spools with no AI.
- A service visit can say where it is. Pick a place you used before or type the address, and the visit's calendar event carries it, with directions on your phone.
- The batch numbers printed on a spool's box are kept on its record, counted per batch, even though a spool has no use-by date.
- A Vault for personal documents: folders whose documents are encrypted and opened only by the people you choose, never by search, Cobb, shares or anyone else in the workspace, its admins included.
- Records can carry an address. The picker offers the places your workspace already used as you type, and the address becomes the calendar event's Where, with directions on your phone.
- An order line of several now says whether it is several of the same thing or a pack of different things, and you can scan its units in one by one.
- Connect your other Cobblr to copy or move selected items into an existing workspace, with a field preview and separate source restore.
- Upload a registration renewal to update the right car, keep its original in your Vault and undo the change in one step.

### Improvements
- A bill of sale filed from Personal documents now keeps its original in your Vault, encrypted and visible only to the people its folder lists, and the car's page lists it once the car is known.
- Cobb's instructions are now sent in the same order for everyone, so a Claude key reads them from its prompt cache instead of paying for them on every message: a cached chat message costs about a sixth as much. The AI page shows how much of each call came from the cache.
- What's still here? now starts from a table too: tick the rows to check and press it in the bar along the bottom, or take stock of everything in the table that has no place. Scanning a jar's barcode in the sheet ticks its row.
- What's still here? is on What's on hand now: press it in the view's header to take stock of exactly the things the view shows.

### Fixes
- What's still here? works on any place in a workspace of more than 2000 records. It refused every place there, saying to take stock one place at a time, even for a shelf of two.
- Cards no longer lose a link when their state changes. The bell's notifications row keeps Settings after you turn notifications on, and adds Send a test. A token you just made still links to API tokens, where you can revoke it. A turned-off action label can still be renamed. A connected Discord can send a test DM at any time, and a connected bridge has About this bridge, with what it can and cannot do and how to remove it. On a phone, the bell no longer shows a collapse arrow that did nothing.
- Correcting a renewal term, changing a renewal or a service schedule, or pausing its reminders now says what changed with an Undo, and Undo puts the term, its cost and the registration's expiry back.
- A day you pick now stays that day wherever you are. A service visit booked for the 16th no longer reads "due 10/15" west of Greenwich, and its countdown, reminders and calendar entry count from the 16th. Project dates, task due dates, warranty and expiry dates, and link expiries keep the day you gave them, and a date the api sends back is the plain day (2026-10-16) rather than a midnight timestamp.
- A forwarded order keeps its variants, its pack counts and its own pictures. Three lines of one product in different colours no longer all say you already have the white one. A 10-pack line adds ten, on the button and in the count, and a forwarded order's own product pictures go on its lines instead of a web search.
- Using some of a roll or a spool from its record now moves Workshop stock's remaining amount straight away, instead of showing the old amount until you reload.
- Searching for a number finds the things with that number in their name (a barcode, a model), not only the item with that asset number. Type `#` before it to ask for the asset number alone.
- Turning on notifications says what went wrong when it does not finish. When the browser allowed notifications but would not sign the device up, pressing Turn on used to bring the same button back with nothing said. The notifications row and the settings card now say what failed, in the browser's own words where it gave some, and what to do next, with Try again. A page whose background worker never started now says so after a few seconds instead of showing "Turning on…" forever.
- A station card shows each action once, and a scanner works beside the camera. A grocery's card at a station no longer repeats Use one beside Used one or Restock one beside +1, and no longer offers forms like Update an item at a kiosk. A station with its camera on also stopped missing reads from a plugged-in barcode scanner.
- A station's card shows what a press did: after Used up it says None left, and Undo puts the count back, instead of still saying how many you had before.
- A licence or subscription with a renewal date shows its renewal on its page again, with Renewed, the cancel-by date and its paid terms, instead of only offering to add another renewal.
- Mint token can now make a token that opens only your Vault: choose Only your Vault under Opens. It was possible through the API but no screen offered it.
- Anything you do that can be taken back now offers Undo. Pressing Use one on a counted record took one with no way to take it back on screen, though Cobblr knew how. Now every action that can be undone shows Undo in the message that says it happened: a record's own buttons, its rows and chips, adding to a list from a scan, a new choice typed into a field, and the others. Sharing a record, deciding on suggestions and saying a thing is no longer yours had an Undo that was never drawn; it shows now. Marking a whole batch of late things as tossed offers one Undo for all of them.
- Guest searches stopped requesting private identifier matches and showing cached matches from another role.
- A copy brought in from a library opens from its record, including the same document copied into a second record
- Reconnecting a library brings back every link made through it, and Reconnect opens on that same library
- A local AI, such as Gemma 4 through Ollama, now reads receipts, documents and scans in the shape Cobblr keeps: each reading tells the AI the exact layout to answer in, and an answer in any other layout goes to your next AI instead of being quietly dropped. Suggesting the kinds in a photo of an assortment, and the vision check for a failing print, now work too.
- An OpenAI connection now offers and answers with OpenAI's newest models, GPT-6 Luna, Sol and Astra among them, by tier: each picker shows "GPT Luna · latest (6)" first and the versions to pin underneath, and everyday jobs default to Luna's latest while plans and documents get Sol's. It used to offer GPT-4o at best. When a provider's model list cannot be read, the offline list now holds every family's newest model too, Claude's Fable included.
- Notifications in the account menu now always opens your notification settings. While Discord was offered but not connected, it went straight to Discord sign-in instead, even if you only wanted your push or email settings. Connecting Discord stays an offer on that page.
- A message at the foot of the screen no longer sits on the buttons you need. On a phone, a confirmation with Undo (after installing an app, importing a plan) or the "new version available" notice used to cover a form's Create and Cancel, or a link in the phone menu, until you dismissed it. Dialogs, the phone menu and side panels now end above the messages, so you can carry on with Undo still there to press.

## 2026-10-10

### Features
- On a workspace's AI page, an owner or admin can now tap a kind of work to change the workspace's order: each step's connection and model (Haiku to Sonnet, latest or a pinned version), its place, and which connections it asks. Models read in words, such as "Haiku · latest (5.5)", rather than as ids.
- A store's order confirmation email is now read by code, with no AI: the order number, every line named the store's way with its price, and the order's discount, checked against the email's own subtotal. A forwarded email reads the same, and an order email that said "Couldn't be read" is filed by Read again.
- A workspace's owner can now choose who reads its personal documents: on this machine only, as before and by default, or one cloud connection the workspace shares, after agreeing to a sentence that names the company they go to. The choice is recorded in the activity feed and can be changed back at any time, and the AI page now says in one sentence what happens to a personal document, with a link to connect a local AI.
- A production test can now drive and watch the board's pins through a fixture I/O controller on the jig: set, pulse and PWM outputs, read levels, count edges, and measure frequency, duty and edge-to-edge timing against the plan's limits. Nothing that powers or drives the board runs until the plan's "jig closed / board seated" check passes, and every run releases the fixture's pins. A plan can also keep bench camera frames for every check and a short clip of each failure.
- A production test can now use a debug probe (OpenOCD, probe-rs or pyOCD over SWD or JTAG) to check that the right chip is on the board, read and write registers, reset the target, and run the board's own test firmware, judging each result line it prints over RTT, semihosting or UART against the plan's limits.
- A production test's firmware step now reads every byte back off the chip and checks it against the firmware, can erase the chip first, sets and checks AVR fuses, and writes each board's serial to the address you choose. pyOCD joins the supported programmers.

### Improvements
- AI work is now arranged in five kinds: Quick look (the first-look question at the camera), Thorough reading (the scan inbox, documents, receipts and invoices), Cobb chat, Organising and text, and Search. Receipts that arrive by email, order emails and pattern materials are read by your Thorough reading choice, not by the AI you picked for Cobb's chat, and grouping, matching and tidying scans follow Organising and text. Your earlier choice for Reading photos carries over to both Quick look and Thorough reading.

### Fixes
- A workspace whose AI is a Claude key reads receipts again. Reading an emailed or uploaded receipt, planning where things go and naming a scan session sent their instructions in a form Claude's API refuses, so each one failed with "the AI errored". They now reach Claude the way it takes them.
- Claude Haiku 5.5 reads receipts. Claude's newest models refuse one of the settings Cobblr sent with every request, so a workspace on them could not read a receipt, plan where things go or name a scan session. Cobblr no longer sends it to those models, and any model that refuses a setting is now asked again without it and remembered.
- A view with a grouping it cannot use still opens. A workspace that installed Lego before 0.6.5 kept a "By part category" view grouped by a field the bricks do not have, and it opened onto an error. Now it shows your bricks in one list and says which grouping it skipped; editing the view or updating the bundle brings the groups back.
- Lego's "By part category" groups your bricks again, without an update. A workspace that installed Lego before 0.6.5 kept a view grouped by a field the bricks do not have. Cobblr now gives that view the current bundle's grouping when it starts, as long as you never changed the view yourself; a view you edited stays as you left it.
- Turn on notifications from the bell. Open the bell and its top row offers Get notifications on this device; one tap turns them on, and once they are on it says so, with Turn off. On an iPhone or iPad in Safari it says to add Cobblr to your Home Screen first and shows you how. The offer can be put away on that device. Notification settings is now at the foot of the bell, one tap away, and goes by that name everywhere: Your account, the notifications tabs and search.

## 2026-10-09

### Features
- A Shopify store's order email is now read from the store's own order page when the AI can't read it: the order number, the store and every line come from the page, the order is filed in Purchases, and its order page follows from there. An order email already showing "Couldn't be read" is filed by Read again.
- A Shopify store's order email has a View order link to the order's own page, which lists each line with its own tracking number. Cobblr keeps that link, sealed, and reads the page until everything is delivered, so each line gets its own number with nothing forwarded or typed.
- Check my mailbox, on the Gmail connection, runs Cobblr's order rules over your whole mailbox and shows what they would file, store by store and year by year, without filing anything or asking any AI. Mark any email "Is an order" or "Isn't an order" and the next check remembers it for that sender.
- Your receipts address now has a name: every email Cobblr sends about a forwarded receipt comes from that address as "Cobblr Receipts", so typing "Cobblr" in Gmail's To field finds it. The address card can email it to you or add it to your contacts, and Sales' order address is "Cobblr Orders".
- Lay out a room like a puzzle: windows and doors that show their swing, closets, racks you can turn a quarter, the gap to the nearest wall while you drag, and an aisle check, on the floor plan and in Plan a layout.
- A kitchen station that reads jars held up to the tablet's own camera. A station can now show the front or back camera on its Ready screen, so a wall tablet with no scanner reads a barcode you hold up and shows that item's buttons (used one, opened, used up, threw it out) with Undo. Each tablet can flip its own camera, and a station opened from a Home Assistant dashboard shows a button back to it. A printed QR opens the station on a phone: anyone signed in uses it as themselves, and the owner can let people without an account press a few chosen buttons, until a new label is printed.
- What's still here? A place's Stock here panel lists everything still recorded in it. Tick what is really there, set what is left of a part-used one, and the rest is marked gone in one go, with one Undo. Gone means gone by today, not recorded how, so it never teaches Cobblr how long food keeps.

### Improvements
- Your receipts address is now named "Cobblr Receipts" and your workspace's name, as in "Cobblr Receipts Alex's Workshop", from the start, so the contact you save never changes. Only that address ever goes by that name: Cobblr's no-reply mail is just "Cobblr", so typing "Cobblr Receipts" in Gmail always finds the address that files receipts. After "Email it to me", the card says where it went and, if it isn't in your inbox within a minute, to look in Spam or Promotions and mark it "Not spam"; Resend and Add to contacts sit right beside it.

### Fixes
- A pattern photo that could not be pulled says why. When a design's photo could not be saved or attached from its pattern PDF, the panel kept saying "reading the pattern" and every visit started the pull again. A failed pull is now recorded, the panel says what went wrong, and Try again runs it once more.
- A table's things are called what the table calls them. In a Lego workspace the Sets table no longer offers "Delete part" or "Deleted 2 parts" for whole sets, and the Bricks table speaks of bricks. The same goes for every table built on Inventory, Projects, Machines, Assets and Records: the delete buttons and confirmations, the counts after a bulk delete or tag, the page title, the search box, the empty state and a record's own page now use the table's word, including a plural it declares ("2 shelves").
- The AI log now shows which model actually answered each call, beside the one asked for ("gemini-3.5-flash → gemini-3.6-flash"), and the model picker marks a model the provider redirects to a newer one.
- Sharing a thing, or changing a share, no longer answers "their Cobblr has not taken the change yet" when the friend's Cobblr was busy for a moment checking its copy for edits. The change now waits that moment and arrives before you are told it is done.

## 2026-10-08

### Features
- A forwarded shipping notice for an order you already imported now adds its tracking number to that order instead of answering "you already imported this receipt". When a store ships an order in several boxes, each notice's number goes on the lines in that box, the rest show "Not shipped yet", and each box is followed and confirmed on its own. Amazon Logistics numbers (TBA…) are now followed too, for free, with nothing to set up.
- You can add tracking to an order yourself: paste the number or the carrier's tracking link, and tick the lines that are in that box. Each line, and the thing it becomes, now says where it is ("On its way: Amazon Logistics TBA…, expected Oct 11") or "Not shipped yet". An online order with no delivery date says "On its way, no date yet" and is asked about once, a week later.
- Cobblr can now send notifications to your phone and computer, the iPhone and iPad Home Screen app included: turn it on per device under Communication preferences, choose what is worth a buzz (high and urgent by default), set quiet hours, and send a test. A self-hosted server does this with no account and no cost.
- Production test now keeps versioned plans, jig maintenance and per-board evidence, with offline stations and customer test certificates.
- You can now choose your own AI for each kind of work on Your account, Connections, such as Gemini Flash Lite for reading scans and Claude Sonnet for chat, with fallbacks, and every workspace you use follows it unless that workspace chose its own.

### Improvements
- A sheet's leftover now keeps its real shape. A triangle cut across a sheet leaves a triangle offcut that reads about half the sheet, where it read nothing left before, and the sheet drawing shows it as a triangle. Laser nesting places parts into that leftover, and the slivers between nested parts still never count.

### Fixes
- A build that names what it makes, in a workspace without Inventory, now says Inventory is off instead of failing with "something went wrong".
- Stopping sharing something, turning a share's prices or notes off, or changing what a share holds now reaches the friend's copy before you are told it is done, instead of up to a minute later on a busy server.
- A sheet's "percent left" now counts only material you can still cut a part from. A sheet nested with outlines, such as a sheet of stars, used to count the slivers between the parts as left; it now reads the free rectangles at least the minimum usable size, the same area that decides whether a part fits.
- The Inventory list now shows what is left of a sheet or a wearing tool: a plywood sheet reads "About 49% left" and an end mill "7.5 h left", where both used to read 1 with a + button. Rows measured in grams, metres or millilitres show their unit once instead of twice, and millilitres are written "ml" everywhere.
- An order email's own photos and pack sizes are used. Each line of an emailed order now takes the product photo printed beside it in the email, and the web is searched only for a line with no photo of its own. A filament bundle like "10KG / Multicolor" files as ten 1 kg spools, its sibling lines are read the same way, a multicolour pack reads "Mixed (10 colours)", and a code taken off the whole order is shared across the lines to the cent.
- The model pickers for a Claude or OpenAI connection now list the models your own key can use, so Haiku, Sonnet and Opus 5.5 appear without an update. Each Claude family offers "latest" first, which follows new versions by itself, and exact versions to pin underneath. Existing Claude choices now follow their family's latest, and the activity feed says so once, for example "Now using Haiku 5.5. Pin 4.5 in Configuration → AI if you want it back." A call to a model whose price is not known yet shows "price unknown" instead of $0.
- The phone menu keeps each group together. Each heading and bundle family (Machines, Kitchen, LEGO) is now a block: its name, then its own destinations, so a collection never sits under a group it is not in. Your other collections follow under Collections, then the tools. LEGO's Sets and Bricks, or Filament and Vendors, are ordinary rows under their header instead of two links squeezed into one cell. A name too long for half the menu gets the whole row rather than being cut short, and there are no empty cells. The "…" on each row and the unexplained dot are gone from the everyday menu: tap Arrange at the top, or hold a row, to split or join rows.
- Sharing invites someone by email, on one page. Sharing a list, a selection or a record opens one page with everything on it: what is shared, who it reaches now, and a field for someone new that is there from the start, with no extra "Invite a new person" click and no name to fill in. Type their email and choose Share: when this Cobblr can send email, the invite goes to them, and the link shows too in case it lands in spam. Only an account with that address can use it. Leave the field empty to make a link to send yourself, with Copy and, on a phone, Share. Cobb can invite by email too.
- Links into a workspace land where they say. "Change the order" on Your account → Connections now opens that workspace's AI page at Who answers, for the workspace you are in and for any other, instead of the dashboard. The same mistake sent thirteen other links to the dashboard too: the scan inbox's AI link, the low-stock shopping list link, a found sheet, a bench reading's record and screenshot, a kiln's station and pieces, the printer station, ESPHome's place and attached record, the scan result sheet's inbox link, and the station pages' back links. A link to a section of a page now scrolls to that section once it has drawn.

## 2026-10-07

### Features
- New item forms can scan a barcode to fill empty details and a photo, keep what you typed, and show when you already have the item.
- Added logic analyser captures and Riden supply readings, with ESPHome connections and optional confirmed supply control.
- Added embroidery previews, hoop and owned-thread checks, USB exports and measured thread use, plus a Sewing bundle for maintained machines, needles and pattern PDFs.
- Added browser stations for large-format printers with reviewed PDF and image jobs, ink alerts and tracked roll media.
- Every list now has a Share button to share the whole list or selected records, invite someone new and see who it is shared with.
- Personal documents can now be read here or by your local AI, with missing values shown for checking and no cloud AI fallback. Identity, vehicle, insurance, tax, medical, bank and licence papers count as personal wherever they are uploaded, including a bill of sale; a photo of a thing is still identified as usual.
- Added send files to copy or move items between Cobblrs with their photos and documents, a field preview and a separate source confirmation.
- Added approved print batches with a printed count, a bed-clear press for each next copy and one-tap cancellation of the remaining prints.
- Your assistant can change a service schedule, a renewal's term and the overdue reminder cadence, and each change has an Undo. Through Claude or another assistant connected to Cobblr, "change the oil change to every six months", "the inspection actually cost 50" and "remind us monthly about overdue maintenance" were refused, because none of them could be put back. Each now undoes to exactly what it was, so the assistant makes the change and you can take it back. Renewing and ending a renewal still ask you first.

### Improvements
- A Station shows something new at the size it shows what you have. Scanning something new at a Station used to open the scan inbox's whole card, with Add a small button partway down. It now shows the photo, the name it read and how many, with one big Add. Not this? Fix the name corrects the name, and More opens the full card for everything else.
- A custom app block can mark a Lego set built in the Sets table: the Set status action can now set a table's own state field (the Sets table's State: sealed, built or taken apart) to one of its choices, as well as the older free-text status, so a member granted it can update the set from the club's app.

### Fixes
- A grocery scanned without AI files to Groceries, and its brand reads as the brand. A box of pasta could arrive with its name and brand but none of its food categories, and in a workspace whose food table is Groceries it was filed as an Inventory part. Cobblr now reads those categories when they are missing. A brand also drops the company's legal form, so Coca-Cola no longer reads "COCA-COLA SERVICES SA/NV".
- A member can now take an order, plan a purchase, add to a list and do the rest of what the page lets them through Cobb and the API too, without asking for a grant. Anyone can answer their own service job, keep or delete a change the assistant held for them, and undo their own tap, whatever their role; somebody else's still needs a grant.
- A USB or Bluetooth scanner can read a component reel's label. Digi-Key and Mouser reel labels carry separator characters that a keyboard-mode scanner sends as Ctrl key presses, and the scan was thrown away at the first one. A scanner now reads them the same way the camera does, and a Ctrl shortcut you press yourself still works as before.
- Adding from the dashboard never makes a second copy of something you have. A capture of a thing already in your workspace, such as a printer an assistant added again, was offered Add, and Add made a duplicate record. It now sits under "Already in your workspace" and opens its card, where you can add to the one you have.
- A laser batch now uses the free corner of a partly used offcut instead of leaving it empty and reporting room for fewer parts than the sheets hold.
- The signup link now opens the account form, and signing in from a login or signup link takes you to your workspace dashboard.
- Build notes are now available through shared record reads and connected assistants.
- Cobb spent fewer steps finding actions and explained failed changes more clearly.
- The dashboard no longer raises an uncaught error when a guest's record read is refused.
- Laser sheets and prepared cuts now show their drawing names throughout fabrication, and idle laser fleet cards no longer show Pause or Stop controls.
- A grouped table's totals now add up only what adds up: a length or a price per item is totalled as that value times how many you have and called a total, years, sizes and temperatures are no longer summed, and each row's count says its own word (2 balls, 1 skein).
- A guest's pages no longer ask the workspace for things a guest is not allowed to read, so they load without refused requests.
- Counts of words ending in "ss" read right: a laser station says "2 passes", not "2 pass", and the same holds for glasses, classes and the like everywhere a count is written.
- What a member can do on a page they can now do through Cobb and the API too, without asking for a grant: adding a line to a purchase order, shipping or cancelling a sale, logging maintenance, tagging a record and the rest. Anything they cannot do on the page they are refused everywhere alike.
- Prints check the loaded filament before sending, and bridge outages keep cached files visible with a reason.
- Fixed shared suggestions closing before confirmation, missing clash details, and recorded AI turns reporting missing tool results as provider outages.
- Machine stations now use their own machine kind in the title, with a clear fleet link when no station screen is available.
- A drawing's curves keep their size on the way to the laser: a scored ring in an SVG no longer becomes a dot. Adding one capture from the dashboard keeps you there and names the table it went to, with Open; the table setup it opens no longer closes by itself. The activity log says "Robin joined the workspace", and a laser connected under its own name is named once.

## 2026-10-06

### Features
- Added bench instrument readings with units, saved results on boards and test runs, scope screenshots, and confirmed power supply output-off.
- CNC stations now review posted programs, check stock and tools, pause for tool changes and track stock use and bit runtime.
- Ask Cobb to stop a machine and it stops at once. "Stop the laser", "stop that print" or "pause the resin printer" no longer waits for you to press Confirm, and an assistant connected over MCP can stop a machine too. Stopping is all it can do: starting or resuming a machine still asks you first. An owner can also ask Cobb to take a station's Start away ("kick that tablet off the laser station"); that one asks first.
- Added vinyl and craft cutter stations with mirrored heat-transfer cuts, tested material profiles and roll usage recorded in Inventory.
- Bring a whole house into the floor plan from one file: ask any AI with the copied prompt, paste what it gives you, check the preview, and import it as one step you can undo. Export gives the same file back.
- Added direct BJJCZ galvo jobs with lens and source confirmation, a red-guide Frame and station-only Start.
- Added direct Ruida laser jobs with station review, laser-off Frame and independent Stop.
- Tracked licences, subscriptions and warranties with cancellation reminders, paid terms, vendor totals and receipt drafts.
- Returns now live on the order: start one on any line of an arrived order, follow it from Started to Refunded with one tap per stage, and the store's own return emails move the same return. When the store has it, the count comes off the shelf once, and the refund comes off the order's spend once.
- Added copy and move between your workspaces, with a field preview and undo.
- Renewals now keep linked terms with coverage dates, costs and documents, and let you end a renewal without losing its history.
- Added resin printer stations with shared LAN control, liquid stock and wearing part tracking, wash and cure checklists, and Formlabs monitoring.
- Added encrypted secret fields with permission checks and a history entry for every reveal or copy.
- Run several shop machines through one bridge, discover nearby controllers, and use a tablet as each machine station.
- Added board revision imports, component stock checks with attrition and moisture warnings, shortage carts and tracked fabrication purchases.
- Added board inspection photos, rework history, QR labels and tested-batch traceability to reel lots.
- Added assembly work orders with whole-kit reservations and refrigerated solder paste handling against each jar's label limits.
- Added calibrated pick-and-place exports, feeder QR checks and authorized station recording with placed-board stock.
- Added paste reflow profiles, oven bridge drivers and authorized station logs with temperature limit checks.
- A tablet and a barcode scanner can be a station of their own: scan something and it shows what it is, with +1, Used one, Set total and Move to, or the scan inbox's card for something new.

### Improvements
- Choosing one of a few options is now a row of rectangular buttons with the chosen one filled, instead of round radio buttons, on every screen that had them.
- A task's Assigned to field is on every task now, ready to fill in, instead of hiding behind "+1 more field" until someone was assigned. Any field a record's own module declares shows the same way; fields other modules add still wait behind "+N more fields".
- A rack's shelves read on a phone: each shelf's name and how much of it is used ("1,278 of 1,220 mm: 58 mm too wide") sit above the things on it, the rack is drawn the full width of the screen, and a long name is shown whole with one tap.

### Fixes
- A sandbox that was being set up could occasionally fail to start when the cleanup of orphaned guest accounts ran at that exact moment; it now leaves new guests alone for ten minutes.
- Number boxes (cutter settings, laser layout, quantities and others) can now be emptied and retyped instead of snapping to 0, and open the phone's number keypad.
- An assistant's printer lands in 3D Printers. A printer added by an assistant with a bed size could go to Machines or even Inventory and lose the bed size. The values it comes with now choose the list when only one list has a field for them, the same model goes the same way every time, and a name like "Bambu Lab P1P (ex-print farm)" is kept as written.
- A printer added by its name goes to 3D Printers. A printer added as "Bambu Lab P1P" with nothing else could land in Inventory, especially when the same maker's filament sits in Spools. A known printer model now names 3D Printers on its own, and the same maker's filament and parts still go where they belong.
- A station keeps the scans it cannot use yet. Scanning stock at the Stock station before naming a slot, or a sheet at a laser station with no job waiting, opened the record and left the station screen. The station now keeps the scan and says what to do first.
- The activity log names what happened in plain words: a module you turn on is named ("You turned on Lists"), the setup Cobblr does for a new workspace reads "Set up for you", joining names the workspace, and a field is said in full ("quantity", not "qty"). Times read "3 min ago" with the exact moment on hover. Letter tiles for records without a photo are readable in dark mode.
- Cobb no longer says a step was done when it failed. Asked to create a list and file things into it, Cobb could try the filing before the list existed, see it fail, make the list, and then say both were done.
