Now the list is made first and the filing runs once it exists, so everything lands. A step that still fails is said as not done, with its reason and a Retry. The steps Cobb took stay under the answer, open on the one that failed. A list Cobb names, like a record, is a chip that opens it.
- The overdue-reminder default reads properly in dark mode. On Configuration → Notifications, the Maintenance reminders box was a pale card on a dark page. It now sits in the same card as the rest of the page, in either theme.
- An overdue renewal card keeps Renewed as its one main action: the snooze and stop choices sit behind "Pause reminders…". A renewal or service far off or long overdue is said in weeks, months or years ("due in 4 months", "overdue by 6 years"), not as hundreds of days.
- Scanning a distributor package now receives the component purchase it was bought on, and the purchase is marked arrived once every line is scanned in. A fab order with a tracking number now reminds only the person who ordered it.

## 2026-10-05

### Features
- Recent activity now names what changed in plain sentences, links to your things, shows photos and keeps the time in one place.
- Track registration and inspection expiry dates, renew without losing time, and snooze or stop overdue reminders.
- The dashboard groups captures into compact rows with photos, expandable duplicates and an Add all button for each collection.
- Added a floor manager with all machine queues, material dispatch, stock shortages and a shared monitor screen.
- Grouped table views now show counts and totals by one or two columns, with separate totals for each currency.
- Added kiln monitoring, firing logs with linked pieces, saved schedules, shared liquid glaze use and replacement reminders by firing hours.
- Added direct G-code laser jobs with material profiles, a settings review, laser-off framing and station controls without requiring LightBurn.
- Added shared USB and TCP laser connections with reviewed jobs and machine station controls.
- Prepare SVG and DXF laser batches with material profiles, sheet layouts and station approval.
- Added a laser station that checks the loaded sheet, reviews prepared LightBurn files and approves Start beside the machine.
- Added outline nesting for laser, CNC and cutter parts around used sheet areas, with rotation choices and a longer search option.
- Plan a layout for a room before anything moves: drag racks and things into a draft, keep several options and compare them, print one to scale, and apply it as one step you can undo.
- The floor plan draws things at their real size: a printer with an outer size takes its real footprint on the floor and its real width on a rack's shelf, and a shelf says whether what is on it fits ("3 machines, 1,278 of 1,220 mm: 58 mm too wide").
- Added sheet stock with cut outlines, undoable free-area edits, offcut search and QR labels for sheets and loaded spools.
- Cobblr can now sign you in through the identity provider your server already runs, such as Authentik, Authelia, Keycloak or Pocket ID, so one login (and its two-factor) covers Cobblr too. Link it once from your account; after that, Sign in with on the sign-in page takes you straight in.
- Added component package receiving from distributor labels, moisture exposure tracking, bake timers and placement counts with attrition.
- Added Snapmaker U1 and legacy HTTP printer setup with touchscreen pairing, four-tool readings and reviewed Start at the machine station.
- Added touchscreen pairing and reviewed print starts for Snapmaker SACP firmware on 2.0, J1 and Artisan.
- Added stock shapes for sheets, rolls, spools, liquids, wearing tools and counted supplies, with job use, replacement reminders and QR loading at a browser station.
- Your own assistant reads your notes. Ask Cobb, or an assistant you connect over MCP, how many parts mention something you only wrote in a part's notes, and it counts that part instead of saying none. Nobody else's assistant reads them: a friend you share with sees a note only if you turned Private notes on for that share, and a field you limited to some people stays limited. Configuration, AI now says this under What Cobb reads.

### Improvements
- A connected service that answers straight away, like a parts catalog search, now shows its results as soon as it answers. Cobblr used to ask it a second time before showing anything, which added a request and at least a second to every search.
- A connected service can take several files in one input, so a design service can be handed the exact parts to fit (a bearing, a screw) and build around their real shapes. A file sent under a name the service does not take is now refused with the names it does take, instead of being quietly left behind.
- Every − and + counter now has a number you can tap and type with the phone's number keypad (the inbox row's quantity, stock counts, copies to print), instead of only flashing the buttons.
- The app downloads about a quarter of what it used to before the first page appears: each section, dialog and heavy tool (the barcode reader, QR codes, Markdown, PDFs) now loads when you open it, and the section you point at starts loading before you click, so it still opens at once. On a slow phone connection the dashboard appears about two seconds sooner and the scan inbox about four.

### Fixes
- A scan card says only what actually went wrong. A scan with no name used to say "The AI could not read this one" even when no AI had been asked, which looked like an outage. Now a card names a failure only when one was recorded: the AI could not be reached, its allowance ran out, or its answer could not be read. Otherwise it says "Not named yet" and offers to identify it or name it.
- More failures now say so instead of leaving the screen as it was: resending the verification email, a photo that could not be made the cover, refreshing a printer's files, the backup destinations list, labels that did not queue (one at a time or in bulk), a catalog match or a parent link that did not save on a new item, and items that did not move or could not be removed in a bulk action on the scan inbox. A scanned location that could not be nested no longer says it was. Saving a bundle's features or a table's word no longer adds another "bundle installed" to the activity feed.
- Scanning something kept in several places never guesses the place. When Cobblr could not read where an item is kept, a scan could add to whichever place it found first, and picking the right place could be refused. It now says it could not check and asks you to try again.
- A suggested place says why. A scanned item could show a place with only the word "suggested" beside it, and some of those places came from nothing but where most of your general inventory sits. A place is now suggested only when something about the item backs it (similar things are kept there, or it needs to stay cold), and it always says why: "3 similar items are stored here". An area you set on one scanning session no longer carries into the next one.
- An Amazon sticker is read off its label. Scanning the barcode on an Amazon fulfilment sticker used to say "We couldn't find this barcode. Take a photo" although the scan already had the label. It now names the item from the product title printed under the barcode, read off the scan itself, and searches its picture. When the title cannot be read, the card says it is an Amazon label and offers any text it did read as a one-tap name. Other codes no catalog looks up (a store's own label, a book's ISBN, a link) say what they are too.
- Cobb never shows you an error meant for a program. When something goes wrong behind a request, Cobb says so in a plain sentence and tells you nothing was changed, instead of showing a code error, a technical message or a block of data.
- Finished jobs stopped appearing as running outside Cobblr on the floor after their results were recorded.
- Laser Library cards now show SVG previews, bridge pairing waits until Desktop is online, and the floor shows clearer machine names and production guidance.
- Laser jobs kept their nested layout and sheet name in the final review, with clearer station guidance and machine names.
- Laser dispatch now starts with one part and readable layer names, while stations show clearer sheet previews, running progress and job facts with a primary good-result action.
- Sheets added through the Sheets bundle appeared on the floor manager with their remaining area and became available for material dispatch.

## 2026-10-04

### Features
- Libraries from other modules can now show their own webhook setup and match items from their notifications.
- Added bin lights through WLED or Home Assistant, with grid mapping, Show me, and picking that lights the next bin.
- Paid, fully fulfilled sales orders can close when delivered, with a notification, Undo and a setting to keep them open.
- Added ESPHome devices for bin lights, sensor readings and switches without Home Assistant.
- Filament spools show their use and remaining length, accept scale and firmware measurements, and can send Cobblr counts back to Spoolman.
- Sales orders gained invoice PDFs saved locally through your browser and CSV export for your accounting tool, both with recorded payment state.
- Added a native Mac bridge installer with automatic updates, LightBurn pairing, and checks that explain what the laser needs before a job.
- Added LightBurn file upload and live status through its REST API, with cut settings and material library details to review before approving each laser job.
- Added NFC tags for record labels, with Android writing, bulk writing from Labels, tap scanning and a tag-link copy option for other phones.
- Added NFC stations that file scanned things into a place, write or link tags from any phone, and let you review and undo a session.
- Sales gained automatic order intake from WooCommerce and a free email feed, with SKU mapping, source links, duplicate prevention and failures shown in Settings.
- Record actions now sit in menus you can arrange, with your own pinned shortcuts and a single Actions sheet on a phone.
- Sales reads order emails into drafts you can review and confirm, and keeps each order in its own currency.
- Makes can now design a request, keep its source and preview for review, and resume slicing after a restart while requiring approval of the exact print shown.

### Improvements
- The scan inbox's session buttons now say where things go ("Add 2 to Sets", "Turn on Sets and add 2"), with a line under the header listing the destinations, and "Install" is gone from the wording.
- The line under a scan session's header now names the place filing will use ("Spices, in Kitchen › Spice rack (suggested)") before you press the button.

### Fixes
- A failed human check when starting a sandbox now shows a message with a retry instead of a raw error page, and a check we could not run says it is on our side.
- A scanned value now keeps every source that contributed to it: the card's headline names the one that decided it, Source data lists them all, and the two can no longer disagree.
- An installed app that was left on an older build now updates itself instead of showing "Importing a module script failed", and the error page offers "Get the latest version".
- Feedback from a try sandbox is now marked as having no one to reply to, so the console no longer offers a reply that goes nowhere, and a sandbox visitor can leave an email to be told when it is fixed.
- "Checking this against your photo…" now shows only while that check is actually running, and never stays beside a finished result.
- Opening Cobb beside a page no longer repeats the same cloud AI-plan notice.
- Fixed Home totals and low-stock warnings to include the whole collection instead of stopping at the first page.
- Added named tooltips to the Home mark and the tip-dismiss icon.
- When a barcode only names the brand, the name is now completed from the front label in your photo (for example "Zero Carbon Tea Pumpkin Spiced Chai"), including when you add a second photo afterwards.
- The phone menu now lines up its rows and dividers, with full-width section headings and a plain More apps row.
- Record actions explain what they do, placement and archiving have clear separate controls, and photo history keeps its picture in the detail card.
- Record creation and editing forms now use a shared shell that widens for more fields and packs short fields together.
- Scanning stock you already have now skips zero in the change stepper, offers one-tap use and waste actions with Undo, and keeps adding a separate record secondary.
- Added named tooltips to the phone menu controls and the side panel Close button.
- Orders now show stock and production shortfalls before fulfilment, schedules distinguish estimated finishes from completed work, and print jobs explain missing inputs and use the shop's machine names.
- Recently committed now shows a split photo as a header with its filed pieces under it and counts only what was filed, and sending a session back names any item it could not return, with the reason, on its own row.

## 2026-10-03

### Features
- Sharing with a friend whose Cobblr is somewhere else no longer means editing the invite link by hand: the invite asks where their Cobblr is and makes the link for it. Someone with no account there yet can make one from the invite and comes straight back to it, ready to accept.
- Selected records can now have a built-in or custom field set or cleared together, with skipped rows named and one Undo for the batch.
- Connection tests gained amber warnings that explain the limitations of a working connection.
- You can now hand things over to someone you share with. Give to… on a thing's page (or on ticked rows) offers it; it arrives in their scan inbox with its photos and details, where they Take, Pass or choose Maybe later. Once taken, your record is marked given away to them, with its history kept, and when they file it, it says it came from you. They can send it back within 14 days, and you can withdraw an offer until they answer.
- Added Hide empty to Locations and Tags, with a count of what is hidden and your choice remembered on this device.
- See what each field was before an edit and restore a saved version from the record's history.
- Parts and places now share the photo picker for pasted images, image links, web search and AI Pick.
- Lend something to the person you share with and it appears in their shared workspace until you get it back. Any record can now be shared on its own: Share with… on its page or in a list's selection bar, and its page says who it is shared with, with Stop beside it.
- Added a daily low-stock summary with counts and minimums, optional alerts for existing workspaces, and a way to leave individual items out.
- Prices kept their original currency, and totals converted them into your workspace currency at purchase-date rates.
- Adding Sets offered BrickLink as an optional service, with existing BrickLink data kept out of the sidebar.
- Added photo editing to every place page, with camera, upload, paste, web search and a gallery, and the main photo appears in the tree and on shapes with photo fill on the floor plan.
- See what everything in a place cost, including sub-places and containers, and download an insurance list as CSV or PDF.
- Floor plan outlines now use workspace tag colours and names, with existing manual colours kept as a Custom option.
- The label already on your things can now be a public door: turn on "Its things' labels open it" for a public page, and a stranger who scans the label sees that thing as the page shows it, while you and your members still get the full record. Booth mode makes a page, its links and its labels private again on their own at a time you choose.
- A public page can have a memorable address, like /p/your-workspace/my-printers, that people can remember and type. It is off until you set one, shows what the page shows, and ends the moment you clear it.
- Choose how a public page looks: which fields a thing shows and in what order, a few words and links shown with every thing, or your own HTML for the whole look, which is only ever handed what the page shows. A page also counts how often its labels are opened, and nothing about who.
- A thing on a public page now says so: a Public chip beside who it is shared with, or "Public until Sun 6 pm" in booth mode. Public pages wear your workspace's colours and logo, with an accent of their own if you like, and search engines are told not to list them unless you say they may. A public card with no links no longer shows a stray 0.
- Make a public page of your things: choose which with the same rules as a share, then give it as many links as you like, each with its own detail and its own end. Anyone with a link sees only what that link allows, with no account; revoke a link or give it a new address at any time.
- Deleted records can now be restored with their original details and contents from Recently deleted for 30 days.
- Edits suggested by someone you share with now wait where you look: on the thing itself, on a new Suggested edits page, and in Needs you. Accept or reject each one, a whole record's or a whole person's at once, and undo either. When you changed the same field too, you choose whose value stays. You hear about their edits once per burst, and they hear your answer.
- Something the person you share with adds to the shared workspace now reaches you: take it into your own collection or leave it. When you both changed the same thing, their copy tells them what yours says now. An edit they make right after you decide on the last one is no longer lost.
- What the person you share with changes now comes back to you. By default you review each edit first (Accept or Reject under Their edits); for someone you trust, let their edits change your records at once. Either way your history names them, each applied edit can be undone, and when you both changed the same thing it waits for you instead of one overwriting the other.
- A shared thing now has one conversation across both workspaces: what the person you share with says about their copy is in your thing's Discussion, and your replies are on their copy, each marked as coming through the share.
- See a share the way the other person sees it: View as in Settings, Sharing opens their shared workspace in a new tab, read-only, for half an hour. They can see that you looked.
- A share now holds the things you choose. What's shared picks whole collections, things whose field says something (loaned to Vince), or things one by one, and shows how many match as you build it. Their copies stay in step as you change them, and prices, private notes, real places and restricted fields stay out until you turn each on. Any field can now be marked Private.
- You can now share part of your workspace with someone, even when their account is on another Cobblr. Settings, Sharing, Share with someone makes an invite link; they accept it on their own Cobblr and get a workspace of their own for it, named after you. Each share shows when it last synced and what is waiting, keeps trying while the other side is offline, and either of you can end it.
- A thing you sold, gave away or threw out can now say so. No longer mine… on its page asks how it left, when, for how much and to whom; it then leaves your lists and totals (an asset no longer counts in "paid X in all"), stays findable under Gone and in search, and its page reads "Sold 2026-09-12 for $180 to a neighbour · kept 3 years 2 months". Bring back undoes it, and you can tell the assistant ("I sold the old Ender 3 for $120").
- Sets and Bricks share one navigation row, and you can join or split collection rows from their menu or by asking Cobb.
- Turn on Tidy to get up to five overnight ideas for organising your workspace, with review, Undo and remembered dismissals.
- Added warranty and purchase dates to the calendar and iCal feed, with separate filters and optional 30-day warranty reminders.

### Improvements
- Ask Cobb about suggested edits. Cobb can now tell you what the people you share with have suggested ("what has Vince suggested?"), grouped by thing and by person, and act on it: "accept Vince's suggestions on the CR-10" or "take what Vince added" comes back as a card to confirm. The same works for assistants connected over MCP.
- Modules gained alternate names that keep existing workspace data, and retired modules now explain why they are no longer available to enable.

### Fixes
- A big collection's page loads. On a workspace with a few hundred assets, the assets list and each asset page could fail with "URI Too Long" because the page asked about every record's picture in one very long address. It now asks in the body of the request, so a collection of thousands loads, and every record shows whether a picture is suggested, being looked for, or was not found.
- A photo the free photo identify could not read straight away, because every AI it has was busy or used up for the night, now says "Identifying, back shortly" instead of saying it could not be identified. Its name arrives on the card by itself once an AI answers. A photographed receipt waits the same way, and its lines are filed when the read comes back.
- Made Inventory tabs distinguishable from the app menu for screen readers.
- Added New from template to item lists, Add a thing, and scanner filing, with editable defaults before saving.
- Local thinking models answer in seconds. A model run with Ollama that can "think" (such as Qwen 3) used to think through every request, taking up to a minute where a few seconds would do. Cobblr now asks it to answer directly. An empty reply from a local model is treated as a failure, so the next AI you have set up answers instead.
- Containers on a floor plan can now be opened with the keyboard, with their full names available even when the drawing is too small to show them.

## 2026-10-02

### Features
- Every dropdown is now one picker you can type in: it opens with what your records already hold, typing filters it, and a value that is not on the list is simply the value, remembered for next time with an Undo. Brand, vendor and service jobs offer what you used before, and a vehicle or a 3D printer suggests its usual service jobs.
- A hint like "8x S-12415" on a scanned card now sets the count to 8 and looks up S-12415 itself: the card is renamed to what the part number is, its picture is searched for by the part number, and if nothing is found the part number is kept as the model number. Scanning the same part number in another place offers Add as another place, so eight bins in the kitchen and four in the garage are one item with a row for each place, never a second item. The photo search no longer picks up words for what is inside or beside the thing, like the roll of tape in a stack of bins.
- A scanned bill of sale is now read the way a form is and checked before it is believed: the VIN's check digit, the year against the VIN, and the money adding up. A VIN that passes names the vehicle and finds the one you already have, so a misread model line still gives "2019 Honda Civic Hatchback EX" with It's this one. When a check fails, the page goes to your AI's document reader and its answer is checked the same way, and whatever still does not add up is shown on the card to check. A name that is not words is never searched for a picture.
- Ask Cobb to make a list and file your scans into it. Tick items in the scan inbox and say "create Mugs and file these in it": Cobb offers one plan that makes the list, kept like a kind you already have, and files the ticked items into it, with one Confirm. Undo puts the items back in the inbox and removes the empty list, and an empty list's own page now offers Remove this list.
- A vehicle fills its empty Model and Year from its name and VIN, never over what you typed. Adding a field that means the same as one the kind already has is noticed before it is made, and a seller you keep noting is offered as a field of its own, once.
- Money reads as money everywhere. An asset's Paid typed as 4300 shows as $4,300, not a bare 4300, and it shows the same way in lists, exports, the assistant's answers and notifications. Every price and cost field takes 4300, 4,300 or $4,300.00 alike, and there is a new money field type for your own fields.
- Drop or paste a bill of sale or a receipt on a record's files and Cobblr reads it onto that record. It files the document, links a purchase, and fills what you paid, when and where you bought it and the fields the paper names, such as a vehicle's VIN, make and model. It never writes over something you typed: where the paper disagrees it says so, with Use it beside the paper's value. Undo takes back what it filled. An inbox card for a bill of sale whose VIN matches a vehicle you already have offers It's this one, which does the same. Parts now have a Photos and files section of their own.
- A floor plan or drawer can show a grid to place things by. The grid button turns it on for that plan and it stays on; its cells follow the plan's units (inches, feet, centimetres), sized so they stay readable on a phone, with a Gridfinity 42 mm option for drawers. With the grid on, boxes and pinned things snap to it.
- A floor plan or drawer can group the things pinned on it by one of their own fields, such as a drawer of sockets by drive, with each pin tinted by its group and a legend saying which is which. Measured values now read lengths too (10 mm, 3/8 in, 1-1/4 in), and the new Hand Tools bundle fills each tool's size, drive and type from its name.
- On a phone, tapping a pin on a floor plan opens a card with the thing's whole name, its size, how many and where, with Open to go to it. A place can wear a colour on its plan (set from Edit layout, or ask the assistant to make the chest drawers red), and a legend under the plan says which places carry each colour.
- The Contents of a kind of record can be called what you call them, like Parts for a computer or In the bag for a backpack: a pencil on the panel heading renames it for every record of that kind, and the word follows into messages, history and the assistant's answers.

### Improvements
- A bill of sale, from a person or a dealer, is now read as one vehicle at its unit price, with no AI, and its VIN, make, model and year fill the vehicle's fields. Fees, taxes, title and registration, documentary and processing fees are kept as charges on the purchase on any receipt and never become inbox items or go looking for photos. An extra like wheel locks stays on the purchase with Make it an item beside it. A vehicle you already have is offered by its VIN, whatever you named it.
- When Google's newest Flash is overloaded, Cobblr asks an older Flash. Google's free tier sometimes turns away every version of its newest Flash model for twenty minutes or more while an older version answers fine. Cobblr now steps down to the older versions Google still serves (found from Google's own list, so nothing breaks when a version is retired), remembers which models were busy for a few minutes so the next request does not wait on them again, and never reads a document with the lighter Lite model.
- Cobb plans deletes and removals with your AI's stronger model. When you ask Cobb to delete something, remove a feature or make a new list, the plan is now made by the fuller model of the AI you chose for chat (Flash rather than Flash Lite on Google's free tier), and it never steps down to the lighter one when busy. Questions and everyday chat stay on the quicker model.
- An inbox session now says where it came from once, in its header. A receipt that read cleanly is a small chip there ("eBay email · 1 line") that opens it, and the full document card appears only when the document needs you. Documents are named by what they are ("Email from eBay, Aug 12"), an assistant's adds read "Added by" its connection's name instead of a note on every card, and a code off an emailed receipt says it was read from the email, never from a photo.
- Things your assistant adds now land where you already keep the same thing: two identical printers both go to 3D Printers, never one there and one in generic Machines, and Replay keeps them there. What it said about each one stays on the card in that list, who it came from shows beside where ("3DPrintopia 2026 · RainMotorSports"), and Replay fills the seller from the notes of things added before. A value it could not keep is reported back to it with the reason instead of being dropped.
- One request to Cobb is one plan with one Confirm. "Delete this and Yarn as a whole" used to come back as separate cards, one of which kept the records you asked to remove and still counted the one the first card deletes. Now it is one card with its steps in order, each worded after the ones before it and saying whether it can be undone, and one Confirm that runs them all. An earlier card about the same thing folds away as replaced, so there is never more than one Confirm for what you asked.
- A bin or shelf that holds one thing now counts it in one row: minus 10, 5 and 1 on the left, the count in the middle (type over it to recount), plus 1, 5 and 10 on the right.
- When Cobb's panel leaves the selection bar too little room, its actions show as icons with their names on hover instead of scrolling out of sight, and the words come back when the panel closes.
- What you tick is what Cobb is talking about. Ticking rows on a list, the Scan Inbox and saved views included, now puts them in Cobb's context straight away, shown as the "About:" chip above his message box, so "file these in Mugs" means exactly the ticked ones and Cobb never asks which. Before, you had to press Ask Cobb in the selection bar first. The × on the chip drops them, and unticking them does too.
- Rows you tick on a list stay ticked when you refresh the page, in that browser tab, along with the selection bar and Cobb's chip. They clear when you press × or when a bulk action finishes (Undo ticks them again), and a row that has gone since drops out of the selection on its own.

### Fixes
- A bill of sale for something you already have now goes on it however the card found it: no more "+1 more" for a car's paperwork, and Paid, Purchased at and Bought from are filled along with the VIN, mileage, year, make and model. A value that failed its check is shown under Please check to correct and never written, and when the AI's daily limit is used up the card says so and reads the page again later.
- A record finds its library item by an outside id at any time. A cartridge given its IGDB id after RomM was connected never found its ROM when the ROM was titled differently, because that match was only tried once, when the library was first read. Now the ids a library's items carry are remembered, and a record holding one finds its item whenever it is matched. Libraries already connected are read again once to fill this in.
- A sale form is kept whatever the AI calls it. When the AI read a dealer's sale form correctly but called it an invoice, the whole read was thrown away. An invoice, a bill of sale, a receipt, an order, a retail order and a purchase agreement are now read as the same thing: the record of a sale. A page the AI names as something else, such as a manual, is no longer taken for a sale.
- Cobb offers changes for you to confirm on every AI connection. Chatting with Cobb through a Claude subscription, a change that needed your confirmation was refused with "this connection has no way to show you a confirm step". Now Cobb offers it as the same card with Confirm, whichever AI is answering, and nothing changes until you press it.
- Cobb's offers come back after a reload, and on your other devices. Reloading the page could leave Cobb's offers as plain titles with no card and no Confirm. Now every offer is kept with your conversation: an offer you have not answered comes back with its details and its Confirm, one you confirmed or cancelled says so, and the same conversation opens in another tab or on another device. Pressing Confirm twice, or in two tabs, does the change once.
- Cobb takes a choice off a list when you ask, and always answers. Asking Cobb to remove a choice from a field that several kinds of record share used to come back with "entity_kind and field are required" and directions to Settings, and asked again, nothing at all. Now Cobb finds the list that holds the choice, offers the change with how many records still use it, and every reply is in plain words.
- On a scan inbox card, Acquired from now opens the same two-column picker as the record page, with where it came from on the left and the seller on the right, and its name shows once. When an assistant adds something "acquired at 3DPrintopia 2026 from Jai", Jai is kept as the seller instead of being dropped.
- A drawer with things pinned in it no longer says "Nothing drawn" over them, and on a phone pins that sit close together show their sizes ("10 mm", "13 mm") instead of writing over each other. A drawer seen from above now marks its back and front.
- Place items opens on the room or container the floor plan was showing, and a thing you picked up stays in your hand when you switch to another layout, along with "Show things that have a place".
- Building a workspace from a description works again on Google, OpenAI, a local Ollama and the Claude bridge. Since building got its own AI job, every AI except Anthropic answered it with "not implemented", so the build failed before the model was asked. It now runs on each of them.
- The model menu lists what each connection actually has. A local Ollama, a self-hosted OpenAI-compatible server or a bridge now offers the models it reports, so a Claude bridge shows Sonnet, Opus and Haiku and no longer lists models it does not have. If a connection cannot be reached, the menu says "Couldn't list models" with a Retry. The AI settings accept any model your connection reports, so a model you pulled yourself can be chosen. Model names show in full, with the description underneath.
- The picture you see is the one kept. Web search results in the scan inbox now load through your workspace, and Use this image, a tapped tile and Pick best keep that same copy, so a site that blocks a second request can no longer refuse a picture on your screen. A catalog photo counts only once it is saved; one that could not be saved says "Couldn't save this one" on its card. A record photo that arrives broken or cut short is fetched once more, and if it still will not draw the tile shows the record's initial instead of an empty square.
- "These" means what the list shows. On the scan inbox filtered to one session, a search or a filter, asking Cobb to "file these in Mugs" with nothing ticked now works on the items on screen. They ride along as a "11 inbox items shown" chip you can drop. Ticked items still win, and an unfiltered inbox is never sent along.

## 2026-10-01

### Features
- A computer, a machine or any asset can now be built from parts you have recorded: install some of a part from its page (2 of 4 RAM sticks), see each part's value and the total, take parts back out with undo, and hide what has nothing on hand from the Inventory list.
- Add a product straight from a shopping app. On Android, Cobblr now appears in the share sheet: share a product from Amazon or any shop app and it lands in your scan inbox. On iPhone, copy the share and paste it anywhere on the inbox. Either way, the product's name comes with its link and is kept as the item's name, so a shop page that refuses to be read no longer leaves you with an unnamed link. Anything the lookup finds (a photo, the brand, the model) is added, but your name is never replaced with a worse guess.
- An AI assistant connected to your workspace, or Cobb, now adds the things you own through the scan inbox, the same way a scan does. It checks whether you already have it, picks the table, fills the fields it can, adds a picture and a place, and tells the assistant which fields are still empty so it can ask you. Anything it adds waits in the inbox marked "from your assistant" unless it files it straight away, and it never files a second copy of something you already have.
- Ask Cobb to remove a bundle. "Get rid of Yarn and everything in it" now offers to remove the Yarn bundle, with a card that says what goes: the table and how many records are deleted for good, the fields, views and automations it brought, and any feature switched off because nothing else uses it. Ask to keep the records and the card says that instead. Before, Cobb could only offer to turn off a feature, which a bundle's table is not, so the offer failed when you pressed it.
- Your receipts, manuals and warranties in Paperless-ngx now show up on the things they belong to. Connect Paperless once under Your account → Connections → Add a service (directly, or through your bridge if it runs at home), and every record gets a panel with the documents that match it, already found by the time you open it. Link one with a tap and open it in Paperless from there; Cobb and assistants connected over MCP can find and link documents too.
- Sales can now follow the parcels you send: turn on Follow parcels I send in Sales settings and each shipped order is marked delivered when the carrier says so, using your own tracking connection, never a paid one of Cobblr's.
- Your games in RomM now show up on the cartridges and discs they belong to. Connect RomM under Your account → Connections → Add a service, and each game you own gets its ROM matched by title and platform, or by IGDB id, with its cover, its platform, whether it plays in RomM, and a link to open it there.
- Measured values: a new field type for values like 4k7, 100n or 16 V. However a value is typed (4k7, 4.7k, 4700 Ω), it is one value. It shows with the best prefix, search finds it by value and unit (10k is never the 10 kHz crystal), filters compare it and lists sort it numerically. Values typed as text before this become searchable without being changed.
- Anything that holds things, like a backpack or a toolbox, now works as a place: headphones in a backpack read "Backpack, in Desk drawer" in the list, search and the assistant, and the backpack can be picked wherever a place is chosen.

### Improvements
- The Activity page's details now say what each entry changed or recorded in the same words as a record's History, under each field's label, instead of a block of raw data with internal names and ids.
- Moving several parts no longer opens a dialog over the list. Move to… opens from the selection bar itself: search your places, pick a recent one or something that holds things, confirm in the same box, and undo from the toast. Tag on the bar opens the same way on Inventory, Assets and Machines. Dragging a sidebar row now stays in the sidebar instead of sliding off to the right, and the search icon at the foot of the sidebar sits evenly beside Scan and Build.
- Asking Cobb to delete a record and turn a feature off gives you two cards. With a record above the box, "delete this and yarn as a whole" used to come back as one card that only turned the feature off, titled "Turn off a feature" with "The feature, by its name in the app (e.g. Shipments): yarn" underneath. Now the record gets its own card, "Delete (the record's name) (permanent)", and the feature card reads "Turn off the Yarn feature" and says what that does: it leaves the menu, its records are kept, and the fields and automations it brought are removed. Every card now shows an action's details as plain labels, without the hints written for Cobb.
- Record forms waste less space: short fields like Year, Trim, License plate and Mileage now share a row (up to four on a computer, two on a phone) even when they carry help, which stays folded under its own field. The "which one, specifically?" box no longer appears under every dropdown: only the field that says where a thing came from offers a detail, as a small pencil, and a detail already saved is shown beside its value.
- A record's History now says what each edit changed: every field, custom ones included, from what it was to what it became, under the field's own label, with money, dates, places and linked records in words. One person's edits within half an hour are grouped, a line per field. A photo change names the record and where the picture came from, with a card showing the picture, its file and when it changed. Built-in fields can now carry a label (Paid, Bought from), and editing a build or a catalog now shows in its History.
- You can paste a copied picture or a screenshot wherever a photo can be added: a record's photo, a part's gallery, Add photos, a knowledge image, design files, the Files page and more. It goes to the place you last clicked, and pasting text into a field is never mistaken for a picture.
- AI Pick now uses the photo it chooses, with Undo, and the photo it replaces stays on the record. A record's photos and files share one row with the main photo first and starred, each photo labelled Your photo or Catalog photo, and any photo can be made main. The photo search remembers its results after a reload, outlines the one in use and keeps the AI's mark, and the chosen tile looks the same in every picker.
- Any app that is on can be added to your sidebar from All apps with its pin (filled when it is in, an outline when it is not, with an Undo), and a sidebar row can be dragged between Collections and Tools and stays there. Labels shows its print queue as a badge on its own row instead of a second row, the phone menu no longer ends on a half-empty row, and Search is an icon at the end of the Scan | Build row.

### Fixes
- A monthly task on the 31st comes back to the 31st. It lands on the last day of a short month, then returns to the 31st when the month has one, instead of staying on the 28th.
- A scanned PDF, such as a bill of sale from a scanner, is now read from the photo of its page instead of failing with "no line items", and its inbox card says it was read from the scan. When a read fails, the message is about what you gave it: a PDF is never told to check the picture.
- A bundle suggested on a page now says why. The Lists page offered "Pairs well with: Wine" with nothing saying what wine had to do with lists. The box is now Works with Lists, and each bundle in it says what it would do there, in its own words: Wine reads "Buy again on a bottle puts it on your Shopping list." A bundle that cannot say what it does on a page is not suggested there. The × puts one away for you for good, and a bundle your workspace uninstalled is not suggested again.
- Cobb's offers survive a page reload. A reply that offered changes came back after a reload as one bare line, such as "Turn off a feature", with the cards gone. Now the conversation comes back as you left it: offers you have not answered can still be answered, and ones you did answer show what they did, with Undo.
- Put away no longer plans milk or anything else that must be kept cold into a pantry with the bread. A cold thing goes to your fridge or freezer, and when there isn't one yet, the plan says so and leaves it for you to place. Anything the plan leaves out now shows up waiting for a spot instead of vanishing.
- Discuss on a record opens that record's conversation. On record views that open as a window over a list, Discuss used to open the workspace-wide conversation with no sign of the record, so a message written there was not about it. Now the side panel opens on the record's own conversation, the message box shows the record as a chip (the same chip Ask Cobb uses), and what you send stays with the record. × on the chip talks to the whole workspace instead.
- A PDF now shows its first page as its picture in the scan inbox and in a record's Files, read or not, and opens in the PDF reader. A document in the inbox is named by its file, with where its reading stands in words. Opening the original of a scanned PDF no longer shows its raw bytes as text: what a file is comes from its contents, not the type it arrived with, and a file that is not text offers a download instead.
- The Live box no longer appears when nothing is live, the floating controls in the corner stack instead of covering each other (the Live box sat on Feedback on a phone), and the Purchases header wraps on a phone with the status filter fully readable.
- Pasting a file now works wherever the upload button takes files. A PDF copied in your file manager and pasted onto the scan inbox, or into its box at the top, is read as a receipt instead of putting its file name into the search. On a record, a pasted picture goes to its photos and a pasted PDF to its Files, with no question asked.
- The selection bar no longer disappears when Cobb's panel opens. On a computer it stays, centred in the space beside the panel, with your rows still ticked; on a phone it comes back with the selection when the panel closes. Clearing the selection on the bar also clears it from Cobb's message box.
- A suggested photo now has to show the thing: a printer is no longer offered a banner that only shares its maker's name, and when nothing clearly fits there is no suggestion. On the record, the suggestion fills the photo box with Use this photo and Not this one, stays put on every open, and a picture you pick from the web is kept even when its site refuses the full-size copy, or you are told why not.

## 2026-09-30

### Features
- A file now has its own page: a link to a file from search, a tag, a view or Cobb opens it, with a preview, the records it is attached to, and rename, move, open and delete.
- The email with your sandbox's work now has a Continue on Cobblr button that brings everything straight into a workspace once you sign up or sign in, pictures included, and a new workspace's first screen can take a backup file or a sandbox's link directly.
- You can download a copy of what Cobblr holds about you from your account page: your profile, the workspaces you belong to, your tokens and connections (never their secrets), your notification settings, what you did and what you sent us.
- Boards, calendars and timelines now move things when you drag them. Drag a card to Done on a board and it is done. Drag a calendar entry to Friday and it is due Friday. Drag a timeline bar a week later, or pull its end out to give it more time. The change shows straight away and says what happened, and if it is refused it goes back and tells you why. It works with a mouse, with a finger (hold, then drag) and from the keyboard. On a phone the calendar becomes a list of the days that have something on them, a busy day's "+N more" opens that day, and the timeline scrolls sideways under a column of names that stays put. Tasks and projects now say which statuses, priorities and sizes they take, so a board of them has exactly those columns, and a task's own fields show in views like a project's always have.
- Every record has a History. Open anything and its History panel lists who changed it and what, newest first, so "who renamed this, and when" has an answer on the record itself.
- The floor plan editor has undo and redo. The arrows beside Done, or ⌘Z and ⌘⇧Z, take back a move, a resize, a new wall, a rename, a set of added drawers or a whole drafted plan, one change at a time, and put it back again.
- Press ? to see every keyboard shortcut. Only ⌘K was ever written down. Now ? (anywhere outside a box you are typing in) opens a list of every key Cobblr answers to, what it does and where it works: Esc, Enter and Space, ⌘Enter to answer and run, the arrows in lists, the photo viewer and the tour, Tab to take the chat's suggestion, and the comma and Backspace in a box of choices. The command palette shows its own keys at the bottom with a link to the full list, and finds it when you type "shortcuts". The list comes from the same place the keys do, so it cannot leave one out.
- Lend a thing out and get it back. Lend it names who has it and the day it is due back, a late loan shows in Needs you, and Got it back clears both. A service schedule's sheet now also asks when it was last done.
- Merging or deleting a tag can be undone, and the tag comes back on every record it was on. Because either one reaches every record carrying the tag, they now take an owner or admin; a member can still add, remove and rename tags, and can ask for the rest.
- A place's page can move everything in it to another place in one go, with the places inside it if you like, and can merge two places that are really one. It can be undone, and Cobb can do it too.
- PDFs and design files can be much bigger now. A scanned pattern PDF, a STEP or STL model, a 3MF or a gcode file can be up to 200 MB on your own server (100 MB on the hosted app), where every file used to stop at 25 MB. Photos, videos and other files stay at 25 MB. A file over its limit says how big it is and what the limit is for that kind of file. Big uploads are also easier on the server, which now writes them to disk as they arrive instead of holding them in memory. And when a phone loses signal partway through, the page now says the upload was cut off and to try again on a steadier connection, instead of "Load failed".
- Print this: press it on a model file or a Make, or ask Cobb, and Cobblr picks the printer the part fits with the material loaded, slices it for that printer, and waits for your one yes before anything prints.
- Put a thing away instead of deleting it. Put away takes something you sold or lost off the register and out of search and Needs you, and keeps its history and what it cost. Restore brings it back.
- Service anything by how much it is used. Mark a number field like Mileage or Hours run as what the thing counts, and a service schedule can come due every 5,000 miles, not only every few months.
- On a phone browser, the dashboard now suggests putting Cobblr on your home screen, even if you skipped the tour and never scanned, and the account menu always has Install Cobblr on this phone. Suggestions like this come one at a time, at most one new one a day, and one you put away stays away.
- Deleting a place can be undone. The message after a delete has an Undo that puts back the place, every place that went with it, and each thing where it was, and Cobb's undo does the same. The delete dialog now says how many places and how many things go with it. Deleting a room or rack with places inside it is an owner's or admin's call: a member can still delete an empty shelf, and can ask for the rest.

### Improvements
- A custom field can now be renamed, made required or optional, and changed to another type after it is made. Changing its type first shows which values it already holds would not fit, and nothing is rewritten.
- A service job that would act in the world, like sending a print, waits on its card for your yes, in the chat, the bell or a Discord DM. Your yes starts exactly what the card showed, once; a job changed after the card was shown, or a card pressed a day later, starts nothing.
- A value kept from a scan or an import that does not fit its field (a word in a number field, a size the dropdown does not list) now says so on the record, and a list counts those records beside its filters and shows just them with one press.
- The Activity log now reads like the dashboard: each entry says who did what to which thing, with a link to it, instead of an action code and a record id. The code, the id and the raw change are still there under "details".
- An asset's state is picked from a list. Working, needs repair, broken, in storage, and how it left (sold, given away, lost and the rest), in that order, so a board of assets grouped by state reads as its life.
- Ask Cobb about one service and it reads that service's options, such as a design service's templates or a slicer's printer models, instead of guessing. When a service stops a job to ask you something, the job card shows the pictures it sent, like a model that is not watertight, next to the choices it offers.
- A reply to your feedback now comes once, on the channel you chose: pick Discord only for feedback responses and it arrives as a Discord message with no email. The feedback emails also carry a short Discord section, an invitation to the community and a one-tap switch of your replies to Discord, shown only while it can help and never more than three times.
- Keeping a trial sandbox with an address that already keeps one now works: we email that address a link, and opening it adds the sandbox as another workspace with its own two weeks. The ending page and the email now say the same thing about how long your sandbox lasts, the file of your work leaves out the sample records unless you ask for them, and a backup file now calls itself a backup.
- Notifications arrive the moment they are sent. A new notification used to take up to 15 seconds to reach the bell, and the list stayed as it was while the bell was open. Now the count and the open list update at once, and a notification you read or delete in one tab disappears from your other tabs too.
- Notifications are named, and you pick which ones a channel gets from a list. The notifications page showed a code under some notifications ("digifab.print.failed") instead of what kind they were; every kind now has a name, like "A print failed" or "Approval requested". Choosing which notifications go to Discord, Slack or email used to mean typing one of those codes into a box; you now pick from the kinds your workspace sends, by name and under the app that sends them, or All events.
- Every picture opens in the same viewer, and it steps through the set. A feedback report's screenshots, a record's photo, a part's or a design's gallery, the Files page and a print's camera frame all open in one viewer now. Use the arrow keys, the buttons at the sides or a swipe on a phone to step through the set; it shows where you are, like "2 of 5", and Esc closes it. A report's screenshots used to open one at a time, and some galleries opened each picture in a new browser tab.
- On a sales order you can now change a line's quantity or price right where it is, instead of removing it and adding it again; removing a line asks first. Orders also have an order date and a ship-to address.
- The Sales page can search, filter by status and sort your orders, and says how many there are ("50 of 812") with Show more for the rest. Before, it stopped at the newest 500 orders and the older ones could not be reached. The Customers list and the customer picker can be searched too.
- A serial or a code finds its thing from every search box. Only the command palette used to put an exact identifier first; the header search and the search page now do too, marked "Exact match". In the header search, the arrow keys move through the results and Enter opens the one you are on. The search page offers only the kinds a search can actually reach, and picking one on its own lists it. Screen readers now hear which result the arrow keys are on in the palette and the header search.
- The Tags page can be searched, shows how many things carry each tag (so the unused ones stand out), and its pin, edit and delete buttons are big enough to tap on a phone.
- Delete a notification, mark one unread, or clear everything you have read. Each notification on your notifications page now has Mark unread and Delete, for "I read it and I still have to do it" and for the ones you are done with, and Clear read deletes every one you have read, in every workspace, after asking. Read notifications are also removed on their own after 180 days, so the inbox no longer only grows; unread ones are kept until you read them. Browser push is no longer offered as a channel, because it is not built yet and sent nothing; a binding made before now says so, with the way to remove it.

### Fixes
- Things in a named collection can be lent. A collection of assets such as Tools now has Who has it and Due back like the main Assets list, so Lend it works there too.
- A send to a bridge whose workspace has been deleted now says the workspace no longer exists, instead of telling you to check the edge bridge on your own computer. A hiccup while checking why a bridge did not answer now says so, instead of guessing that it was your personal bridge.
- Pressing Print again twice by accident no longer starts two prints: a second press within a few seconds asks whether you want another copy first.
- A required custom field can no longer be emptied once filled, through the form, the API or the assistant. A new record's form asks for required fields before saving (with Save anyway), anything that adds records without a form is never refused for them, and a list shows how many records still need a value.
- A print job sent twice at the same moment, by a double tap, a retry, two open tabs or the assistant and you together, now reaches its printer once instead of printing twice. The second send says the job is already on its way.
- A link to a sales order, a customer, a build, a knowledge entry, a tracked metric or a project task now opens that record instead of its list. Opening one also puts its address in the bar, so you can share it, and Back closes it.
- When Inventory, Lists or Purchases cannot load, they say so. A dropped connection or a server error used to show an empty page, "No lists yet", or Purchases offering to start from scratch, as if everything were gone. Each now says it could not load, with Retry.
- The MCP server you run yourself now offers an assistant only the tools its API token can use. A token that may only look is no longer offered ways to add, change or delete records or install bundles, each of which was refused when tried, and a token for one workspace uses that workspace without being told.
- A link to a tag opens the tag: everything that carries it, by name and grouped by what it is, each one opening, with pin, edit, merge and delete on the same page. Tag links used to lead to a page that was not there.
- The wire editor only offers events that actually happen, and a wire waiting for an event nothing sends is marked in your list with what to do. Wires now fire when an app is created, changed or deleted, when a design draft is made or applied, and when a task is updated.
- A workspace from which someone once sent feedback can now be deleted. Deleting it used to fail with an error; the report stays with the team, under the workspace's name, and the workspace goes.
- Asking an assistant "how many" now counts every record. A collection of more than 200 was counted as 200 and reported as complete.
- Screens that name your own things now say "an order", "an item" and "an Inventory" instead of "a order"; installing a bundle that turns on apps names them as your menu does and no longer says it added nothing to the menu; search counts "1 result".
- A browser no longer reuses an answer meant for an earlier sign-in: keeping a trial at the end of its hour now opens the workspace instead of showing the ending again.
- Communication preferences no longer offer feature announcements, workspace updates or messages from Claude. Nothing sent any of the three, so choosing a channel for one waited for a message that never came. Each can come back once something actually sends it.
- Deleting a place takes out everything kept in it and in the places inside it, so nothing is left pointing at a place that no longer exists; the things themselves stay, with no place set. The delete question now says so in plain words.
- Knowledge entries, tracked metrics and catalogs now show the fields your workspace adds to them on their own pages, and you can fill them in there.
- A knowledge base with more than 500 entries shows all of them. The list stopped at the newest 500 and said nothing, so older entries could not be reached from the page or the pinned panel.
- Every change to a sales order now shows on its history and in the workspace activity: adding, changing or removing a line, Fulfill and Ship moving its status, and deleting it. Automations that watch an order's status now also run when Fulfill or Ship moves it.
- Links to the print farm's settings, in a farm notification, a search result or a setup guide, now open the machines page instead of a page that did not exist. Links in notifications you already have work too.
- Lists, list lines, machine jobs, Makes, files and tags now show the fields your workspace adds to them on their own pages, and you can fill them in there. A list line opens its fields from the arrow beside it.
- Importing places from a CSV no longer scrambles a tree where two places share a name (a Shelf 1 in the Attic and another in the Basement): write the parent as its path, "Attic › Shelf 1", and each lands where its row says. A row that could mean two places is shown as blocked with the paths to choose from, the file imports only when every row is clear, and Export writes paths wherever a name repeats so the file imports back the same.
- Purchase orders, vendors, customers and places now show the fields your workspace adds to them on their own pages, and you can fill them in there.
- A place's own page can move it: Edit details now has the same "Inside" picker as the list. Naming a place like one already in the same spot says so plainly and offers to open that one or make a second anyway, instead of a message about a setting you cannot reach.
- Add a thing from the command palette works, and the search page shows everything it finds. The palette's "Add a thing" closed the palette and did nothing; it now opens the same box as the dashboard's "Just add it", from any page, and says where what you added went. The search page showed ten of each kind of thing, whatever it found; it now shows twenty, says when there are more, and "Show more" brings the rest. It also waits until you stop typing before it searches, instead of searching once per letter.
- Search, views and an assistant now see print jobs, sales line items and the orders in a named sales collection. Each of them used to answer as if it held nothing, with no error, so "how many print jobs are queued" came back zero while the jobs sat in the queue.
- Cancelling a sales order now puts back on the shelf what it had taken from stock, with Undo. For an order that has already shipped, Cobblr asks whether it was returned and only restocks if it was.
- Search finds what you typed. A `%` or `_` in a search used to act as a wildcard, so "50%" found everything with "50" in it; both are now plain characters everywhere you can search. A record in a collection like a Bookshelf is found by the fields the collection added, such as a book's author, not only by its name. And a search with a bad setting names the setting instead of saying a search word is missing.
- A sales order and a build now show the fields your workspace adds to them (a due date, where an order came in, a selling price) on their own pages, and you can fill them in there. Every sales order also gets a number when you do not give one, shown on the order, so two orders from one customer are easy to tell apart.
- Renaming a tag to a name another tag already has now says so instead of failing, and tagging a record twice at once no longer fails either. Every tag change (rename, delete, merge, adding or removing a tag on a record) now shows in the workspace activity, with what a renamed tag was called before.
- When your parcel tracking service is full, the shipment panel now says it is at capacity (so you know to free a slot or upgrade) instead of saying the carrier did not answer, and Cobblr stops asking it until you do.
- A file over the upload limit now says how big it is and the most a file can be, instead of an internal error.
- The Activity page says how many entries there are and can show older ones, as far back as the log goes; it used to stop at the newest 100. Another member's personal API token now shows as "an API token" rather than by its name.
- Your activity (under your account) now lists only what you did. In a shared workspace it used to list every member's changes as if they were yours, and its count could be higher than the list.

## 2026-09-29

### Features
- Set up a small shop in one install. The new Small shop bundle turns on Sales, Builds, Inventory, Purchases, Digital Fabrication and Shipments together and gathers them under one Shop menu, instead of six modules switched on one by one. Your Home page opens with the boards a shop checks every day: orders by status, stock, products and purchase orders. Orders get a due date and where they came in, parts get a SKU and whether you make or buy them, and products get a sell price.
- A task can repeat. Open a task, tick Repeats and choose how often, like every week or every 3 months. When you mark it done, the next one appears, due one interval later, with its checklist unticked. Choices in a task now show the moment you make them, even on a slow connection.
- A task has a checklist. Open a task to list the small steps inside it and tick each one off as you go. The task's line on the project shows how far along it is, like 2/5.
- Open a task to see and set everything about it. Click a task on its project and it opens with its status, due date, priority, size, details, custom fields, tags and discussion, all editable there. Set what it waits on by picking another task, and see which of those are still holding it up. A task past its due date shows as late on the project. Links to a task from search or Ask Cobb now open that task instead of the project list, and deleting a task asks first.
- Connect a service you installed yourself, such as a slicer, from Your account → Connections → Add a service: Cobblr reads what it offers from the service, tests your key, and says exactly what is wrong when something is.
- Keep what an asset cost, where it came from and who has it. An asset now has Paid, Bought from and Who has it, which can be anyone in your workspace's people, even someone without an account. The Assets page adds up what everything cost.
- You can change the email address you sign in with. The change happens only when you open a link sent to the new address, every device is signed out, and your old address is told, with a way to undo it for 7 days.
- A bundle update that changes something you customised now shows exactly what: your version beside the update's, with a choice for each (keep mine, use the update's, or keep both for a list of options). It also lists what is new in every version since yours, and an update that only adds options you already have no longer asks at all.
- An owner can delete a workspace from its General settings. It goes after a two-week grace period, everyone in it sees the date, any owner can keep it until then, and a full backup taken just before it goes stays downloadable from each owner's account for 30 days.
- Every list now has an Export button that downloads it as a spreadsheet, and a saved view exports with its own filter and columns. The file holds only what you may see, and it goes back in through Bring your spreadsheet without making duplicates.
- Deleted files now go to Recently deleted for 30 days, where you can restore them or delete them forever, and after that they are removed from disk for good.
- Give a task to someone. Open a task and pick who it is for from your workspace's people, including someone without an account. They are told when a task is given to them, their name shows on the task's line, and Mine on a project shows only your tasks.
- Cobblr now knows which spool is on which printer. Every printer's page has a Loaded filament panel with one row per slot (4 for an AMS, 5 for an MMU). Load a spool by searching, by scanning it with the camera, or by scanning its label with a scanner while the printer's page is open. The spool it replaces goes back to its own shelf. When a print finishes, each filament's grams come off the spool in its own slot, so a two-colour print draws on both, and each spool's history names the print.
- Every print now keeps its whole history as a Make. What was asked for, the design, the model, the slicer and its settings, the printer and the spool, and how it came out, in order, with every file in one folder. A Make can start from your own model or G-code, can be made again from any step, and shows up on the printer's and the spool's pages.
- Something you keep in several places can now go to one place in a single step. Every lot keeps its expiry and lot number, each place records the move in its history, and the places it emptied are archived rather than deleted. Ask the assistant to "move all the saline to the van", or drop it on a place in the floor plan once that arrives. A move made on the floor plan says so in the activity ("via floor plan"), and a thing dropped there keeps the spot it was dropped on.
- Cobblr opens without a signal. Installed or in the browser, a page you have opened before now opens offline, showing what you had with a bar that says how old it is ("You're offline. Showing what you had as of 14:02."). Changes wait until you are back, and the page refreshes by itself when the connection returns. Opened offline before anything was kept, it shows its own offline screen with Retry instead of the browser's error.
- A sales order can now put what it needs in to be made: "Make this" on a line plans a work order in Builds for that order, the line shows how many are made (only parts that came out good count, and the oldest order is filled first), the work order closes itself when enough are made, and the order ships what exists.
- Put things where they live, on the floor plan. Place items shows your plan beside a list of everything that has no place yet: drag a thing onto a shelf, a toolbox or a drawer on its face (or long-press and tap on a phone), drop it on the bare floor to pin it to that spot, and see how many things each place holds. Every table of physical things has a "Place on the floor plan" link.
- A 3D printer now has a Printing profile (its model, nozzle, build volume, default quality and a slicer preset per filament), and Cobblr can pick the best printer for a print: one the part fits, already loaded with enough of the right filament, and idle.
- Put a project's tasks in your own order. The arrows beside a task move it up or down, and the order stays. A new task still arrives on top, and you can ask the assistant to put one first or last.
- Something running low can go on a shopping list without installing a bundle. An item marked low in Inventory has Add to list beside it: one tap puts it on the list it last went on, and the first time it asks which list (or starts one). On the Lists page you can also say yes to having running-low items added to a list you pick by themselves. That is off until you turn it on, and nothing is added or created until you ask.
- Search now finds a pattern by the words inside its PDF: *magic ring*, *worsted* or *amigurumi* bring back every pattern that says it, with the line it is on.
- Cobblr now keeps an eye on the services it runs for you. A service that stops answering is checked on and handed to the next one in your order, a job waiting for your yes gets a reminder before it expires, and a run that gets stuck sends you one notice with Retry, Skip and Cancel. Every run has its own page showing each step and what it is waiting on.
- Ship a sales order with its tracking number: the carrier is worked out from the number, the order shows where the parcel is and keeps a history of what happened to it, and the customer gets a shipping notice with the tracking link, emailed from your own address when you tick "Email the customer", or copied for you to send when your workspace has no email set up.
- A wire can now run one of your services when something happens, and chain one service after another. Pick No record and Run a service in the wire editor, choose the service, and fill in the form it asks for, using what the last job answered. Anything that acts in the real world, like starting a print, still waits for a person to say yes.

### Improvements
- Rename a list, and correct a line where you read it. A typo in a list's name or one of its lines used to mean deleting it and typing it again, which also dropped who said they'd get it. Rename list and each line's pencil fix them in place, and a line's note now shows under its name.
- The Inventory list offers only the filters your things can answer. Warranty, insured, lifecycle and the rest appear once some item fills them, so an empty or simple shop is not greeted by questions it has no answer to. An empty list shows no filters at all, and on a phone they fold behind one Filter button.
- A print plate can come out partly good, and that is the build's yield. When a plate makes more than one, clearing the printer now offers "Some scrapped…" to say how many came out good; those count as made and the rest as scrap. The build shows its yield ("Made 9 good · 1 scrap, 90%") and each work order shows how many of its run are made, all from that one verdict.
- A service job that would print something or cost money now waits for your yes, on its card in the chat or as a notification with a Yes button when an automation started it, and it runs exactly as it was shown or not at all. You can also say yes ahead of time, like "power off after this print": the job waits for the print to finish, shows "Will run when…", and can be withdrawn until then. A yes nobody gives within a day lapses.
- A filament type's fields now sit under three headings, Identity, Printing and Spool, and you can tap a heading to fold its fields away. Every heading starts open. A bundle can now file its fields under headings like these, and if you move a field to another section it stays there when the bundle updates.
- A spool scanned from a maker's tag now keeps every colour it carries: a dual-colour or gradient spool shows each colour in its swatch, and a colour that came in as a hex code in any spelling is stored the same way as one you picked, so the same colour always matches.
- The service log says what each service is for. Each entry on the Maintenance page now names the thing it belongs to, and the name opens it. On a thing's Service schedule, each schedule has a Done button that logs it today and schedules the next one, a pencil to change how often, and the services done before are listed underneath. The Recurrence field on a service entry is gone, since it never repeated anything: a service schedule does that.
- On a phone, adding a photo to a record, an item's Photos or a scanned item now opens Cobblr's camera instead of the phone's photo library, with Choose beside the shutter for a picture you already have. A computer still opens the file picker.
- Add and correct an order's lines on the order itself. Add a line with the item, how many and the price, or fix a count or price a receipt misread, and remove a line you do not want. A line whose stock was already added keeps its count, so the order and the shelf never quietly disagree. Cobb can correct a line too.
- All apps is now a grid of cards grouped the way your sidebar is: each card shows the app's icon, its name and a plain sentence on what it does, the whole card opens it, and a filter box narrows the list as you type. Every stock app's description was rewritten in everyday words.
- A bridge keeps printer status on time when it is also carrying slower calls, such as a local AI or another service on your network: slow calls get their own share of the bridge and can no longer crowd out your printers. A call through a bridge now keeps its method and the service's response details, and a service that stops answering is cut off at its deadline instead of holding the bridge open.
- Cobb can use the outside services you connect, like a part designer or a slicer: ask for the work and it picks the right service, starts the job, and shows it as a card in the chat. A job that would print something or cost money waits on that card until you say yes, and Cobb says it is waiting rather than done.
- Connect Discord from where reminders matter. When your dashboard first has something going off or coming due, it offers a Discord message before things go off, one tap to connect. The same door is on a new collection's Set up card, first under Your account, Notifications, in the account menu, and in search (discord, notifications, reminders, dm). You come back with the test message sent and things due today set to arrive as one message at 08:00 your time.
- Drawing your space takes a minute now. Name a room and give it a rough size and it is drawn; new pieces land where you are looking; "Add drawers" lays out a toolbox's drawers in one go; a toolbox's face no longer stretches when you move it on the floor; size fields always show what you selected; and it all works with a finger on a phone. Search "floor plan" to find it.
- Every kind of connection on Your account → Connections is now checked by its own test when you save it, not only AI connections, so a connection that cannot work says so before it is used.
- A bridge can now carry large files between Cobblr and a service on your own network, such as a model to slice or a finished print file: they stream through without a size limit, a transfer the connection drops part way picks up where it stopped, and a file that arrives different from what was sent is refused rather than used.
- Picking Source: Ribblr on Import patterns now shows how to get your patterns out of Ribblr, right under the import: what can be downloaded, what cannot, and how to bring the downloads in.
- Anyone can leave a workspace somebody else owns, from the workspace switcher, its General settings, or (for a guest) the portal's header. Before, only an admin could take you out.
- The apps not in your sidebar are easy to find. The end of the sidebar now has a ⊞ More apps button that says how many apps are off or hidden, the first hide or turn-off tells you that is where they went, and the Search row says it finds apps, things and settings.
- An asset or a machine now has one category. What you type in Type and what a scan files land in the same field, the Assets and Machines tables show it as a Type column, and the separate Category field that scanning used to add is gone, with its values moved into Type.
- Cobb's model picker now lists your own AI connections, including ones set to "Just me", in the order they are asked, and a Claude bridge offers Sonnet 5.5 by name. Picking one changes only your chat.
- Scanning a product now fills its barcode into the record's UPC and the plain facts in its name (like 1.75 mm or 1 kg) into matching fields. A scanned filament spool's type gets its UPC, so the next scan of the same box finds it. Spools filed before this get the same, plus the temperatures, density and empty spool weight their tag carries.
- On a phone, search and your notifications are now in the top bar, one tap away, instead of inside the menu.
- Every search box has an × to empty it. Type in any search or filter and an × appears on the right; it, or Esc, empties the box and keeps you in it. A collection's header also lines up now: its name, count, filters, search and buttons share one height and one line, on a phone too.
- Cobb can now send a queued job to its machine for you, and starting a machine always asks you first, whichever way it was asked for.
- Outside services you connect follow one rule about asking first: anything that would print, move a machine or cost money always waits for a person's yes, whoever or whatever asked for it, and a workspace owner can set per job whether it asks first, runs, or is off.
