- An outside print-failure detector (PrintGuard, Obico and the like) keeps its address and key in Connections, where it can be tested and changed in one place. Which printers it watches and its camera map stay on the detector.
- Take one off from any view. In a view of something you count, every item with a count now has a minus and a plus right on it, whether the view is a list, a board, a table or a gallery, and so do the tiles on the parts wall. You no longer have to open an item to record that you used one.
- The AI page's "Who answers, in order" is now "Who does what, in order": outside services you connect show there too, one list per job with your own connections movable, and an owner can set, per job, whether it asks first, runs, or is off in the workspace. Anything that prints or costs money always asks.
- Your account has its own label in the sidebar. The account row at the foot of the sidebar is now two parts with a bar between them, like Scan and Build: Your account opens your account page, and your name with the arrow opens the account menu. Tapping your name used to open the account page, which was not something you could guess.

### Fixes
- A build adds what it makes to stock. A build made on the builds page never added its product to stock, because nothing on the page could say which part it makes. A new build now asks what it makes, starting from its own name, and the build sheet says so above the Build button. A build that makes nothing says that before you press Build. On a phone, the Build button now fits on the card.
- A filament type's page no longer offers Set status or Return home: those belong to a spool you can hold, not to a type. A new type named in one line keeps the maker's colour name, so "Polymaker PLA Royal Blue 1.75mm" is Royal Blue instead of Blue, and a colour name the browser does not know, like Fire Engine Red, still draws its swatch from its colour word.
- Finishing a task frees the one waiting on it. A task set to wait on another task stayed blocked after that task was done. Now it counts as free as soon as the task it waits on is done or cancelled, and waits again if that task is reopened. A task can no longer be set to wait on itself or on a task that is already waiting on it.
- A USB or Bluetooth barcode scanner now opens what the camera opens. Scanning an action label, or a QR code that links to one of your records, opens it instead of turning the link into an item in your scan inbox. A QR code that links to Cobblr's own site is ignored, as the camera already does, and a sorting session says a link is not an item instead of filing it.
- A QR label whose item has no page to open now says so. It used to say action labels could not be confirmed from the web, which was wrong twice: this label was not an action label, and action labels have had their own confirm page for a while. It now names the kind of thing on the label and offers to open the workspace.
- A link to a list, a line on a list, or a vendor now opens it. Search results, tags and links from Cobb pointed at addresses that opened nothing: a list's link showed the grid of lists, and a vendor's showed nothing at all. They now open the list (a line's link opens the list it is on) and the vendor, and opening a list gives it an address you can share.
- A member can now make their own API tokens and connect their AI assistant from Configuration, which shows them only their own pages. A settings page opened by a link that is not theirs now says it is for the workspace's admins instead of showing buttons that fail.
- A page for something that isn't turned on says so. Opening Inventory, Lists, Purchases or any other app that is off in your workspace used to show its whole page with buttons that could not work. It now says it is off and offers to turn it on, or, for a member, to ask an admin. On a phone, an empty Purchases page no longer cuts its sentence off.
- A plain project no longer shows knitting panels. Every project showed "What this pattern needs", "Add a yarn" and "Pattern & photos", even with no yarn anywhere in the workspace. Those panels now appear only on the Yarn bundle's Patterns table, where each record is a pattern.
- A finished print now takes its filament off a spool in grams. Before, 40 g came off a spool kept in kg as 40 kg, which emptied it. The minus on a spool's tile in a list now asks how much you used, like the spool's own page, instead of taking a whole kilogram per tap.
- A private view stays yours. Another member who had the link to a view you kept private could open it, see its rows, rename it, delete it or share it. Now it behaves as if it is not there for them. Making a shared view private again is left to an owner or admin, since it hides the view from everyone else.
- A scanned grocery lands where it keeps, even with no AI. Milk goes to your Fridge, spaghetti and spreads to your Pantry, frozen peas to your Freezer, worked out from the product's category or its name, so the shelf's label lists them. Tapping Add now files into the spot the card suggests, as File all already did, and Put away fills your Pantry and Fridge instead of proposing a new "Groceries" bin.
- A server without Discord no longer offers to connect it. On a self-hosted server with no Discord app or no bot to send the messages, Connect Discord appeared on the dashboard, a new collection's first steps, Your account, the account menu and a conversation, and every one of them ended on a page saying Discord was not set up. Those doors are gone there, the Discord schedule and the Discord DM binding with them. The notifications page tells a server admin, once, which settings turn it on.
- A spool added with + Spool on the Filament page is now weighed in kg, so you log what you used in grams like any other spool instead of stepping it by whole spools. Spools that were added this way before are fixed the next time the Filament page opens.
- A spool's weight is no longer changed by a fixed tap. Use some takes the grams you used, so a tap can never empty a spool. Every spool now has Mark empty and Remove this spool, for one entered twice. A spool with nothing left reads "empty" instead of "sealed". A spool scanned twice is no longer listed twice: the card asks whether there really is another. The same typed amount applies anywhere a count is kept in kilograms, metres or litres.
- A spool's page now shows its type and lets you change it: pick another type and the spool moves there, instead of the link refusing to come off. A spool kept in kilograms no longer offers Use one, and asking for it anyway explains that one would be a whole kilogram, so you say how much was used instead.
- A count you type into an item now reorders like any other change. Editing an item's quantity below its minimum, on its page or by asking Cobb, never said it was running low, so no shopping-list line or draft order followed, and the item's history had no entry for it. It now does both, the same as the − button. A recount records a history line too, marked as a correction so it never reads as use. And the + on a row that was at zero now starts a fresh lot with its use-by date, the same as every other way of adding stock, so milk put back from empty goes off on time.
- A filament type's page shows its fields again. After its fields were filed under Identity, Printing and Spool, the page showed none of them. A record's fields now also follow the workspace's own labels and hidden fields.
- A turned-off action label is no longer a dead end: Labels keeps it under Off with Turn back on and Turn on and reprint, and scanning one says how to bring it back. On a phone, a history row made through a label shortens the label's name instead of hiding the thing that was taken.
- Recent activity names the thing every stock change touched, and the name opens it, instead of saying "stock moved part". On a narrow phone the camera scanner keeps its close button on screen and never draws its instructions over the capture drawer's buttons.
- An asset has its service schedule. Opening an asset now shows its Service schedule, so you can set how often a lawn mower or a boiler wants servicing right there, the way a machine always could. Records and catalogs pick up the same panels, including tags and discussion.
- An assistant working through your chat can now answer "what does Alex own". It is no longer offered tools that could only refuse, such as re-planning put-away on a look-only connection, so it stops reporting them as broken.
- An order that arrives adds its stock, and only once. Marking an order arrived with its button, or answering "yes, it turned up", marked it arrived without adding anything to stock; only the status dropdown did. And an order set back and then arrived again added its stock a second time. Every way of saying an order arrived now adds each line's stock exactly once. Logging a past purchase is the other case: New order with status arrived now asks Add these to stock, ticked by default, and unticked the lines are recorded without changing stock.
- An order you typed in line by line now counts in your spend. Spend figures read only the order's total, which a receipt or a typed total fills, so an order entered by hand, and every past purchase filled in line by line, counted as nothing spent. When no total is entered, the lines plus shipping are the total now, in the list, the spend chart, top vendors and each vendor's total. An empty number field (an order's Total cost with nothing entered) now stays blank instead of showing a 0 nobody typed.
- On a narrow phone, Ask Cobb's header no longer draws Clear conversation over the Pinned tab: every tab keeps its name and the buttons sit apart down to 320 px wide.
- Available keeps up with the count. Taking one off in a table, like the milk in Groceries, changed the count but left Available (and whether it reads low) where it was until you reloaded. Every figure the count feeds now moves the moment the step lands, in the table and in saved views.
- Restoring a backup brings back the links between records, the workspace's people, form sections, menu headings and history, not only the records. If a restore declines anything, it says what and why.
- When an edge bridge refuses to reach a printer, for example because the address is not in the list you allowed on the bridge, the print now says so in the bridge's own words instead of telling you to check an API key.
- Ask Cobb finds a thing by the words you use for it ("my PLA offcuts" finds your PLA offcuts), and when several things share a name it asks which one you mean instead of answering about one of them.
- Count mode no longer offers to add a second copy. Counting a shelf with the camera's Count mode, a scan of something you already have offered "Add to" beside Set total, one tap away from a duplicate. In Count mode a thing you have now offers only its total.
- If you use dark mode, the page is now dark from the first moment it loads, instead of flashing white while the app starts.
- Dialogs now work from the keyboard: opening one moves focus into it, Tab stays inside it instead of wandering into the page behind, and closing it puts you back where you were. Pressing Enter on Cancel in a delete confirmation now cancels.
- Adding "Discord DM" as a notification channel now works. If your Discord account is not linked yet, Cobblr tells you to link it under your profile first, instead of failing with "Bad subscription payload".
- Editing a table view keeps its grouping. Saving a grouped table view, even just to rename it, turned it into one long ungrouped table. It now keeps its grouping, and you can choose how a table's rows are grouped when you make or edit the view.
- The top bar now always has a door to All apps, even when every app fits on it, and an app under a bundle's heading can be hidden like any other, from its card on All apps or from Menu & surfaces. On a phone, the notes about hidden apps now point at the menu instead of a sidebar.
- Every asset is counted and reachable. With more than 500 assets, the Assets page stopped at 500 and still said "500 of 500", so the rest could not be found. It now loads them all and counts every one. Machines and records get the same fix.
- The notifications page now reaches every notification you have. Older ones load with "Show older", and choosing a workspace or "unread only" asks for exactly those, so a quiet workspace's notifications are no longer pushed out of sight by a busy one's.
- Every order is reachable, and the spend counts all of them. The Purchases list stopped at 500 orders with no way to the rest, and the spend figures and chart were added up from what was loaded. The list now loads 200 at a time with Load more, search and the status filter look through every order, and the figures count every order that matches. Deleting an order or a vendor is now for owners and admins, the same as deleting an item in Inventory.
- Small grey text (counts, hints, timestamps, section labels) is a little darker in light mode and a little lighter in dark mode, so it meets the contrast people with low vision need, and links inside a sentence are underlined so they do not rely on colour alone.
- The floor plan now fits on one screen however far down the page it starts, and two places tapped onto it in quick succession land side by side instead of on top of each other. A thing dropped on the bare floor shows its name beside its pin and the note says it was pinned there. Every tab of a collection of physical things, not only its table, offers Place on the floor plan, and one scan now shows as one line in recent activity.
- When you add Cobblr to an Android home screen, its icon now fills the launcher's shape cleanly instead of being clipped at the edges or shrunk inside a white circle.
- After importing a spreadsheet, the result says how many rows did not go in exactly as written, shows a few, and lets you download the full list to fix your sheet from. Before, it only said how many were added.
- If your device is set to reduce motion, Cobblr's sheets, menus, collapses and loaders now appear and disappear without sliding or spinning.
- Asking Cobb to group sections of the sidebar under a heading now works in every workspace, not only the few that had switched it on by hand.
- New fields reach workspaces made before them. A field that a part of Cobblr adds in an update used to appear only in workspaces that turned that part on afterwards. Now every workspace that has it turned on gets the field on the next update.
- A sales order can no longer ship parts that are not in stock: Fulfill says what is short and offers to make more or ship what you have. Parts from a print now count as made only when you judge the plate good, so a failed or scrapped print adds nothing to stock.
- Removing a bundle now asks the same question wherever you start: keep its records (the default, with Undo afterwards) or delete them too. Before, Remove on the Bundles page kept them and Remove in the bundle's sheet deleted them. A failed bundle install or feature save now always says so, a bundle keeps its id when you change its features, and tapping a feature on a collection opens the bundle with that feature ticked.
- Orders that may not have added their stock are yours to check. Before a fix, marking an order arrived with its button didn't add its stock. Cobblr can't tell which past orders that affected, so it never changes stock by itself: the Purchases page lists the lines from orders marked arrived since mid-August, and for each one you choose Add to stock or Already counted.
- The print farm no longer sends a new plate to a Klipper printer that is busy with a print you started at the printer, and Duet printers now show whether they are printing. A printer's manager dropping off the network for a while no longer fails a print that kept running: the job says how long it has been out of reach and keeps checking. PrusaLink prints can now be paused, resumed and stopped from Cobblr, and a job caught half way through sending when Cobblr restarts goes back to the queue on its own.
- The print farm now reports what the printer is really doing. A print is charged to its spool once, not once per status check; a Klipper printer still heating after Start is no longer read as a finished print; a print stopped at the printer reads as cancelled and takes no filament; and Cancel only shows a print as cancelled once the printer confirms it, saying so plainly when a printer can't be stopped from Cobblr. When a send or a print fails, the job says why in plain words, using the printer's own message when it gives one.
- Sending a print to a printer behind an edge bridge now works for large files: a sliced model or a long print file streams to the printer and is checked on arrival, where before anything over about 20 MB could not get through.
- The sorting plan no longer calls yarn or filament "similar" to spices. Put-away only compares a thing with things of its own kind, splits a group that mixes kinds, and leaves an item without a proposal rather than sending it to a shelf of something else. A plan you just made, or just re-planned, no longer says your items changed since it was made.
- Wires set to run when a record is created, updated or deleted now run. Before, they were saved but never fired. Where a kind of record does not report one of those moments, the wire editor now greys that choice out instead of saving a wire that would never run, and an existing one says so on the Wires page.
- Six fixes from a walk through scanning groceries. A product whose maker is listed by its legal name (a company's "SA/NV" or "GmbH") no longer carries that name in its title. Put away says once that AI is not connected, not twice. After Accept, the put-away bar offers the walk as the next step instead of saying items are left. A grocery on a location's page is labelled Groceries, not Part. The camera's Add names and files into the same table the scan inbox names. On a camera without a light, the mode buttons no longer leave room for one, so Count shows its name.
- Search no longer fills its results with notes, tracked metrics and their readings, builds and sales orders that have nothing to do with what you typed. Each of those now matches on its own words.
- Set a service schedule from a record's header now asks for the unit (days, weeks, months or years) or the counter (like print hours) from a list, in plain words, and waits until both halves are filled in. A service's overdue days now read the same on the record, the Maintenance page and the notification.
- If a new workspace cannot be set up, signup now says so and asks you to try again, instead of leaving you in a workspace that answers every page with an error. Workspaces an earlier signup left half made are finished automatically.
- Keyboard users can now press Tab once and choose Skip to content instead of tabbing through the whole menu on every page, and screen readers now announce the new page's name when you follow a link.
- Test connection on an edge bridge now runs a real round trip through the bridge, a short prompt and a test photo, and marks it Verified with the date, the model, how long it took and whether it can read photos, or says exactly why it failed. It used to stay Unverified however well the bridge answered. A bridge that comes back online is re-tested on its own, and the backup offers on a connection are one line, "Can back up Gemini in 6 workspaces · Choose…".
- When the sandbox page shows the "are you human" checkbox, its heading now says Tick the box to start your sandbox instead of claiming it is already setting one up.
- The end of a trial now counts what you made: two photographed bottles are two items, not four, because a photo is part of the thing it shows. A ready-made trial also waits untouched until you arrive, so its automations no longer add shopping-list lines before you get there.

## 2026-09-28

### Features
- A join link paces itself. A wall QR's join link now runs until you revoke it, with a burst guard instead of an end date: 10 joins a day by default (or any number per day, week, month or year). More than that pauses the link and tells the admins, who can Resume it or give it a New code so the old QR stops working.
- Say whose a thing is, even if they are not a member. A new person field type, with the meaning Who it belongs to, picks from the workspace's People: every member, plus anyone you add by name (family, a friend whose things you keep). Configuration → People lists them, and an admin can invite someone on the list to join; when they accept, everything that was theirs stays theirs. My things (in your account menu) lists what is yours across every collection, and each person on the People list has the same page; a saved view grouped by Owner makes a column per person; a label template's `{{owner}}` prints the name; and the assistant answers "what does Alex own". Removing a member who owns things asks who gets them, and someone who already left can have theirs handed on from the People list. Names stay in the workspace.
- A thing knows what it cost from its order. A line on a Purchases order can now say it bought anything, a printer, an asset or a part, and the thing gets its Acquired from, Acquired on and Paid from the order with a from order link under each, while the thing and the order link to each other. A printer filed from a receipt is linked the same way. Change the order's vendor or date and the thing follows, unless you changed the field yourself: what you type is never written over.
- A join link for a wall QR. A club, a makerspace or a shared shed can put one QR on the wall and let everyone join: make a join link in Members, press Print QR, and anyone who scans it joins as a member (as many people as you like, or stop after a number). You see who joined through it and can remove anyone, and revoking it closes it for everyone. A member who scans it again goes straight in. After that, the label on each item is the door: scan it and press −1.
- Action labels. Print a code that does something when you scan it with your phone's camera. Stick one on the bathroom shelf, and scanning it opens "Toilet paper: 12 → 11" with a big Confirm. Nothing changes until you press it, and you can undo it. At the minimum it offers to add the item to your shopping list, and your history says it was the label.
- Bring your spreadsheet: drop an Excel, LibreOffice or CSV file, or paste a Google Sheets link, and Cobblr shows exactly where every tab, column and row would go before it changes anything. Adjust what you like, import it in one go, and undo the whole import if it is not what you wanted.
- A scan changes the count of something you already have, both ways. When a scan matches something you count, the card shows one control: Change by, starting at +1 for what is in your hands, where you can step, type any number, or go below zero to take one (scan the empty box, one is used up), with "You have 3 → 4" worked out for you; or Set total, a recount in its own colour, filed as a correction rather than as something used. Why is an optional chip, never a question. A take that empties the shelf offers Add to shopping list, and the camera's new Count mode opens every scan in Set total for a stocktake. The card no longer says "you already have this" four ways: one line, one control.
- A collection can put its own buttons on its records. A button made on the Wires page, or by a bundle, can have its own name, fixed settings, and one question it asks before it runs, such as a tasting note when you open a bottle. Use one now keeps that note, dated, in the record's history, and an app's total can multiply by a count, so a value counts every unit.
- New Connect an AI assistant page under Configuration → Connections. It gives you your workspace's MCP server URL, a button that creates a token for your assistant, and a snippet to paste into Claude Code, Claude Desktop or any other MCP client. Searching Configuration for "mcp", "claude", "chatgpt", "connector" or "ai assistant" now finds it. claude.ai and ChatGPT connectors are listed as not available yet: they sign in with OAuth, which is still being built.
- You can now add Cobblr to claude.ai and the Claude phone apps as a connector: paste one address, sign in, and choose the one workspace it may use and whether it may only look or also make changes. Connected apps are listed on the Connect page, where Disconnect ends one at once.
- Filing from the inbox now leaves the row in place saying where it went, with Go to and Undo, and a first scan files in one tap: the scan sheet offers Add to the right table, sets that table up if needed, and opens it.
- Every app is one search away, even when it is not in your sidebar. Type an app's name (or what it does) in the search palette: one that is on opens, one you hid from your sidebar opens and offers to come back, and one that is off offers Turn on, or Ask an admin if you cannot turn it on yourself. All apps, at the end of your sidebar's collections, lists everything this workspace can do in the same three groups.
- A colour can now be a gradient or several colours. Add a second, third or fourth colour to any colour field, reorder them, and choose whether they blend or sit as bands, and every place the colour shows draws it that way: gradient and dual-colour filament, variegated yarn. A name that plainly says its colours, like Dual Color Red and Blue or Rainbow, fills them in by itself.
- Import from Ravelry, from the Yarn table. One button on the Yarn table (and in Cobblr for Yarn, which has no Configuration page) brings your Ravelry stash into Yarn and your projects into Patterns. The first time it asks for your two Ravelry keys and checks them; after that, pressing it again updates what is already here and adds what is new.
- Install a bridge with one line and a code. Setting up a bridge now gives you a short code and a single command to paste on the box: it installs the bridge as a service that starts at boot and keeps itself up to date, on Linux, macOS and Raspberry Pi, including 32-bit Pi OS and a Pi Zero. The bridge's key is made on that box and never passes through your clipboard. Docker is still there for people who want it.
- Links show on both sides, on everything, once, in words. Link a mod to the printer it is on and the printer lists it under Links ("has installed"), and a part says what uses it; parts and every other record now have Link too. Picking how two things are linked lists what they can link to straight away, so linking a mod to its printer and a part is six taps. The same link twice is refused and says why. The new Printer mods bundle is a table of mods that link to their printers ("installed on") and parts ("uses part"). New thing no longer suggests Machines for a name like "Printer mods".
- Service schedules. Tell a machine, a car or anything else you look after how often it wants servicing, either by the calendar ("every 3 months") or by use ("every 200 print hours" on a printer). Its next service is always on the log. Logging the service moves the schedule on from the day you did it, and anything due or overdue shows on Needs you, in your notifications and in Discord.
- Needs you shows each collection as one card, and each row finishes the job. Overdue, today and coming up for Groceries are one card with a Today line between them. Each item has Used up, Tossed and +3 days right on the row, with Undo, and a stale backlog clears with Mark all as tossed. Two rows that look like one thing can be put together from the card.
- Supplies: a pattern knows its stitch markers, safety eyes and stuffing too. The Yarn bundle gains a Supplies table (on in Cobblr for Yarn; call it Notions if that is your word), and a pattern's notions are read from its materials list beside its yarn and hooks: stitch markers, holders, a yarn or cable needle, safety eyes and their size, stuffing, buttons. Each is checked against your supplies, 6 mm eyes against 6 mm eyes, and what you lack goes on your shopping list in one tap.
- Filament is one page. Each filament type is a card with its spools underneath, each with where it is, whether it is sealed or opened, and a minus and plus for what is left. The dashboard has one Filament tile, and the nav has one Filament entry. Spools that a scan left in the types table, or without a type, are put under their type when the page opens, and every spool and type shows its colour as the same swatch however the colour came in.
- A connected AI assistant can now read one scan inbox item in full: every field the scan found and would file, where each value came from, how sure it is, why it is flagged, where it would go, its pictures as links and what has happened to it so far. It no longer has to guess from the one-line summary in the inbox list.
- The same thing kept in several places is now one item with a row per place. Gauze pads in a supply closet and in five ambulances read as one card: the total, how many places, and a row for each place with its own count, its own minimum and its own low warning, so one short ambulance shows even when the closet is full. Add a place from the item's page, split a place back into its own record, or switch the list to one line per place. Restock one place from another and the soonest-expiring lot goes first, keeping its expiry date and lot number, and deliveries can be entered with the date and lot printed on them. A location's page shows what it carries and what is short, with a minus and plus on each line for that place, and a Going off list shows every lot about to expire, place by place. Records that were entered once per place are offered as one item (never combined without a yes), and scanning something kept in several places asks which place it is for.
- A record without a photo, however you made it and including the ones you already have, is now offered one: Cobblr looks it up on the web in the background and shows a Suggested photo you can use, swap for another or turn down.
- Every Ask Cobb answer can be saved. A crochet or knitting pattern becomes a Pattern that lists the yarn, hook and notions it names, checked against your stash like a PDF pattern; anything else becomes a Knowledge Base note that says where it came from.
- The scanner reads the year a bottle was made. Scan a bottle and the vintage on its label fills the field you marked as *The year it was made*, a new field meaning. It reads the vintage and not the year the house was founded, and because one barcode covers every vintage, the year is never shared as a fact about the barcode.
- One machine record can now stand for several identical machines, and any of them can have a name of its own under it. A record of five printers says so in the list and counts as five printers, not one entry, including when you ask the assistant how many you have. Name one to give it its own notes, service log, label and links while it stays one of the five, split it into a record of its own at any time, or make a single machine one of a group again.
- The label is the door. A join link can now be offered from the workspace's item labels: someone who scans a label without an account sees "Join the club to take one", and joining takes them back to the thing they scanned. It is off unless you tick Item labels offer it too. People without a password can ask the join page to email them a link that joins them and opens the item. "Sign in to join" on an invite now keeps the invite instead of losing it.
- A personal API token can now be held to one workspace and to Look only, Look and make changes or Full access. A new token starts on the workspace you are in with Look and make changes, and the Connect an AI assistant page makes one for its workspace only. Tokens made before this are marked Full access, all workspaces, with a button to narrow them.
- A view can ask for what is ready today. A saved view can now compare a custom date field with today, so "Ready to drink" or "Past its best" answers for the day you are reading it on. Two new field meanings, *When it is ready from* and *When it is at its best until*, describe a window instead of a deadline, and a filter that cannot run is now shown on the view instead of being quietly skipped.
- The AI page now shows who answers each kind of work in order (photos, chat, organising, search), with plain warnings when there is no backup, when a model can change under you, or when a connection cannot read photos, and you can move your own connections up or down.

### Improvements
- You hear when a bridge goes offline. Ten minutes after a bridge stops answering, the workspace's owners and admins get one notification naming it, and another when it is back. Anything that needs the bridge while it is down now says which bridge, how long ago it was last heard from, and what to check, instead of a line about a channel.
- An item's history says who took it. When a member presses −1 or Use one, the item's History shows their name beside the change, so a shared closet can answer "who took the last one". A rename shows on old entries too.
- A record now shows the actions a module you have not turned on would add, marked off. With Labels off, a part shows Print label; tapping it turns Labels on (or asks an admin to) and prints in the same tap.
- When an AI answers after another was busy, the card now names both by the names you gave your connections, with the model each used, instead of the provider's generic name. The AI log shows which connection made each call.
- A board reads in its field's order. A board grouped by a field with set values, such as a machine's state or any dropdown field, now lays out its columns in that field's order instead of alphabetically, and keeps a column for a value with nothing in it, so a fleet board shows Rebuilding at 0 rather than no Rebuilding at all. Records with no value come last, under No value.
- Your bridges are called what you named them. The name you type when you set up a bridge is the name every screen uses, and you can rename it on the bridges page. Each bridge shows whether it is online, when it was last heard from, the machine it runs on and its version, and it stays listed while it is offline. A form that sends through a bridge picks your only bridge for you, lists several by name, and offers to set one up when you have none.
- A busy AI provider is now asked again, and when it stays busy the workspace's next connected AI answers, with the card saying which one did. A busy provider is named as busy rather than unreachable, and a hint you type is applied to the item's name even while the AI is away.
- A home address picks your bridge for you. Type an address that is on your own network, like 192.168.1.50 or partdb.local, into Part-DB, Homebox, a sync connection, a print manager or a 3D printer connection on a hosted Cobblr, and the form says so straight away. Where it can, it routes the connection through your bridge by itself; where it cannot, it says what to do next, with a link to set a bridge up. Before, nothing reacted until the connection failed. A self-hosted Cobblr, which usually sits on the same network, asks for nothing.
- The import API can now plan a spreadsheet without writing anything: which collection each tab belongs in (one you have, one to install, or a new one named after the tab), where every column goes, and whether each row would be added, updated or left as it is.
- The import API can now read a spreadsheet (.xlsx, .xls, .ods, .csv, .tsv, or a shared Google Sheets link) and tell you what it holds without changing anything: the header row under any title, what each column is, which column names each thing and which says where it is kept, and every row it would leave out and why.
- A screenshot of a listing files what the listing says. Scan a marketplace listing and the site, seller, price and condition land in your table's own fields, marked as coming from the listing. The picture is cut from the listing's own photo even when the first read misses its edges, and a web picture of a different version of the model (a "V2" when yours is the original) is no longer picked for it. A machine's name no longer picks up the colour the camera saw.
- The location picker starts with where it probably goes. Setting where a scan session goes now shows a suggestion first, with the reason ("where your other 3D Printers are"), then your recent places and a search box, above every room and bin. A scanned printer is suggested the room your other printers are in, not wherever printer filament happens to be. Counts on a room now say what they count ("73 inside"), and one scanned thing reads "Where does this one live?".
- A laser cutter or CNC machine now talks about jobs and a Job Manager on its own page, in its new-machine form and in its bundle, instead of prints and a Print Manager. A 3D printer still prints, and the new-machine form only asks what kind of printer it is when it is one.
- The sidebar foot gives you one row instead of two: your name opens Your account, and the arrow beside it opens the account menu.
- A pasted picture shows up at once, and pasting it twice makes one item. Paste or drop a picture anywhere on the scan inbox and it appears as a card straight away, saying it is uploading and then being read. The same picture again within ten minutes answers "Already added just now" instead of making a second item, with Add another copy if you meant it; two copies already waiting are offered as one to keep. An item still waiting on its lookup says why after a few seconds, with Try again beside it.
- Photo tiles are now the same size whatever the photo. A tall picture no longer stretches its row on the vending board, the machine and item tile views, galleries or the scan inbox, names and counts line up along each row, desktop walls use smaller tiles with more across, and a tile no longer repeats its name as its second line.
- Scanning a Polar Filament spool's QR now fills in everything the maker publishes for it: the nozzle and bed temperature ranges, drying temperature, density, empty spool weight, the exact colour swatch and the box's UPC, all in °C, grams and millimetres. Any maker that publishes the open OpenTag3D format can be added the same way.
- Scan and Build share one line at the foot of the sidebar again, Scan on the left, so the sidebar gives a line back to your collections.
- A scan that could be any of several things you have asks which one once, with the choices to tap, instead of two panels asking the same thing. A session's location control stays in its header even when every item still needs a look, a table's name on a scan card is no longer cut short when there is room for it, and a long name on a tile wraps to two lines before it is shortened, with the whole name on hover.
- Search now finds things you scanned but have not filed yet. The command palette lists your records first, then what is waiting in the scan inbox with where each is headed, then uploaded files behind one "Files" row, then pages and settings. A collection's own search shows the scans headed for it underneath, each with an Add that files it there.
- A scan inbox result in search now opens that card right there in the inbox, scrolled to and outlined, instead of a full-screen review on top of the list. Back and reload land on the same card.
- Search tells you when it could not look everywhere. If some kind of record could not be searched, the search page, the search bar, the command palette and the link and mention pickers now say which, instead of quietly showing fewer results.
- See what a bridge can reach, and cut one off. Each bridge now shows what it will reach, and you can hold it to a fixed list of addresses with a setting on the machine it runs on, which nothing in Cobblr can widen. Disconnect stops a bridge at once by turning off its key. The setup screen explains in plain words what a bridge can and cannot do.
- Notions is now Supplies, and a table answers to every word it offers. The Yarn bundle's Notions table is renamed Supplies on this update, with a line under its name saying what goes in it (markers, needles, safety eyes, stuffing, buttons); everything in it stays. If you call them notions, pick that word in the table's Settings. Search (⌘K) now lists your tables too, by their name or by any other word they offer, so typing "notions" opens Supplies and "skeins" opens Yarn.

### Fixes
- When Google AI Studio's default model is overloaded, Cobblr now asks the other Gemini model on the same key before giving up, instead of asking the busy one nine times. The AI log names the model that was actually used rather than "default". A second AI connection that isn't used in any workspace now says so, and offers to back up your first one where it answers.
- A card in any view opens its item from anywhere you tap it. On the "What's on hand" wall only the photo and the name opened the item, so a tap on the count or the middle of the card did nothing; board cards, list and table rows now work the same way, and the plus and minus still only count.
- A saved view's columns use the collection's own words. A table view was headed with storage names like manufacturer and drink_by; it now says Producer and Drink by, as the collection does. A note kept with a use of an item shows in full in its history instead of being cut to one line, and a year read off a label shows on the scan card before you file it.
- Picking a colour saves once. Dragging across a record's colour picker saved the colour on every tick of the drag, dozens of times in a minute, each with its own line in the record's activity. The colour now follows your hand as you drag and is saved once, when you let go. Sliders, such as the print-failure sensitivity, work the same way.
- Connection forms no longer get your saved login filled in. Chrome filled your account email into server addresses and your password into API tokens on forms like Import from Part-DB; API keys, tokens and device passwords are now masked fields no browser takes for a login, and server addresses are address fields. Choosing Default bridge in Import from Part-DB or Homebox now lets you connect, and a greyed Connect button says what is missing.
- A date you already set shows in its box. An order's Ordered at (and any other date stored as a timestamp) showed an empty date box on the order even though the order had the date.
- A record never hides a value it holds. A machine imported as "5 of these" into a specialised collection showed no quantity at all, and nothing on the record could see or change it. A section a collection folds away now stays on any record that has something in it: the quantity of 5 shows and can be edited, a warranty date that is set stays in view, and units on file or contents inside a thing are always listed.
- Filament opens from the dashboard. On a phone, the dashboard's Jump to tile for a pair of lists shown together (Filament, with its types and spools) went nowhere. It now opens the first list of the pair.
- Members can take one and put one back. A member pressing −1 or +1 on an item's count, or Use one, was refused and the page showed nothing. Taking stock now works for every member unless a custom role says otherwise (guests still only look), and when any change is refused the reason now appears in words beside the button you pressed.
- A window opened from Ask Cobb is the one on top. On a phone, a record or a sheet opened while the Ask Cobb panel was open landed underneath it, with only its title showing and nothing in it you could press. Whatever opens last is now always on top: a record over the panel, a menu over the record, a confirmation over both.
- A scanned grocery shows up on its shelf's label. When a scan reads where something is kept, like Pantry, and your workspace has that location, the item is filed there, so the Pantry's label lists it. Groceries' separate Storage field is gone: what you had set there moves into the matching location on this update.
- A scanned filament spool now files as a spool under its filament type, instead of into Inventory or the table of types, and says so: "Added 1 spool of Cookiecad Silk PLA (new type)" with Go to it. A scan whose only name is its barcode number now waits for a name instead of being filed as a number.
- When an AI assistant searches one kind of record by text, each result now says what matched, with name matches first, so a printer whose notes mention another brand is no longer read as that brand's printer.
- A second thing in the photo no longer renames or splits the item. A barcode scanned with another product beside it kept the name the code resolved to, instead of taking the other product's label, and nothing was reported to the shared barcode lookup. A skein half out of frame behind the one you scanned is no longer offered as a second item to split off.
- A different version is a different thing. A scanned Voron 0.2 kit was offered as "the same one" as a Voron 0.1 you already have, matched by name. A version or mark in the name (0.1 and 0.2, Mk3 and Mk4, V2) now tells two products apart, and the same version on both sides ranks that match first. This applies to "you already have this" and to combining two waiting scans.
- Recent activity says how something was done, not just who. A change made with an API token names the token, one an assistant made over MCP says so, and one an automation made names the automation, on the dashboard as well as the Activity page. Rows no longer read "someone", and a file saved from a link is named after the file instead of the link's query string.
- Saving a record with a field name its type doesn't have is now refused, with a list of the fields it does have and a "did you mean" when one is close, instead of quietly storing the value where no form shows it. This applies to scripts, the API and AI assistants alike. AI assistants also now see workspace-wide fields, such as a provenance field on every physical thing, on each record type they apply to.
- An AI assistant connected over MCP can now read every record in a long list, a page at a time, instead of stopping at the first 50. Records, search results, activity, notifications, maintenance and the scan inbox all page the same way.
- Log service works again. Pressing it on a printer, an asset or a location now asks what was done before it saves the entry, instead of failing with "requires name". The same goes for any button whose action needs something from you: it asks first, and it won't run until the answer is there.
- The scanner's light now comes on only when you ask for it. Auto light starts off and the flashlight and Auto light choices are remembered on your phone. A new 1× / 2× chip lets you zoom back out, and the camera stays where you put it.
- A record's custom fields now read back where they were written: an AI assistant or a script reading a record gets `board` at the top level, the same place it wrote it, instead of finding it only inside `metadata` and reporting the field empty. Assistants are also no longer told that notes or serial numbers on a collection are unreadable when they can read them.
- When a server's free photo identify covers only some workspaces, the others are no longer told their photos will be read. The scan page now says the free identify here covers other workspaces and offers to connect your own AI, and a photo it could not read says the same.
- Menus stay on a phone's screen. On a phone with larger text, the scan card's File into menu ran off the right edge and its options were cut off. Every menu now moves itself back inside the screen, opens above its button when there is no room below, and the scan row's tools wrap to a second line instead of making the whole page wider than the phone.
- An item's picture is never a stock photo site's watermarked preview any more: the search skips them and uses the next picture, and an item still waiting in the scan inbox that already had one gets a new picture.
- A picture Cobblr saves for you is stored once. A catalog photo fetched twice used to be kept as two files and listed twice in search; copies already made are folded into one, and every place that used either copy still shows it.
- A receipt read with Purchases off says so. It used to read into your inbox and quietly record no purchase. Now it says "Turn on Purchases to keep this as a purchase", the receipt keeps that offer, and one tap turns Purchases on and records the purchase with every line on it.
- When you file a scanned item into a table that keeps its own categories (like Inventory), the review card now offers a "New category..." choice, so you can coin the right category on the spot instead of being stuck with the closest existing guess.
- Searching finds purchase orders and projects again. A search that reached orders or projects asked for a field those records do not have, failed, and came back empty, so they never showed up in search, pickers or the assistant. Both are searchable by the fields they actually carry.
- A sync no longer erases a location you set. If the source points a machine at a location the sync hasn't brought across yet, Cobblr now leaves the machine where you put it instead of clearing it, and the sync's row under Integrations says what it couldn't match. Once that location does come across, the next run picks it up. A place you made yourself before the other system had it is now matched up instead of stopping the whole locations sync, and one record the sync can't take no longer holds up the rest. Every change a sync makes to a record now also records the value it replaced.
- A sync no longer makes a second copy of something you already have. A machine, part, asset or location you made by hand (or with an assistant) and tagged with the source's id is now updated in place by the next sync, and the import preview shows it as a match instead of a new record. Two syncs writing the same new record at once leave one copy, not two. Every record a sync creates or updates now says so in its history, named after the connection it came from.
- A connected machine's live status now sits inside its tile on the machines wall, on the same line as every other tile's, instead of hanging below the card.
- Uninstalling a bundle now puts back what it set up for you: the modules it turned on because it needed them are turned off again, and the empty places it made are removed. Anything you have used since stays, and the uninstall says so ("Kept Pantry: 12 things live there"). A module you turned on yourself and a place that was already there are never touched.
- A saved view now shows every record it holds and counts them all. A board over 56 machines used to show and count only the first 50, and could drop a whole column; pinned cards, app blocks and shared views had the same limit.
- A shared AI connection that a workspace owner stopped using no longer keeps answering for that workspace. A workspace's own AI providers are now always tried in the order they were added. Testing a bridge connection now checks it for real, including whether it can read a photo, instead of leaving it marked unverified.

## 2026-09-27

### Features
- A pattern knows what it needs, and takes it off your stash when you finish. Upload or import a pattern PDF and its yarns and hooks are read from its own materials list, each yarn with its brand, colour, weight and length, with no AI needed for the usual layouts. Each yarn is matched against your stash: you have it, you are short by so much, or nothing fits, with Add to shopping list for what is missing. Use it holds the balls for that yarn, Change yarn halfway keeps what you used on the old balls and moves the rest to the new one, and marking the pattern done takes each yarn's length off its own balls, from any screen.
- A product somebody has already named is known to the next person who scans it. When you file a barcode item that no catalogue knew, the name you settled on goes to Cobblr's shared barcode network, so the next scan of that barcode anywhere is named at once, without an AI identification. It shows as named by one person until someone else agrees, and you can always correct it.
- Scanning a barcode now fills the lot printed beside it: Yarn's Dye lot, or any field you mark as a lot or batch. A lot belongs to that one unit and is never shared with the barcode network.
- Making your Cobblr account happens right on the sign-in page now: your name, email and a password, then Create account, and the link in your email brings you straight back, signed in. The sign-in page is one card, Sign in or Create an account, with "Email me a sign-in link" underneath, instead of four different ways in.
- The guided tour on a phone now walks you through putting Cobblr on your home screen, step by step, with a drawing of each screen and an arrow at the first tap. It is offered again once after your first scan, on Android it raises the browser's own Install prompt, an app already installed is never asked, and "Not now" is remembered.
- A product without a barcode is now known to the next person who photographs it. When you file something the AI named from a photo, the product words on its label that are also in what you filed go to Cobblr's shared barcode network, so the next photo of that product gets the name you settled on.

### Improvements
- The page at the end of a sandbox's hour says what happened and what you can do: "Your hour is over. Your sandbox is saved for 24 hours." The main button is Create my account, which moves everything you made into your own Cobblr account; if you want more time first, Give me two weeks keeps the sandbox going and emails you the link. Download what I made, Start with an empty account, Try a new sandbox and Self-host Cobblr are a short list of links, and it all fits a phone screen.
- What's on hand shows your photos and fills the page. Each tile now leads with the item's picture and is bigger, the board is as wide as the table beside it, and on a phone every tile has its plus and minus right there. On a wider screen Use / restock still shows and hides them, without the tiles changing size.
- Every password field has an eye button now, so you can check what you typed before you create an account, sign in or set a new password. It works the same on API keys and tokens in connection settings.

### Fixes
- The Yarn table no longer shows an Import from Ravelry button that did nothing. It called an importer that had been replaced by the Ravelry sync connection, so pressing it only ever sent you to a page that could not connect Ravelry either. In a full workspace, Ravelry connects under Configuration → Integrations → Live sync, which brings your stash and projects into the tables you pick. The Pattern page's materials suggestion is also described as what it is today: a one-off reading by your AI, matched by weight and fibre, and not saved on the pattern.

## 2026-09-26

### Features
- Call your things what you call them. Yarn asks whether you count in balls, skeins or hanks, and Groceries, Lego, Bookshelf and Home Inventory offer their own words (pantry items, kits, titles, belongings). Pick the word when you install, or change it later in the table's settings: every label, the New button, the count on each record and what Cobb says all use it, and an update never puts the old word back.
- Files have folders now. Make folders inside folders on the Files page, upload straight into one, rename a file or move it with "Move to…", and find any file by name from search. Uploading a file you already have keeps the one copy and says where it is, and a folder that still holds something cannot be deleted, so tidying never loses a file.
- Yarn's Designs are now Patterns, and your pattern PDFs have a home. Import patterns takes a pile of PDFs or a zipped downloads folder: each PDF becomes a pattern with its file, its cover photo pulled out of the PDF, a zip's category folders fill in the category, and a PDF you already have is never added twice. A pattern also keeps its designer, where it came from and how hard it is, and it opens right in the app, on a phone too.
- A sandbox can become a real Cobblr account in one go. Make this yours on Cobblr sits beside Keep it on the sandbox's strip and first on the page at the end of the hour. Press it, make an account on Cobblr (or sign in), and what you added, photos included, moves into your new workspace. The sandbox then ends, so there is one copy and it is yours.
- PDFs open in the app, every page. A pattern, a manual or any PDF on a record or on the Files page opens in a viewer with zoom, a fit-to-width button, pinch-zoom on a phone, and "Keep screen on" while you work from it. "Open in a new tab" and Download are one tap away.
- Drop a whole folder of files in at once. Pick many files, or a zip of a folder, on the Files page: a zip is unpacked into its own folders under the one you are in, a file you already have is kept once, and one summary at the end says what was stored and why anything was not.

### Improvements
- A Discord notification says what it is before you open it. A digest arrived on your phone as a blank "Cobblr" alert, because the card it carried has no text a phone can show. Every direct message now leads with one line, like "1 thing to use up: Boneless Skinless Chicken, expires in 1d", and that line is the notification.
- The Files page says how much your workspace keeps, and a free trial can now upload a few files of its own. A trial holds up to 100 MB of your files, where it used to take none, so you can try folders, the PDF viewer and bringing in your patterns. A server can cap how much each workspace keeps; past it, an upload says how full the workspace is instead of failing quietly.
- Signing up on the hosted Cobblr now starts where it should. A link that says "Sign up" lands on Create a Cobblr account, opened straight on the account form, instead of a sign-in form you have no password for. The link you came from, such as a card you scanned at a show, is remembered through the sign-up, so we know it worked.

### Fixes
- A count and its word now agree everywhere: a yarn record reads "3 hanks" and "1 hank", not "3 hank", and the same goes for the stock history, what Cobb says when it adds something, and every word a table lets you pick.
- A pattern's PDF opens again. Pressing the PDF on a Design (Yarn's patterns) showed an error page instead of the pattern. It now opens in a new tab, and a PDF anywhere in the app (a record's files, the Files page) opens in the browser's viewer instead of downloading, on a phone too.
- Updating to the current compose file keeps your database. If your instance took the automatic Postgres 17 to 18 upgrade on the older compose file and you then switched to the current one, the database could come back from its pre-upgrade copy, losing everything written since. It now keeps serving the upgraded database it already has.

## 2026-09-25

### Features
- A photo of a label now fills in its item. Photograph a bulk bag or a lot sticker after scanning the item and the count, lot, colour, SKU and case code printed on it are written onto that item, marked as read from the label. Anything you typed yourself stays yours, and combining a case label with one of its items counts the case once.

### Improvements
- The community is now easy to find: a "Join the community" section in the account menu and at the foot of the phone menu lists the Discord and every other community place your instance offers, and it is hidden when none is set up.

### Fixes
- A barcode the lookup cannot answer right now no longer sits on "Trying again" for twenty minutes. It tries a few times over about eight minutes, says whether the service was busy or could not be reached, and then says "Couldn't look this up. Take a photo or name it." with both on offer.
- A CSV of purchases with no shop in it no longer files its column header ("description,qty,unit price,amount") as the shop every line came from. The shop is taken only from a column that names one, and is left blank otherwise.
- Opening a session's menu no longer jumps the page to the top and opens the menu off screen: the page stays put and the menu opens beside its button, above it when there is no room below. On a phone the menu button no longer catches taps meant for the File button next to it.
- A session's merge now names the session it joins ("Merge with Sep 24, 6:00 PM, above") and joins exactly that one, the session shown directly above. It used to say "the session above" and join one further down, skipping past a receipt.
- "Not right" on the camera now sticks: the wrong name is turned down on the item, and looking again, a replay or an empty name can no longer bring it back. The item waits for the name you type, and an empty name asks for one instead of being accepted.
- A scanned item's picture now has to show the colour the item says it is. A catalog or web picture of another colour (a beige skein for a dark-blue yarn) is set aside and your own photo is shown instead, and a product's case barcode is recognised as the same product as the item's own barcode.
- In File everything, a line added to something you have now says what your choice will do, with the count before and after ("Replace Sourdough loaf: 2 → 1 (the old one went bad)"). It used to keep saying "+1 to" whatever you picked.
- A scan that matches something you already have now says where it goes. A receipt's milk that matched the milk in Groceries showed Inventory as its table while "Replaced the one that ran out" updated Groceries. The pill now reads "Whole milk in Groceries", and the dashboard, File N and File everything add to that record instead of making a second one. Picking another table, or "Not the same", files it as a new item and is remembered.
- Renaming a scan now re-checks whether you already have it, and every button agrees. A renamed scan could offer "+1" to a record on its card while File everything planned a new one. The match is now worked out once, under the name you gave it, and the card, File N, the dashboard and File everything all read that answer. A scan that matches several things equally now asks you to pick instead of adding to the first.
- A scan session's header on a phone keeps the shop's name, the date and the count on screen however long the order number is: the order number gives way first, and a date or time now drops a whole word ("Sep 1") instead of being cut to "Sep 1, 8:4…". The session's ⋯ also takes less of the line.
- Every scan whose card says Review is now under "To review" and in its count, including a named item whose table is only a guess or not chosen yet. Before, those showed Review on the card but were missing from the filter.

## 2026-09-24

### Improvements
- A sandbox link can now open straight into the camera scanner: add start=scan to the sandbox link, and one big Try it: scan something button makes the sandbox and opens the camera, ready for a barcode. A new sandbox also has Home Inventory next to Groceries and Bookshelf.
- Keeping a sandbox no longer asks how long. It is kept for two weeks, the button says so ("Keep it for two weeks"), and the week, two weeks and month choices are gone.
- File everything now offers the same restock choice as a scan's card on every line it adds to something you have: add to it, replaced (the count becomes what you just bought) or went bad. Before, every such line was added on top.
- With both Spice Rack and Groceries installed, a seasoning now goes to Spice Rack. Spices are the narrower shelf inside the pantry, so a blend like "Garlic Seasoning Blend" lands with the spices even though its name has other grocery words, and asking Cobb to move the spices out of Inventory moves them there. Without Spice Rack, seasonings still go to Groceries. The same rule applies to any two tables that name the same thing: the more specific one wins.
- The page you see when a sandbox's hour is up is shorter and says one true thing: "This sandbox is closed. What you made is kept for 24 hours, then deleted." What you added is one line ("You added: Bookshelf, Groceries · 4 items · 3 photos"), and the list of what stays behind, which contradicted the line above it, is gone.
- Yarn now counts in balls, the word everyone knows: Length / ball, Full ball, Price / ball, and Track each ball separately and Open a ball on a yarn's page. Yarn you already have moves over to balls when the Yarn app updates, and a yarn you set to another unit keeps it. A label or search that says skein still finds your yarn.

### Fixes
- When many people sign in from the same network, the sign-in page no longer says it could not reach Cobblr and tells you to check your connection. It now says the network is making too many attempts, how long to wait, and counts the Try again button down. Loading the sign-in page also no longer counts against the sign-in attempt limit, so a busy shared network can always see the form.
- When Cobb asks you to confirm a change, the card now lists every value it will save (the room, the count, the price) and, for a move, where it goes from and to, such as "Move Desk fan: Bedroom → Garage". A value you did not give him is marked as his suggestion, and you can leave it out before you confirm.
- A saved view pinned to Home now groups its things exactly as the full view does, so a By room card no longer says an item has no location when the view itself shows it in its room.
- A new sandbox no longer opens on "Update now" for bundles you never chose. It starts on the current version of every bundle it comes with, and a sandbox never asks you to update anything.
- In the camera, a photo taken after pressing +Photo now always joins the item you pressed it on, however the photo arrives. Supplying it as an image file made a second item instead, while the camera still showed it was waiting for the extra photo.
- A scanned item no longer gets a catalog picture that contradicts it. When the item's name or photo says white plastic, a stainless or black picture is never chosen for it automatically; with no picture that fits, your own photo stays and the choice is left to you. Pick best follows the same rule, and when it cannot pick it now says why in the provider's own terms, so a usage limit reads as a usage limit rather than a provider that could not be reached.
- A receipt that could not be read no longer turns up in the inbox as an unnamed item asking for a name. It now says the receipt was not read, with Read again as the first button, and Treat it as one item if you meant it as a single thing. This holds on the phone list, in item review and on the desktop card, whether the receipt was uploaded alone or together with photos. It is no longer offered a table, an Add or Review button or a quantity either, since there is nothing to file until it is read, and the dashboard counts it as a receipt that could not be read rather than as a scanned item without a home.
- Splitting a phone photo of several things now gives each piece its own crop. A photo a phone stores sideways was shown to the AI sideways while its answer was read as if upright, so the pieces lost their crops and quietly kept the whole photo. Now the AI sees the photo the way you do, and a piece that still could not be cut out says so, with a Crop it button beside it.
- The inbox's "these look related" suggestion now counts and changes only the rows the session shows. It could say "These 3 look related" and then offer a category for "10 of them", because it was reading every pending scan in the session, including ones not on screen. Both numbers now come from the same rows, a category meant for only some of them names which, and the button says how many it changes.
- Something Cobb moved for you could end up listed in both its old place and its new one. A thing now has exactly one home however it was moved, and anything already listed twice is settled on the place it was moved to last.
- A saved view now shows its own fields on a phone, not just each item's name and location. Insurance valuation's cards show the replacement value, the purchase date and the condition, the first few on the card and the rest behind a "more" tap, in the view's order.
- A new yarn with two unopened skeins no longer reads "of 200 skein, 1%": the consumption gauge only compares amounts in the same unit, so it now reads 200 m each, full. Ask Cobb how much is left and it answers from the same running balance the page shows, so a skein with 40 m used is 160 m, not the 200 m of a full one.
- Where chat is switched off but photos are still identified, the page no longer says "AI is off here". It now says chat with Cobb is off and photo identify works, and an item's Re-run AI button stays live, because re-running reads the photo again.
- Saving, rerouting or removing an AI connection now changes what Cobb and the scanner offer straight away. Before, Cobb could keep saying the workspace had no AI until you reloaded the page.
- A scanned item no longer gets an ISBN, serial or other identifier that nothing on it shows. A blank notebook was given another book's ISBN, found by searching for a description of it, and offered as ready to add. An identifier is now filled only from the code you scanned, text on the item, or a catalog match whose title is the one printed on it; anything else is marked as a suggestion and is not filed.
- Scanning a book's barcode no longer names it after an unrelated product. A food or general product database sometimes holds an entry under a book's ISBN (one had "Fit Shake" for a well-known programming book); that answer is now refused and the book catalog names the book instead.
- When a scan matches something you already have, the Scan Inbox card now offers the same answers as its details: beside +1 more, an Other menu holds Replaced the one that ran out (this one is all you have now, with today's dates) and Old one went bad. On a phone they lead the row's ⋯ menu. The details' "what gets filled in" list is folded away until you open it.
- A table you pick for a scan stays picked. Choosing a different table under "Add to" on an item now saves that choice right away. Replay, a rename, installing a new table and File all keep it, and the list shows the table you picked instead of the suggestion.
- Cobb now reads each field of a record with the label and explanation its form shows, and only the fields that hold a value, so he no longer calls a replacement value the price you paid, or fills in a price that was never recorded.
- When Cobb writes his own link to one of your records, it now shows as the chip that opens that record, instead of printing the brackets and a made-up address. A word that only shares its first word with a record, like a shop named after it, no longer opens that record.
- A photo the scanner could not identify no longer shows as "Captured item" with Looks right. While it is being read the drawer says Identifying…; if nothing comes back it says it couldn't tell what this is, and its button is Name it, which opens the name ready to type. Looks right only appears once there is a name to confirm.
- Quick edits to several fields of one item all save. Changing a few custom fields in a row (a value, where you bought it, when) could keep only the last one after a reload, with no error shown. Each edit now saves only the field it changed, merged into the record as it stands, on every kind of record: items, assets, records, machines, projects, orders, lists and locations. Clearing a field removes just that field.
- Filing a scan into an app you have not installed yet now installs it first, whichever button you press. A scanned book headed for Bookshelf used to fail with "No instance 'bookshelf'" from the session's File button, one-tap Add or the dashboard, and only worked through the card's Install & add. Every way of filing now installs the app and files the book there.
- Filing something from the Scan Inbox now updates Recently committed straight away. It used to keep its old count and leave the item out, so Send back was out of reach until you reloaded the page. Every way of filing (Add, File all, the dashboard's Add all, the put-away plan) now refreshes the inbox, its history and its counts together.
- A scan identified before the ISBN fix no longer offers its unconfirmed ISBN, serial or other identifier when you fill an existing record from it. "Is this the same one?" lists only what the scan can back up and names what it left out, and filing, filling in and File everything all leave such an identifier off the record. Drafts from before the fix now show those identifiers as suggestions.
- When a photo can't be identified because today's photo identify allowance is used up, the scanner now says "Photo identify is used up for today here." and offers Name it, instead of leaving the capture unexplained.
- A part's label prints who made it. The Printer Parts label reads the part's name, then its maker and voltage, but the maker's slot always printed blank because the manufacturer field was kept from labels. A part's manufacturer is now readable by labels, views and other modules like its name; where you buy it and what it cost stay private.
- A scan with no name no longer offers Looks fine. Pressing it hid the review but still left Add disabled, because nothing can be filed without a name. Such a row now offers Name it instead, and Looks fine appears only for a doubt it actually settles.
- A scan headed for an app that is not turned on shows what you can do about it. A member used to see a plain Add, press it and read "Enable it in Configuration → Modules", a screen they cannot open. Now an owner or admin gets Install & add, which turns the app on and files the item; a member gets Ask an admin, and the request names the app; and an app that is not available here asks for another table instead.
- When a scanned barcode matches nothing, the result now says so plainly: "We couldn't find this barcode. Take a photo so we can tell what it is." Take a photo is the main button, and the photo joins the same scan. Naming it yourself still works. The old "Not it - retake" is gone; the scan was fine, the lookup just came up empty.
- The scanner's capture drawer now has Not right beside Looks right. It opens the item with the name ready to type over, a field for the barcode on the item, and Look again, so a wrong guess is one tap from being fixed.
- A scan goes where its card says, from every button. The dashboard's "Add all to Inventory" could include an item whose card said Groceries, and File all could plan it there too. Every way of filing now reads the same destination the card shows. Once filed, Recently committed names the table the item went into, with no guessed alternative beside it.
- One countdown for one date. The Home page's attention list and its Up next could name different days for the same item ("in 4d" beside "in 5 days"), and the grocery wall could change its answer in the evening. Every place that says how long until something expires now counts calendar days from your day, so they agree.
- A book has one Print label, and it prints the book. A Bookshelf book showed two identical Print label buttons: one queued the title alone, the other queued "Unknown / title / ?" with a second QR that the scanner offered to file things into. There is now one button, it uses the collection's own label (author, title, year), and the QR opens the book. Every shipped label that names a bundle's field (a Lego theme and set number, a fastener's thread and length, a tool's maker) now prints the value instead of its placeholder. A label already printed the old way still opens its record.
- The Scan Inbox's counts no longer change as you scroll. "To review", "ready" and "waiting" count every scan in the inbox, not the ones loaded so far, and the review and waiting filters find every matching scan, including ones further down the list. A session that spans the end of a page now loads whole, so its count and its File all cover all of it.
- A receipt session in the Scan Inbox now reads on one line on a phone, like every other session: "Lidl · Sep 24 · 3 items", with the receipt's date in the line instead of on a separate one. The session's ⋯ menu has its own larger tap target, so opening it no longer folds the session, and a receipt session is never offered a merge. Merging two scan bursts is now called "Merge with the session above".
- A photo taken with the scanner's shutter is now checked for a readable barcode before anything guesses from the picture. A label with a clear barcode is looked up by that code, with your photo kept on the scan, instead of being named from the label's words.
- The home page's scan counts now match the Scan Inbox however many scans are waiting. With more than 50 pending it used to say "Waiting to file (50)" and count only the first 50, so its numbers disagreed with the inbox and the put-away card. Every count there is now the whole inbox's, and "Add all" files exactly the scans its number counts.
- The strip at the foot of a sandbox now reads in whole sentences: "This is a sandbox. You have an hour left." instead of "This is a sandbox. an hour left.", and near the end "This sandbox is nearly done. You have 12 minutes left." A kept workspace reads "This workspace is yours until 12 Oct.", and the save dialog's error messages say who could not do what.
- The scanner's flash no longer turns itself on in a dark room or when the camera starts. It comes on only when a barcode has been in view for a moment without reading, and goes off once it reads or leaves the frame. The flash button still turns it on whenever you want.
- Combining two scans can now be undone. A scan that was combined into another shows Undo combine in Recently deleted instead of a Restore that refused, and the combined scan offers it too, on its card and in its tools menu. Either one puts both scans back in the inbox with their own quantities; a count or photo you changed on the combined scan since stays as you left it.
- When the scanner isn't sure what a photo shows, the drawer now says so: "Best guess: …" with "Not sure? Name it", and Name it is the main button, with Looks right beside it. A confident identification still reads plainly with Looks right first.
- When the catalog has no picture for a scan, the item screen shows your photo as the item's picture instead of an empty "No picture" box beside it. Adding a catalog picture is still one tap, on a quiet line under the photo.

## 2026-09-23

### Improvements
- How long ago something happened now reads the same way everywhere: "just now", "5m ago", "3h ago", "4d ago", then a date. The scan inbox, approval requests, discussions and the printer's AI watch each had their own wording ("5 min ago", "3 h ago", "2 days ago").

### Fixes
- A scanned breadboard or beanbag chair no longer lands in Groceries. The router counted a food word inside a longer word ("bread" in breadboard, "bean" in beanbag) as evidence, so with a brand that is also a fruit it read a single-board computer kit or a chair cover as groceries. Only the last part of a compound word counts now, the part that says what the thing is: a minivan is still a van.
- A link to a workspace you can no longer open (deleted, or you were removed from it) now takes you to your own workspace, or to the page for making one, and says the old address is gone, instead of opening an empty workspace with a blank name.
- A named edge bridge is now reached by everything that sends to it.
