A bridge name longer than 60 characters, or one with a space at either end, could register under one address while print jobs, sync sources, AI providers or parcel tracking sent to another, so the bridge looked offline while it was polling.
- A collection you nest under its module no longer vanishes from the nav. Choosing "nest under Machines" for a collection like 3D Printers, while Machines itself held nothing, hid Machines as empty and took 3D Printers out of the nav with it. The module now stays as the collection's way in, with the collection in its menu.
- A book you scan by its barcode is filed under that ISBN, with its author. Scanning a book before you had a Bookshelf offered to add one, and that offer could carry another edition's ISBN (a reprint, an ebook) and no author, because the number was looked up from the title instead of taken from the barcode you had just scanned. The same scan no longer offers Bookshelf twice either. Scanning a vehicle's VIN into a table you have not added yet fills its decoded fields the same way.
- A scanned phone no longer lands in Groceries because of the shop that sells it. The router read every word a barcode catalog returned, including the names and links of the stores listing the product, so a phone whose seller's name ends in "egg" matched the grocery word, and with a fruit for a brand name it went to Groceries without asking. Store names and links no longer count as evidence about what a thing is; the listing's own title still does.
- On a phone, the scan inbox's counts lead with what needs you. The line at the top reads *"3 to review · 5 ready"*, with the total in the ⋯ menu as *"8 pending in all"*. A busy inbox used to push *to review* off the edge of a phone screen, and the total opened the line as a bare number.

## 2026-09-22

### Features
- The end of a sandbox can now bring what you built into a real account: the bundles, collections, fields, automations and records it listed, with your photos, made again in the new account the same way you would have made them there.
- You can delete your account: Your account → Identity & password → Delete my account, confirmed with your password. If you are the only owner of a workspace you are told which one first, before any password is asked, and you can delete it or hand it over; workspaces you share stay with their other owners. An operator can remove an account from Super admin → Users, and a person with no workspace now lands on a page with doors (make one, sign out, delete the account) instead of a dead line.
- A scan made where there is no screen can now be watched on one that is: open Live scans from the inbox's menu on a tablet, a second monitor or a phone propped at the bench, and the last few captures appear there within a couple of seconds, largest first, with where each is going.

### Improvements
- Your database dump now runs at an hour you choose (`BACKUP_AT`, default 03:00 local) instead of "24 hours after the backup container last started", so the time no longer moves every time you update. A finished dump also writes `data/backups/last-dump`, so a job that copies the dumps off the machine can run after today's dump instead of guessing.
- The desktop auto-update feed now serves multiple apps at once. Releases are namespaced by product, so the Edge Helper and the new Studio cockpit each get their own update stream from one instance. A release for one is never offered to the other. Backward compatible: the existing unprefixed feed keeps working unchanged. This also fixes Studio's updater, which shipped pointing at its own feed before that feed existed and had been answered with a 404 ever since.
- The assistant is told which arguments an action cannot run without, before it calls it rather than after. Asked to group two fields under a heading, it used to learn that the heading and the kind of record were needed by having its first call handed back, then try again; now the action's own listing says which arguments are required and which pair is an either-or, so the first call carries them.
- The end of a sandbox lists what you built there. When the hour is up, the ending page now says what you made in your own words for it: the bundles you installed, the collections, fields and automations you set up, and the records you added, counted, with the ones you scanned. It never lists the demo's own stock, nor anything you deleted since, and it says plainly what stays behind (the stock, the activity feed, chats with the assistant, the guest account). The same list is what a real account will be offered to bring along once that door opens.
- On a phone the guided tour now opens on a welcome instead of a desktop layout question, points at the menu that holds every place, and says the phone's version of each step (no keyboard shortcut, no "pair your phone" to the phone in your hand).
- The tour's "Your places" step now describes the navigation you actually have: on a narrow window, where the row folds everything but Home behind "more", it says where the rest went instead of naming rows that are not on screen.

### Fixes
- The line a card shows after Do it says things the way your workspace names them: "Colour (text) added to parts", "Spices created, a list of its own", "By rack saved as a board view of Spices", "automation Low stock alert is now on". It used to print the internal record-kind id or the automation's id in that sentence.
- Cobb no longer offers to do something your workspace has not turned on. In a workspace without Purchases it used to offer to draft a purchase order, and confirming the card failed with "check the log". Actions of a module that is off are now left out of what the assistant sees and of a record's actions, and asking for one anyway says which module to turn on.
- Actions you run on a record now use that record. Setting a field on an asset, logging a measurement on a metric, sending a command to the device linked to a thing and recording a machine's hours all took their target only from a typed argument, so asking for one on the page you were looking at ran the action and changed nothing. Four more that sweep a whole table (moving a field into Location, lifting items into types, recording an order) no longer offer themselves as a button on every record; they are workspace actions, and the assistant still reaches them.
- A saved view that groups by location (Home Inventory's By room, any table or Kanban you group by location) now groups by the room each item rolls up to, the way the list page does, instead of putting every row under "(none)".
- A card now says what it will do and what it did in plain words: "Put Purchase date and Supplier under Buying on parts" instead of "Put 2 field(s) under Buying on Part", "Change Supplier on parts" instead of naming the internal kind, and "4 hours recorded on Bench drill" instead of a bare "Done."
- Scans brought in through Import an export no longer sit under "finding the best table" for three minutes when the workspace has no table to offer them yet. Every imported row is stamped done at import, so the inbox reads it as waiting for a home straight away.
- Asked to move something into a place that does not exist ("move the multimeter to bin 4" with no Bin 4), Cobb now says so and asks whether to make it and move the thing in, instead of quietly creating the bin and moving nothing, or putting the thing in a different bin with nothing said. A move into a container that does not exist is refused outright, so a confirmed card can no longer file something in a place no list can show.
- Every Discord link on an instance now points at the same server. An operator who set both `COBBLR_DISCORD_INVITE_URL` and the older `DISCORD_INVITE_URL` got one server in the community list and another in the account chrome. The prefixed name wins everywhere now, as the self-hosting guide says.
- A record's photo actions (cover, cut out, remove) now sit in a row under each photo instead of across its bottom edge, where they used to land on top of whatever the product printed there and neither could be read on a tablet. Phones show photos two across so the row always fits. And a photo you make the cover from the gallery now wears its cover badge; it was set correctly all along but the tile never said so.
- "Put milk on the shopping list" adds milk to the shopping list, making the list if you do not have one yet. It used to create an empty list called Shopping and leave the milk out, because adding to a list only worked on a record you already had.
- A part filed from a scan now lands in Inventory's own categories, so the list's category filter finds it and the part form asks Category once instead of twice. The scan card shows the category and lets you change it before filing, and File all puts a batch under its agreed category. Workspaces that already had scanned parts are moved over on update: those values become categories in your tree, and a part you had categorised yourself keeps your choice.
- "The Rostock Max ran four hours yesterday" now records the hours on that machine: the usage action took the machine only as an argument and ignored the record it was run on, so the assistant's correct call was refused as "no machineId". And "put purchase date and supplier under Buying on parts" groups the fields: the door read a list of fields as one string, so a list sent as a list read as nothing and was refused as "required". Both were held out of the assistant's nightly benchmark as model failures for a week; their transcripts showed the doors were wrong, not the model.
- The public repository can build its own api image again. Its Dockerfile copies the demo seeds that a trial workspace is stocked from, and those seeds were not among the files published, so `docker build` stopped at that step for anyone building from the public source. The seeds are published now, and a check on every change proves that every file the published Dockerfiles ask for is a file that gets published, so this cannot come back quietly.
- A saved view that groups by one of a bundle's own fields (Yarn's By weight, Warranties' By category, any view you group by a custom field) groups by it again, instead of showing a single "(none)" section over every row. Yesterday's fix for grouping by location had narrowed the reading to the module's native columns.
- Dates are your dates now, not the server's. West of Greenwich the app used to roll over to tomorrow at teatime, so a reminder could say "expires today" the evening before, something restocked at 6pm was dated tomorrow and counted its shelf life from a day it had not reached, and a form pre-filled the wrong day. A reminder meant for a whole workspace now waits until the day has started for everybody in it, and anything you date by acting takes the day it is where you are, from the timezone in your communication preferences.

## 2026-09-21

### Improvements
- Recent activity says what an automation did, in words. A row for something an automation ran used to read as an event id and a reactor name in monospace ("ran an automation core-scan.scan.received → identify-photo"), which on a phone truncated to something that looked like an error, and three of the first six rows a new workspace showed were like that. The row now carries the action's own sentence for what it did, or its name, and an automation that found nothing to do no longer appears on the dashboard (it stays on the full activity page). An update whose record cannot be read any more names the field and the value that changed instead of a bare word like "type".
- When your scans fit a table you have not set up yet, the dashboard now shows them once, at the top, with your photos and one press per table ("2 look like Groceries, Create Groceries & file all 2"). The summary card that pointed at them is gone, and the put-away card counts only the scans that still have nowhere to go.

### Fixes
- The dashboard's "you've scanned things that fit a table you don't have yet" card no longer grows a row per suggestion. The chips are shorter (the bundle and how many scans fit it), they wrap into a box about two rows tall, and past that the box scrolls down, so a workspace with eight matching bundles keeps its dashboard where it was.
- Two small things from the first two minutes of a new workspace: setting up a table from a scan no longer says so twice (the banner on the new table speaks, the toast that used to cover the first row stays quiet), and the dashboard no longer asks the purchases module for orders on a workspace that has not enabled it.
- The scanned-item tiles on the dashboard no longer show a white square while a picture is still arriving; the tile shows the item's initials until the photo paints, and the photos load as soon as the page does rather than waiting for a scroll.
- The morning something expires, the reminder now arrives. The "expires today" line was being prepared and then dropped whenever nothing new was coming up in the same hour, which is most mornings, so since early September it only reached you when a new heads-up happened to ride along. A reminder that could not be delivered is also no longer marked as sent; it comes back on the next hourly check instead of being lost.
- Scanning something you already have: the three answers are one size on a phone, and the confirmation says which one you pressed. The re-buy card used to draw "Replaced the one that ran out" large and filled with "+1, still had some" and "Old one went bad" small beside it, and every tap confirmed as "+1 → … (now ×3)" whichever button it was. Now all three are the same size and one tap each, the card is no taller, and the confirmation starts with the words of the button you pressed and ends with the new count.
- When your scans fit a table you have not installed yet, the dashboard says so once. The card at the top now reads "3 scans fit Groceries and Bookshelf" with a Show me button that jumps to your scanner queue, where the one press that creates the table and files the scans already lives; the put-away card beside it no longer counts those same scans as homeless.

## 2026-09-20

### Features
- "Add Aran to the yarn weight choices" and "remove Coffee from the category choices" are commands now: typed into Ask Cobb, they are offered in your own words, run the field change themselves with no AI needed, and say what they did ("Added Aran to the Yarn weight choices (DK, Worsted, Aran)").
- Connect your Gmail once and your orders, parcels, returns and cancellations track themselves with nothing forwarded and nothing typed. Cobblr asks Google for read-only access, then reads mail from stores and carriers only (Amazon, UPS, FedEx, USPS, DHL, OnTrac, and any store you add), the last 7 days first and then as it arrives; every shipping, return, order and cancellation email lands exactly as a forwarded one would, and a delivery is a card in your DMs within minutes of the store's email. No message body is kept, nothing is labelled or sent, and Disconnect revokes the grant at Google. The card under Connections shows the mailbox, the workspace it feeds, what it reads and when it last looked, with Check now; a workspace's Integrations page lists the mailboxes feeding it. Self-hosters provide a Google client once; without one the forwarding address remains the door.
- Forward a store's shipping emails to your receipt address and your parcels track themselves, with nothing to type. Amazon's "Shipped", "Arriving today" and "Delivered" emails are read as the tracking events they are, matched to the order they belong to, and announced the same way a carrier update is, so a delivery is a card in your DMs within seconds of the store's email. A shipping notice that arrives before its order confirmation opens the parcel on its own; the confirmation reads into it later. Returns are read the same way: a store's return request, drop-off and refund emails are recorded on the order they came from and announced as they happen, so a refund on its way is a card, not a search through your inbox. A cancellation is recorded too: one that covers the whole order stops it being watched for an arrival, and an order that was only ever waiting for its receipt is removed rather than left waiting. A mail rule does the forwarding, and the one email that used to defeat the setup, your mail provider's forwarding confirmation, is relayed to your own inbox so you can click it. A store's promotions that the rule forwards along with the rest are dropped rather than filed.
- When a sandbox's hour is up it is held for 24 hours instead of deleted on the spot, and the ending page offers three doors: keep it as a trial for a week, two weeks or a month (your pick, and it ends when that time is up), start a real account from scratch, or, once it exists, bring what you built into a real account.

### Improvements
- A scanned code only the web could name is a tap, not a name to retype. When no catalog knows a barcode (an earbuds box, a cable, a skein of yarn, a pack of batteries) and one web page names it, the card used to show that name in its note and leave the name field empty, so you typed it back by hand. Now the card asks "Is this it?" with the name as a tap, marked found on the web and not confirmed, and typing your own stays beside it. The name is never asserted for you; tapping it is your choice, the same as typing it. Names taken from a listing also read like the product rather than the listing, without the shop's name or the search engine's cut on the end.
- The "things to use up" reminder puts each item's Used up and Threw out buttons beside the item, on Discord and in the app, instead of listing the items and then stacking every button underneath. Pressing one settles that line only; the others stay, so a batch can be dealt with from one message. A very long list shows twelve with their buttons and says how many more are waiting in the app.
- The activity log now records a list you make and a scan you file. A table or list made by hand (or by promoting a category into its own table) and every scan you file are in the workspace's activity log as what they are, with the table it went to, the name and the pictures, so what you did in a workspace can be read back later as "made a Spices list", "scanned six things".

### Fixes
- A new workspace groups its scans by category from the day it is made. The scan category field on a workspace's catch-all table used to be created only when the server restarted, so a workspace made between two restarts scanned without it until the next update; on the hosted service that could be a day. It is now created the moment the workspace gets a table scans land in.
- With no AI connected, a photo the scan could not name says so once. The inbox row used to add "The AI could not read this one" and a Retry under the note that already said no AI provider is connected; nothing had read it and Retry did the same nothing again. Now the row says the one true thing, and a Retry or Connect button appears only when asking again, or fixing the connection, can actually answer.
- Cobb's "which one?" now works however he looked, and counts where things are. Two gaps in the question he asks when a name matches two records. He only asked when he had searched for the name; reading through the whole list of machines and picking one walked past the question, and now gets it too. And "take the CubePro out of the Garage" was being asked "which one?" even though only one CubePro was in the Garage and he had just looked: any fact he read on the way now counts, so the one in the place you named is the one meant. When he does ask, each tap says what he read about that record ("CubePro #9 (in Garage)"), and he no longer states where something is unless he looked.
- A scanned spice the catalog calls a spice goes to your Spices list. Cumin, coriander, mustard and fennel seeds landed in Plants because of the word "seeds"; now what the catalog says the thing is outranks a word in its name, unless the name says outright what it is (a "spice blend" is a spice wherever a catalog shelves it) or the catalog only names an aisle like Groceries.
- On a tablet, a photo's cover, cut-out and remove buttons are visible without a hover, and the feedback bubble steps aside whenever the page changes under it, not only when it scrolls.
- Moving a scan inbox keeps every picture. Export then Import now carries the group photo a split item was cut from, the photos added to an item's gallery and a crop cut from a screenshot, each as the destination's own file. Before, a moved item kept only its main photo and its catalog picture, so Crop again had nothing to work from and added photos showed as broken tiles.
- Expiry reminders no longer stop for a whole workspace because one item's date could not be read. A best-before printed as a month alone ("2028-02", the way a tea box or a tin prints it) used to break the hourly expiry check for everything in the workspace, silently; it now counts as the last day of that month, on the calendar, in the reminders and on the item. A date nothing can read ("see lid") no longer hides the others either: the dashboard lists it under "dates that can't be read", naming the item and the field, so you can fix it.
- On a phone, the feedback bubble now steps aside when the page grows under it (it sat on the Locations card's arrow after your first add), the "is set up" banner and the "looks like" line wrap instead of squeezing beside their buttons, the Configuration title's identity line drops under it, and placeholders in the dark theme are readable again.
- The sandbox's arrival page and its hour-is-up page now carry the Cobblr mark and wordmark, in the same header as the website, so leaving cobblr.xyz for the sandbox no longer lands you on a page with nothing you recognise.
- A part's create form and detail page asked "Category" twice: once for the table's own categories and once for the grouping scans use. The second now reads "Scan category", with a note on what it is for, on every workspace.
- On a phone, the welcome tour no longer opens by asking which desktop layout you want; the phone always uses the menu, so the tour starts with the main areas.

## 2026-09-18

### Fixes
- A receipt is read in a fifth of a second on a busy server, not a minute. The text engine behind receipt review spun up threads that fought each other whenever the server had other work, and read every uploaded receipt twice. It now reads once, on one thread, and the wait does not grow with the load.

## 2026-09-17

### Features
- A refusal now explains what is missing and lets you ask for it. When something you try needs a permission you do not hold, or a bundle only an owner or admin can install, Cobblr says so in words (not an internal identifier), names who can decide, and offers Ask. The owners and admins get a card with Approve and Deny in the app and, if they have Discord notifications on, as a Discord message; a yes gives exactly what was asked to exactly you, the answer reaches you as a notification, and Finish reopens what you were doing and completes it once. A no tells you why. What cannot be asked for (a read-only role, an owner-only act) is said plainly instead of pretending. Approving never changes anyone's role.
- Members now land in the workspace, not the member portal. A member does the day-to-day work: the scan inbox, every module's pages, every record's own link, scanning and filing, adding records where a module allows it. Configuration, the bundle composer and the builder stay with owners, admins and editors and are simply not shown to a member; the portal remains reachable from the account menu and offers a way back. A guest still lands in the portal and reads what the workspace shares there. In the scan inbox, a row a member cannot file says Ask an admin on the row, on its screen and in the session strip alike, and the strip says "waiting on an admin" instead of offering to file it.
- Every form of fields, on a record page and on the scan item screen, lays itself out from what each field needs and how wide the screen is: two short dropdowns share a row on a phone, a date gets a column it fits, a note takes the row, and nothing overlaps or runs off the edge. A compact card never prints the same value twice and every field label reads as words.

### Improvements
- A design's materials and hooks pickers search your whole stash. Both were a drop-down over one page of 200 records with no search, so a workspace with a few hundred grocery rows never saw its yarn in the picker at all, and nothing said so. Type a name and the picker finds it across every table, showing what is free; when the stash is bigger than one page the list says so at the top. A hook a design already needs is listed whatever else the stash holds.
- A confident identification no longer speaks for a field the AI guessed. Every field a scan fills now records where it came from (the code, the photo, the receipt, the AI's own knowledge), the card lists the ones the AI supplied rather than read, and a field the item itself contradicts (an author printed on the cover that is not the author the AI gave) holds the row for review with both values as taps, whatever the confidence number says. Two authors who share a surname but not a given name are two people, so the row asks rather than guesses. The catalog pane says where its picture came from, a "4 Pack" in a title is a pack size and never your quantity, three different character jugs of one maker are no longer offered as one, and a long thin thing photographed lying flat shows upright in the phone row.
- Find a book by the name you know it by, and read its title your way. A titled work now keeps its other forms (the original, a translation, a transliteration) when it is filed, so searching "Yellow Fog" finds Желтый туман, and the record's title reads in your Titles in another language format on the record, in search results and when the assistant names it, the same way the scan inbox already showed it. Change the preference and every served title follows, on the parts table and an item's own page too; renaming still edits the stored name.
- The part page, the location form, the bundle metadata forms, a notification binding and a scan rule's extract fields now lay themselves out like every other form: from what each field needs and how wide the screen is. On a phone a part's fields pair up (min quantity beside cost, category beside location) instead of one per line, and a number shows as the number rather than with the zeros the database pads it with.
- An asset's, a machine's, a purchase's and a record's built-in fields, and the form that creates a part, now lay themselves out the way custom fields already did: from what each field needs and how wide the screen is. A typed name gets a column as wide as it is, so it no longer scrolls inside a half-width box on a phone, and a create form with a field of the same name as a built-in one shows both instead of the built-in twice.
- A review row asks in one line, and More tools says why once. A row the scan is unsure about now heads its question with the thing to do ("Check the name", "Confirm where it goes") and one short line of why, in place of the routing paragraph that repeated on every such row; the paragraph still sits behind Source data. When the AI was asked and did not answer, the row says so and asks you to check the name, never to set up an AI. Behind More tools, a tool that might apply says its reason once and each button says what a tap does. And a table you picked for a row stays picked through every re-run, including the background heal of older keyword routes.
- On a phone the scan inbox's chrome behaves: every footer action stays whole (the button says Add or Install & add, and the note beside it says the same words), the identity block no longer floats a band above Pictures, content scrolls under an opaque status area when the header folds away, the last row can scroll clear of the floating pills, the "same product" offer is a paragraph with its buttons under it instead of a screen, one suggested-spot action sits beside the Location field, and a session's housekeeping lives in its ⋯ instead of two lines under it.

### Fixes
- A book whose title ends in a word like "parts" lands on the Bookshelf. "JavaScript: The Good Parts", read by its ISBN with the category Books, was routed to the general Inventory table because its last word matched that table's name. The general table now yields to the table the catalog's category names.
- Cobb now sees most of a long tool result instead of none of it. The list of actions and the list of record kinds in a workspace with many modules were cut down to an empty string on their way to him, so he worked from what the prompt already said and never from what he had just read.
- "How much do I have of the black yarn" now finds the four in Inventory even when a Yarn list exists: a search that finds nothing in one list says so and looks across every list before Cobb says you have none, and a no given from one list is handed back to him to check everywhere first.
- Cobb asks which one before changing a record whose name two records share. "I need a label on the CubePro" with a CubePro #9 and a CubePro #10 in the workspace used to print a label for whichever came first in the search. Now Cobb names both and asks; the two names appear as taps under his question, and tapping one sends your sentence back with that record named. If your words already say which ("the CubePro in the garage", "CubePro 10"), no question is asked. Questions about your things are still answered from whatever matches.
- Groceries and Yarn tables now teach the scan where things go. The "similar items are stored here" suggestion, the Organize plan's list of unplaced things, the bin census and the duplicates merge all walked a registry that never listed a named table's own kind, so a Pantry full of groceries taught them nothing and a grocery merge was refused as "not scannable". They now read the workspace's own kinds, named tables included.
- The assistant no longer proposes an action it cannot run. Asking for "turn on provenance" or "rename machines to printers" could produce a card that looked right and failed the moment you pressed it, because the action was called without something it needed. Every action now says which of its details are required, the assistant is told what is missing before a card appears, and pressing a card that would have failed answers with what it needs instead.
- An action that did nothing no longer reports success. Pressing an action on a record that no longer exists, or asking for one with nothing to go on, could answer with a green tick while nothing happened, or create an empty item, order or purchase order. Now the action refuses and says why: the record is not there, or what it needs. Recording machine usage by hand adds no print you did not mention; a wire that fires on a finished print still counts it.
- A scanned book is a book, even when a product catalog has junk for its ISBN. An ISBN that a grocery or product catalog answers first now asks the book catalog before the answer is accepted, so a well-known title no longer lands in Groceries as a "Fit Shake". A wrong answer cached earlier corrects itself the next time anyone scans that book.
- An item Cobb adds never lands in a list you do not have. "New spice: garam masala" could be filed under a "spices" list that did not exist, reported as added, and then be nowhere you could open; a list that does not exist is now refused with the ones that do, a list is matched by its name or its label, and the confirmation says what was added and where.
- An editor can now do everything an admin can do with the workspace's contents, not only install things. An editor could install a bundle and then be told "ask a workspace admin" when creating the first record in it, because the capability check kept its own list of roles and had left editors off it. Every capability-gated action (creating records, adjusting stock, printing labels, using a worker app) now reads the same rank model as the rest of the product: owners, admins and editors hold every capability, and grants remain the way to give a member a specific one. Managing people, defining roles and deleting the workspace stay with owners and admins.
- A count read off a photo is bounded the same way on every path. When the identify step says how many of a thing it sees, that number was capped at 999 on one path and not at all on two others, so a label read as a count (a capacity, a weight) could seed a record with thousands. Every path now reads one rule: at least one, at most 999; you can still set more yourself.
- A scanned item can no longer be filed with a quantity of zero. The camera's quantity stepper never went below 1, but the filing step accepted 0, so a record could be created empty while the stepper refused to set that value afterwards. Both now follow one rule: a count starts at 1, and zero is only ever reached by using something up.
- The "better table" chip uses the scan's own routing rule. A product whose brand happens to be a fruit (a phone, a SIM card, a Raspberry Pi board) is no longer offered Groceries, a tea with "orange" in its name is not filed there, and File all never moves a table you picked yourself.

## 2026-09-16

### Features
- A scan row says what it needs once, and every screen agrees. Each inbox row now reads Add, Install & add, +1 more or Review, with one short sentence naming the problem and what to do, and the phone row, the item screen, the desktop card and the session's File N all show the same words. File N counts only the rows whose own button would file, and says Install & file N when filing installs a table. A destination the system picked is swapped for a better table that appears later; one you picked yourself stays put. Names read with a first capital on every card, without changing what is stored, and a new Titles in another language setting under Your account chooses how a foreign title reads (original with the translation in brackets by default).

### Fixes
- "Remove tea category from inventory" now takes the "Teas" choice off the Category dropdown: a choice is matched the way you say it (exact, then case aside, then a unique start such as "Tea" for "Teas", and a question back when two match), the kind is worked out from the field when only one kind has it, and a list of names is read as the names. Each of those used to be a refusal, and three refusals in a row ended in directions to the settings page.
- A long conversation with Cobb no longer goes red. Past forty messages in one panel, every send came back "Bad request body" until the panel was cleared; the panel now sends the newest part of the conversation and the server reads the newest part, one number for both, and the whole scrollback stays on your device.
- A question is never answered with an offer to create something. "How much do I have of the black yarn" could end in a card that would have made a second black yarn beside the ones already owned; a question is now answered from what Cobb found, and a "how ..." question without its question mark is read as the question it is.
- "Looks fine" now clears the short-barcode warning everywhere. Tapping Looks fine on an item scanned from a short (8-digit) barcode left the amber "double-check this is the right product" on the row, on the item screen and on the desktop card, with nothing left to dismiss it. The warning is now part of the item's state and goes away the moment you say it looks fine, on every surface; the "Identified via…" provenance stays readable in the Source data box.

## 2026-09-15

### Features
- Photos you already took are one tap from the home screen. An "Add photos" tile sits beside the scan shortcut on the dashboard (and a "Photos" button beside "Scan" on a new workspace's first screen). It opens the same picker the scan inbox has, so you can pick several at once; they land in the inbox as one new session with identification already running, and the page opens on that session. On Android and desktop, long-pressing the installed app's icon offers "Add photos" too.
- On a desk the scan inbox's cards are as tall as what they have to say: the tools sit in one row beside the title instead of a rail down the side, and a row that still needs you asks its one question with the answers a tap can give, instead of a paragraph and a percentage.
- A scan inbox row has one name, yours when you gave it one: typing in a card's box is never interrupted by the row refreshing underneath, the desktop card's title edits in place with a tap, and a re-run of the AI offers its read beside your name instead of replacing it.
- On a phone the scan inbox starts with its items: the put-away strip, a series offer and each session's header are one line each, the page runs closer to the screen's edges, a card offers a better table beside its filing pair, and the picture strip outlines the catalog picture.
- On a phone the scan item screen grows a dropdown's choices from the form ("New category…"), an empty picture slot is a tap that opens your camera roll, and the pinned footer sits on the screen's foot in the installed app instead of a band above it.
- Typing a model number now looks it up. Type "PB287Q" into the dashboard's box (or the scanner's) and the inbox row gets the web lookup, the product's name and picture, then the match, the same way a scanned barcode does; a typed product code takes the catalog. The card under "what you've added" opens the row it made and shows its progress while it fills in. Tapping a box on a phone no longer zooms the page.

### Improvements
- A split child shows its crop and the group photo it was cut from. The "Your photos" strip on an item cut out of a group photo now shows its own crop and the group shot (badged "group"), with a "Crop again" button beside the group shot to cut it differently. The column is never drawn empty: a row with no picture of its own has no "Your photos" column on a phone or a desk.
- The community entry in the account menu and under Send feedback now reads "Discord Community", and every community place is named the same way on every surface: the account menu, the feedback widget and a server that predates the list all read one table.

### Fixes
- Pending items routed without AI before a routing rule improved are re-checked against the new rule and moved when it says so, instead of keeping the old table beside items scanned after the change. Anything you typed on, picked a table for, or marked as looking fine is never moved, and the old route stays on the item.
- "Remove Tea from the category choices, it is no longer a valid option" now takes that one choice off the dropdown and leaves the rest, and "add Aran to the yarn weight choices" adds one, without Cobb needing to know the whole list. The same ask used to come back as a refusal drawn as a finished step, followed by directions to the settings page: a change the workspace refuses is now said as a refusal, and Cobb tries again or asks.
- Splitting a photo of several things no longer gives every piece the series the whole photo was read as. A Wicked set beside a Holiday picture frame used to make both "part of the Wicked series"; now a piece keeps the group's series, category or kind only when the piece itself names it, the series offer never counts an item whose own theme says otherwise, and pieces split before this heal on their own with the value kept aside on the row rather than deleted.
- Background housekeeping no longer crowds out the database at startup. Every periodic pass that visits each workspace (expiry checks, arrival reminders, picture and routing heals, the printer pump's discovery) now runs on one shared walk with one connection budget, its first round spread over its cadence instead of everything firing minutes after boot. On an instance with hundreds of workspaces this is the difference between hundreds of refused connections an hour and none, and the printer pump no longer opens every workspace's database every fifteen seconds to find no printer.
- The api no longer hangs in silence when it runs out of database connections. A request that cannot get a connection within ten seconds now fails with a clear error instead of waiting forever, the health endpoint reports each connection pool's numbers, the log says when connections are starving, and the hourly maintenance jobs stop holding a request's connection for the length of their run.
- A destination picked on a scan inbox card no longer vanishes when the next thing is scanned into the same session: the session group kept its key on its newest item, so every arrival rebuilt the group and reset every card in it.
- The camera scanner no longer says "Identifying..." over a store's own code while the inbox says it needs a name. A store code is known the moment it is scanned: the result sheet reads "A store's own code, nothing to look up. Name it to file it." on its first paint, offers a name field right there, a way to the inbox, and Save & next as before, and the inbox card stops offering to retry an AI that was never asked. Every sentence a scan card shows is now one short line, with the longer explanation in the tooltip.

## 2026-09-14

### Features
- On a phone the scan inbox is a list of compact cards and each item opens as its own full-screen review: the picture, what the AI read, the table it is going to with the override right on the card, a real form for its fields, and the filing action in reach of a thumb. A value you type on a pending item now stays on it until you file it, through every filing door.

### Improvements
- When Cobb works out a move from your page without AI, a record now goes to the list whose own vocabulary names it, the way the scanner files it: with a Spice Rack installed, a "Garlic Seasoning Blend" goes to Spices even though the word "spice" is not in its name, a record that fits two lists goes to the more specific one, and the line says why. The records the plan could not place are their own labelled block, "Left in Inventory, could not tell (6):", open when short and collapsed with the count when long, with what pressing enter would do about them under the label.
- Everything that floats over the page (the sandbox strip, the feedback bubble, the Live pill, Quick access, the labels basket, the bulk action bar, Back to top, the toasts) now shares one foot: bars at the bottom stack instead of overlapping, the page keeps exactly that much room at the end of every list, corner pieces sit above the stack, and all of it steps aside while a dialog, a sheet or the camera is open.
- A scanned item's menu now offers the tools it could actually need first. Split into items, Read as a receipt, Turn into a bin and the box state used to be listed on every item; each item now says which apply from what it knows (two things in the picture, a picture that reads as a receipt, a tote by name, a code read off packaging or packaging in the picture), and the rest wait behind one More tools row with the reason each is folded. Nothing is removed; a laser engraver photographed on the bench is simply not asked whether its box is empty.
- Where a scanned item came from is now checked against its receipt or order. When Acquired from says one place and the receipt says another (Facebook Marketplace on an eBay order), the card says so where Add would be and holds the item for you instead of filing it or quietly picking a side; spellings of one place count as one place, the seller named on an order is never a contradiction of the marketplace it sold on, and "Gift" or "Bought used" are never flagged. One tap takes the receipt's word for it as your own answer, a re-run never overwrites what you typed, and filing a flagged item writes the receipt's source rather than the guess.

### Fixes
- A catalog picture that arrived with the product adrift in a white field is trimmed to the product whichever way it came in, including scans mirrored from another workspace, and pictures already stored that way are trimmed where they sit over the next little while. Each scanned item now records what the trim did, or why it left the picture alone.
- A scanned item flagged for review (a crop that could not be cut, two pieces of one photo that disagree, a doubtful lookup, no name) is now left for you by every bulk action: the card offers Review with the reason instead of Add, File N counts only the rows without a flag, File lists it under "left for you" with the reason, and the dashboard's "ready to put away" does not count it. Naming it, choosing the field in question, or Looks fine clears the flag. A receipt read without AI is not flagged just because the code chose the table.
- A scanned photo no longer fills in where you bought the thing: the shop, the seller, the price and the order number come only from a receipt, an order or your own words, never from a guess beside a picture. Two pieces of one split that share a name never show two different weights from the same photo; both are left for you to choose, with the reason. And a split's crops now cut correctly when the AI writes its boxes in thousandths of the frame, so each piece gets its own picture.
- Scanning a book's ISBN a second time keeps its author and year: the cached answer now carries everything the first lookup learned, so a later scan does not ask the book catalog again and cannot lose the details to a throttled reply. A book found through the Open Library fallback is cached too.
- A scanned seasoning no longer files into a craft table because its name ends in "Blend": a word that says how a thing is put together (blend, mix, kit) is not what it is, and a food catalog's category now outranks any word its name happened to graze, so "Groceries" from the catalog lands in Groceries. A table the category contradicts is not offered at all, and an imported scan is routed with the same facts a live scan has.
- Cobb no longer offers an action that only an automation can run (one fired by an event, with its record named by the event), so "mark the task done" reaches the task itself instead of an action with nothing to act on; actions with no button on a record's page, like placing a thing in a bin, tagging it or adjusting its stock, stay Cobb's as before. Adding a field and switching on a set of fields, and a heading on a record's form and a heading in the sidebar, each now say which is which, so a request for one is not answered with the other.
- Cobb now sees what is inside a location, bin or box, and where a thing is, when he follows a record's links. "Queue labels for everything in Bin 7" and "take the CubePro out of the Garage" used to be answered with "it is empty" when the things were put there by placing them, which is how things get put in bins.
- Turning a feature on updates the records you already have open. After enabling a bundle feature such as labels, the new entry appeared in the navigation but an open item still lacked its new action (Print label) until the page was reloaded. Every change to the workspace's setup, a feature saved, a module turned on or off, a bundle installed or removed, a category created, a field edited, now refreshes everything on screen at once.
- Use / restock on the What's on hand board updates the card at once. Using one or restocking one saved the change but the card kept its old count and "in stock" until the page was reloaded, so it was easy to tap twice. Every action now refreshes everything on screen that shows that record the moment it completes, on the board, the table, the dashboard and the record's page, and Undo does the same.
- On a phone, a flagged scan says what needs your review, and Cancel takes you back. The Review button on a row is drawn like Add. Opening it leads with the one thing the row still asks (the reason in words, the answers you can tap, Looks fine, fixing the barcode) before the pictures and the form. Cancel leaves the item screen instead of hiding a button and leaving you there. The correction box is shorter: the hint field shows its whole prompt and grows as you type, and Replay and Re-run AI sit on one row.
- When a sandbox's hour is up, the page now ends: the workspace is replaced by one screen saying so, with the ways to carry on. It used to show a strip at the foot of a dashboard that was still drawing, sometimes with the welcome tour opening on top, and the server kept answering for a few minutes after the hour. It refuses from the first second now.
- The sandbox countdown strip no longer covers the camera's shutter or any open dialog on a phone: it steps aside whenever something is open over the page and comes back when it closes. For most of the hour it is now one quiet line with a single Keep button; the full bar with both choices appears for the last fifteen minutes. The feedback bubble rides up over the strip instead of sitting on its buttons.
- On a phone the Scan Inbox's selection toolbar no longer slides under the top bar as you scroll (the count and the Organize, Location and Add to actions stay tappable), and a receipt session's header keeps its merchant, order number, date, count and open link on narrow screens instead of dropping them beside the filing controls.
- A yarn's weight is read from its name when the name says it: "4 Ply" is fingering, "DK", "Aran" and "Chunky" are what they say, whatever the AI guessed, and the card says what the label said. Two pieces of one split that share a name now also treat a value on one and nothing on the other as no answer: both are left for you to choose, with the reason; a colour or a dye lot stays each piece's own.
- Two things with different numbers are two things: a set number, a part number, a model number, a SKU, an ISBN, a serial, a VIN or a licence plate is the thing's identity, so two scans that carry different ones are never offered as one product and a scan is never told it is "already on the shelf" as the other, however alike their names read. A run of four or more digits in a name now counts as such a number (a "10 pk" or a year does not).

## 2026-09-13

### Features
- When Cobb works out a plan with several destinations from your page ("get the grocery and spices out of inventory and into their sections"), the card now has one section per destination, each with its own Do this button, and a Do all button at the bottom. A part you run ticks off in place with what it did and its own Undo; a part that fails says why and keeps its button; a card you leave half done is still half done after a reload.
- The Scan Inbox row now shows how many captures are waiting to be filed, in the sidebar, the top bar and the phone menu, and the full-sidebar foot puts Scan first on its own row with your account last.
- Once a workspace's sidebar holds six rows or more it groups them under two quiet labels, Collections (the things you keep) and Tools (Locations, the Scan Inbox, Labels and other things you do), so a busy sidebar reads at a glance; a small workspace stays flat.
- The first time the top bar runs out of room it asks once whether you want the sidebar, and the more menu always carries a Switch to the sidebar entry; nothing changes your layout without a click.

### Improvements
- A feature change is one step, and it tells you what it kept. Turning a bundle's features on or off (Designs for Yarn, Outfits for Wardrobe) is now a single change that re-applies the bundle around your collections instead of removing and reinstalling it. Afterwards the confirmation says how many records it read back in place, so "your entities stay" is checked rather than promised. A collection only a feature you turned off had set up is removed when it is empty and kept, with its records, when it is not. The activity feed records each change with what turned on, what turned off and what stayed.
- An empty collection invites your first one. A fresh Bookshelf opening on its cover wall, or a Groceries board with nothing on it yet, now says "Add your first book" (or item) with the same add door the table has and the scan door beside it, instead of "No matching books." A view whose filter hid everything still says "No matching", and offers everything.
- Install a bundle by its id, and hear why when that is not possible. `POST /bundles/install` now takes a bundle's `id` as well as its manifest: the deployment's own catalog answers first, then the configured registry. An id nothing knows says so in a sentence (no registry configured, send the manifest; or not found in the registry named), instead of a bad-request error about a missing field, and the health check reports whether a registry is configured and how many bundles the build carries.
- A new or replaced AI key is tested the moment you save it, on the workspace AI page and under Your account → Connections: the row says Verified, Key not valid, Could not verify, or Unverified, the save says which, a rejected key is only saved if you say so, and a rejected replacement leaves the key you had in place. A Test connection button checks a saved key again. When a provider fails, Cobb, the scan card and the connection row now say what the provider said and what to do about it (a key that is not valid, a usage limit, a model it does not serve, a provider that did not answer), never a bare status code.
- Your account's Layout setting and the welcome tour now offer all three desktop layouts, Top bar, Sidebar and Sidebar only, each with the same small preview, and picking one saves it to your account in a single step.
- When a plan Cobb worked out from your page matches some records and not others, the card now says what it left in three parts: a sentence with the count, then every one of those records on its own line (each opens the record, with its reason where it has one), then what sending the message would do about them. The offer that appears as you type and the card in the reply say it the same way. Before, the records sat in a parenthesis inside the sentence, cut off at three with "and 1 more".
- When Cobb names one of your records in an answer, the name is now a chip you can open, not only in the cards for changes he made but in his read answers too: ask which books you have and each book in the reply opens its own page, the words around it kept. The chip works from the keyboard, tells a screen reader what kind of thing it opens, and says so if the record has been deleted since. Cobb never links a name he did not just look up, and a name two records share stays plain text rather than a guess. The answer that appears while you type, "what is low?", and the result of a change (a done card whose words do not name the record still shows it as a chip) name their records the same way, and the chips stay when you come back to the conversation.
- A receipt dropped into the scan inbox becomes a receipt session before any AI is asked: the inbox reads the text off the picture itself and, when it has the shape of a receipt (lines ending in prices and a total), sends it straight to the reader. The reader then reads a clear receipt image with no AI at all when its lines add up, and asks the AI only when they do not. When a picture does go to the AI, the AI is asked outright whether it is a receipt, so one with a big product on it is no longer filed as that product. A photo the AI could not name now says the one reason why, with the fix beside it, instead of listing every possible reason.
- Choosing a starter deep in "More ways to start" now puts its "Set up" button right inside the card you pressed, so the next step is under your thumb instead of in a panel that scrolled off the top. The install dialog leads with what you get and what you can do first; the package name, version and counts sit behind "details".
- A split can be undone. Every piece of a split group photo carries Undo split, and so does the photo in Recently committed: the photo comes back to the inbox exactly as it was before the split, with its name, its suggestions and everything the AI read, and the pieces go to Recently deleted. A piece you already filed blocks the undo until you send it back, and the message names which one.

### Fixes
- A belonging no longer borrows a grocery's verbs. With Groceries installed beside Home Inventory, a kettle offered Opened, Threw it out, Restock one and Draft a purchase order. A record's face, and the verbs that come with it, now come from the fields that record actually has; Draft a purchase order belongs to things you count.
- A typed or scanned code that resolves to nothing is no longer given a made-up name like "Store Item" or "Unidentified Item" with an Add button. The card shows the name field where the title would be, says why underneath (a store's own label, or nothing found for the code), counts as needing your review, and offers Review rather than a one-tap Add until you type a name.
- A scanned item keeps its name when its colour is filed as a swatch. A receipt line like "Blue cotton yarn 100g 200m" showed in the inbox as "#0000ff cotton yarn 100g 200m" once the Color field had been filled with the hex, while the preview and the saved record kept the words. The inbox title, the File everything preview and the record now read the same name, and the colour shows as a swatch beside it. A colour you say in words (a hint, or what the photo shows) still reaches the title as a word.
- Changing a bundle's features keeps your records. Saving a feature change on a bundle (turning Designs on for Yarn, say) removed the bundle's collections and every record in them before putting the bundle back, under a dialog that promised your entities stay. A feature change now leaves the collections and their records exactly where they were, at the same links. Records are deleted only by the Uninstall confirmation that names them.
- A receipt's printed order or invoice number reaches the session however the receipt was read: a receipt read without an AI model (a clear photo, a text or PDF export) used to leave the number off, so the session header offered to add one that was printed on the paper.
- Splitting a photo of several things now cuts each piece from the picture the way you see it, phone photos included, and refuses a cut that shows nothing rather than handing a piece a patch of the table as its photo. Each piece keeps the name the group photo's read gave it (a set with its set number), the details that photo could vouch for, and the sentence about it; a piece whose crop reads blank keeps the group photo's identity and is marked for your review instead of taking a detail the AI guessed.
- A scan's note names the step that actually decided. A store's own label (a deli, produce or in-store code that is never looked up) scanned in a workspace whose AI was erroring showed "The AI errored on this one, so it was matched by keywords" instead of saying it is a store's own label. The card now leads with what the lookup found and adds the routing's sentence after it, as its own, when there is one.
- Importing twice in a row no longer says a sync is already running. After an import, the connection's own poll ran again straight away and held the connection, so a second import (or Sync now) pressed seconds later was refused. The import counts as the poll's run; the next one is a cadence away.
- Checking an item off a shopping list, and clearing a printer's bed with a verdict, now answer only after the stock they move has moved. Before, the page could refetch a moment too early and show the old count until the next refresh.
- Cancelling a print job right after sending it now always puts the build's materials back and removes the output credit. The reversal could race the send's own stock movement and lose, leaving output credited for a print that never happened.
- Cobb now links a record he names by a natural shortening of a long title ("the blue cotton yarn" for Blue cotton yarn 100g 200m), so every record in a mixed answer opens, and "where is my lamp" is answered with the room the record is in rather than the list it sits in.
- Deleting a workspace while a request is still using it no longer restarts the api. A workspace deletion terminates every database connection of that workspace; if one of them was still opening when the deletion landed, the api process exited and every workspace on the instance lost a few seconds to the restart. Every database connection now carries its error handling from the moment it is created, a request that lands mid-deletion gets a clear "this workspace is being deleted" answer instead of a dead connection, and the deletion waits a bounded five seconds for in-flight requests rather than as long as the slowest one.
- An empty view uses the words you chose for a collection, plural included. A collection renamed in Presentation (a "person", several "people") said "No matching persons" on an empty view; the empty state and the dashboard's counts now use the plural you set.
- Restocking from the shopping list now dates the fresh stock even when the item was created by hand. A "Good for (days)" typed on the new-item form was being kept as text, so the check-off's fresh lot got no date and the record kept the old Expires while the row had promised a new one. The number is a number now whichever way it is entered, and the row says when something already on hand keeps the earlier date: "1 already on hand, dated 12 Sep, stays first". Once that one is used or thrown out, the fresh lot's date takes over by itself.
- Once you install a tracker (Yarn, Home Inventory), captures that were waiting for it offer to file into it instead of offering to install it again: Home's "looks like Yarn, install", Waiting to file's "Create Yarn & file all" and the card's "Install & add" all read the tracker you have. Installing one that is already there now says so, rather than claiming it added a table.
- An open skein with yarn left no longer shows as no yarn. When you track yarn skein by skein and open the last one, the Yarn table used to say Qty 0 and Available 0 while the skein still had most of its metres, and the item went "low" the moment you opened it. The table now says "0 skeins + 1 open · 160 m", Available counts the open skein, and running low waits until that skein is finished too. Filament, tape and anything else tracked per unit reads the same way. A saved view no longer lists the opened skein as a yarn of its own; archived things stay out of lists and views unless a view asks for them, while search still finds them, marked archived, after the live ones.
- What is left in an open skein reads in metres, not skeins. The Yarn table's "+ 1 open · 160 skein" now says "1 open · 160 m": the remainder takes the unit of the length a skein was entered in, and the count keeps its skeins. Same on the tile, the record's page and the dashboard.
- When your AI could not be asked (key rejected, limit reached, service down) after Cobb had already worked out a plan from your page, the reply carried only the refusal and the plan was lost. It now says the refusal in one line and still offers the plan as the same Do it card the workspace runs without AI.
- Print label from a record's page works again on a hosted instance, and a button that cannot run says why. The item page's Print label shortcut refused on an instance with no public URL configured (the Labels page queued the same item fine) and showed only a flash of ERR. It now uses the address you are on, the way the Labels page does, and when any action cannot run, its own reason stays beside the button until you dismiss it.
- Accepting a put-away group files it, and the walk covers everything you accepted. Accept on a put-away plan used to give the items a destination and say "Filed" while they sat in the inbox waiting for Add; the walk listed only the plan you happened to be on, so a group accepted a moment earlier was missing and "All put away" was not true; and a finished walk came back as "Resume put-away walk" after you navigated away. Now an accepted item is filed into its collection right then and the confirmation names the records it filed (an item that cannot be filed yet keeps the destination and is named with the reason), the walk lists every accepted group still to place from every plan and says what it left out and why, and placement is remembered on the item, so a finished walk stays finished.
- Print label says what happened: queued, with the count. Pressing Print label on a record queued the label and, in the same breath, said the Print module was not enabled, as if the press had failed. The button now says "Queued (3 in the queue)"; a second line appears only when a walk-up printer you set up cannot be reached from here, and says what would make it print. The Home Inventory starter's Labels option now says what it gives without a printer.
- A receipt's numeric date is read the way the receipt itself says (a euro or pound total reads day-first), else the way your other receipts read, else as the most recent day it could mean, instead of whichever way the AI happened to guess. The session's date shows the month spelled out and, on hover, says which way it was read and why.
- A receipt that couldn't be read no longer sits in the scan inbox as "not read yet, 1 finishing…" for ever. The session says why (no AI provider connected, the provider's limit, no line items found), has a visible Read again button, counts in "to review" and on the dashboard, and keeps the original. Read again after fixing the AI reads it into its lines, and the failure becomes history instead of a leftover item the put-away planner tried to file.
- Importing a scan-inbox export no longer drops the photos the AI had not named yet: a picture is a picture whether the export linked it or embedded it. And on a browser with no camera, a barcode typed into the scanner (or read off an image) now shows its result sheet instead of only landing in the inbox.
- Sign-in says so when it cannot check what this deployment allows. When the server's sign-in settings could not be fetched, the sign-in and app start pages assumed signing up was open and drew the form, which on an invite-only deployment refused you after you had typed everything. Both now say they could not check and offer to try again.
- Uploading a picture to the scan inbox no longer asks whether it is a photo or a receipt: the same AI look that names a product tells a receipt when it sees one and reads it into its lines. The question is asked only in a workspace with no AI that can look at an image, and it says so. A receipt the look missed has "Read as a receipt" on its card.

## 2026-09-12

### Features
- "Sort the rest of inventory into the right sections" is now worked out without AI: each record goes to the section its category names, a category with enough records for a section of its own gets one, and what is left (alone in its category, or with no category) is named for you or Cobb to place. One card, every line, Do it, Undo.

### Improvements
- A scan you add to something you already have now restocks it properly. Pressing "+2, still had some", "Replaced the one that ran out", or filing a receipt line onto the milk you have, dates the new stock the way a check-off does: a fresh lot from today, good until today plus its shelf life, with its bought-on date. Before, the count went up and the old dates stayed. Sending the scan back removes that lot again, or, if you have used some since, takes back what is left and says so.
- Undoing a scan attach can now take back the purchase it recorded. A purchase filed by a scan carries a reference to that scan, and undoing the scan removes the purchase from the consumption history rather than recording a correction beside it, so the learned re-buy rhythm is exactly what it was before.
- A collection opens on the view its bundle calls the first thing to see (Groceries on What's on hand, a Bookshelf on its Covers) instead of the table, and remembers the view you last picked on this device, Table included. A link that names a view still wins.
- Every action Cobb may run without asking now offers Undo on its card, and Undo puts it back: a stock move restores the count and the lots, a tag comes off again, a reorder goes back to the previous order, a grouped section returns to where it sat, a logged entry or measurement is removed, a completed task or maintenance entry reopens. A run that changed nothing offers no Undo, and a card that cannot be undone from the chat says so instead of a bare "Done."
- Keeping a sandbox now says so: the strip at the foot of the page reads "Yours until <date>" instead of vanishing, the workspace stops being called Sandbox and you stop being Guest, and you can set a password right there so getting back in never depends on an email arriving. Every account trial gets the same "yours until" line.
- The page says one thing about AI, not two. When the operator has switched it off it reads "AI is off here. Barcodes, ISBNs and typed items still work" and offers no connect link that would lead nowhere; it used to ask you to connect a free model and then say AI was off for the whole server in the same breath. And on a workspace you have not put anything in yet, including a pre-filled sandbox, the "what do you want to do" panel now sits above the alerts and previews instead of under them.
- The dashboard's overdue and coming-up alerts now carry their context. Each row expands to the items it counted, most urgent first, saying how late or how soon, with Open on each; the arrow lands on the collection's soonest-first view (Groceries' "Use it or lose it") instead of the whole table.
- When the "are you a person" check on the sandbox start page fails or times out, the page now says so and offers a way on: try the check again, or make an account instead. It used to keep saying "Setting up your sandbox" with nothing to do.

### Fixes
- Saved views name the collection and the view in plain words (Bookshelf, Gallery, Cards) instead of showing the internal kind and renderer ids; a location's contents and search results say the collection too; the bin count sheet calls a book "the only book in this bin"; and the activity feed counts "3 books" rather than "3 parts" and says "ran an automation" rather than "ran a wire". A table's declared item noun now reaches every surface that asks for it.
- A book on a Bookshelf no longer dresses as groceries. Adding one book (quantity 1) used to mark the whole shelf as stock, so every book offered Use one, Used up, Threw it out, Restock and a reorder point. One of a thing is not stock now; a collection turns stock when something is counted above one, given a reorder point or a measured unit. The Bookshelf bundle also says outright that books wear no stock face, which holds on shelves that already flipped, and the parts-category picker is gone from the book form. A number field shows the number (1, not 1.000).
- Scanning the ISBN of a book already on your shelf now offers that book instead of filing a second copy with blank fields, and the duplicate finder pairs two books that carry one ISBN whatever their titles say. A scan of a new ISBN fills author, year and ISBN, and names the book by its title rather than its publisher. Bookshelf and Lending Library carry the field roles this needs and update on their own.
- A store's own barcode (the variable-weight deli and produce labels and in-store codes that begin 02, 04 or 20 to 29) is no longer looked up as a product. Those numbers mean something only inside the shop that printed them, so a match was always a coincidence in someone's database. The card now says it is the store's label and asks you to name it, it files like a typed item, and nothing about it is cached or shared.
- "+1, still had some", "Replaced the one that ran out" and "Old one went bad" work on groceries, teas and spices again. A scan that matched something in one of those tables answered "groceries:item is not a scan target" when you pressed any of them. The record's table is now read off the match, so the buttons land on the record you already have.
- The re-buy buttons learn from groceries, teas and spices again. The consumption history for an item in one of those tables read as empty when asked for by the table's own name, so "about N days of the last one left" and the went-bad hint never showed for them. Both names now read the same history.
- Checking an item off the shopping list adds exactly what you bought, once, and the line says so first. The Groceries, Spice Rack and Tea bundles declared their check-off wires twice, so one check-off added 2 and recorded two purchases, which doubled the learned re-buy rate on every shop. The line now reads "+1 to stock when checked" with a stepper to correct the amount before you check it, and the amount you settle on is what goes onto the record and into its purchase history. Existing workspaces heal on their own: the doubled wires are dropped at the next start, and the wire engine runs the same action on the same record once per event regardless.
- When you send a message the workspace already worked out without AI (a move between lists, say), the reply now carries that plan as a Do it card whatever the model made of the sentence. The model used to be asked to run the plan and could wander off instead; now it narrates, and anything it adds beyond the plan is its own card.
- Exporting a table to CSV now includes every field the table defines, not only the built-in columns: a bookshelf's file carries author, ISBN, year, reading status and rating. Importing that file back puts those fields where they came from, and an import started from a named table (a Bookshelf, a Pantry) lands on that table instead of the base inventory.
- The actions that exist only to undo another (put a part back, remove a logged entry, reopen a task) no longer appear in Cobb's list of what he can do, and he will not run one on request: "put it back" after a change means the Undo on the card, not a guessed restore.
- The top bar's "more" menu opens when you click it. It opened on hover and the click closed it again in the same motion, so anything folded into it (Lists, for instance) looked like it was not there. The same fix applies to a module's categories dropdown.
- When Cobb looks through the records a no-AI plan left behind and names the ones it judges covered ("the remaining grocery item: Cheese Pizza"), those names now become a Confirm card for exactly those records, into the plan's destination, instead of a "want me to?" question. A reply that only offers to do what you asked is also handed back to the model once, to do it.
- A count can no longer go below zero. Using one of something you had none of quietly wrote -1. Every way of taking stock away now stops at zero: on an empty record it says "Nothing on hand to use" and changes nothing, and taking more than you have takes what is there and says so. This matters now that the assistant can adjust counts without a card in between.
- The plan card in a reply says it was worked out from the page with no AI, rather than "learned from" a template, and no longer tells you to send a message you already sent.
- "Put it back" or "undo" right after Cobb made a change now presses that card's Undo, with or without AI, instead of Cobb composing a fresh change to get to the same number.
- Restocking from the shopping list dates the fresh stock, and something you have used up is no longer "overdue". Checking milk off the list used to bump the count and leave the old lot's dates on it, so the milk you just bought read "expired 5 days ago". Now the check-off starts a fresh lot: bought today, good until today plus the item's shelf life, and the row says so before you check it. An item at zero drops out of the expiry schedule and the dashboard until it is restocked; there is nothing left to spoil.
- A saved view's order and rows now hold on the table that shows it. Groceries' "Use it or lose it" lists what expires soonest first (a record with no date last); "Re-buy soonest" and "How often you re-buy" order as they say; a view that is a subset (the lego shelf's Built and Unbuilt chips) shows only that subset. Views written the older way still order the same.
- Workspace search now finds records in your named collections (a Bookshelf, a Groceries list) and no longer returns the same shopping-list lines for every word you type. Searching "milk" brings back the milk; "Dune" brings back the book.
- A trial opens on a kitchen that reads as a kitchen, not a backlog. A ready-made sandbox could wait days before a visitor got it, and its groceries had drifted past their dates by then: a first screen showed six overdue items with blank locations. Sandboxes now wait at most six hours before being replaced, every seeded grocery is filed in the place its storage names, and the seed shows one item past due and one due today, the rest ahead. The dashboard's "needs you" list now counts by day the way the schedule does, with its own "due today" row, so the two never disagree.
- "Get the grocery and spices out of inventory and into their own sections" (or "grocery/ spices ... the dedicated sections") is now read without AI: each named thing goes into the list called that, or a new section named after it, in one card with every line. Several things into one named list ("the chamomile and earl grey into the Tea list") is one move.
- The "Get Cobblr for Yarn" start page no longer asks for a name, email, and password on a server that will refuse them. It checks first: where signup is open you get the form as before; where it is invite-only it says so and offers the waitlist; where a Cobblr account can get you in, that is the button, and a new account made that way starts as the app you asked for rather than as a plain workspace.
- Picking a catalog picture on a scan card that matched something you already have now shows that picture, instead of keeping the record's old one behind a "Catalog photo updated" toast. When you merge, the picture you picked becomes the record's photo; a picture the lookup found on its own still never replaces one already on the record.
- A table you install now routes with every word its bundle declares, not only its own short list, so the dashboard's sample drink proposes Groceries before you press Add instead of plain Inventory, and a lookup's category ("Carbonated beverages") routes by its kind the way a name does. Groceries no longer claims the word "tea" from the Tea table.
- Undoing a restock now removes the lot it started. Undo on a "Restock one", a check-off or a scan attach put the count and the dates back but left the new lot on the record, so the item still showed stock arriving on a day nothing arrived. The lot goes with the rest now.

## 2026-09-11

### Features
- Moving records into another list can now be undone from the chat, and the card after a move offers to open the list they went to. Under the hood an action can declare its own undo, and the card offers Undo exactly when one exists; whether an action needs confirming first is unchanged.

### Fixes
- A failing assistant benchmark no longer holds back the nightly release. It used to fall back to the newest commit the benchmark had passed on, which left the nightly days behind while unrelated fixes waited on one regression. The benchmark still runs before the cut and its verdict is reported in the morning summary, so a regression can be fixed before it ships; continuous integration still has to be green.
- Three benchmark cases about moving records between lists had failed every night since they were written, because the workspace they are measured against contained neither the records nor the list they name.
