- Added a scan relay: a small always-on device (a Raspberry Pi, or a $6 ESP32) can host a wireless barcode scanner's dongle and post every scan straight to your Cobblr inbox over WiFi, so a finger/ring scanner works anywhere in the house with no phone or computer in the loop.

### Fixes
- Improving an AI prompt now actually reaches the photos you already scanned. Cobblr caches every AI answer so the same photo is never paid for twice. But the cache remembered an answer by the picture it was given, not by the question it was asked, and for photo identification the question lives in Cobblr rather than in the request. So when we improved the wording of that question, nothing changed for any photo already in the cache: it kept handing back the answer the old wording produced, and there was no expiry, so it would have done that forever. The prompt is now part of what the cache remembers, so improving it invalidates exactly the answers it should. This also fixes the internal tool we use to measure whether a prompt change was an improvement, which had been quietly scoring the old prompt's answers and reporting them as the new one's. One-off effect: cached AI answers from before this change are discarded, so the next scan of an old photo pays for a fresh read.
- A barcode that nothing on the web can identify no longer invents an answer, and no longer teaches that answer to everyone else. A bare twelve digit barcode looks like a phone number to a search engine, so a product with no listing anywhere reliably turns up phone directory pages instead. Cobblr was taking the first of those and calling it the product: a pack of Harbor Freight silicone ties came back as "411 - White Pages, Find Phone Numbers". Worse, it then wrote that guess into the shared barcode database that every workspace on the instance reads, so one bad scan was about to become everybody's answer for that barcode. Three things change. A name from a web search is now only trusted when something actually backs it up, meaning the model recognised the product or several independent results agreed on it. An unsupported name is never written to the shared database, and the guesses already in there have been cleared out, so they will be looked up properly next time. And when a barcode has no trustworthy match but you photographed the item, Cobblr now reads the photo to identify it rather than treating the photo as a mere sanity check, because the label in front of you beats a search for a naked number. Real catalogue lookups are unaffected.
- Fixed: Add field was dead when you scoped a field to a class of things. A field aimed at a class has no single entity kind by design (the system works it out from the traits you tick), but the button was still waiting for one, so it stayed greyed out no matter what you filled in, and it never said why. The button now reads the same readiness rule the form itself uses, so the two cannot disagree, and when it is greyed out it tells you what it is waiting for. Choices and Renderer now also share a row rather than stacking as two full-width bands.
- A burst of edits to the same item no longer risks quietly dropping each other's changes. Several parts of Cobblr write to a shared bag of details on an item, and a number of those writes were rebuilding the whole bag from a copy they had read a moment earlier, which meant a change made in between could be silently overwritten. Every one of those writes now updates only the specific keys it owns, directly in the database, so concurrent edits to different parts of the same item all survive. This closes out the last of a class of bug that a build check already prevents new instances of.
- Fixed: the New asset and New machine forms, and the asset and machine list columns, ignored a bundle's rename of the manufacturer field. Install Vehicles, which renames it to Make, and those surfaces still said Manufacturer while the detail page said Make. They now read the name from the same place every other form does, so a rename reaches every surface at once.
- The scan-item correction buttons that write to the shared cross-workspace barcode database (This is wrong / Right: needs detail / This is good, lock it in) are now limited to the platform operator, so a member can no longer change what every other workspace sees for a barcode. Everyone still has the research hint and Re-run AI, which only re-identify their own item.
- Two fixes to the scan inbox's bulk-select toolbar. It is no longer see-through (you could read the cards through it) and it is tighter, so it takes one action row on a phone instead of wrapping to three: Discard and clear are icons now, and the labels are shorter. And a card now shows where it is being filed. Setting a location on selected items writes their filing bin, but the card only ever showed the location a bundle's AI happened to guess (a Home Inventory item's `room`), so "Set location" looked like it did nothing. The bin you chose now shows on the card with a pin, distinct from any guessed field.
- Scanned items of the same kind land together, even in a workspace that uses several tables. Two fixes to how the scanner files things. First, the category it assigns is now the broad, everyday kind you'd actually file something under, rather than the hyper-specific label a product database returns. Those databases split one kind into dozens of narrow sub-types, which is too fine to group by. Second, when a workspace has several named tables and its generic default table is hidden, the scanner previously had no catch-all, so anything it couldn't place confidently scattered across the named tables. There is now always a catch-all (the default table, told apart by category), and it reappears the moment something lands in it.
- The inbox stopped offering to merge two products that just happen to be the same brand and colour. It was suggesting you combine a Leviton wall plate with a Leviton rocker switch, because the two names share the words "decora" and "white". Half of Leviton's catalog shares those words. Meanwhile both items had been resolved from their own barcodes, which said plainly that they were different products, and the suggestion ignored that. Barcodes now decide first: two items with the same scanned barcode are the same thing, two with different barcodes are not, and the one honest exception (a ten-pack of the very same unit carries its own barcode) is recognised from the pack size rather than from word overlap. Items identified from a photo with no barcode still fall back to comparing names, as before.
- Combining two scans of the same thing now keeps what each of them saw. The scanner can capture one object two ways and produce two entries: reading a code gives you the identity but not the colour, while a photo of the same object gives the colour but not the code. Combining them used to keep one entry and throw the other's details away. Now the combine keeps whichever entry has the stronger identity and fills in its blanks from the other, so you end up with everything that was learned in one record rather than losing half of it. Your own entry's values are never overwritten; the other entry only fills gaps, and only from an entry that was headed to the same table.
- A scan no longer loses what you told it. The scan pipeline writes to one shared bag of data on each item, and several passes write to it at different times, some of them minutes apart. A few of those passes were replacing the whole bag instead of adding to it, which quietly deleted whatever anyone else had put there. In practice: a re-run threw away your answer to "keep these together, or split them?" and asked again; a re-run erased the "2 different items" offer and then paid for a second AI call to rediscover it; and when a barcode provider was briefly rate-limited, the retry marker replaced the entire bag, so a receipt line silently fell out of its receipt and an imported row lost the key that stops it importing twice. Every one of those writes is now a merge, so a pass can only touch the keys it actually owns. A new build check (`lint:jsonb-merge`) fails any future write that replaces one of these shared bags, so this class of bug cannot come back.
- Three fixes to the phone scanner. The Filing into… confirmation now shows at the top of the frame instead of at the bottom, where it sat directly on top of the capture button and blocked it. Holding the camera on the same QR code no longer spams a new toast every couple of seconds: a code held steady in the frame is now treated as one scan, and only scans again once you move it out of view and back. And the live preview no longer stutters when a location QR is read. Reading a bin or location label sets a filing target and makes no inbox item, so it no longer grabs a full-resolution photo it would only throw away.
- A custom field scoped to a whole class of items (e.g. "Acquired from" on everything physical) now shows up when you scan an item into a bundle you haven't installed yet, not just on bundles already in your workspace.
- Splitting a group photo into separate items now actually uses the AI you were paying for. When you split a photo of several things, Cobblr asks the AI to find each item and draw a box around it, so each new entry can be cropped to just its own item. That request was being quietly discarded before it reached the model: two of the three AI providers ignored the question and asked their standard "what is this item?" question instead, which of course came back with no list of items and no boxes. So the step fell back to the names it already had, and every split paid for a picture-reading call that could never have answered. It had never once worked. The question now reaches the model, so a split can crop each item out of the group shot as intended, and repeated splits of the same photo reuse one answer instead of paying again each time.
- Fixed: a barcode scanner that mangles a VIN label no longer sends you down a rabbit hole. A real scan of a door-jamb label came back with a stray leading character, which was enough for the VIN decoder to decline it, so the code fell through to the product-barcode lookup instead and came back as a completely unrelated product, name, photo and all. Two fixes, neither of which costs an AI call. A VIN is now repaired from a messy scan when the maths can prove the repair: the letters I, O and Q never appear in a real VIN, and every VIN carries a check digit, so a wrong reading is caught roughly ten times out of eleven. When it cannot be proven, the scan is left alone rather than guessed at. Separately, product barcodes are only ever looked up when the code could actually be one: a real product barcode is a fixed run of digits, so an eighteen character mix of letters and numbers is no longer handed to a product database that will cheerfully return its best guess. The repaired VIN also replaces the mangled one on the record, so you are not left holding a code that exists nowhere.
- Fixed two things a scanned vehicle got wrong. The VIN field stayed empty after a VIN scan, and was still labelled "Serial number", even though the Vehicles bundle relabels it to VIN and declares that it holds the VIN. Two causes: the confirm form's built-in fields were hardcoded, so a bundle's relabels never reached them (every other form already reads them), and the fill planner recognised the "this field holds the code" role and then ignored it. The VIN now lands in the VIN box, and the box is called VIN. Separately, the colour was invented. A silver van came back as a blue-grey hex, because vision was asked what colour the car was and duly made up six hex digits. A colour resolved from the paint code stamped on the vehicle's own label now wins over the model's guess, and the photo pass is asked to read paint and colour codes off labels alongside the serial numbers it already reads.

## 2026-07-13

### Features
- Match a new set or part to the catalog as you create it. The new-item form now shows a "Match to a catalog" search on catalog-backed instances (Lego Sets, where it used to be hidden), and that search now reaches hosted catalogs. Type a set name or number, pick the Rebrickable entry, and the new set is linked to it, so its theme, year, pieces, and photo come along without typing them. Instances with nothing to match against stay clean, and scanning still auto-matches the same way.
- The new-item form is wider, and matching a catalog fills in the fields. The new set/part dialog now uses a two-column layout instead of one tall scrolling column, so it fits the screen. And when you match to a catalog, the entry's data flows into the fields: pick a Rebrickable set and its set number, theme, release year, and piece count fill themselves in, blanks only, so anything you already typed is kept. Which fields a catalog fills is declared on the catalog, so it works for any catalog that maps its columns to an instance's fields, not just Lego.
- Consumables can now be tracked one unit at a time. On a yarn's page, under Consumption, turn on "Track each skein separately" to get a plain count by state ("3 skeins, 2 new, 1 open") with the metres left in the skein you are actually pulling from shown quietly, never a blended total across skeins. It is opt-in per item and reversible, so existing consumables keep their single gauge until you ask for the per-unit view.
- Per-unit consumables (opt-in per item) now track parallel projects. Open a skein and assign it to a project, and its withdrawals post to that skein's own statement with the project as the reason; a second project can have its own skein open at the same time, and there is still never a total across them. When a project finishes with metres still on the ball, a clearly reusable amount returns to the pool silently while a small leftover asks a one-tap "keep it, or done with it?" (writing off leaves an explicit "written off" line, never a silent disappearance) - the threshold is a tunable percentage of the skein (15% by default). When a project's skein runs out you can continue it onto a fresh one in a tap. The per-skein/per-project cards appear only when you actually have parallel skeins or a binding, so a single working ball stays the simple count. Under the hood, consuming a reservation (and every stock-adjust) now writes the consumption ledger, so a project pulling from a bound unit finally leaves a statement line and the running balance is always right.
- Printers can now reach a CUPS printer on your LAN from a hosted Cobblr through the on-site edge bridge. Add Printer's manager field is now a "how Cobblr reaches it" chooser: Direct URL (self-host or same network, as before) or Via edge bridge. Install a bridge inline if none is running yet, or pick a connected one, and Cobblr hands the print job to the bridge, which speaks IPP to CUPS locally. The bare URL box that promised an edge bridge with no way to set one up is gone, and a hosted direct-to-LAN address now explains the fix ("run an edge bridge") instead of failing with a raw SSRF error.
- A Sets form leads with sets, not minifigs. When you quick-match on a new item, results now put the instance's own catalog first, so typing "falcon" on a Lego Sets form surfaces the Falcon *sets* before Falcon minifigs. The preferred catalog is worked out from the fields the instance already declares, so it needs no extra setup and applies to any catalog-backed instance, not just Lego.
- A custom field can now apply to *all physical items*, not just one kind. Say you want to track where something came from: FB Marketplace, a gift, bought new. That question is the same for a part, a machine, a vehicle, a whole room. Until now you had to create the same "Origin" field once per kind, and create it again by hand every time you started tracking a new kind of thing. On the `/fields` page, the destination picker now offers All physical items (and All digital items) above the list of individual kinds. Pick it, and the field lands on everything physical you track: parts, assets, machines, vehicles, locations. Anything physical you add later gets it automatically, with nothing to redo. If you want it worded differently on just one kind, add a field with the same name to that kind and the more specific one wins.
- Scan a vehicle and Cobblr now fills in its color too. It reads the paint code off the door-jamb label (the one already in your scan) and resolves it to the real color name: a built-in table covers the common makes instantly and for free, and a code the table doesn't know is looked up with a quick web-search, so the color fills with no AI tokens for the usual case. It only fills an empty color field, and if it can't resolve the code it leaves it blank rather than guess.
- Enter a vehicle's VIN and the form fills in the year, make, model, and body for you, pulled from the free NHTSA lookup. It only fills fields you left blank, tags each one "filled from VIN, double-check", and one tap undoes the lot.
- Scan a VIN and get the whole vehicle. Point the camera at a car's door-jamb VIN barcode and Cobblr decodes it against the free NHTSA database, then mints a record already named "2003 Honda Accord" with make, model, year, body, and fuel filled in, the same one-tap flow a product barcode uses (a UPC still goes to the product catalogs; a VIN goes to the vehicle database, decided by the code's shape). You can still type a VIN into a vehicle form to fill the same fields. It's a suggestion you can double-check: only empty fields are touched, and a bad or partial VIN says so instead of guessing. Works on the shipped Vehicle Maintenance bundle and on any make/model/year table you build.

### Improvements
- Assets instances (Vehicles, Plant Care, Documents…) now open the full page, not a bare list. Instead of a name-only stub, an assets instance renders the same rich Assets page scoped to it: thumbnails, the list/gallery view toggle, saved views, and full detail/edit, so a Vehicle on /vehicles is a complete record. Adding a new one and opening detail stay inside the instance, and "New …" uses the instance's own noun.
- Automatic backups nudge you to turn on a schedule. A backup destination starts manual-only, and a manual backup nobody remembers to press never happens. Now any manual-only destination shows an amber reminder with one-tap Daily / Weekly buttons, scheduled rows show their next run time, and connecting Google Drive confirms the connection and points you straight at the schedule.
- Your dashboard now nudges you to install the bundle your scans are asking for. If you've scanned things that fit a starter bundle you haven't installed (a pile of VIN scans with no Vehicles table), the dashboard shows a "3 look like Vehicles, install" card. One tap installs the bundle and files every one of those scanned items into its new table. It only appears on an established workspace (a brand-new one already gets the same offer in its first-run panel) and only for owners/admins.
- Enable more of a bundle's features right from its page. If you installed a bundle in a hurry (say the one-tap "Install Vehicles & add" from a scan) and skipped its optional features, the instance's page now shows a "More you can turn on from <bundle>" strip listing what's still off (maintenance schedule, connected-car…) with an "Enable features" button that opens the bundle's modal to switch them on. Renders nothing once everything's enabled.
- The "Migrate from another app" section is now app-first: you pick the app you are coming from (Homebox today, more to follow) and then choose how to bring it in (live connect or CSV) as a second step, instead of every method for every app crowding the section as its own card.
- Scanning something you don't have a table for is a first-class flow now, not a buried card. When a scan's best match is a bundle you haven't installed (a VIN → Vehicles), the closed inbox card shows a one-tap "Install Vehicles & add" (no need to open it), and if you do open it, that bundle is the default destination in the picker with its real, editable fields (year, trim, mileage, fuel, color…) pre-filled from the scan, instead of silently defaulting to Inventory with read-only chips. Nothing is created until you confirm; picking another table opts out. (Also: a flaky external catalog thumbnail now falls back to your own photo instead of showing broken on load.)
- Scan something you don't have a table for yet? Install the right bundle and file it, in one tap. When a scan's best match is a bundle you haven't installed (scan a VIN with no Vehicles table), the confirm form now leads with an "Install Vehicles & add this" card instead of quietly dropping the item into generic Inventory. It previews the fields the scan already filled (make, VIN, year, model, fuel…) and, in one tap, installs the bundle and files the item straight into its new dedicated table.

### Fixes
- Automatic backup destinations work again. The "Add a destination" button on the Backup settings page was stuck disabled because the page loaded its destinations and drivers from the wrong URL (it dropped the "/backup" path segment), so the list always came back empty. Corrected the path, so you can add and schedule a server path / NAS or Google Drive backup destination again.
- Bundle marketplace cards show their own icons again instead of a generic box. The self-hosted registry index fills in a placeholder box glyph for bundles whose icon is a web-only field, and that placeholder was overriding each bundle's real icon on the card. The card now prefers the bundle's authoritative icon over the index placeholder.
- The "N queued" label pill no longer collides with the feedback button. In top-bar layout the queue pill now stacks neatly above the feedback pill instead of overlapping it. In full-sidebar layout it moves into the sidebar foot as a row alongside Notifications and Feedback, so there's no orphaned floating pill left in the corner.
- Fixed a cluster of pages that scrolled sideways on a phone. Deeply-nested Locations now indent less on small screens and each card's meta + buttons wrap onto their own line instead of pushing the page wide; the trait fingerprint on Actions (physical · fungible · …) wraps between axes; and a long project title no longer stretches the project page past the screen edge. Nothing changes on desktop.
- On the Presentation settings page, the KIND/INSTANCE tag column is now a fixed width, so the name column starts at the same place on every row and the list lines up left to right instead of jittering between the shorter KIND and longer INSTANCE tags.
- Vehicle scans now name the body style and reliably fill the color. A scanned VIN mints a fuller name ("2019 Honda Civic Hatchback EX", adding body + trim), and the paint-code color now resolves even when the label read just lists the codes without labeling one "color": it matches each code against the paint table, so the real one wins (a Honda "NH830M" among "TGG K LJ5" becomes "Lunar Silver Metallic"). The catalog photo search also folds in the resolved color, so the suggested picture matches the actual car.

## 2026-07-12

### Features
- Bundle updates now apply by their version number instead of nagging you for every change. Small fixes (patch releases) apply themselves quietly in the background. Feature releases (minor versions) apply themselves too and show a toast telling you what was added. Big releases (major versions) still ask first, so you review and confirm exactly as before. Anything that would collide with a field you customised keeps prompting, so nothing you changed is overwritten silently.
- A catalog that ships with a source URL (like the Rebrickable catalogs the Lego bundle installs) now has a "Pull from source" button that imports its rows in one tap, instead of running a script. The very large set bill-of-materials still uses the bulk seeder.
- Hosted catalogs are now browsable. Open a hosted catalog (Rebrickable's sets, parts, minifigs, colours, themes, part-categories) and you can scroll its entries and search them, the same as a catalog you imported yourself. The rows come from Cobblr's shared reference catalog on demand, and colours show their swatch. The one exception is the set bill-of-materials: it powers Disassemble behind the scenes and isn't meant to be paged through, so the page says so instead of looking empty.
- On a hosted deployment, a large reference catalog (Rebrickable) can now be served from one shared, centrally-refreshed copy instead of imported into every workspace. Installing such a catalog links to the shared copy automatically; matching, hydration, and disassembling a set all read from it. Self-hosters are unchanged: catalogs import into the workspace as before.
- Label codes now show everywhere you work with labels, not just on the printout. The on-screen preview and the browser (⌘P) print sheet draw the code in each QR center, every queued row shows its code as a chip, and a new Codes button lets you find an item by typing its code, rename a list's prefix, or change what a kind's codes group by.
- Printed labels now carry a short human-readable code (like m1, p42, b7) in the center of the QR, so you can read an item off a shelf or type it into search without scanning. Each list of things gets its own prefix and its own running count, the codes ignore case and shrug off look-alike typos (o vs 0, l vs 1) when you search, and printing warns you if a code would ever crowd the QR too much to scan.
- Five fixes to the Labels page. The code in the middle of a QR now draws as a clean circle for short codes and a stadium pill for longer ones (a two-character code used to squish into a mangled oval). You can now hide the code from the QR center per kind of thing, from the Codes panel: handy for singular kinds like a single Office where a code adds nothing. In the queue list the full label URL moved onto its own line so it is never cut off. The sheet preview now fits the column it sits in, so the second copy of a two-up label is no longer clipped. And in the Add-labels browser an already-queued row lets you take it back out: it reads "Added" and turns into "Remove" on hover.
- Disassembling a set now opens the sorting planner over exactly the parts it just spawned, so you go straight from "taken apart" to "here's a plan to bin these." More generally, any action can now hand off to a first-party flow when it finishes, and the sorting planner is reachable as one of those flows from outside the scan inbox.
- The bundle marketplace catalog now comes from Cobblr itself instead of a GitHub repository. Your instance builds and serves its own catalog, so it works offline and always matches the bundles in the running version.
- A table view now lets you tick rows and hit "Organize selected" to open the sorting planner over just those items, so you can bin a hand-picked set from anywhere you can see them, not only from the scan inbox.

### Improvements
- Ask Cobb can now read your workspace through a local AI that runs its own tools. Some local AI backends are agents that run tools themselves and only return text, so they never hand tool calls back the way a metered API does, and Cobb couldn't see your data through them. Now you set "How this AI runs tools" on the connection (Ollama, OpenAI-compatible, or Local AI via edge bridge) to the "runs tools itself" option, and Cobb reads your real records through it. Each turn, Cobblr mints a short-lived, read-only grant scoped to just that one workspace, and hands the backend its own workspace address to read from, so one backend can safely serve several workspaces, each with its own grant. It is read-only through this path (Cobb can look, not change); to have Cobb make changes, connect a tool-calling provider (Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, or a tool-capable local server) instead.
- Ask Cobb keeps your conversation across a page refresh. The chat used to reset to empty whenever you reloaded the page. Now each workspace's conversation is remembered on your device, so a refresh (or reopening the panel) picks up where you left off. Only the plain back-and-forth text is kept, not live buttons like a pending Confirm card, and a new trash icon in the panel header clears the conversation whenever you want. History is per workspace and stays on your device (it is not uploaded).
- Ask Cobb now sees more of your screens. After the Scan Inbox, Cobb also knows what's on your Purchases (open orders + how many are in transit), Machines (count + a breakdown of whatever states your machines use), and Inventory (item count + how many are low on stock) pages. So "what do I have going on?" reflects where you are, and you can ask about what you're staring at.
- Ask Cobb now knows what screen you're on. Ask "what do I have going on?" while looking at the Scan Inbox and Cobb can reference the backlog you're staring at ("19 pending, 16 waiting 2d+"), instead of answering as if the page didn't exist. Each screen tells Cobb a one-line summary of what it's showing; Cobb uses it for relevance but still answers your actual question (a workspace-wide question still gets a workspace-wide answer). The Scan Inbox is the first screen wired up.
- The inventory item page got four yarn-driven fixes. Quantity now has inline +/- steppers sitting right next to its unit ("[-] 1 [+] skein"), so a tap adjusts stock with no pop-up. The "Reserve for" search names your own workspace's tables (a yarn and designs workspace reads "search a design, project…") instead of hardcoded generic nouns. And the Yarn table now treats yarn as the consumable it is: it hides the Maintenance log (that section is for machines) and turns on skein-by-skein consumption tracking by default. Existing yarn tables pick up the section changes when the bundle updates.
- Location labels no longer print the little code in the middle of the QR by default. A location is unique by its name (there is one "Office"), so a disambiguating code drawn in the center is just noise, unlike a part or machine where "p42" tells apart one of many similar items. Locations now default that center code OFF while everything else keeps it ON, with no setup and no re-print of existing labels. Your own per-kind toggle still wins if you want it a different way.
- Empty module pages now explain what the tab is for: the Purchases page shows a short "What's Purchases for?" hint (drawn from the module's own description) when you have no orders yet, so a fresh tab isn't a mystery.
- Added a guided Bluetooth printer self-test you can open from a support link. It connects to your label printer in the browser, prints alignment, print-quality, and QR test labels, and sends the results straight to your support ticket, so we can diagnose a printer we don't physically have.
- Photo scans now read a serial number or service tag off an item's label and file it into a real Serial number field on the item, not just the scan notes. It is read verbatim (never guessed or completed from a partly-hidden label), and Machines gained a native serial number field to receive it (Assets and Inventory already had one). Relabel it per table if you like, "Service tag", "VIN", and so on.

### Fixes
- When we close several of your feedback items in one combined email, replying to that email now threads your reply back into Cobblr as a followup, the same way replying to a single-item resolution already did. Previously a reply to a combined email had no tokenized reply address, so it landed in an unmonitored mailbox instead of reaching your conversation.
- Updating a bundle that ships a reference catalog no longer risks the rows you imported into it. Bundle upgrades now preserve catalog data (the shell is refreshed in place, your imported rows stay), and only an explicit uninstall clears a catalog. This also closes an edge where a new version that removed a catalog could have dropped your imported rows without asking. Catalog-bearing updates still prompt for confirmation during the rollout, so nothing you imported is ever lost silently.
- Capturing an item into a capability-rich bundle now shows what it can do. Type "lego set" into *Add your first thing* and the matchmaker's Create button used to install the bundle with its defaults and file your item silently. Now, when the bundle carries optional capabilities, Create opens the same chooser the recipe path uses so you pick what it does first, then your captured item lands in the tracker it builds. Bundles with nothing to choose still create in one tap.
- Editing an AI connection now shows its saved settings. The edit form used to blank every non-secret field on open, so a dropdown (like "How this AI runs tools") snapped back to its default and looked like it wouldn't save. Now the form pre-fills what's actually stored (base URL, dropdown choices, model), and saving merges your changes instead of replacing everything, so leaving a secret key blank keeps it. Secrets are still never shown.
- Dates on a design (target and completed) now save the instant you pick them, and a small "saving / saved" note confirms your edits are being kept, so there's no wondering whether progress was recorded. Previously the mobile date picker wasn't reliably triggering the save.
- Setting up a bundle with optional capabilities from onboarding now shows what it can do. Picking a recipe like Lego in "What do you want to do?" used to install it silently and drop you on an empty page, with no hint that Bricks bins, the Rebrickable catalog link, Disassemble, labels, and scanning were even part of it. Now, when a recipe has optional capabilities, "Set up" opens its detail card so you see and choose them first, then land in your new tracker. A recipe with nothing to choose still installs in one tap. Also fixed a bundle card that printed its description twice.
- Scanning into a named instance now defaults the confirm form's "Add to" to that instance immediately. Before, while the workspace's table menu was still loading, a routed scan (a yarn barcode → Yarn) briefly showed the generic base table (Inventory part) as the destination, so a fast confirm could file it into the wrong table. The routed instance is now seeded from the match itself and shown on the first render.
- An item whose identity is a colour, like a yarn with a colourway, now shows a colour swatch as its list and detail thumbnail instead of a generic catalog photo. Any item carrying a colour field with a hex value gets the swatch; the catalog photo still lives in the item's Photos section.

## 2026-07-11

### Features
- Describe your home to Cobb, right next to the plan. The little hint box handles quick corrections; describing places is a conversation. Tell Cobb on the put-away plan opens the assistant alongside it (both stay usable side by side). Cobb can see your live plan and backlog through a built-in tool, and as you describe your home ("there's an electrical closet in the hallway, the garage has a chemicals shelf") it proposes real location records, each created only when you confirm. And Cobb finishes the job itself: once your places exist, it re-plans for you (a refresh of the proposal only, nothing files without your accept) and the open plan updates in place with a small "Plan updated" note. Talking to Cobb never closes the plan, and pressing Escape in the chat leaves it alone.
- Rebuilt the Lego bundle. Installing it now gives you a Sets table of its own (each set sealed, built, or disassembled) instead of Lego fields bolted onto the generic parts table, with checkboxes to also track individual Bricks in bins, link the Rebrickable catalog so sets and parts match real data, disassemble a built set into its bricks, and print QR labels.
- The plan now covers what's already set, and your hint survives the session. Items that already have a home (you filed them by hand, or scanned them with an active bin) used to vanish from the put-away plan as "already filed"; now they lead it as ready groups: "All set, just put them away," pre-accepted and included in the put-away walk with nothing to click. And a hint you give the planner carries for your whole session: scan more items or send one back, and the recomputed plan still honors it; the plan line shows *guided by your hint "…"* with a clear link. Also in this pass: the plan dialog matches the inbox column's width, the hint field and its Re-plan button are one joined control with "or tell Cobb instead" beside them, and Tell Cobb now opens the assistant with ghost guidance in the composer instead of pre-typed text.
- Put away a fresh pile even without AI: the plan now proposes starter bins by category. When you plan a backlog on a workspace that has no bins yet (and AI is not connected), the planner used to leave every item marked "Needs a destination" with nowhere to send it. It now reads the category each item already carries and rolls them into a handful of common shelves (Groceries, Beverages, Liquor & Spirits, Books & Media, 3D Printing & Filament, Personal Care, Electronics & Cables, Household & Home, and so on), then proposes a new bin for each. A pile of a hundred scattered items collapses into a short list of bins you can accept, rename, or send elsewhere. Items with no category still cluster by name or wait for you to pick a spot. Detail and edit modals also use a bit more of the screen height.
- Ready groups get an "I did it!" button, and every destination is clickable. Items that already have a home now show an I did it! button in the put-away plan: one tap files them as real records at their set location and clears them from the backlog (previously they were quietly folded into a walk, which also spawned a phantom "resume put-away walk" nag just from loading the page; that's fixed). You can re-route a ready group by clicking its location chip, and a proposed new bin now has two editable parts: click the name to rename it, click the "in <location>" to change which location it goes under (both use the proper location picker). A related fix: confirming an already-located item now keeps its location instead of dropping it.
- Scan a printed Cobblr QR label and Cobblr jumps straight to it. If you have a USB or Bluetooth barcode scanner, it now works like a remote for the app: from any page, scan a bin, location, or item label and that thing opens on screen. Before, scanning a bin label from somewhere other than the Scan page dropped a mystery row into your inbox instead of opening the bin. The first scan asks once whether you want labels to jump automatically (Always / Just this once / No), and remembers your answer per device. Product barcodes still land in the scan inbox as before, from anywhere. Separately, the second-screen "drive this screen with scans" control (for a wall tablet that follows a scanner on another device) is now a small link instead of a large panel, since most people never need it.
- The put-away plan is now a lens on the inbox, not a popup. The sorting plan is just your pending scans regrouped by where they should go, so it lives right in the scan inbox behind a header toggle: By session (grouped by scan time, the default) or Sorting plan (grouped by destination). Flip to Sorting plan and the inbox becomes the plan in place, using the full height of the page instead of a cramped modal. Everything you could do before is still there: category starter bins, evidence-backed existing bins, ready groups, accept, rename, re-route, the hint box, and handing it to Cobb. The dashboard and scan-page "Put them away" buttons flip straight to the Sorting plan lens. Nothing is committed until you accept, and you can switch back to By session at any time.

### Improvements
- Open a printer's controls straight from its machine page. The Print manager panel on a linked machine now has an Open controls button that opens the full cockpit (camera, temperatures, pause/resume/stop, on-printer files, and print history), the same modal a fleet tile opens. Operating a machine now feels identical whether you reach it from Digital Fabrication or from the machine's own page. The button waits until the manager is reporting the printer live (a printer it can't currently see has nothing to control).
- The label print queue is now a live preview: each queued label's QR follows your current label base URL, so changing the base updates the preview (and what prints) without re-queuing. The queue row shows the URL each code will encode, and a note reminds you that printing is what bakes the address onto the physical labels.
- The label sheet preview moved to the bottom of the page. Order is now queue, then the browse-to-add panel, then the preview. You build the batch first (browse, add, set copy counts) and the preview sits at the end as the final check before printing, instead of pushing the browse panel below a tall mostly-empty sheet.
- Change how many copies of a label to print, right from the queue. Each row in the label queue now has minus and plus buttons next to its count, so you can bump a bin from one sticker to six without removing it and adding it back at a different quantity. The sheet preview and page count update as you go.
- Each row in the label print queue now shows the full URL its QR will encode, in the row's empty space, so you can see the address (and spot a mis-set base URL, or an old bare-path label) before you print.
- The labels page uses the width of a wide screen. The queue and its sheet preview now sit side by side on a wide desktop (preview on the right), and fall back to stacked on a narrower or portrait window. The browse panel's Locations tab lays its areas and containers out in two columns to match the other tabs, instead of one tall single-file list with empty space to the right.
- A "Needs you" printer now says why. A machine flagged for attention on the fleet floor used to show an amber ring with no reason, most visibly on the camera wall. It now names the cause right on the tile in every view: clear the bed (a print finished), print failed, or a printer error. This covers prints that finished outside a Cobblr job (a Bambu started from its own slicer), which previously read as "Needs you" with nothing to explain it. (A firmware update being available is not a "Needs you" reason, and never was.)
- The notifications inbox now shows each notification as its own rounded card with clear spacing, an accent tint on unread ones, and the workspace and time as a header line, instead of one unbroken wall of text.
- Jump from a printer's controls to its full record. The cockpit (the camera/controls modal a fleet tile opens) now has an Open machine link in its header that takes you straight to that printer's machine record, on its own collection page, with the detail open. Together with last change's Open controls button, you can now round-trip freely between operating a machine and editing its specs, mods, and notes, from either side. Under the hood, machines in a named collection (3D Printers, Laser Cutters) are now correctly deep-linkable, which also fixes cross-app links that used to point every machine at the default Machines page.
- The sorting planner now matches items to bins by their category, not just by name, so parts land in the right bin at a finer grain (a "1x1 plates" bin apart from a "wedges" bin). It also plans a specific pile of items on request and handles large batches without an AI ceiling, so a whole disassembled set can be organized at once.
- The put-away planner no longer dead-ends silently when there is no AI. Without a connected AI provider the planner can only reuse bins that already hold similar things, so a fresh workspace ended up with everything "unassigned" and only a faint "Similarity plan (no AI)" label to explain why. It now shows a clear, actionable notice: "No AI connected, the planner is grouping by where similar things already live, and won't propose new homes, so brand-new items stay unassigned," with a Connect AI link. When AI is connected but the plan call failed for a run, it says so and offers Re-plan instead.
- The put-away planner now self-heals a stale no-AI plan. Plans are cached (so reopening the sheet does not silently burn AI tokens), which meant a plan first built while AI was not connected stayed the similarity-only version forever. Now, when you open the planner and the saved plan is the no-AI one but your AI is connected, it re-plans exactly once with AI, caches the result, and never re-runs on its own again. The explicit Re-plan button is still there. Separately, the planner now passes the requesting user to the AI call, so a user-scoped personal AI connection (bring-your-own credentials) actually resolves instead of quietly falling through to the managed provider and landing on the heuristic.
- The put-away card now names both what needs a home and what's ready to go. Instead of only "3 scanned items without a home," the dashboard card and scan strip acknowledge the whole picture, e.g. "3 scanned items without a home, and 15 ready to put away", so items you've already assigned (waiting on an "I did it!") are counted too. The card also shows when everything is assigned and only needs putting away.
- The QR "New-code style" chooser now shows both options side by side as equal-width cards, each with its own description, so you can compare Self-describing vs Opaque without clicking to find out what the other does.
- QR label URLs are much shorter. A descriptive token is now a short kind code plus a 12-char slug (e.g. /qr/loc/Xk7d2mNq9pLc) instead of the entity's full UUID, so the QR is less dense and scans more reliably on small labels. Already-printed labels keep resolving. Public labels stay opaque and unguessable (a longer slug, no readable prefix).
- The scan inbox Export button is no longer a blunt "dump everything." It opens a chooser: pick exactly which items (checkboxes, pre-ticked from whatever you had selected), and choose how photos travel. Link keeps the file small and hands the destination a per-file, expiring link to fetch each photo (you pick the expiry: 1 hour, 24 hours, or 7 days). Baked in embeds the images in the export file itself, so it is fully self-contained and works for an offline or LAN-only destination that cannot reach back. None exports metadata only. The instance sets the default mode (self-hosters default to Baked in; a hosted instance can set `SCAN_EXPORT_DEFAULT_PHOTO_MODE=link`) and you override it per export. On the security side, a Link export now mints a per-file token scoped to only the file it names, instead of one org-wide token, and the default lifetime drops from 14 days to 24 hours. The importer restores baked-in photos with no network and still fetches links when present.
- The scan inbox is harder to disturb by accident. The merge control, which folds one scanning session into the previous one, used to sit on the collapsed session header with a down-arrow that was easily mistaken for the expand arrow, and a stray tap mixed unrelated sessions together with no way back. Merge now lives inside the expanded session as a plainly-labeled "Move into the previous session", and every merge shows an Undo. Separately, the standalone green "Scan session active" banner no longer repeats the session row it sits above: when the live session is already shown in the list, its active dot and End control move onto that row instead.
- Sent-back items get surfaced, and a session shows when it was last edited. When you send a committed scan back to the inbox, it now returns to its original spot in history (its scan time is preserved) and the page scrolls to it and flashes a highlight so you don't lose it. And a scan session whose items were touched well after the session (a later fix, or an item sent back from a commit) now shows a faint "edited <time> ago" next to its real scan time, so the header reflects when you scanned, not when you last poked it.

### Fixes
- AI features that quietly ignored your connected AI now use it. Several AI calls (scan identify, receipt reading, web-search product lookup, pattern extraction, template matching, catalog matching) were not telling the AI layer which user was asking, so a personal AI connection routed to just you would not resolve and the feature fell back to no-AI. All of these now pass the requesting user, so your bring-your-own AI is used everywhere it should be. A new build check (`lint:ai-invoke`) keeps it that way: every AI call must pass the user, or be explicitly marked as a background job, so this whole class of "why is there no AI here" bug can't come back.
- Machine and asset tiles show their photo when browsing to label them. The label page's browse panel drew an initial-letter chip for every machine and asset even when the record had a picture, because those two kinds dropped the photo on the way to generic lists. They now carry it through, the same way inventory parts already did, so a printer shows its printer and a tool shows its tool.
- Cobb's opening line now matches your write-mode chip. It only promises to check with you before changes in Ask mode; in Auto it says changes apply as you go (tracked and undoable), and in Off that it won't change anything. A fresh Laser Cutters install also pins "Laser fleet by state" (always populated) instead of "by tube type" (empty until you fill it in).
- Clicking "Ask Cobb" again now hides the assistant panel instead of doing nothing. The button toggles, and its tooltip reads "Hide Cobb" while the panel is open.
- Labels added from the Labels page's browse panel now encode a scannable QR URL (your label base URL plus a QR token) instead of a bare internal path, so a phone camera opens the record. Previously only the per-item "QR" button did this, and browse-printed labels ignored the custom label base URL.
- Opening Labels no longer crashes with a "Something broke on this page" error while the list of labelable items is still loading. A hook in the browse panel ran only on some renders; it now runs on every render.
- The put-away plan no longer shows two conflicting messages at once while re-planning. When you re-planned, the "Re-planning… the current plan stays visible" banner and the stale "AI couldn't build a plan / No AI connected" notice both showed at the same time. The AI-status notices are now hidden while a re-plan is in flight, matching how the other banners already behave, so you see one clear message instead of two contradictory ones.
- Adding a scanned item no longer wrecks the plan you built for the others, and unrelated things stay separate. Two planner fixes. First: if you'd grouped some items (say a headset and a mount into "Aviation Accessories" via a hint) and then scan one more unrelated thing (a TV), the plan now keeps your existing groups and just adds the new item, instead of re-planning everything from scratch and losing your work. Second: items that have nothing in common no longer get lumped into one "Unassigned" group. Each unrelated orphan is its own entry with its own destination, so a TV, a headset, and a tablet mount are three separate decisions, not one impossible-to-file pile.
- Sending a committed scan back no longer rewrites its scan time. Un-confirming an item (Send back) used to stamp it with the current time, which dragged its whole scan session's header time and inbox position forward to the moment you undid it. Un-confirm is an undo, so the item now returns to its original spot with its original scan time intact; only its "last touched" time moves.
- The workspace list (the menu you open from the workspace name) is tidier. Names sit further left now that the active workspace is marked with a slim accent bar instead of a reserved checkmark column, so more of each name fits. Hovering a row no longer squeezes or hides the name: the rename, members, and default-star buttons fade in over the right edge instead of pushing the name aside. The drag-to-reorder handle is hidden until you hover the row, so it stays out of the way.

## 2026-07-10

### Features
- Cobb (and MCP) can now reach everywhere. The AI tool registry grew real coverage of the whole workspace: purchase orders + vendors, sales customers + orders, builds, locations, tags, and catalogs are fully creatable/editable/deletable through the AI (digifab jobs: edit/delete; files: delete). Your custom fields: including their dropdown choices. Now ride along with every kind, so "set the finish to matte" works on a field you defined yourself. A new list_related tool follows a record's links ("which projects use this part?"). And tagging + placement are now invokable actions, so "tag these summer-stock and put them in Bin 4" just works, in Ask Cobb (with the usual confirm cards + undo ledger) and over the MCP server alike. Long lists are honest too: a capped page says "there may be more" instead of letting the AI overclaim.
- Cobb gets an autopilot, because every change he makes is now tracked and undoable. The "Propose changes" toggle grows into a three-way mode, Claude-Code style: Ask (every change needs your Confirm; the default), Auto (record creates, edits, and deletes apply immediately), and Off. Auto is safe to hand him because of the new AI change ledger: every write Cobb performs, confirmed or automatic, is recorded with a full before-image, and gets an ↩ Undo button right in the chat. Undo a create and it's deleted; undo an edit and the old values come back; undo a delete and the record is recreated. Actions (print a label, adjust stock) still always ask; they can't be un-done in the real world. And the classic record kinds (parts, machines, assets, projects, tasks, lists) are now fully editable/deletable through Cobb too, not just createable.
- Cobb can now actually look at your workspace, and do several things at once. Ask "which of my yarns would suit this pattern?" and Cobb *reads your real records* (search, list, fetch, under your own permissions) before answering, instead of guessing. Ask him to "save that pattern as a note and add the hook to my shopping list" and you get each proposed change as its own card to confirm or skip: writes still never run without your say-so, now including updating fields on a record and deleting one (where a module allows it). Works with every provider that supports tool calls (Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, tool-capable local models); anything else falls back to the old behavior gracefully.
- You decide what Cobb may touch: right in the chat. Two toggles now sit at the top of Ask Cobb: Read my data (may Cobb search and read this workspace's records to answer you, worth knowing: that record data is sent to the workspace's AI provider, which for a shared AI is another member's connection) and Propose changes (may Cobb suggest creates, updates, deletes, and actions, each still needs your Confirm). Both are on by default, per-person per-workspace, and enforced on the server, flipping one off actually withholds those abilities from the model; it doesn't just hide buttons.
- Number fields can declare their unit. Adding a number field (Configuration → Fields) now offers a Unit picker: "mm", "g", "in", or any unit from your workspace vocabulary. Values render as proper quantities everywhere ("12 mm", honoring your symbol-vs-word display preference), the input shows the unit while you type, and bundles can ship fields with units built in (the CNC tooling, fasteners, yarn, laser, pet-care, and plant-care bundles now do). A declared unit also tells Cobblr what a number physically *is*, which is what upcoming size-aware features read, never your field's name.
- Fill a parts rack with one grid. A layout's edit mode gained + grid: rows × columns and a naming scheme (A1…C8 or Bin 1…24) create and place a whole set of bins at true scale in one shot; a 24-bin rack stops costing 24 modals. Each bin is an ordinary location afterward: scannable, labelable, heat-mapped.
- Per-layout snap grid: 42 mm = exact Gridfinity. Every layout now has its own snap pitch (edit mode, "grid"): a drawer set to 42 mm quantizes bin positions and sizes to true Gridfinity cells, the edit dots show the real pitch, and the panel reads sizes in grid units ("3 × 2 u"). The garage keeps its coarse default.
- Named instances are first-class kinds now. Every named instance (a "3D Printers" copy of Machines, a "Yarn" copy of Inventory) registers its own entity kind, synthesized from its module's declared primary kind. Instance items now place on floor plans, resolve in search, and are reachable by AI tools, all through the one registry, with instance-rooted endpoints that are never guessed.
- Live Sort now works on a totally empty workspace: bins that name themselves. No bins yet? Set up bins in a Live Sort session creates numbered Bin N locations (plus an optional Unsorted catch-all); you write matching numbers on real containers with a marker. Scan the first item of a brand-new family and the directive becomes a bind offer: "go to Bin 4, this starts your Fasteners bin". Confirming names the bin ("Bin 4 · Fasteners"), the number on the container stays right forever, and the next fastener routes there automatically. Undo un-names it. QR labels stay optional: print them from Labels any time, the bin's identity never changes. The new-workspace homepage now offers the whole flow as a first step ("Got a pile of stuff to put away? Start a Live Sort").
- Live Sort: zero-tap sorting and a paired big screen. Two additions to the sort session. On the phone camera, an opt-in auto-Done toggle makes the next scan confirm the previous directive: point at thing after thing, zero taps, with the Undo chip covering mistakes. And the sort session now pairs across devices: open Live Sort on a PC, scan with your phone in Sort mode, and each directive appears on the big screen the moment it's minted; confirming on either device advances both. Same session, two screens, no setup.
- Live Sort: scan a thing, get told where to put it. The scan page's new Live Sort button (and the camera scanner's Sort chip) opens a streaming put-away session. Every scan comes back with a destination directive ("go to Bin 3", with the evidence: "4 similar items already live here"); you drop it in and confirm with one gesture (spacebar at a bench, a big Done button on the phone, or scan a bin's QR to file it there instead). Similar items in a row route instantly ("Same as the last one."), a no-confidence match parks honestly in your catch-all bin, an Undo chip reverts the last confirm, and End session shows the payoff: items sorted, per bin, plus what's left for review. The Guided Organize put-away walk now runs on the same session engine, so a walk resumes exactly where you left it, and a phone sort session resumes on the PC.
- Your fleet tiles and your machine records are finally the same machines. A fleet tile whose device is linked to a machine now wears the machine's own name (the connection label demotes to the subtitle), and on the camera wall a printer with no camera shows the machine's photo instead of a dark void. In the other direction, the 3D Printers / Laser Cutters pages now show a small live chip on each linked machine (printing 46%, needs clearing, idle, offline) right beside its lifecycle state. The registry and the floor stopped being two disconnected identities: first slice of the one-machine-two-lenses plan (docs/design-decisions/machines-digifab-unification.md).
- The put-away plan gives you the last word on everything. Four new controls on the plan sheet. Rename a proposed new bin inline (pencil next to the chip): your word for it beats the AI's, and the renamed bin becomes the evidence that routes every future item of that family. Split any item out of a group with the × ("not related"): it stays in the inbox, unfiled. Expand any item (the chevron on its row) to open its full card right in the plan, accordion-style, and fix a wrong name, photo, or identification in place; the card is a fixer there (no confirm-into-a-table, no discard, so nothing gets accidentally filed mid-plan), and the sheet notices your edit and offers Re-plan. And a hint box lets you tell the planner ground truth ("these are camping gear") and re-plan with it folded in; groups you already accepted stay filed.
- The floor plan's whole someday list, closed out. The describe seed now also places the furniture you name (matched to existing sub-locations, never invents or overwrites); big rooms on a floor plan preview their contents inline from the zoomed-out view; members get read-only plans at `/portal/…/locations/…/plan`; and search results grew a where-is-it location chip. Plus the floor-plan end-to-end drive joins the e2e yardsticks.
- Items on the plan, photos in the rects, trapezoids for the wrenches. A layout can now place the things themselves: machines, assets, and parts each take a cell on their location's plan, with an "items here, not placed" strip in edit mode and drag-to-re-file into any box. Placed rectangles (items and bins alike) can carry decoration: photo fill, or a silhouette outline (taper-left/right, triangle) drawn inside. Overlapping rectangles are fine; the smaller stays clickable on top.
- The put-away front door. Scanned things and never put them away? Now the dashboard says so: whenever captures exist with no home, a card appears ("14 scanned items don't have a home yet") with one action: Put them away. It previews where everything should go (grouped destinations, with the evidence), nothing moves until you confirm, and the guided put-away walk handles the physical half. The scan page carries the same strip above the inbox, and the card disappears when everything has a home. Live Sort stays where scanning starts (the scan page, the camera's Sort chip); if you re-scan something already captured mid-sort, it gets routed, never duplicated.
- A wrong commit is redoable. Committed a scan into the wrong table, or as the wrong thing? The scan page's new Recently committed list has a Send back button: the scan returns to the pending inbox for you to fix and redo, and the entry it created is removed from its module. A scan that merely attached to an existing entry (add-quantity, link, move) never touches that entry when sent back. The mirror of Recently deleted: every scan decision now has an undo.
- Your external Claude can now fully read AND write your workspace. The MCP server gains `cobblr_create_record`, `cobblr_update_record`, `cobblr_delete_record`, and `cobblr_search_records` (cross-kind search) alongside the existing reads and actions, so a Claude Desktop / claude.ai connector can save a knowledge entry, fix a record's fields, or clean one up, not just look around. Under the hood these come from a new shared tool registry (`@cobblr/workspace-tools`) that the in-app Ask Cobb chat is being wired to as well, so the two AI surfaces stay capability-identical by construction. What's writable is honest by design: a record kind is creatable/updatable/deletable only where its module declares the route, no guessed endpoints, no surprise 404s.
- Organize now knows what fits. Tell a container its interior size (Edit location → "Interior size (mm)", optional) and give your measurement fields units, and the put-away planner stops suggesting physically impossible homes: a 300 mm drill bit never gets routed into a 100 mm bin, however similar its neighbors, and if a plan does target a tight bin, the group carries a visible "may not fit" warning instead of a silent mistake. Declared data only: no dimensions declared, no size logic. Cobblr never guesses physics from names.

### Improvements
- The homepage is more useful. A pinned view that groups its data (a kanban "by state", "by type") now shows the group breakdown with counts and proportion bars, so you see the distribution at a glance instead of a flat list of the first few rows. The redundant full/half width toggle is gone. And your scanner's waiting-to-file queue (with the "what do you want to do" shortcuts) is now a proper section above recent activity, where you can reorder or hide it, instead of buried at the very bottom.
- The Fleet tab on 3D Printers shows your 3D printers, not the whole shop. A machine collection's Fleet tab (3D Printers, Laser Cutters, ...) now scopes to devices linked to that collection's machines: the laser no longer photobombs the printer floor. Nothing is silently hidden: a footer counts the farm devices outside the scope and links to Digital Fabrication, which remains the whole-shop floor (pools and production runs cross collections on purpose).
- The plan hint can name places that don't exist yet. On a fresh workspace with no locations, tell the planner "I have an electrical closet, electrical items go there" and the plan proposes New location: Electrical closet with the matching items grouped into it; accepting creates it. Your first locations can be made by describing your home.
- Entity kinds can now declare a `listEndpoint` in their manifest, alongside the existing create/update/delete declarations. First-party generic surfaces (the floor plan's entity occupants, future pickers) enumerate a kind's rows through its module's own list route, with the module's own role gating, instead of hardcoding module names. Machines, assets, and parts declare it; any future module joins with one manifest line.
- The dashboard "at a glance" tile for a multi-type module (Machines, Assets) now lands on that module's page instead of diving into just one type. When the base list is empty but you have types like 3D Printers and Laser Cutters, the page shows those as a chooser with counts. So "Machines · 59" opens a page that actually shows printers and lasers, not just the biggest one.
- Organize plans stopped guessing. A put-away plan could previously file items into a room-level location on vibes ("Garages is the natural home for vehicle equipment") with no evidence anything similar lived there. Now: destinations backed by what's actually in a bin show their evidence as before; a pick with no evidence is either converted into a proposed new bin under that room (rooms aren't homes, bins are) or clearly labeled as the AI's suggestion, not a match (amber). Plans also use your workspace's own vocabulary for group names instead of invented retail categories, plural forms and the Accept-all button now say what they count, and the dialog title matches how you opened it ("Put away your scanned backlog").
- "Put them away" opens instantly, and the plan sheet stopped being clunky. The dashboard card and scan strip now pre-compute the plan in the background the moment they show the count, so clicking reveals a ready plan instead of a spinner (it refreshes automatically when your backlog changes). The sheet itself got wider (the same width the inbox uses), every item row expands with a proper right-side dropdown arrow (the whole row is the click target), a proposed new bin is renamed by clicking its chip and typing right there, and clicking any destination chip opens the location picker directly beneath it instead of a control stranded three levels down.
- The floor plan's entity occupants now derive placeable kinds from the entity-kind registry (declared list + update endpoints, physical trait) instead of a baselined hardcoded module list. Any future module's items become placeable with one manifest line; the lint baseline is empty again.
- When the scanner confidently recognizes a specific known item (a named book, film, album, or a product by make and model), it now fills in fields it can know for that exact item, like a book's ISBN, publisher, and publication year, even when they aren't printed in the photo. Fields filled from catalog knowledge rather than read off the item are flagged in the notes so you can double-check them. Generic or uncertain items stay conservative and only use what was actually observed.
- The "A new version is available" prompt now appears as a toast in the bottom-right, alongside the other toasts, instead of a pill floating in the middle of the screen. Tap Refresh to load the update.

### Fixes
- Tightened the spacing between the actor, action, and item name in each recent-activity row so they read as one sentence and more of the item name fits, especially on mobile.
- A pinned grouped view on the dashboard now groups strictly by its own group-by field. Before, a view grouped by a field that was empty on the items (like "Laser fleet by tube type" when no laser has a tube type set) fell back to showing each item's subtitle, which is a different field, so it displayed the wrong data under the wrong heading. Now an unset value is its own bucket, and when nothing carries the grouping field the card says so and points you at the fix instead of drawing a misleading breakdown.
- Operator Console light mode no longer shows a dark band across the top. The header now follows your theme (light on a light page, slate in dark mode) while staying visually distinct so it's clear you've left a workspace.
- Opening the guided put-away planner or Live Sort from a deep link no longer leaves `?organize=pending`, `?livesort=1`, or `?sort=1` stuck in the URL. Before, the parameter stayed behind, so every page refresh reopened the planner (or re-armed Sort mode even after you turned it off). These links now trigger once and clear themselves.
- The scan inbox location picker is wider now, so a nested "Room > Bin" path shows in full instead of truncating to a few characters. On a wide header the "Email receipts to" block is pushed to the right edge to make the room.
- Once you tag a series on the scan page, later-arriving books in that same series now tag themselves automatically instead of resurfacing the "1 of N isn't tagged yet" banner. Vision identifies items incrementally, so a straggler that finishes after you tapped "Tag series" used to slip through. Now it's swept in.

## 2026-07-09

### Features
- Organize what you already track. The scan page's new Organize header button points the put-away planner at your *committed* things that have no location yet: everything Cobblr tracks but doesn't know a home for, grouped and routed to bins the same way a scanned pile is (evidence, proposed new bins, per-group accept, the put-away walk). Homeless inventory gets homes without re-scanning anything.
- Organize a whole pile at once. Select 2+ scanned items and hit Organize…: Cobblr groups them by what they are and proposes where each group should go: an existing bin where similar items already live (with the evidence shown), a sensibly-named new bin, or "needs a destination" when there's no defensible answer. Accept per group or all at once; nothing moves until you say so, and anything you already filed by hand stays put. Works without AI too, grouped by where siblings live. Then Start put-away walk for the physical half: bin by bin, each destination becomes your active filing bin and you scan (hardware scanner) or tap items off the checklist: progress saves, so a Resume put-away walk chip picks up where you left off.
- Homebox is now a live sync connector. Alongside the CSV import, you can connect a self-hosted Homebox instance under Configuration → Integrations → Live sync → + Add connection → Homebox: paste its URL + an API key. It brings your items across whole (name, quantity, cost, serial, model, manufacturer, warranty, custom fields, and each item's photo), rebuilds your location hierarchy, and files each item into its location. Import once, or leave the sync on to keep mirroring; matched by Homebox id so a re-run updates in place. (A quick heads-up for the curious: Homebox's API lists items in summary form and keeps locations as a tree, so the connector re-fetches each item's detail and flattens the tree; you don't need to think about any of that.)
- Switching from Homebox? There's now a one-stop migration under Configuration → Integrations → "Migrate in from another app". Pick Homebox, upload its CSV export, preview exactly what will happen, and import: your items become Inventory parts (with serial, model, warranty, cost and all), the location paths rebuild your Locations hierarchy, and labels become tags. Anything Cobblr has no column for is kept safely in each item's details, so nothing is lost. It lives right beside the live sync connectors (Ravelry), because Homebox's CSV is its most reliable interface. (Photos aren't in Homebox's CSV, a follow-up will bring those.)
- One-click "Import from Homebox (live)." Under Configuration → Integrations → *Migrate in from another app*, a new Homebox: live card imports your whole Homebox in one step, paste your Homebox address + an API token, and it brings your locations, items, custom fields, and photos across (photos are the thing the CSV export can't carry). When it's done it asks whether to keep it synced (future Homebox changes mirror automatically) or import just this once: either way the connection is saved under Live sync, so you can re-run it, turn on syncing later, or archive it anytime. A Homebox on your local network is reached through your workspace's edge bridge. The old CSV import is still there as Homebox, CSV for when you'd rather not use the API.
- Whole-house floor plans: floors as tabs, rooms that zoom. A building's page now shows one plan per floor behind tabs ("Main floor" / "Basement"; "+ floor" creates them sized). Rooms draw on their floor as visible rectangles; clicking one zooms into the room's own plan in place, fully editable, without losing the house view. A toolbox's laid-out face zooms the same way. Dropping something inside a room re-files it there and lands it on the room's plan at the same physical spot.
- Floor plans: draw a space to scale, and let it file things for you. A location's detail page can now hold a real 2D plan: the room as a rectangle (dimensions in whatever unit you think in: "33ft", "7.3m"), interior walls with door cutouts, and every rack/toolbox/bench placed where it stands. Each rectangle IS a storage location: click to open it, drag it into a bay and it re-files there. Zones show as faint toggleable labels, never chrome. Containers get the same treatment in elevation, a toolbox's face lays out its drawers at true scale, partial-width ones visibly partial. Or skip the drawing: describe the space in a sentence and the AI drafts the plan for you to true up. The day-to-day view shows no numbers at all, measurements live in the data and appear only while editing.
- OpenRouter, first-class: one key, any model. Add an OpenRouter connection (workspace AI or your personal AI connections): paste your key, name a model (`anthropic/claude-sonnet-5`, `openai/gpt-5.5`, …), done, no base URL to know, and switching models later is editing one field. The generic OpenAI-compatible provider gains the same optional Model field, so vLLM and other exact-name servers work on a personal connection too (LM Studio still needs nothing). And Ask Cobb's create-from-chat got honest and smarter: modules now *declare* which records can be created from chat (knowledge entries and tracking metrics join in), Cobb is told each kind's real field names so a saved pattern lands with a proper `title` instead of erroring, and it never offers a create that would fail on confirm.
- The floor plan works for you now: scan into, heat, print. Every plan's toolbar gained three buttons: scan into opens the camera already filing into that space (rooms' zoom views arm directly: "I'm standing at the workbench"), heat shades each box by how much is stored in it (rooms count their whole subtree), and print opens a paper-white plan in a new tab, one page per floor for a whole building.
- Tell a bin what it is: pair its QR with the product's UPC. Scan a bin's QR label to make it the active bin, then scan the container's own product barcode (a "Sterilite 6qt", say) and tap "This is ‹bin› itself" on the result card. The product identity lands on the bin's own location record, brand/product under its details, the catalog photo as its picture, instead of filing a new item into it.
- Self-hosting gains a fourth TLS mode: `COBBLR_TLS_MODE=tsnet`. The bundled proxy joins your tailnet as its own virtual node and serves Cobblr at `https://cobblr.<tailnet>.ts.net` with a real, auto-renewed certificate. The box needs no Tailscale install and nothing exposed; you paste one reusable auth key into `.env` and `docker compose up`. The blank-`COBBLR_ACME_EMAIL` crash in the DuckDNS/Cloudflare modes is fixed in the same image (the optional line is now dropped when empty).
- Docs now ship with the feature, and publish with the release. Every feature changeset carries its user documentation (`## docs` + `docs_target:`), written in the same PR while the change is fresh, enforced at the merge gate. `scripts/docs-flush.mjs` publishes each blurb into its target doc only once the feature is actually live, so public docs never describe something that hasn't shipped and release-day docs work is zero.
- Sync connections (Ravelry and friends) get a history. A connection you're done with: imported once and don't want it polling, say, can now be Archived instead of removed: it drops out of the Live-sync list into an Archived section below it and stops syncing, but nothing is lost. One click Re-activate puts it back where it left off (its id-map and settings survive). Archive ≠ remove, *Remove* still deletes for good; *Archive* just tucks it away for later.
- Zones: areas inside an area draw as thin dashed outlines, not nested boxes. Bay 1 / Bay 2 / Bay 3 divide a garage. They aren't rooms inside it. A nested *area* in the Locations tree now renders as a featherweight dashed outline with its name pinned to the border line, and its contents stay at full width in the parent's own flow. Dashed = zone, solid = container, so the two grammars read apart at a glance. Pure re-draw: nothing about the hierarchy, scanning, or filing changes.

### Improvements
- Ask Cobb got noticeably smarter and more consistent. Two workspaces used to give very different answers to the same question: one cause was a prompt-delivery bug where the managed AI silently dropped Cobb's workspace instructions (personal-bridge AI kept them), so they now behave the same. Cobb also stops deflecting real questions with "I only manage records"; it answers helpfully (general knowledge included) and *offers* to save the result. And it can now create more kinds of records straight from chat, anything a module exposes, like a knowledge entry, instead of a fixed short list. (Groundwork for full tool-calling, reading your workspace's contents and taking multi-step actions, is written up in docs/design-decisions/ai-chat-tool-calling.md.)

### Fixes
- Cleanup from field-testing the shared-AI flow. The owner's offer email now says Ask Cobb (one last "Ask Cobblr" straggler); a workspace chatting through a direct Ollama provider gets Cobb's workspace instructions again (a prompt-delivery fix had missed that one adapter); resolving an offer clears the stale notification for every owner, not just the one who clicked; and the Edge bridge ● online/offline indicators now also cover URL connections that ride your bridge (LM Studio via bridge transit), not just the dedicated Local-AI connection.
- Saved views open as a real page, and their rows are clickable. Opening a pinned view from the dashboard now lands on a full-page view (`/views/‹id›`) instead of a preview modal floating over the Views configuration list. And every row is now a link: dashboard pinned-view rows, kanban cards, and list rows all click through to the machine/asset/part they name (registry-driven, so instance kinds like 3D Printers resolve too).

## 2026-07-08

### Features
