- See what's inside your things. A machine's detail now has an "Installed components" panel: add the parts that live inside it (a printer's mods, a server's PSU/SSD), see them at a glance, and remove them. It's a generic containment panel that will show up on other containers next.
- Scan components straight into a machine or server. Point the camera at a machine or asset's QR label to make it the active bin, then scan each component's barcode. It's identified and dropped *inside* that container automatically. The "scan into a location" flow you know, now for any container.
- Saved views got a real builder, and sorting now works everywhere. Creating or editing a view is now a field-aware form instead of a raw text box: pick filter fields and operators from dropdowns (`is`, `is any of`, `is not`, and numeric comparisons), pick columns and a group-by field, and (new) add sort rows to order a view by any field, ascending or descending, with tie-breaks. "Is any of" lets one filter match several values at once (e.g. machines that are *building* or *rebuilding*). Sorting is now honored by every module's list (machines, assets, and orders join inventory and projects), so you can finally order your things by most-recently-updated, cost, priority, or whatever field matters.

### Improvements
- When a member offers to share their AI with a workspace, it no longer sits unseen. The workspace owner now gets an email as well as the in-app notification, a dashboard callout with Approve / Decline right there, and an amber dot on Configuration: instead of just an easily-missed bell item. And on phones, the header's notification bell finally has a home: the menu now carries a notifications row with an unread badge (and a red dot on the ☰ button when you have unread items), so mobile users can actually reach their inbox. The person doing the sharing also sees, on their AI connections page, whether each share is *awaiting owner approval*, *approved*, or *active*. No more wondering if it went through.
- Your personal edge bridge (the local-AI tunnel) now shows whether it's actually connected: the same green "online" / grey "offline" dot the workspace's 3D-printer and device bridges already use. It appears on each edge-bridge connection in Connections, and, if you run one, as an Edge bridge ● row right in your account menu, so you can glance at it from anywhere instead of guessing why local AI isn't answering. Same liveness signal under the hood, just surfaced by *your* bridge instead of a workspace's.
- Groundwork for tracking what's *inside* your things: a new containment relationship so a component can live inside a machine or server (and a part inside a printer). This step adds the plumbing; the panel to see components and scan them straight into a container arrives in the next update.

## 2026-07-07

### Features
- Adding a printer to a detection service (like PrintGuard) from Cobblr now works for any connection type the detector supports: including Bambu: driven by a small manifest mapping rather than hardcoded per type. Mirror an existing Cobblr machine and it reuses the credentials Cobblr already holds (they stay server-side, never sent to your browser); for a Bambu you pick which printer (serial). For a single-printer connection you can tick "stop polling it in Cobblr" so only the detector talks to that printer, no double-load.
- When an external detector (like PrintGuard) owns a printer, Cobblr now fully stands down for that printer: per printer, so it works even inside a shared multi-printer connection (e.g. one printer of a Bambu farm moved to the detector). Cobblr stops its own AI watch and camera pull for it, won't send it jobs, and marks it *"watched by detector"* on the floor, so the printer's camera is only ever touched by the detector, with no double-load. Its telemetry (status/progress) keeps flowing, so you still see it.
- New "PrintGuard (send frames)" detector option: Cobblr grabs a frame and posts it to PrintGuard for a verdict, instead of PrintGuard pulling the camera itself. This is the path for cloud Cobblr + a LAN camera (or any setup where PrintGuard can't reach the camera but Cobblr can). It uses PrintGuard's new `/classify` endpoint, which ships in PrintGuard 2.3.0, and Test now reads the running version and tells you clearly if the box is too old ("needs PrintGuard ≥ 2.3.0, found 2.2.2") instead of silently doing nothing.
- Ravelry is now a proper sync connector you point at any table. It used to be a fixed card on your profile that only imported into the Yarn bundle. Now it lives with your other integrations (Configuration → Integrations → Add connection → Ravelry), and you choose which inventory table your stash lands in: the flagship Yarn table or one you built yourself. You no longer need the Yarn bundle installed. You get the standard sync tools too (a before/after import preview and ongoing reconcile), and length is stored as Ravelry provides it (metres), with yd/m display left to your unit preference.

## 2026-07-06

### Features
- Print-failure detection can now use a self-hosted detection service you run anywhere: point it at an Obico ML API or PrintGuard box (or any HTTP model on your LAN) and the spaghetti watch scores prints through it. Pick "External detector" as the backend, add the service's URL + token, and go. Nothing is hardcoded: each service is a small manifest, so new ones drop in without a code change.
- Printed QR codes can now outlive this instance. Under QR codes (owner/admin), set a Label base URL: a stable name you control (a domain, a DuckDNS, or a Tailscale name) that forwards the `/qr/…` path to wherever your workspace lives. New codes encode that base instead of the instance's own address, so if you later self-host, change domains, or leave the hosted app, you just re-point the forward and every printed label keeps working. Leave it blank to use the current address; already-printed codes are unaffected.
- Your lists now show the right face on their own. A collection you count and reorder (filament, screws, groceries) keeps its full stock view: quantities, reorder points, allocations. A collection you just catalog (films, books, recipes, anything with no quantities) reads as a lean list of your own fields, with none of the stock machinery cluttering it. The platform decides from your data, so there's nothing to choose; a one-tap Auto / Always track stock / Catalog only control in the list's Settings is there for the rare case you want to override it. Your list also speaks its own words now: name a collection "Films" and it says "New Film", "search films", "No films yet", never "part", with a "What do you call these?" setting to fix the odd plural. Two new saved-view layouts arrive too: a gallery (a poster wall) and a heatmap (a habit-tracker grid).
- The scan inbox can now export itself to a file, not just import one. The new Export button downloads your whole inbox as an interop file (JSON or CSV): photos included, via short-lived no-auth links another instance can fetch. So moving a scan inbox between workspaces or between Cobblr instances is now Export here → Import there: no re-scanning, no database surgery. It round-trips both ways (and still interops with companion app); on re-import Cobblr re-runs its own matchmaker.

### Improvements
- Feedback moves into the sidebar in full-sidebar mode. When you hide the top bar, the Feedback button is no longer a pill floating over your content. It becomes a row at the top of the sidebar foot, in the same "notices" area as the verify-your-account nudge, set apart from the workspace tools below it. Top-bar mode keeps the floating pill (there's no sidebar foot to host it there). Same one-click Send-feedback dialog either way.
- The quick theme toggle can now offer to set your account default too. Flipping a screen to dark (or light) with the header or ⌘K toggle still only changes that one device, the way it always has. But if you haven't set an account-wide default yet, a small prompt now offers to use it on all your devices. Take it and the choice syncs everywhere (and that device goes back to following your account default); ignore it and nothing changes. It stops asking once you've made an account-level choice, only ever appears a few times, and has a "Don't show again" if you'd rather it stay quiet.

### Fixes
- New workspaces provision more reliably. If a momentary database collision hit while your workspace was being created, signup could return a workspace that wasn't actually set up yet (its first page load failed). Provisioning now retries through that brief contention, so the workspace lands ready.

## 2026-07-05

### Features
- Knowledge Base entries can now hold an image: upload a screenshot (e.g. a scanner's config barcode, which must be *scanned from the image*, not regenerated) or any visual reference. It shows as a thumbnail in the list and full-size in Quick Access, so a pinned entry's barcode is scannable at a glance.
- New Knowledge Base module: keep rich Markdown entries (notes, references, SOPs, prompts, papers) in one place. Each entry has a Markdown body (with the Write/Preview editor), a category, an optional code, and a pin for quick access; search + filter by category. Enable it from Configuration.
- The 3D Printers page (and every Machines instance) now has saved views as chips: one click to switch perspective instead of re-checking a filter. Pick the shipped "Printer fleet by state" to group your printers by state, or hit Save view to name your own (which states to include + optional grouping) and pin it as a reusable tab. Your saved filter becomes a first-class chip, the same way Inventory and Projects already work.
- New QR field renderer: any text field (and a Knowledge Base entry's code) can render as a scannable QR of its value. Great for *owned* codes: a UPC, a location or asset tag, a URL. Pinned entries with a code now show a scannable QR in Quick Access.
- New Quick Access drawer: a floating button (shown when the Knowledge Base is enabled) opens a slide-over of your *pinned* entries, so a reference, code, or SOP you check often is one tap away from any page. Pin an entry in the Knowledge Base to add it here.
- Fields can now be Rich text: a Markdown editor with a Write/Preview split and a formatting toolbar (bold, italic, headings, lists, code, links). Ideal for long descriptions, notes, SOPs, and the knowledge base. Renders as formatted Markdown on detail pages and a clean one-line summary in tables.

### Improvements
- When Cobblr can't be reached (a deploy blip, a dropped connection), you now see one calm "Can't reach Cobblr, reconnecting…" bar at the top instead of scattered raw errors. It clears itself automatically the moment the service is back.
- Ask Cobb answers more out of the box. Even with no AI connected and nothing trained, Cobb now fields ~20 common questions: "what is Cobblr", find & search, add/edit/delete something, enable a module, custom fields, invite people, settings, export & backup, labels & QR, mobile, account & password, and feedback, on top of the originals. It's the untrained baseline every workspace gets; you can still tweak or add to it under Configuration → Assistant.
- DMing the Cobblr Discord bot a bug or idea now just gets a 👀: the "this is feedback & support" heads-up shows only once, on your first DM, instead of on every report.
- Picking a location no longer means scrolling one endless indented dropdown. The scan inbox's location pickers (New-scans target, bulk "Set location", and the confirm form) now use a drill-down: rooms open into their contents with a breadcrumb (Kitchen ▸ Rack 1 ▸ Shelf 2), plus a search box to jump straight to any bin, and "New location…" inline. Tap a name to pick it, tap ▸ to open it.
- Scan routing suggestions are less noisy and more consistent. The matchmaker now asks the model for one primary table plus at most one secondary (only when an item actually belongs in two), and never to list the same table twice, instead of padding "up to 3" with a duplicate or a marginal table. So a batch of the same kind of thing (a shelf of books) routes the same way, instead of some getting a stray "Collections" and others a duplicate chip.
- Self-hosters can now control exactly which third-party product catalogs barcode scanning contacts: a master switch (`COBBLR_SCAN_EXTERNAL_LOOKUPS`), independent per-provider toggles (upcitemdb, Open Facts, DuckDuckGo, go-upc), and API-key fields where a provider offers one. go-upc uses its official API when you supply a key; its web scraper is now off by default.
- Photographed books now get their ISBN backfilled from Open Library. An ISBN is almost never printed where a camera can read it, so when a book's match has no ISBN, Cobblr looks it up by title + author: preferring the edition whose publisher matches the book in hand (e.g. the Scholastic paperback, not a random hardcover). It only fills a blank ISBN (never overrides one the identify already found) and leaves it empty when there's no confident match rather than inventing one.
- Every "take a photo of this" spot in the scanner now uses the same in-app capture drawer (the scan result card's *Nice photo* and *Not it) photograph it*, plus the inbox item's *Retake for catalog* and *Add photo*. None of them launches the phone's native camera anymore: on the live scanner they reuse the running viewfinder, and elsewhere the drawer opens its own camera (with a photo-picker fallback when there's no camera). One consistent shutter → Retake / Use this flow everywhere.
- Scan cards now show what a match will fill, right on the collapsed card: the actual field values as mini chips (e.g. `genre Fiction · author Laura Ingalls Wilder · publisher Scholastic`) instead of a meaningless "fills 4". No need to open the item to see what's going into the table.
- Taking a "Nice photo" in the scanner no longer launches the phone's native camera on top of the one already running. It now opens an in-app capture drawer off the live viewfinder: frame it, hit the shutter, then Retake or Use this: and the catalog photo swaps in place with a clear "updated" confirmation instead of a fire-and-forget toast. (Desktop, with no live camera, still uses the file picker.)
- Scanning a whole series now routes uniformly. On top of the tightened matchmaker prompt, a deterministic pass aligns the *secondary* table suggestions across same-series items in a session: a shelf of Little House books either all offer a given secondary table (e.g. "Collections") or none do. No more the AI offering it on some books and not others. It only ever removes a stray secondary, never fabricates one, and never touches the primary table.

## 2026-07-04

### Features
- Public download for the headless edge-bridge binaries: GET /api/v1/desktop/bridge/:os/:arch streams the prebuilt standalone binary for that platform, so the headless install script works with no token on or off the tailnet (it server-side-fetches the private release asset). Needs the operator to set EDGE_BRIDGE_REPO_API + EDGE_BRIDGE_REPO_TOKEN.
- Meet Cobb: the cobblestone-headed craftsman is now the face of the assistant (the Ask Cobb chat and the Build page). And Ask Cobb no longer dead-ends without AI: if your workspace has no AI provider connected, it tells you up front: a "basic mode" strip before you type, and still answers the essentials with no AI at all ("what can you do", "how do I add a part", "where do I scan", "build my workspace"), pointing you to Connect AI for anything about your actual data.
- Teach Ask Cobb what to say without AI. A new Configuration → Assistant page lets you edit the answers Cobb gives when your workspace has no AI connected: reword a built-in reply, turn one off, or add your own keyword-triggered answers (give it trigger words like "returns, refund, send it back" and the reply). A live "try it" box shows exactly what a question matches. It's per-workspace and matches by keyword, no AI involved, so basic mode goes from a fixed floor to something you can actually train.
- New Mobility capability: give a tool, bin, or asset a home, mark it mobile, and Cobblr flags it away when its current location drifts from home, with a one-tap Return home that snaps it back. Fixtures (the default) are untouched. The away age is preserved across unrelated edits and shows as "3d ago", not a timestamp. Works on inventory items *and* assets. Along the way the field system grew two generic pieces every module now gets: relation fields render as a real picker (choose the record by name, never paste an id), and server-managed fields render read-only: no more inputs that silently revert.
- The New-job form now reads the filament material and grams straight from the gcode/3MF you pick, instead of asking you to retype what the slicer already computed. Pick a printable file and Cobblr parses its slicer metadata (PrusaSlicer / SuperSlicer / OrcaSlicer / Bambu comment headers, plus 3MF `slice_info`), pre-fills the grams, and matches a spool by material (PLA → your "PolyTerra PLA" spool): all still editable. On completion the grams deduct from that spool as before. Multi-extruder prints sum correctly, and the material type is recorded on the job even when no spool is matched.
- Reply directly to a Cobblr feedback email and your reply now threads back onto the report: no need to open the app. Inbound email (feedback replies and forwarded receipts) now runs through one shared address handler.
- Cobblr now predicts when you'll run out and reorders *ahead* of empty: no counting, no waiting for zero. It learns each item's burn rate from the usage you already log (every "use one", every stock change), and when something's a few days from running out it lands on your shopping list early. Parts show a live "≈ N days left" estimate. Items with their own signal (a replace-clock, or stock tracked by another system) opt out automatically.
- The honest answer to "how does Cobblr know I used something": no chore, no counting. Two zero-friction ways to log consumption arrived:

### Improvements
- Your activity feed no longer stops at "100 actions". It shows the real total ("50 of 412") and loads older activity as you scroll. Cursor pagination is now the platform default for growing lists.
- Emailing things into Cobblr is now easy to find: Configuration → Integrations → Email in shows your forward-a-receipt address and explains reply-by-email, and searching "email" or "receipt" jumps you straight there.
- The New location form's *Kind* is now two labelled buttons: area (a region: room, corner, workshop) and container (things go INTO it: bin, drawer, shelf), instead of a dropdown, so each option's meaning is visible at a glance. It also guesses the Kind from the name as you type: "White Bookshelf", "Bin 17" or "Kitchen Drawer" pre-select container; "Storage Room" or "Workshop Corner" pre-select area. A small hint shows what it guessed, and tapping a button yourself always wins, the guess only sets the default so you stop choosing it by hand on every location.
- The ⌘K command palette (and the header search bar) now find every settings and system page, no longer only a hand-picked few. It reads the same registry the Configuration hub uses, so anything you can reach in Configuration you can now reach by typing it: "permissions", "backup", "healthcheck", "openapi", "form builder", plus synonyms the label doesn't say ("roles" → Permissions, "audit" → Activity log). And searching "dark mode" (or "theme" / "night" / "light") now surfaces the theme toggle right in the palette: one keystroke flips your per-account light/dark preference, instead of hunting for the sidebar icon (which a branded workspace hides).
- The scan inbox now tells you clearly when the AI is actually done. Enrichment lands in a few passes: the tidied final name, cover, genre note and where-to-file keep arriving for up to a minute after the first result, so a session header now shows a live "N finishing…" pill that flips to "✓ All set" the moment every item is settled, and any card still being refined wears a quiet finishing… tag (distinct from the bold AI reading… of the first pass). No more wondering whether a name is final or about to change under you.
- Three scan-inbox clarity fixes. Each identified item now shows its series as a chip on the card (e.g. *Little House on the Prairie* on a stack of those books): the most useful at-a-glance media label, not buried in the "tag them all" banner. Two suggestion chips that read as the same table are collapsed to one: a workspace with two tables both named "Bookshelf" no longer shows "Bookshelf · 3 fields" next to "Bookshelf · 1 field" with no way to tell them apart (the richer match wins; either is still pickable in the confirm form). And the item subtitle no longer starts with a stray "· " when there's no barcode/ISBN, the author/publisher line is now built from the fields that exist, so nothing reads as missing.
- The per-device theme override (Profile → Appearance → This device only) gains a Match OS option: make just this device follow its own operating-system light/dark setting: live, so it flips with your system day/night schedule, even when your account default is a fixed Light or Dark. Previously a device could only use the account default or hard-lock to Light/Dark; now "follow this screen's OS" is a first-class choice, independent of and without changing your global preference. The Appearance card was also tightened to about half its previous height.
- Theme is now two separate things: a global account default that syncs to all your devices, and a per-device lock that overrides it on just one browser without ever syncing. Profile → Appearance shows both: Account default (Match device / Light / Dark) and This device only (Use default / Light / Dark), so you can, say, keep a light default everywhere but force one shared display dark. Precedence is *this device → account default → the device's OS*, and the quick theme toggle in the header now sets this device only (toggling back toward your account default releases the lock so the device syncs again). The device override persists locally across reloads; it's never written to your account.

### Fixes
- Appearance is now an explicit setting in Profile → Appearance: Match device · Light · Dark. Before, the only way to change theme was a session toggle (an icon that's hidden on branded workspaces), and it only saved a preference if you happened to hit it, so if you never did, each device just followed its own OS and two devices could disagree ("dark on my Mac, light on my phone"). Now picking Dark (or Light) makes that choice explicit and it follows your account to every device, overriding each device's OS; Match device is a deliberate, saved choice that follows each device's OS live. Persists per-account, unchanged for other members, and a workspace with its own branded theme still shows that theme.
- An empty multi-instance module (Projects, Inventory) no longer shows a permanent "0 / none yet" card on the dashboard. Its per-instance tile now collapses into the quiet "Also enabled" line exactly like every other empty module, and a stale duplicate (the module appearing in "Also enabled" *and* as a card) is gone: dashboard tiles now clean up their empty-state report when they're swapped out (which happens the moment a module's instances finish loading).
- The scan matchmaker no longer offers to install a bundle whose table name collides with one you already have. Previously, if your workspace had a "Bookshelf", the menu could still surface a community "Bookshelf" bundle right beside it: two chips you couldn't tell apart, and confirming the wrong one would create a duplicate same-named table. Now a not-yet-installed bundle entry is dropped when its display name matches a live table's, so you file into the table you actually have.
- Three scan-inbox papercuts. Re-run AI now respects an image you picked: a plain re-run keeps your chosen cover and only re-images when you explicitly flag the item as *wrong* (the identify used to wipe the lock and clobber your pick). The "part of the series" banner counts every book: it now folds in a same-author title whose series the AI happened not to label (so 9 Little House books read as 9, not 8), and its wording reads *"N items are part of the "…" series."* And a card no longer shows the same table twice (a duplicate "Bookshelf" chip): candidates routing to one table are collapsed to the richest single suggestion.

## 2026-07-03

### Features
- AI failure detection (the spaghetti watch): turn it on in Setup and Cobblr watches each printing machine's camera, builds a rolling failure score, and auto-pauses + alerts you when a print is clearly failing. It uses a local model on your bridge when available: the frame never leaves your network and costs no AI tokens, and falls back to your workspace's vision AI. Tune the sensitivity, the detector, and whether it auto-pauses or just alerts.
- Pin the workspace that opens on a fresh device. In the workspace switcher, a star on each row sets your default: the one a new device (or a bare URL with no history) opens into. Before, a fresh sign-in just landed on your first *owned* workspace, which isn't always the one you want; now you decide. A device you've already used still resumes wherever you last were; the default is the no-history landing. One default at a time; click the star again to clear it.
- Auto-update feed for the Cobblr Edge Helper desktop app: GET /api/v1/desktop/updates/:target/:arch/:version answers the Tauri updater (204 when current, else the signed download), and super-admins publish a release via POST /api/v1/desktop/updates/publish. Lets the (private) helper auto-update through the hosted instance without public GitHub releases.
- Elegoo printers, no middleman. A new built-in `elegoo_sdcp` driver speaks the Centauri Carbon's own SDCP protocol directly on your LAN: discovery, status, upload, print start/pause/stop, with no manager software in between. Point a connection at the printer's IP and it joins the fleet, pools, and production runs like everything else. *Hardware-unverified first release, written to the SDCP spec and field-observed firmware behavior; tell us what your printer does.*
- Production runs: tell the farm *"make 250 of these, stop when done."* Pick a pool, a plate file, and a target quantity (with parts-per-plate); Cobblr queues exactly the plates needed, drips them onto free printers, and replaces failures automatically. A plate only counts when you clear the bed with a good verdict: scrapped plates are reprinted without you thinking about it. The run closes itself at target and can be paused, resumed, or cancelled mid-flight from the Digital Fabrication floor.
- The print-history panel is now a proper analytics view: a prints-per-day trend (completed vs failed, stacked, over 7/30/90 days. Hover a day for its counts and filament) sits above the success-rate / filament / hours tiles, and the By-printer rollup now shows each machine's success rate % so a problem printer stands out at a glance.
- Completely sidebar. Sidebar mode grows up: hide the top bar entirely (the panel icon in the sidebar's top-right corner) and the sidebar becomes the whole chrome: brand + workspace at its head, your nav in the middle, and a labeled action list at its foot (Build · Scan · Search · Notifications · Ask Cobb · Profile · Configuration) with your account row and its compact menu flying out beside the sidebar. Nothing in the sidebar opens a floating dropdown: the workspace switcher accordions in place, and you can drag nav rows to reorder them right there (same per-device order the top bar uses, the two always agree). Theme, pin/auto-hide, and top-bar toggles are small icons at the sidebar's top right; the verify-email nudge docks as a compact card instead of a bar over your pages; Calendar rides with the quieter surfaces in the nav. Everything is reversible from the same corner it came from.
- Your nav, your way: top bar or left sidebar. A busy workspace runs the top navbar out of room fast, so, Vivaldi-style, the tiny panel icon left of the cobblr wordmark now flips navigation into a skinny left sidebar (and back). In sidebar mode every dropdown child, specialisations, instances, grouped categories, is simply *there*, indented in the hierarchy, no hover menus and no "more ▾" fold. The sidebar can be pinned (a fixed column) or auto-hiding (a thin left-edge strip that slides the panel over the page when you reach for it), flip that with the pin at the sidebar's foot. Per-device preference; mobile keeps its menu either way.
- A design can now lift its finished-object photo straight out of its pattern PDF: one tap on "pull photo from PDF" in the pattern & photos panel. Cobblr reads the images embedded in the PDF (crochet patterns, manuals, spec sheets all carry them), picks the real photo over the diagrams, and attaches it: no separate upload needed.
- Emailed invoices now unpack themselves: attach a PDF/CSV invoice (or a photo) to an email sent to your workspace inbound address and each line item arrives as its own confirm-ready row in the scan inbox: grouped so one tap turns the lot into a single purchase order. Multi-item email bodies ("1x Pico W, 2x 10k resistor") split the same way, and an email that can't be parsed always lands as a note, never lost.
- The scan inbox can now import a whole batch from a file: companion app inbox exports (JSON or CSV) work as-is: 497 real items in one go, photos fetched, duplicates skipped on re-import, and any other system's CSV works with a quick column mapping in the preview. Imported items arrive as ordinary pending scans with the source's suggestions riding along as hints, so Cobblr's own matching still decides where things go.
- Scan a stack of books (or movies, albums) from the same series and the inbox now recognises it: the photo identify names the series a titled work belongs to (Harry Potter, Little House on the Prairie), and when two or more pending items share a series, a one-tap banner offers to tag them all with it: the tag rides to each item's entity when you confirm, so the whole set is grouped under its series regardless of where each lands.
- After a scan session, the inbox now spots a shared theme and offers to act on it in one tap: tag every item with a derived label (scan a pile of aviation gear → "tag them all 'Aviation'") and suggest a category for the non-book items ("Aviation Accessories"), all derived on the fly. Nothing pre-baked. The tag rides along and attaches to each item's entity when you confirm it, so books land on the Bookshelf and accessories in inventory, but all carry the same tag.
- Dark mode follows *you*, everywhere. Your light/dark choice is now saved to your account, so it carries across all your workspaces and every device you sign in on: flip it once and it sticks. It's purely how *you* view Cobblr: it never changes another member's view, and a workspace that wears its own branded theme still shows that theme to everyone (your preference just governs the normal, unskinned workspaces). Toggle it from the same places as before (the sidebar's theme icon, or Dark mode in your account menu). A device you haven't set still follows its own OS setting until you choose.

### Improvements
- The Actions page stops burying its lede. Tunable actions come first: and there are more of them: "Run a device command" is now trait-scoped to physical things (it no longer offers itself on subscriptions and tasks), joining Print label as per-workspace adjustable. Everything below sits under an honest "fixed scope" heading where each row says *where* its scope comes from: a module's precise kind list, or a deliberately-universal wire-driven shape, instead of a shrug.
- The "Build it for me" plan now shows everything it's about to do. When the AI creates a new tracked section (like a "Bookshelf") or groups your nav under a parent (like "Machines"), those now appear in the "Here's what it'll do" preview: the new section with its fields, and the nav grouping, instead of only "Will enable: …". No more applying a change you couldn't fully see.
- The dashboard tells the truth and wastes nothing. Overdue tasks now lead the "needs you" feed (they were invisible: the feed only read tracker date fields, never the projects module's own due dates). Activity bursts stop lying: "created K1 Max ×2" for two *different* machines now reads "created 2 machines: K1 Max, Voron 2.4, Beta". Enabled-but-empty modules collapse from full-size zero tiles into one quiet "Also enabled: …" line. The scan entry shrinks to a single row, and the "add more" bar moves below your data, prime rows belong to what's actually happening in your workspace.
- Act where you see it. The "needs you" rows now carry their actions: overdue tasks show inline checkboxes right on the row (≤3 items: check them off without opening anything), a printer holding a finished plate shows Good / Scrapped verdict buttons in place, and rows with more items expand inline to a per-item list. Printers waiting for a bed-clear verdict and failed prints now appear in the feed at all. At-a-glance tiles slimmed to stat-chips (same info, half the height), an empty *your views* section now invites you to pin a saved view (dismissible), toast bursts cap at 4 with a "+N earlier" pill, and the account menu gains Pair phone. The standing scan card is gone from the dashboard, scanning lives in the sidebar, the header, your paired phone, and any barcode reader (which works from every screen).
- "Camera unreachable" now tells you *why*. When a camera tile can't get a frame, Cobblr cross-checks the live bridge status and the printer's own reported state before pointing a finger: bridge offline ("nothing on-site can be reached"), printer unreachable ("the bridge is connected, so it's probably the printer: powered off, maybe on purpose, or unplugged"), or camera unreachable ("the printer is responding and the bridge is fine, check its camera / LAN-access settings"). No more blanket "(bridge?)" when the bridge is provably fine.
- The library reads your slicer's mind. Uploading a plate file now parses the slicer's own comment headers (estimated time, material, layer height, filament grams) plus the `4x Name_…_PLA_…_1h30m` filename convention (which is what carries parts-per-plate). Library cards show material / time / parts-per-plate chips, and picking a file in a production run pre-fills parts-per-plate and shows the per-plate estimate. No more counting parts off the plate preview.
- The printer cockpit's file list now recognises what's on the card: an SD file that matches a print in your cloud history shows that print's proper model name and picture (the cryptic cache/ filename drops to the subtitle), recognised entries sort above the anonymous ones, and files the history can't name get their preview read straight out of the gcode/3MF the slicer wrote. No more wall of blank squares.
- Printer file rows now say where their knowledge comes from: an SD chip means the file is on the card and printable right now, a Cloud chip means the cloud print history knows it (name, picture, outcome): a matched entry wears both.
- The fleet floor now loads instantly: the server remembers each connection's last-known machines and shows them while it re-checks live (surviving restarts), so no more "checking your machines…" wait. The Cameras wall now shows a hybrid Bambu's own chamber camera automatically (no manual URL or relay needed), and Arrange, drag machines onto the floor grid, positions remembered, works from the Cameras view too.
- Arranging the fleet is now just dragging: no Arrange mode, no cell grid: grab any machine tile and the others shift to show where it lands, drop on the strip between rows to start a new row in place, and the layout is remembered for the whole workspace. Machines you haven't placed flow in their own trailing row, so a new printer never scrambles your floor.
- Sidebar mode never stacks two sidebars. With navigation in the left sidebar, opening Configuration used to add a *second* sidebar beside the first. Now the main sidebar simply becomes the configuration panel: hub link, settings search, and every grouped destination fold into it, with a quiet "← workspace" row to swap back, and the settings page gets the full width. Leave the configuration area (or flip back to the top bar) and everything returns to normal.
- Fields can now reference another record. A new `relation` field type stores a link to another entity (e.g. a location) and renders it as that record's name: the "link to another record" building block. Lists, tables, search, and the generic entity API all show the referenced name instead of an id. First used by the upcoming home-vs-current location tracking; a general-purpose picker in the field builder follows.
- Scan inbox cards now show the author (or director/artist) under a titled work's name instead of the publisher: "Little House in the Big Woods · Laura Ingalls Wilder" reads better than "· Scholastic". Normal products still show their brand.
- Bigger scan photos, and nothing cut off on mobile. Inbox cards now show the item photo at the full height of the card: a book cover or product shot reads at a glance instead of squinting at a thumbnail, with a consistent width so every card's text still lines up. And the expanded item detail no longer runs off the right edge of a phone screen: the photo/source/hint columns now wrap to fit any width.
- Better catalog-photo search in the scan inbox: the image search now sharpens a weak title with the author and a media word (so "Farmer Boy" searches "Farmer Boy Laura Ingalls Wilder book" and finds the real cover, not generic farm pictures), there's now an editable search box to type your own term, and a photographed item auto-suggests a clean catalog photo on identify (it used to only do that on a barcode mismatch).
- Series detection now works for barcode-scanned media too, not just photos: when a scanned book/movie/album's barcode resolves to a titled work in a known series (Harry Potter, Little House on the Prairie), the series is captured, so scanning a boxed set by barcode groups it under its series just like photographing the covers does.
- Scan inbox card subtitle now shows the author *and* the publisher for a titled work: "Little House in the Big Woods · Laura Ingalls Wilder · Scholastic", rather than only one of them.
- Settings links all behave the same way now. Modules, Members and + New thing open as pages inside the settings sidebar: same dialogs, same logic, no more overlay-vs-page lottery (old `?open=` links redirect). And Digital Fabrication in settings shows Setup only: connections, pools, print-update rules, with a one-click "Open the shop floor →" out to the operate surface where it belongs.
- Toasts land in the sidebar. In full-sidebar mode, message bubbles now appear in the sidebar's quiet middle: stacking upward from just above the action cluster, instead of the far bottom-right corner. Where you're already looking, never over your content. Every other layout keeps the classic bottom-right stack. (Dev builds: clicking the env chip fires a sample toast of each kind, one per click, the old companion app tradition.)
- Automations can now fire only when a field actually changes, not on every edit. A wire condition can compare two live values: e.g. `{{event.after.location_id}}` vs `{{event.before.location_id}}`, so "when status changes to Done, notify" runs on the transition, not every time the row is saved while it merely *stays* Done. Entity updates now carry a before/after snapshot for this. Groundwork also lands for server-managed fields (values the server owns and stamps, which a client write can never overwrite), the foundation for upcoming home-vs-current location tracking.

### Fixes
- The scanner "waiting to file" tiles on the dashboard no longer crop the image: book covers (and other tall photos) showed with their tops and bottoms cut off; they now fit the whole picture inside the tile.
- Three settings paper-cuts, cut. Files shows actual thumbnails now (images were fetched without credentials and every one rendered broken; opening a file is fixed the same way). A bundled custom field tells you which bundle owns it and links straight to managing that bundle, instead of the dead-end "uninstall the parent bundle to remove". And the settings search moved into the sidebar: filter every destination from any settings page, Enter jumps to the first hit; no more detour through the Configuration hub.
- Dragging a machine tile no longer bounces: the tile you grab hides in place and a dashed slot shows exactly where it will land, the other tiles shift once and stay put, and the "new row" strips between rows now actually take the drop (the hovered strip swells into a full slot and holds steady under the cursor).
- The fleet floor now appears the instant the page loads (it paints from the last-seen snapshot while the live check runs), and a machine's card reads the same in every filter: narrowing to "Needs you" no longer swapped a printer's name with its connection label.
- Print-update rule editor: the chamber-light step now reads "Light on" / "Light off" so a narrow phone dropdown can't hide which one you picked, and the params hint now says what actually happens: a line is shown only if at least one of its {{fields}} has a value (an all-empty line is hidden), instead of the misleading "a line with a missing param is dropped".
- Scan faster: the camera stops flickering and the confirmation gets out of your way. After each photo, the "saved: identifying it" note now appears briefly at the *top* of the viewfinder instead of a toast at the bottom that covered the shutter and UPC field, so you can fire off a series of shots back-to-back. And the camera preview no longer blinks/resets when that note comes and goes (a toast was quietly restarting the video stream). The on-screen hint also reminds you that you can snap a photo, not just scan a barcode.
- The scan inbox shows every session, not just your last one. Finishing a scan now opens the full inbox with each scanning session as its own collapsible section (newest on top), scrolled to the one you just finished, instead of scoping the whole inbox to that single session and hiding everything scanned earlier behind a filter chip. Scan six things across three trips to the shelf and all three sessions are right there, grouped, the way you'd expect.
- Three scan-inbox triage fixes: an item the AI routed to a generic home inventory no longer lands on the wrong specialised form (a radio bag was defaulting to the Bookshelf's book fields); a research hint on a photo ("it's actually an RA200 headset") now re-identifies the item: the words override the picture, and "This is wrong" drops a stale hand-picked photo so the re-read isn't fighting it; and books from a series are no longer flagged as duplicates of each other (the combine offer now skips titled works and, when it does fire, names every item it's asking about).
- The settings sidebar no longer abandons you. Views, Tags, Custom fields, Files, Wires, Actions, Activity and Bundles: the settings-family pages that live at their own URLs, now render inside the same sidebar as every /configuration page, with your spot highlighted. Same links, same pages; the map just stops disappearing.

## 2026-07-02

### Features
- Local AI endpoints can now ride your edge bridge: the Ollama and OpenAI-compatible (LM Studio, vLLM, …) providers have a "How Cobblr reaches it" choice: direct, or via my edge bridge when the endpoint runs on your own machine/LAN. The form shows live bridge status while you pick, so "will my local URL work?" answers itself.
- The dashboard now opens with a "needs you" feed: things that are low on stock, dates that are overdue or coming up in the next 30 days (renewals, refills, returns, maintenance: read from your trackers' own date fields, whatever you named them), and captures waiting to be filed. Each line is one tap to act; when nothing needs you, it shows nothing.
- The App Builder learned "now change it". After a build, a new Refine box under Apply takes a plain-English change (*"also add a price column"*, *"drop the label wire"*) and revises the current result instead of making you re-describe everything or hand-edit JSON. Everything you didn't mention is preserved verbatim, the same validation gate applies, and every refine is kept as a linked draft so you can trace how the design evolved. Works with hosted AI or the copy-paste prompt flow.
- The AI builder got real about two things it used to wrongly refuse: "track Books, call it Bookshelf" now creates a proper Bookshelf: its own nav entry and page (a named instance), not book fields bolted onto Assets, and "put these under a Machines parent" now works, because bundles can declare nav headings (a parent nav entry with module/instance members, including ones that already exist). The Build page also spots when a ready-made featured bundle already covers your ask and offers to install that instead of generating a worse one.
- Bundles got an undo. Updating or uninstalling no longer discards the old version: the bundle's detail modal now shows a version history with one-tap Restore (validated before anything changes, and the restore itself is undoable). Updates also show exactly what changes: which fields, wires and views are added, changed or removed, instead of asking you to trust a version number.
- Press ⌘K (or Ctrl-K) anywhere to do or find anything: scan, add a thing, open the inbox, describe a setup, customize the workspace, or type two letters and jump straight to any item you own. The bundles page is now titled "Setups & trackers", matching how the homepage talks about them.
- Stock that reorders itself. When a part drops below its minimum, it now lands on a draft purchase order for its usual vendor at its usual quantity: both learned from the part's own purchase history, zero setup. You review, approve, and the arrival restocks (re-arming the loop). No duplicate drafts while a reorder is in flight; several low parts for one vendor collect on a single draft. Also available as a "Draft a purchase order" button on any part, and it's a plain wire on /bindings: edit or remove it like any other.
- Installing an edge bridge no longer needs any Docker registry: the generated command runs a stock public `node:22-alpine` image that fetches the bridge code straight from your own Cobblr (sha-verified) and keeps itself updated automatically: anyone can finish the install, on any network.
- Edge bridges get a home. Configuration → Edge bridges shows every on-site bridge in one place: which relay agents are connected right now (last poll, in-flight work), every machine manager running through a bridge with its health and a Test-now button, and how to set a new one up: direct-LAN or cloud relay. And search now finds Cobblr itself: type "edge bridge" (or scan, build, calendar, backup, tokens…) into ⌘K or the header search and jump straight to the feature, not just your data.
- Your app, one button, no AI. On Build → "A page for members", *Generate from my workspace* instantly assembles a member-facing app from the trackers you already have: a capture page (scan + add) plus a page per tracker with its saved views and an add form. Regenerate any time your trackers change; then describe tweaks on the same tab if you want the AI to take it further.
- On phones, a bottom action bar puts the three things you actually do on mobile under your thumb: Scan (straight to the camera), Add (a two-tap "write it down, we'll find it a home" sheet that works from any page), and Search. Desktop is unchanged.
- Bring your own AI from anywhere that speaks the OpenAI v1 API: a new OpenAI-compatible provider takes a base URL (and an optional key): point it at LM Studio, vLLM, llama.cpp, LocalAI, or a gateway like OpenRouter/Groq, and every AI feature (matching, vision, summaries, embeddings) runs through it. LM Studio users: paste `http://<your-ip>:1234/v1`, no key needed.
- You can now send someone a premade workspace: when minting a friend invite, tick "send them a premade workspace" and their brand-new workspace arrives already set up with a copy of your chosen workspace's configuration (modules, trackers, fields, views: no data). The join page tells them it's coming pre-configured. And if a friend sends you an exported blueprint file, "New workspace" now takes it directly, pick the file, and the workspace is built from it.
- The thesis dashboard got a face. The operator console's new Product Metrics section renders walls-hit per workspace (what was denied, what failed validation, which wires broke: 7-day and 30-day) plus time to first working app, sorted by who's hitting the most friction. Same numbers as `GET /super-admin/product-metrics`, no curl required.
- Cobblr now measures its own thesis. Every time someone hits a wall: a permission they don't have, an AI-built or pasted bundle that fails validation, a wire that errors or cycles, it's counted per workspace, and GET /super-admin/product-metrics rolls it up: walls-hit this week/month plus time-to-first-working-app (signup → first real item) for every workspace. Telemetry is sparse, best-effort, and self-pruning after ~180 days; it never slows a request.
- Emails can now land in Cobblr directly: forward an order confirmation or shipping notice to a workspace inbound address and its contents arrive as a routed, confirm-ready item in the scan inbox: identified and location-suggested like any other capture, instead of being read out in a chat channel. Set it up by minting an "email" inbound token; wire a mailbox to it with a Cloudflare Email Worker (docs/operations/email-inbound-capture.md).
- Unbox → identify → put-away: once a scanned or captured item is identified, Cobblr now suggests where to put it, from where similar items already live ("Bin 4: 3 similar items are stored here"), with a one-tap "Put here" in the scan inbox. It's a hint drawn from your own placements, never applied without you accepting it, and it stays out of the way when you've already filed the item.
- Scan a bin's QR label and: when that bin holds exactly one SKU (a bin of M3 screws, a drawer of GT2 pulleys), go straight to adjusting its count: +1/+5/+10 and −1/−5/−10 chips, a "set exact count" recount, and undo. The count shown is that bin's row only. Works from the phone scanner, a desktop wedge gun, and the bin's own page. Derived automatically: a bin with a second SKU keeps the normal filing flow, and an "Add something else to this bin" chip hands off to filing when a bin is gaining one.
- Wires can be picky now. Every wire gets an optional "only when…": plain conditions like `event.newQty ≤ 5` or `material = PLA`, joined with AND, editable in the wire composer. The event still fires; the wire just declines politely unless all its conditions hold. Bundles can ship conditions too, and a typo'd condition is rejected at save instead of installing a wire that silently never runs.

### Improvements
- Two settings families stop pretending to be strangers. Access & permissions is now one area: overview & grants, custom roles, and accounts share a tab strip and a single Configuration entry (search "roles", "accounts" or "password reset" and land in the right place). Notifications gets the same treatment under your account: inbox · delivery · channels, one strip, one click between them. Every old URL still works.
- Permissions now opens with a "who can do what" overview: every member on one line: stock role, custom roles, direct grants (or "everything, implicitly" / "read-only"), with links to where each piece is managed. Access control still lives in its four places; now there's one place that reads them together.
- The operator console's 18 tabs now read as five labeled clusters (workspaces · community · platform · ai & scan · ops) instead of one long undifferentiated strip. Same deep links, same mobile scroll row; your eyes just get waypoints.
- Bambu printers on LAN now list the files already on their SD card in the cockpit's "Files" panel, so you can start (or re-run) any of them with one click: no re-upload. Previously the panel was always empty for Bambu because file operations went through the cloud driver, which can't touch the SD card; they now route over your LAN bridge like the camera and controls do.
- The Digital Fabrication page reads like a farm now: the Fleet section owns its space from first paint (a skeleton instead of popping in seconds later), machines sort working-first with a status color band on every card and a stop button next to pause on running prints, and the printer cockpit's files + recent prints merged into one "On this printer" surface: SD files show when they last printed, and prints whose file is gone get a history row with Print again.
- The fleet view now works like an operator's tool: the summary counts became clickable buckets (All / Working / Needs you / Idle / Off), tiles say why a machine is blocked and what it will print next, running tiles show time-remaining and a "~done" clock, a Compact mode fits several times more machines per screen, you can select machines and pause/resume/stop/clear-bed them in one batch, and a library file can be dragged straight onto a machine tile to print it there.
- The fleet can now mirror your actual shop: hit "Arrange" and drag machines onto floor-grid cells: the layout persists, unplaced machines sit on a shelf below, and the floor stays put for everyone who opens the page.
- The Digital Fabrication page is no longer one long stack: it now splits into a Floor tab (the fleet, print queue, file library, and history: what you touch daily) and a Setup tab (connections, pools, and print-update rules, the set-once plumbing). The floor is the page; the config is one click away.
- Print jobs got much harder to lose: a cancelled job stays cancelled (the next status poll can no longer resurrect it), a scrapped/failed/cancelled job now puts its build's materials back into inventory and removes the never-made output, real farms tolerate ~3 minutes of manager downtime before declaring a print failed (and auto-retries wait for you to clear the bed), failed or cancelled jobs can be retried in place, and deleting a connection cleanly cancels and unlinks everything that depended on it.
- Recent prints are now a first-class, full-width section at the bottom of the printer cockpit, and every print that ran as a Cobblr job has a one-click "Print again" that clones the original (same file, routing, material draw and build link) and sends it. Also fixed a quiet hole where a printer's history could show empty because it was matched by display name instead of device identity.
- The printer detail modal is now a proper "cockpit": a wide, two-column dashboard (camera + live temps + filament on the left, controls + files on the right) that fits on one screen with no scrolling, and the motion controls are a real directional jog pad: X/Y as a cross with Home in the middle, a dedicated Z column, and a 1/10/100mm step selector, instead of a flat row of buttons.
- The Digital Fabrication queue is easier to work: click any job to open a detail view with its full error, timeline and the build/material it draws; retry a failed or cancelled job in one click; filter the queue by Active / Failed / Done; edit a connection (rename, move host, rotate its key, pause it) instead of delete-and-recreate; and the page finally fits a phone screen. A fresh workspace now sees a clear "Enable Digital Fabrication" prompt instead of a dead page.
- Two new ways to see the farm: the print queue gained a Timeline view: one row per machine showing what's running (with its finish clock) and the jobs lined up behind it in the order they'll actually run, and the Fleet section gained a Cameras view, a webcam wall where every machine's feed is the card with status and progress as an overlay.
- The homepage funnel now speaks in things you'd actually say. Column 1 lists only kinds of things you track: Labels and Digital Fabrication moved to a small "Add a capability" strip (they act *on* your things; nobody "tracks labels"). Column 2 leads with pre-shaped trackers: one-kind setups like the new Fasteners tracker (thread, length, head, drive as real columns, grouped by thread), with the big multi-module setups tucked under their own "Full setups" heading instead of being the first thing a new user has to decode.
- Scanner group cards breathe better: the photo strip now spans the full card width, and the "Create … & file all N" action is a compact button up on the title row instead of a large block beside the images.
- No-AI matching now understands everyday household things: bedsheets, towels, bedding and furniture route to Home Inventory; detergent, batteries, toilet paper and their kin to Household Supplies; clothing to Wardrobe; pet food, vitamins and fertilizer to their trackers. Under the hood, trackers carry a small taxonomy vocabulary, a keyword that names what the item IS (its head noun) counts as a confident match on its own, and multi-word keywords only match as full phrases, so "paper towel" can't claim your bath towels and a ribbon cable still isn't yarn.
- The tenant-credentials encryption key can be rotated now. `api/scripts/rotate-tenant-creds-key.ts` re-encrypts every workspace's stored DB credentials to a new key: dry-run first, every row verified before anything is written, all-or-nothing, safe to re-run. "Never lose the key" is still good advice, but it's no longer the entire disaster plan.
- A disaster-path restore that doesn't need Cobblr running. If the api won't boot, `api/scripts/restore-backup-offline.ts` loads a backup's data straight into the tenant database with plain Postgres access: dry-run preflight, ids preserved, type-aware, all-or-nothing. The in-app restore remains the full-fidelity path; this is for the day that path isn't available.
- The Refine box now works for apps too, not just bundles. Built a member page and want one more block, a friendlier intro, or a tweak to the custom code? Describe the change under "Create app" and the builder revises the current app: pages and blocks you didn't mention round-trip untouched, same validation gate.
- Three operational safety nets for self-hosters. Downgrade detection: booting an older image against a newer database now logs a loud, named warning instead of silently half-working. Restore preflight: send a backup with `dry_run=true` and get a full report of what a restore would do: rows, tables, files, whether existing data would be replaced, with nothing changed. Audit-log retention: an optional `ACTIVITY_LOG_RETENTION_DAYS` bounds the activity log on public hosts (unset = keep everything, as before).
- The dashboard's scanner groups now show the items themselves: a single row of photo tiles per group (the photo is how you recognize a scan), with a "+N" tile into the Scan Inbox when there are more than twelve. Names alone weren't cutting it.
- The dashboard's scanner queue now consolidates by what things ARE: "20 look like Filament Stash → Create Filament Stash & file all 20": one card per match, biggest group first, with samples. Matches to trackers you already have get "Add all N to …"; anything unidentified collapses to one review link. Twenty scans, one button.
- Scanned items and typed items now live where each belongs: things you type into the homepage stay in its third column as part of the guided flow, while things arriving from your scanner or phone get their own "from your scanner" queue below the panel: a compact preview of the first 8 with one-tap filing, plus a link into the full Scan Inbox for batch review. The "needs you" feed no longer double-counts waiting captures, and a pile of scans no longer forces the guided panel open.
- The homepage columns now use all the height the page gives them: when the right column grows, the kinds and setups lists grow to match instead of cutting off early. And the "↓ more" scroll hint is accurate now: it only appears when something is actually hidden, and it disappears the moment you reach the bottom.
- Settings you can actually navigate. Every Configuration page now has a persistent grouped sidebar: your current page highlighted, every other setting one click away, instead of bouncing back through the hub each time. The hub itself regrouped into six plain-language sections (set up · your data · people & sharing · automation & AI · machines & devices · safety & system), search understands synonyms ("roles" finds Permissions), the Modules/Members dialogs are deep-linkable, and a signpost points to your *personal* settings so workspace-vs-you is no longer a guessing game.
- Custom app blocks get a live preview in the editor. Toggle it under the code box and your block renders in the exact sandbox members will see (real bridge, real data, scoped to your access) with a "refresh with current code" button while you iterate. No more save-and-pray into a red error box.
- Scanner-group photos now actually use the full card width: tiles grow to share the whole row (bigger pictures in big groups, sensibly capped in small ones) instead of huddling small on the left.
- Two reliability touches: scanner-group photo tiles now fall through their image sources (cached catalog file → catalog link → your own scan photo) instead of giving up at the first broken one, and an open tab now notices when a new version of Cobblr ships: a small "tap to refresh" pill appears instead of silently running stale code.

### Fixes
- "What's new" in the account menu actually goes somewhere now. Inside a workspace the link resolved to a route that didn't exist (the changelog page was only mounted on the signed-out router), so clicking it silently bounced you home. The workspace router now serves the changelog too: same feed, your workspace chrome kept.
- Wires can no longer loop forever. A wire whose action re-triggers its own event (say, "when stock changes, adjust stock") used to spin endlessly in the background; the engine now stops the chain after 8 hops and records a wire_depth_exceeded entry in the activity log so you can see exactly which wire cycled: normal multi-step wires are untouched.

## 2026-07-01

### Improvements
- The dashboard no longer opens with a bar restating your workspace name, URL slug, role, and a greeting: everything on it lived in the navbar already. Customize workspace moved into the workspace switcher menu (click your workspace name), bundle-update notices still appear right at the top when there's one to act on, and on workspaces shared with you a slim note says your role. Your actual content now starts one full card higher. Also fixed the Scan inbox toolbar overlapping itself at medium window widths.
- The homepage "just add it" box now actually finds homes for what you type, with or without AI. Typing "my passport", "Netflix subscription", "a monstera plant" or "spool of black PLA" suggests the right ready-made tracker (Important Documents, Subscriptions, Plant Care, Filament…), community setups like Lego and 3D Printer Parts are now suggestible too, and suggestions keep working after your first save (they used to silently stop once anything landed in Inventory). Saving an unmatched item now says where it went ("Saved to Inventory, it's in your navbar") instead of a vague "Filed it." On phones, the add-something box is now at the top of the panel instead of two screens down, and a few "try:" example chips demo the flow in one tap. Also: typing in the kinds column now ranks name matches first (typing "mach" + Enter picks Machines, not Assets), and the dashboard no longer fires a burst of failed background requests on every load.
- A friendlier first week: the homepage and the Build page now say up front when AI isn't connected (and what still works without it) instead of failing quietly, with a one-tap "Connect AI" path. "Describe what you have" now lands on the right Build tab, and the Build tabs speak plainly ("Design my workspace", "A page for members"). When AI is off, "Write a prompt instead" becomes the primary path. Creating a tracker from a capture now lands with a "here's what just happened" strip (scan more / add more) instead of a bare table, and captured names drop leading articles ("A spool of black PLA" files as "Black PLA"). The verify-email banner stays dismissed once dismissed (the reminder lives in your account menu), and young tables hide their all-empty columns behind "+N more columns" until you have real data.
- The scan inbox reaches full companion app parity. Scanning something you already track shows an "Already tracked" banner: bump its quantity, move it to your active bin, link the barcode, or open it, and the camera's new Move mode relocates tracked items hands-free. Photos are first-class: multi-photo gallery, rotate, "retake for catalog" (a nice shot as the display image), and "split into items" turns one photo of several things into separate items. Plus gallery view, session select-all + merge, a stale-items filter, "looks fine" review dismissal, bulk "Add all to…", pack-size and box-state chips, an undo on the scanner, and a "fill photos" backfill.
- The scan inbox now shows every scanning burst as its own session: including old history from before sessions existed (clustered by time gap), and scans typed, wedge-gunned, or photo-uploaded on desktop now join sessions exactly like camera scans.

### Fixes
- Golden-pass polish from re-auditing the companion app scanner source: "Already tracked" matches now show where the item lives and never offer a move it's already had; fuzzy matches exclude entities carrying a different barcode; linking a barcode also files the entity into your active bin; the camera keeps a full undo history for moves ("Undo last N"); the inbox gets a search box (name/barcode/notes/brand/area); gallery triage auto-advances to the next item; split children and URL items carry provenance chips; the stale nudge tightens to 2 days; and an "empty box" item no longer mislocates the entity: the box's location is recorded as a note instead.

## 2026-06-29

### Features
- Typing where something went now files it for you. A freeform capture like "Logic analyzer in cabinet 002" or "Dial calipers placed in Tool Chest 001" (typed in the scanner, or sent from Slack) now splits the item from the location, resolves the location against your workspace, and drops the item into that bin pre-filed. Zero-padded label-reel numbers match too ("bin 0087" → Bin 87). Ordinary names with an incidental "in" are left alone.
- Queue a fabrication job and it draws down the materials. A print or laser job can now be linked to a build (a bill-of-materials). When you send the job to the machine, Cobblr consumes that build's components from inventory and bumps the finished-product count, so "order in → subtract stock → queue the laser" happens in one step. Re-sending the same job never double-deducts.
- Link a fabrication job to a build, right in the New Job dialog. When you queue a print/laser job, you can now pick a Build it produces (and how many) from a dropdown: no API needed. On send, that build's components are drawn from inventory and the finished part is added, just like before. The field only appears once you've defined a build.

### Improvements
- On the new-workspace homepage, the things you add now show up right in the third column with a one-tap action, instead of dropping into a separate "scan inbox" with the next step stranded in a banner at the top. Each capture shows what it looks like and a button to act: Create [tracker] for a ready-made setup it matches, Add to [tracker] for one you already have, or Save it to file it as a general item. And even with no AI connected, the keyword matcher routes recognised things to a tracker and everything else still gets a home: no capture is left in limbo.

## 2026-06-28

### Features
- Scan with your phone from a computer. On a desktop (no camera), the Scan card now has a Pair phone button: it shows a QR; you scan it with your phone's camera, and your phone is signed in to the same workspace: its camera scans then flow straight into the inbox your computer is already showing. The pair code is single-use and expires in ~90 seconds; nothing to type, no separate login on the phone.
- The new-workspace homepage is now a guided "What do you want to do?" funnel that converges modules and ready-made bundles into one place. Pick a kind of thing to track, narrow to a ready-made setup, and the third column walks you the rest of the way, for kinds that hold several collections (Assets, Inventory, Machines, …) it helps you create a category ("Cars", "Printer Parts") and drop you in to add items; for a ready-made setup it installs it and takes you straight in. Examples now match what you picked (a Filament setup suggests "a spool of PLA", not "a pulley"), specialised machine setups (3D Printers, CNC, Lasers) show under both Machines and Digital Fabrication, and the freeform "just add it, we'll figure out the fields" path is always there, including Pair phone on a desktop, so you can scan straight into the workspace with your phone's camera.

## 2026-06-27

### Features
- Adding a category (+ New thing in workspace) now lets you put it straight into a navbar menu: so a home inventory's Pantry, Cleaning Supplies, Tools, Medications, … collapse into one tidy Inventory ▾ dropdown instead of sprawling across the top bar. It's automatic after the first: the first category pre-fills a menu named after the kind, and every category you add after auto-joins that same menu. Deleting a category cleanly removes it from its menu.
- A brand-new workspace now opens with "What do you want to do?": one distilling search over three converging lanes: ready-made setups (recipes), building blocks (capabilities), and build-it-yourself (describe/scan/AI). Type "parts" and all three narrow at once; pick a building block like Inventory and the recipes lane funnels to its ready-made versions (Home Inventory, Filament, …) with a "start blank" option. Modules and bundles finally converge on one on-ramp, so a new user can actually get started.

### Fixes
- The Spoolman import button (3D-printing filament weight sync) now only appears on a filament inventory, not on every list. It was just clutter on a food pantry, medications, or a generic parts inventory.
- A new workspace now lands at the bottom of your switcher. Creating a workspace used to drop it near the top; it now appends after your existing ones (still drag-reorderable).

## 2026-06-26

### Improvements
- The Labels "Add labels" browser now shows a tab for each collection you actually have: your own instances like *3D Printers*, *Laser Cutters*, *Parts*, instead of generic module names, and hides empty collections entirely. So you only see real, non-empty places to pull labels from, matching what's in your navbar.
- The Labels browser now shows your Locations tab as the real hierarchy: an Areas tree with rooms and their nested bins, and a separate Containers section for loose bins, exactly like the Locations page, instead of one flat list. Easier to find the right shelf or bin to label.
- Your custom location order now applies everywhere, not just on the Locations page. Drag locations into the order you want and that order is honoured in every location dropdown, the Labels browser, and any module that lists your places. The page also clearly separates Areas (rooms & regions) from Unsorted containers with their own headings.

### Fixes
- A barcode that resolves only to a generic category ("Whiskey", "Beverages" from Open Food Facts) now upgrades to the real product on re-run / "needs detail": e.g. Maker's Mark Bourbon Whisky. The web identify already found it; an over-strict same-word check ("Whiskey" vs Maker's "Whisky") was discarding the correct answer.
- Locations now sort the same way everywhere: "Bin 2" before "Bin 10", not lexically. The Locations page and the Labels browser now share ONE location tree viewer, so their structure and order can never drift apart again.
- A catalog image you hand-pick (the "other photo options" strip or "use my photo") is now sticky: a later re-run / hint correction no longer overwrites it with the provider's image. Reverting to the auto image releases the lock.

## 2026-06-25

### Features
- - Computed fields can now build clickable links: a new `{{ name | slug }}` template filter turns a value into a URL-safe slug, and computed fields can use the `url-link` renderer, so e.g. a "git repo" field with template https://github.com/me/{{ name | slug }} renders as a clickable link per entity.
- Labels page can now find things to label: a new Add labels panel under the queue, with a tab per kind that supports labels (Parts, Machines, Assets, …), each listing its items with thumbnails and a one-tap Add. Search within a tab to narrow it. The tabs aren't hardcoded: a kind shows up here for the same reason it gets a Print-label button, so enabling a new module with labelable items adds its tab automatically.
- Filter the machines/printers view by state. A "states" control lets you hide whatever machine states you don't want to see (e.g. shelved, sold): the states come from your data, and your choice persists per view.
- Prints started on the printer now show in the queue while running. A print you start directly on the machine (e.g. from Bambu Studio/Handy) (not sent from Cobblr) now appears in the Print Queue as a read-only "on printer" entry the moment it starts, instead of being invisible until it finishes.

### Improvements
- - Navbar: pin individual entries to the “more ▾” overflow so they always fold there even when the bar has room: per-entry toggle in the navbar Customize menu (the ••• icon next to each entry). Keeps a busy header down to the few sections you use most; the rest still auto-fold responsively as before.
- - JSON editors now have a Copy button (next to Format): grab the whole block in one click. - Sync sources: re-syncing a live entity type now shows the same both-sides preview as the first import before it writes, so a mapping/JSON edit is reviewed (create/update/merge/delete) before any data changes, not applied blindly.
- - Sync manifests can declare cross-section references: a field (e.g. a printer location_id) that points at another section by external id, resolved through that section id-map to the mirrored Cobblr entity. So importing 3D printers links each to the location you already imported. Shown resolved in the preview.
- - Import name-merge is now case- and punctuation-insensitive, so near-duplicates link instead of duplicating: e.g. "Prusa Mini" merges with "Prusa MINI+". Truly different names (CR-10 vs CR-10S) still stay distinct, and every merge is shown in the both-sides preview before it is written.
- - Sync sources can pull images across: a section declares "images": { "image_path": "$.image_url" } and the engine fetches each image through the edge bridge, stores it in your file library, and points the record at it. Works for LAN sources (e.g. companion app printer photos) the cloud can’t reach directly.
- - Synced images that don't come across on the first try (e.g. before an edge bridge has self-updated to binary support) now self-heal: the record is re-pulled on the next reconcile until the image lands, instead of sticking with no photo.
- - Sync sections can filter source rows by a field, so one endpoint feeds several sections: e.g. companion app /printers returns printers + lasers + CNC, and "filter": { "from": "$.category", "equals": "printer" } imports only the 3D printers. Each category into its own section/instance.
- - Sync sources: each section now has its own "edit" action: tweak just that section's JSON (mapping, references, images, target instance) in a pre-filled editor, no need to re-paste the whole source.
- - Live Sync settings: a source and its connection are now ONE card instead of two stacked blocks that repeated the section list. The source defines its sections once (with edit/remove); each connection nests underneath carrying only its own sync state (live toggle · synced count · Preview & sync · webhook). Add a second connection and it just joins the same card: the manifest is still edited once above.
- - A sync section can target a module instance: add "targetInstance": "<slug>" (e.g. "3d-printers") and imported rows land under that nav entry instead of the generic base. Adding/changing it re-targets already-imported rows on the next sync.

### Fixes
- Fixed a printer modal flicker. A printer photo set via the web-image search could make the detail modal flash, from a doubled image URL (404 → retry). The file-URL helper is now idempotent so an already-resolved image path is never wrapped twice.
- On mobile, an inbox item's name no longer gets crushed to a single letter while the "AI reading…" badge hogs the row: the status badge now wraps below the name so the name keeps the full width.
- The phone scan card now has a "Not it: photograph it" action: when a barcode resolves to the wrong thing, or to a junk, non-product code nothing can fix, snap a photo of the item and AI identifies it from the package, overriding the bad barcode result. The corrected name appears on the card a few seconds later.
- - Sync sources: one section can now fan a single endpoint out to several instances with "instanceBy": e.g. companion app /printers routes to 3d-printers + laser-cutters + cnc by category, instead of one near-duplicate section per category. - The section editor sanity-checks pasted JSON: structural problems block the save; an instance slug that does not match a real workspace instance warns with a "did you mean" suggestion (catches the targetInstance typo that silently shipped lasers nowhere).

## 2026-06-24

### Features
- - Declarative sync-source engine: external systems are mirrored into Cobblr via installable manifests (data), not source-specific code. Phase 1: the manifest schema + interpreter, proven to reproduce the companion app mapping exactly.
- - Declarative sync sources, Phase 2: install table + API (POST/GET/DELETE a SyncSourceManifest per workspace), org-aware connector resolution (built-in ∪ installed, mirroring digifab resolveDriver), and removal of the hardcoded companion app connector. A sync source is now installable data; Cobblr ships no source-specific code.
- Structured-JSON inputs now go through one shared, validated control instead of a dozen ad-hoc textareas. Wherever you paste a JSON blob: connector credentials, a vendor-resolver manifest, and (incrementally) view configs, app schemas, channel configs; you get live parse errors, "Format" pretty-printing, and inline JSON-Schema validation that points at exactly which field is wrong. Sync connections gained a "paste JSON →" toggle for credentials; the scan vendor-resolver editor now validates as you type.
- - Machines can now be a sync target: the module registers an entity writer (like locations), so a sync source can mirror external machines (e.g. companion app 3D printers) into machines:machine with name-merge, both-sides preview, and live updates.
- Sync connectors can now reach a local source over an edge bridge. When you add a connection you choose how Cobblr reaches it: Direct (the cloud fetches the URL: public URLs only on the hosted service) or Via edge bridge (a small agent on your own network fetches it and relays the result up). That's how you connect a LAN system like companion app to hosted Cobblr, the cloud never touches your private address; the bridge dials out and does the fetch locally.

### Improvements
