- The "same barcode: same item?" merge offer now fires on an exact match too (not just off-by-one), and when the two listings read differently it asks which one to keep, showing both names side by side. Either choice merges, sums the quantity, keeps the scanned barcode, and never loses the photo you took.
- - Ask Cobb panel: opening it now shifts the centered page content left into its margin (on wide screens) so the panel and content coexist, instead of the panel overlaying the content.
- Outbound requests from connectors now route through one per-tenant egress policy. On the hosted service, a sync connector can reach public hosts and your own registered edge connector, but not arbitrary private/internal addresses (a tenant can no longer point a connection at a LAN or internal IP). Self-hosted instances are unchanged (your own LAN is allowed). To connect a *local* system (like companion app) to hosted Cobblr, use an edge connector rather than a direct LAN address.
- When you photograph an item with no scanned barcode, the AI now reads the UPC/EAN off the package (when legible) and captures it into the item's barcode field, marked "(read from photo)" so it's clearly an OCR read rather than a hardware scan. This is the start of the "photograph it to recover the barcode" flow.
- When the barcode read off a photo is a near-match (within a digit or two) to a barcode you scanned earlier, the inbox now offers to merge them: "the barcode read from this photo is one or two digits off from <X> you scanned, same thing?". Merging keeps the photo you took and adopts the scanned (authoritative) barcode, summing the quantity.
- - Import preview now shows each row both-sides: click a row to see the source data coming over and, for a merge, the existing entity it links into with its current fields, so you can verify the match and what data transfers before approving.
- The scan inbox now offers to combine items that look like the same product: when you scan several of one thing but a pack carries a different barcode, a banner appears ("N items look like the same product: combine into one?"). One tap merges them into a single line with the summed quantity, keeping each distinct barcode in the item's data. Always opt-in (a different barcode can be a different size), and dismissable to keep them separate.
- - Edge bridges: connections (sync + machines) now use a standardized bridge picker that lists your connected bridges with live online status and lets you select one, instead of a free-text field. New GET .../digifab/edge/bridges enumerates them.
- - Integrations → Live sync now has an in-app "Install a source" path: paste a sync-source manifest to install it (no curl), then add a connection through it. Installed sources are listed with an uninstall.
- - Sync sources are now a container + sections: expand an installed source to see its sections (entity types), "+ Add section" to sync another thing (paste just that section JSON, e.g. 3D printers), remove a section, or edit the whole container JSON. The source is defined once; you grow it over time.

### Fixes
- A research-hint correction on a barcode item now actually re-identifies it. Before, a hint like "it's 1 unit, not 96 packs" was stored but never re-ran the lookup when the provider already had a (wrong) name, so nothing changed. Now a hint re-derives the name via the web identify and accepts the corrected result even when it's shorter than the wrong one.
- Photo identification now works through the edge-bridge AI (the "Local AI / Claude bridge" path many hosted workspaces use). It was sending a bare "Describe this." prompt for image identification, so the model replied with prose the scanner couldn't read as a result: surfacing as "no vision provider configured." It now uses the same structured identify prompt the OpenAI/Anthropic providers use, so a photographed item gets identified.
- Photo identification now succeeds when the vision model returns a richer result shape. It was identifying items correctly (e.g. a screen-protector package as brand/product line/product name) but the scanner only read a strict "name" field, so a correct identification surfaced as "no vision provider configured." It now pulls a usable name from the richer shape too, and a re-run forces a fresh look (bypassing any result cached under an older prompt).
- The phone scanner's result card now has a "Not right?" action, so you can re-check a wrong identification on the spot instead of waiting to fix it in the desktop inbox.
- Photo identification now works on workspaces whose AI is a personal connection. The detached "identify this photo" step ran with no caller, so it couldn't resolve the owner's connected AI and failed instantly with "no vision provider configured." It now routes through the user who triggered the scan/re-run, and a vision failure is logged instead of swallowed.
- The "combine similar items" offer now recognises more genuine matches: it anchors on the brand plus the shared product words rather than a strict overall name ratio, so "Charmin … Toilet Paper … Jumbo Roll" and "Charmin … Bath Tissue Jumbo Roll" (the same product under different wording) are now offered for combining.
- The scan inbox no longer caps at a fixed number of items. It loads in pages and pulls the next batch as you scroll toward the bottom, with the true pending count in the header. And the "combine similar items" offers now appear inline, right above the items they apply to, instead of stacking at the top of the list.
- A barcode that can't be identified anywhere no longer shows a junk result: the web-search fallback used to accept an "Unknown Item" / "XXXXXXXX" placeholder as the name (and then image-search it). Those are now rejected: the item is left unnamed for a photo or manual entry, with no bogus name or photo.
- When a barcode provider returns a localized, non-English product name (e.g. go-upc handing back "Charmin Papel Higiénico Ultra Soft" for a US item), the scanner now automatically re-identifies it from the web, which prefers English, so you get the English name for the same product.
- Three scan-inbox fixes: the inbox now loads up to 500 pending items instead of silently capping at 100; an unidentified item ("Unknown Item") no longer pulls junk catalog photos (it searched the literal word "unknown"); and an unidentified item no longer pre-selects a commit target (e.g. "Inventory part"). You pick where it goes.

## 2026-06-23

### Features
- Duet printers get a full control panel. The Duet driver now exposes Home, a 3-axis Jog, and Nozzle/Bed set-temp (not just pause/resume/stop), so a Duet on the edge bridge has the same rich modal controls as a Bambu. Ships via the embedded bridge bundle; bridges self-update.
- Duet printers: full controls, your macros as buttons, and print-from-SD. The Duet modal now has live tuning (speed/flow), baby-step Z, chamber temp, part-fan % + LED toggle, a button for every macro on the printer (LED on/off, bed presets, etc.), and a Print button on each file in the list to start an on-SD file without re-uploading.
- Sort the printer file list + see upload dates. Each gcode file now shows when it was put on the printer, and the list can be sorted by Newest / Oldest / Name / Largest.
- Printer modal: live print progress + per-file estimates. When a printer is mid-print the modal now shows a progress bar with % / current layer / time-left / elapsed. In the file list, tap any file to see its slicer estimate (print time, filament length, layer count, height) fetched once and cached.
- Gcode previews in the printer file list. Files sliced with an embedded thumbnail (PrusaSlicer/Orca/SuperSlicer) now show that preview right in the list: same image DWC renders. Previews load lazily as you scroll and are cached, so the printer isn't hammered. Click one to enlarge.
- When a barcode resolves to a thin entry (a bare category like "Bourbon" from a crowdsourced food-facts mirror, missing the brand), the scan now uses web + AI to build the full product title ("Bulleit Bourbon Frontier Whiskey 750mL") and writes the richer result back to the shared Barcode Intelligence DB, so it supersedes the thin entry for every future scan. External databases are a starting point; we enrich our own on first encounter.
- Klipper printers get the full cockpit. Klipper/Moonraker machines now report live job progress, list their on-disk gcode files with slicer thumbnails and print-time/filament estimates, run from storage, and expose the complete control set: speed/flow, home/jog/baby-step, temps, part fan, and every one of your Klipper macros as a button.
- See the files already on a printer. The device modal now lists the gcode files on the printer's own storage (Duet via the edge bridge). It's cached (5-minute server cache, fetched once in the UI, never polled) with a manual Refresh: so a fairly-static list doesn't hammer the printer.
- Edit a Scan Rule as JSON. The rule edit/new modal gets a Form / JSON toggle: flip to JSON to tweak the raw rule and flip back, with the form and JSON kept in sync. Pairs with the Paste JSON button for create.
- QR labels can now encode a self-describing URL: `/qr/<kind>/<id>` instead of an opaque random token. The win: a scanned code stays meaningful even if this Cobblr instance is ever gone or moved (you can read what it points at straight off the URL, and it survives route changes), where an opaque token is a dead pointer without the database. It's the new default; flip a workspace to opaque (shorter, reveals nothing, scans better on tiny labels) on the QR codes page. Both styles resolve identically and anything you've already printed keeps working.
- The scan card gets a proper image viewer: tap the catalog or your-photo image to view it full-size, "Revert to original" to undo a photo pick and restore the image the lookup first gave, and "Use my photo" to make your own scan the catalog image. The original is preserved server-side, so revert works even after a reload.
- Create a Scan Rule by pasting JSON. The External QR Rules page gets a Paste JSON button. Drop in a rule's `{name, match, resolve}` and it's created, instead of filling out the form field by field. Handy when someone hands you a ready-made rule.
- Scans now group into visible "sessions." Whatever you scan with a hardware scanner (or the camera) flows into one session until you idle for 30 minutes, then a fresh session starts. The inbox shows these as collapsible groups with a time, location, and count header ("Today 2:48 PM · Garage · 6 items"), so you can finally see how your scans are batched, and a "New session" button starts a fresh group on demand.
- You can now keep a live link to another system. Add a *sync connection* (the first is companion app), and its records mirror straight into your workspace: connect it once, and e.g. every room, rack, and bin you add in companion app shows up as a Cobblr location, hierarchy and all. It's a real connector, not a one-off: changes arrive instantly via webhook, with a periodic reconcile that backstops anything missed and removes things deleted at the source. Credentials are encrypted at rest, and the same machinery is built to host more connectors over time.
- Sync connectors now do a proper one-time import preview before they touch anything. Enabling an entity type (e.g. companion app locations) no longer writes straight to your workspace. You pull a dry-run plan that shows exactly what *would* happen (create / merge-into-existing / update / unchanged / remove), eyeball it, and approve. The first import even merges a source record into a same-name item you already have instead of duplicating it. Once you approve, that entity type switches to live sync (webhook + poll). Live pushes and the background poll stay withheld until the import is approved.
- Search the web for a printer's photo. The 3D-printer / machine modal now has a "search the web for a photo" strip. Pick a product shot and it becomes the printer's image. It's the same picker the scan inbox uses, now a shared component available anywhere an item needs a photo.

### Improvements
- Connecting a print manager from a printer is now one step. Opening "Connect one" from a printer jumps straight to the edge-bridge machine form (no type-picking), pre-fills the machine name from the printer, and pre-selects the driver from the printer's kind (Klipper/Prusa/Duet).
- Printer file lists & previews are now served from a durable backend cache. A background warmer keeps each printer's file list and gcode thumbnails fresh in the database, so opening a printer is instant and never pulls from the machine itself: the printer is touched only on the backend's schedule, and each thumbnail is fetched exactly once.
- Printer files & previews are cached harder. The file list is cached 15 min and each thumbnail 30 min, on both the server and in the browser, so reopening a printer is instant and the printer itself is touched at most once per window, never on every modal open.
- Printer/asset location pickers now show rooms in your order. The location dropdown for printers and machines lists only areas (rooms/zones, not bins) in your arranged order with natural sorting (Bin 1, 2, … 10, not 1, 10, 2). And you can connect a print manager after the fact: the printer's Print Manager panel now has an inline "Connect one" / "+ new" that sets up a manager without leaving the page.
- One "QR codes" settings window. The separate "QR codes" and "External QR rules" Configuration entries are merged into a single QR codes page with two tabs: Tokens (minted QR tokens) and External rules (the resolver rules). Old `/configuration/scan-rules` links redirect to the External rules tab.
- Scan-rule test shows the actual item it resolves to. The "Test a payload" result now displays the resolved entity's name (e.g. the room) with an Open link, instead of just the entity kind, so you can see at a glance that `…/storage/25` opens Garages, not just `core-locations:location`.
- New green "This is good: lock it in" button on a scanned barcode item: when the listing is correct, one tap verifies its name, brand, category and photo into the shared barcode database, so every future scan of that code, in any workspace, gets your clean entry instead of a thin crowdsourced one. It doesn't re-run the AI or commit to inventory; it just locks the data in.
- The scanned-item correction buttons are now a pair, half-width each: "Right: needs detail" (the product is correct but the listing is sparse, keep the identity, re-check every source + the web to fill in the proper name, size and photo) and "This is wrong" (re-derive the product from scratch). Both steer off your optional note and push the result back to the shared barcode database.
- The source-data panel on a scanned item now shows a short history of what you did to it: "Asked for more detail · 2 min ago", "Flagged wrong", "Locked into the barcode database", newest first, so you can see what's already been tried. The correction buttons are also reordered into traffic-light order: red This is wrong → yellow Right, needs detail → green This is good, lock it in.
- The top navigation bar now spans the full width of your window instead of being boxed into a centred ~1150px column, so the module nav gets the whole row before links fold into the "more ▾" menu. Page content (tables, forms, lists) stays centred at a comfortable reading width.

### Fixes
- Newly-connected print manager auto-selects. After "+ new" creates a manager from a printer, it's now selected in the dropdown (with its printer auto-picked) instead of dropping back to "choose…", so you can Link immediately.
- Duet printers report live temps. The edge-bridge Duet driver now reads bed/chamber/nozzle temperatures (from `rr_status?type=2`), so a Duet on the local bridge shows live telemetry like the others. Ships via the embedded bridge bundle: bridges self-update automatically.
- Live temps in the printer modal for edge-bridge machines. The device-detail telemetry was Bambu-cloud only, so a Duet / PrusaLink / Moonraker printer connected through the local edge bridge showed no live telemetry in its modal. It now pulls live temps + state from the bridge's device list when there's no Bambu cloud report.
- Thin-hit enrichment now captures the spec, not fluff: when a barcode resolves to a bare name and we enrich from the web, the result keeps the package size / quantity (1.75 L, 750 mL, 12 ct, proof) that identifies the SKU, and only replaces the thin name when it actually adds the size or the brand: never just longer marketing wording.
- Scan catalog images are far more accurate for store/own-brand products. The image search now includes the brand in the query (not just the ranking), so a generic name like "Blended Scotch Whiskey" + brand "Kirkland Signature" no longer comes back as a random Johnnie Walker bottle. The brand is skipped when the title already contains it, so the query is never duplicated.
- Scan matchmaker suggestions come back much faster. The AI now only writes an explanatory note when something actually needed reconciling (a title-vs-photo disagreement, an inferred field, an unconfirmed pack count) and omits it for a clean match, instead of always composing a 2–4 sentence paragraph. Typical routing dropped from ~18s to ~5s with no loss of useful explanations.
- Scan cards no longer show a broken-image icon when a catalog photo URL is dead. If the main catalog image fails to load it falls back to the server-cached copy or your own photo; and any "other photo options" thumbnail that won't load is dropped from the strip instead of showing a broken tile.
- Re-scanning a barcode now always merges into the one pending item you already have, no matter which scanning session you're in, and moves it into the current session. Previously dedup was scoped per session, so re-scanning the same code in a new session created a duplicate (and the original looked like it had gone missing).
- The scan inbox now groups un-batched items by day (Today / Yesterday / date) instead of lumping them all under one "No session" header. Explicit scanner sessions still show as their own timed group; everything else reads as coherent day buckets, newest first, so the list makes sense even for items scanned before sessions existed.
- The scan item's research-hint box is now a full-width, multi-line field so its placeholder is no longer cut off (Enter submits, Shift+Enter adds a newline). And the enlarged-image viewer now shows the "other photo options" strip along the bottom, so you can swap to a different photo without leaving the zoom: picking one swaps the big image in place.
- For items identified by web search (e.g. books), the cleaned-up name the AI describes is now actually used as the item name, so the note ("publisher and author stripped from name") matches what you see, instead of the header still showing the raw "Publisher Title (Author)". Sizes/specs are never dropped, and database-identified products are unaffected.
- Scan names now lead with the brand when a sparse source name leaves it off: "Black Label No.7" (Jack Daniel's) becomes "Jack Daniel's Black Label No.7". Names that already include the brand are left as-is.
- Re-scanning a barcode that's already in your inbox, or restoring one from the deleted section, now brings it back to the TOP of the queue instead of leaving it buried at its original position. Previously a re-scan or restore updated the item in place far down the list, so it looked like the scan did nothing and the item had gone missing.
- The scan card's "Source data" panel (provenance + the fields it routed to) is now expanded by default instead of collapsed.
- When a barcode isn't in any database and we fall back to a web search, the AI now picks the product that most search results agree on, instead of occasionally latching onto a single obscure or accessory listing ("Customers also bought…", "Related…"). Pairs with the earlier fix that feeds it real product-page titles.
- New "This is wrong: re-check everything & fix it" button on a scanned item. One press re-queries every source (go-upc, upcitemdb, Open Facts) plus a web search, steered by your optional "what's wrong" note, distrusts the flagged listing, reconciles the correct name + photo, and pushes the correction back to the shared barcode database so the next scan (anyone's) is right. The source panel now also shows when the listing was last updated ("10 min ago"). "Re-run AI" is unchanged.
- Scan items can no longer get stuck spinning on "finding the best table…" forever. If the matchmaker step ever failed (e.g. a slow/timed-out AI call) it left no completion stamp, so the card spun indefinitely with no way to recover. Now the matchmaker records completion even on failure, and the card gives up the pulse after a few minutes, showing the identified item so you can route it by hand.

## 2026-06-22

### Features
- Import & export locations as CSV. On the Locations page, Export downloads your whole location tree, and Import lets you paste/upload a CSV and preview the exact create/update/skip diff (and any unresolved parents) before anything writes. Generic: pick which column matches existing locations, set parent by that same column, and any extra columns are kept on the location's metadata, so you can round-trip another system's ids.
- Locations now has a navbar entry. Previously the rooms/places tree was only reachable under Configuration; it's a top-level Locations item now: per-device show/hide/reorder like any other nav entry, so the place tree is one click away.
- Order your locations by hand. Each location now has up/down arrows to set the display order within its group (rooms in the order you want, rather than alphabetical). The order persists and applies everywhere the tree renders; un-ordered groups stay alphabetical.
- Rename the Locations nav entry to your word. The top-level Locations entry now honors the org-wide display-name override (Configuration → Presentation): call it "Rooms", "Areas", whatever fits your workspace, consistent on every device.
- Locations: unsorted containers get their own section + natural sort. Loose containers (bins not placed in any room/area) now sit in a separate "Unsorted containers" section at the bottom, so the room/area tree stays clean. Names also sort naturally: Bin 1, Bin 2, … Bin 10, Bin 11 (not Bin 1, Bin 10, Bin 2).
- Select-all + bulk delete on Locations. A Select all button (and a Delete action in the selection bar) lets you clear out locations in one pass: handy for re-importing a corrected CSV. Deleting a parent cascades to its children.
- When a scanned barcode finds nothing in the catalogs or on the web but you snapped a photo, the item is now named from the photo instead of being left blank. The AI already reads the photo to describe and sort the item. Now it turns that read into an actual title (e.g. a museum-gift souvenir box, a foreign-language book edition the catalogs miss), so you get a usable name to confirm rather than an empty "name required" field.
- When you add a photo to a scan whose barcode came back wrong, the photo now wins. A barcode/web search can return a spurious listing (a debug probe coming back as a "power supply"); if your photo unambiguously shows a different product, the item is now renamed to what the photo shows: the old wrong title is dropped, the photo-derived details become the ones to work from, and the corrected barcode→name is reported back to the shared barcode database so the next scan is right. (Previously this check only ran for direct barcode hits, not web-search results, so a wrong web-search title would stick.)
- Book and music scans now resolve from the local mirrors. A scanned book's ISBN-13 is an EAN barcode, so it's now looked up through the shared barcode database (which now holds the Open Library catalog): instant, offline, no per-scan call to Open Library's API; a brand-new book not yet mirrored still falls back to the live API. CDs/vinyl already went through that path, so they pick up the new MusicBrainz music data automatically. Provenance reads "Open Library" / "MusicBrainz".
- Bulk-triage gained Set location: tick a batch of scans and file them all into one place in a single step (it appears in the selection toolbar next to Confirm/Discard whenever your workspace has locations). Pairs with the filing context: scan a pile anywhere, select them, and drop them into "Garage Shelf 3" at once.
- Bulk-triage the inbox. Tick a few scans (or "select all") and a toolbar lets you Confirm or Discard the whole selection at once. No more one-by-one. Confirm sends each item to its own best table match with the fields the matchmaker already extracted; anything without a confident match is left in place for a quick manual look, and the summary tells you how many landed. Turns a 30-scan session into one tap to file.
- New URLs intake on the scan page: paste a list of product links (one per line, up to 50) and each becomes an inbox item, identified in the background through the same web-lookup the camera uses for a scanned URL. Handy for cataloging an online order or a wishlist when there's no barcode to scan. Drop the links in, and they fill in names/photos/categories on their own.
- Scanning now has a filing location ("active bin"). Set "Filing into → {location}" on the Scan page (or pick an area in the camera), and every scan: hardware gun, UPC, or camera, is filed into that location, persisting on the item so even a later triage already knows where it was scanned and pre-fills it. Confirming files the new item into that location in your `core-locations` tree. It's the warehouse "put-away" pattern: choose where you're standing once, then just scan; the location rides along instead of being re-picked per item.
- Barcodes that the lookup services can't find now get a little more help. Every barcode's prefix encodes the country where it was registered (a Prusament filament barcode starts 859 = Czech Republic), and the AI identifier now gets that as a hint, so when web results are thin it can still make a better guess at the maker. Free and instant, no extra lookup.
- The scan inbox now has a "⚠ N needs review" filter. After you bulk-confirm the confident scans, flip it on to show only the ones that didn't cleanly resolve: no name yet, a short/ambiguous barcode, a rate-limit retry, or low confidence, so you focus your attention exactly where it's needed instead of scrolling the whole pile. Bulk-select respects the filter, so "select all" + Confirm/Discard acts on just the shown set.
- When you scan an item and the barcode resolves to the wrong product, snapping a photo now gives you a one-tap fix. The photo cross-check already noticed the mismatch; now it also reads the real product off the label and offers a "Use photo's name: …" button. One tap renames the item to what the photo shows, and reports the correction to the shared barcode database, so the next person who scans that code gets the right name automatically.
- Re-scanning an item to add a photo now improves the entry you already have instead of making a second one. When a barcode came back with the wrong (or no) name, scanning it again with the camera attaches that photo to the existing entry (even from a later session) and re-checks the name against the photo, so the "this doesn't look right" flag (and the one-tap fix) appear on the row you were already looking at. No more duplicate rows for the same thing.
- Two scan clean-ups. Clearer provenance: when a barcode resolves instantly from the shared Barcode Intelligence DB (its cache or the grocery mirror) the note now reads "BIdb / go-upc" instead of a bare "go-upc", so an instant hit no longer looks like a live lookup it couldn't have been. Better English names from web search: the fallback that names an item from web results now prefers English titles over foreign-market listings (it was only filtering non-Latin scripts, so French/Spanish names slipped through), fewer "pâte à tartiner" names when an English one is available.
- Scan a location to start filing into it: zero clicks. Print a QR label for a room/shelf/bin (any `core-locations` place), and scanning it makes it the active filing bin: everything you scan next files in there. Scan a container while a room is active and it *nests*, the container is filed into the room and becomes the new bin (so "scan the garage, scan the tote, scan the parts" lands the parts in the tote, the tote in the garage). Scanning another area just switches context (it won't wrongly nest one room inside another), and a move that would create a loop is skipped. Works the same from the camera and a hardware scan gun.

### Improvements
- Locations reorder by drag. The up/down arrows are replaced with a drag handle: grab a location and drag it to reorder within its group (rooms among rooms, bins among bins). Touch-friendly. Order persists as before.

### Fixes
- Bambu connection messaging now reflects hybrid LAN+cloud. LAN and hybrid modes are marked available (they shipped), the capability notes describe what each mode actually does, and the connection badge says "cloud · monitor + light/pause" vs "lan/hybrid · full control" instead of a blanket "control". Stale "coming soon / Phase 3 / no remote control" copy removed.
- Personal/BYO AI (the edge bridge) is more reliable under a flaky agent connection. The agent that carries your AI long-polls the cloud, and in the brief gaps between polls a request could hit "no edge device connected" and fail, which made the scan matchmaker fall back to "Matched by keywords (no AI)" even though AI was connected. Those momentary disconnects are now retried (a few seconds, with backoff) so the call waits for the agent's next poll instead of giving up.
- Scan catalog pictures are more reliable: the image search now retries when DuckDuckGo's image endpoint returns empty (it's flaky from a server IP even when the web search has results), and re-running AI on an item now re-fetches its picture by name, so a barcode whose title was corrected upstream but whose image stayed wrong (e.g. a "Pinecil" showing a ribbon cable) fixes itself on re-run.
- Locations: clearer area icon. The "area" kind icon was a plain square that looked like a second, unchecked checkbox next to the real select checkbox. It's now a location pin. No more double-checkbox confusion.
- When AI isn't doing the matching, the inbox no longer force-fits an item into the wrong bundle. The keyword matcher used to suggest any table that shared a single word, so a "Pine64 LCD Ribbon Cable" got tagged Yarn (because "ribbon" is a yarn word). Now a table is only suggested when the item actually *is* that kind of thing (its noun matches) or a specific attribute matches, otherwise the card just offers the generic "Inventory part." Fewer, better chips (at most two), and no more cables in the yarn bin.
- When an item gets renamed: whether you fix it yourself or the photo correction replaces a wrong barcode title, the catalog picture now updates to match the new name instead of keeping the old product's image. (Previously a corrected name like "Pinecil Soldering Tip Set" could still show the wrong barcode's ribbon-cable photo.)
- Scanning a big batch no longer drops items to a raw, uncleaned name or a scary "502" note. The AI bridge that names and sorts each scan is a single agent, and a burst of scans used to overwhelm it, so some items kept the messy web-title ("… - Botland - Robotic Shop") instead of the tidy name the AI would have produced. Scans now queue gently to the bridge (a few at a time) and retry a momentary overload, so far more items come back with a clean AI name. Also: junk "inventory checker / price tracker" pages (BrickSeek, camelcamelcamel, bare UPC-lookup results) are no longer mistaken for the product name.
- The scan inbox now fits a phone screen. The intake buttons (UPC / URLs / Upload / Receipt / camera) sit in a strip that scrolls sideways instead of overflowing off the edge, so adding new intake options later can't break the bar again. And the per-item "which table" chips no longer stack into a tall wall on mobile: each card shows just the best match plus a "+N" you can tap to see the rest, while desktop still shows them all.
- A scanned physical product no longer gets suggested for admin tables it can't belong to. Scanning a barcode (or a photo the AI reads as an item) will never route to Subscriptions, Warranties, Documents, or Bills: those are records you add by hand, not by scanning. Scannable physical tables like Medications and Collections are unaffected. The guard is deterministic, so it holds even when AI is slow or offline.
- Three inbox fixes. Undo on remove: removing a scan (one or a whole selection) now shows an "Undo" button right on the toast, so a mis-tap is one click to put back instead of a trip to Recently deleted. Right words for a barcode: an item that's just a barcode (no photo) no longer says "Couldn't identify this photo". It says "barcode". No more stuck "Awaiting lookup": when a barcode lookup is rate-limited and the automatic retries are used up (e.g. the daily lookup quota is spent), the item now settles into a clear "couldn't identify, name it" with the name box ready, instead of spinning "retrying…" forever or sitting on a misleading "Awaiting lookup…".
- The "Removed: Undo" toast now names what you removed (the item's name, or its barcode) instead of a bare "Removed.", so a stack of them is easy to tell apart, and they auto-dismiss after a few seconds instead of piling up until you dismiss each one.
- The barcode web-search no longer "gives up" before the AI has answered. On a self-hosted or personal AI connection that's slow (100s+ per call), the web-search identify was timing out at 20 seconds and reporting "nothing found": even though the AI was still working and would have identified the item. The timeout is now generous enough (matching the matchmaker) for a slow model to finish, so far fewer scans fall through to "fill in manually" just because the AI was slow.
- Barcodes that aren't in any database now resolve far more often. The web-search fallback used to ask DuckDuckGo's image index for a name (which can't resolve a bare UPC) so it usually came up blank. It now asks the text/web search too, which returns the actual product pages (e.g. a Cuisinart pan's UPC → "Cuisinart Chef's Classic Nonstick Hard Anodized 11″ Square Griddle"), giving the AI real titles to identify from.

## 2026-06-21

### Features
- Barcode lookups now learn from your triage. When you confirm a scanned item after fixing its name, because the barcode lookup was wrong or found nothing, Cobblr quietly feeds that correction back to the shared barcode database, so the next scan of that product (in any workspace) gets your fix. It's invisible and never affects your own item; only a meaningful rename is sent, tagged anonymously. (Operator-configured via the resolver correction token; off until set.)
- Operators can now review barcode corrections from a public approval queue. When an instance is set to *propose* mode, user fixes to wrong/missing barcode lookups land in Super-admin → Barcodes → "Proposed corrections" with the current value vs the proposed one: Approve makes it the verified answer every workspace's next scan sees, Reject drops it. Approval uses a server-side review token (super-admin only; never exposed to public users), so a public instance can both capture proposals and host the review without handing out write access.
- Scanned items now get a cleaner catalog photo. Image options are ranked by catalog quality: a retailer or brand product page (clean studio shot on white) floats to the top, while recipe blogs, Pinterest, and styled/social photos sink, so the good photo is the first one you see instead of buried at #6. The barcode lookup's own auto-chosen image uses the same ranking. And when you pick a better photo for a scanned product, that choice is fed back to the shared barcode database as an image correction, so the next scan of that product (in any workspace) gets your clean image, beating whatever the providers had.
- Bambu LAN, as a setting on the printer. Open a Bambu printer in digifab → LAN access → enter its IP + access code, and Cobblr adds a LAN transport to the *same* printer (no second connection). Cloud keeps doing live telemetry; file-push (sending an arbitrary sliced file, which cloud can't do) and control now route through your on-site edge bridge over the printer's LAN. (Live camera over LAN is a follow-up; it needs an RTSP update in the bridge.)
- Printers connected through the edge bridge (Duet, Klipper/Moonraker, Prusa) now show live controls: Pause / Resume / Stop: in the printer modal, instead of "no live controls." Prusa printers also report their nozzle/bed temperatures now.
- Live camera over LAN. A Bambu printer with LAN access now shows its chamber camera right in the printer modal: a refreshing still, grabbed frame-by-frame over your bridge (cloud can't do this). Click it to zoom full-screen.
- New file library for digifab: upload your sliced .3mf / .gcode files and Cobblr pulls out the slicer's plate thumbnail for an at-a-glance grid (no slicer or render step: the preview is read straight from the file). Send any library file to a printer or a pool in one click. Multi-plate 3MFs show their plate count.
- A workspace can now run more than one edge bridge: a second site/VLAN, or LightBurn, which has to run its bridge on the LightBurn PC itself. Give the extra bridge an id in the connection setup; it gets its own tunnel channel and the machines you add are pinned to it. Your existing single bridge keeps working exactly as before (no id = the default channel).
- 3D-printer Print updates: post a photo + live status to Discord as a print runs: your rules. Add a Discord channel once, then make rules per printer (or all printers): post every N % / N minutes / N layers (whichever comes first, with an optional "no more than once every N min" cap), with a `{{param}}` message template you can preview, and the live camera photo attached.
- Click a printer in digifab (its name, or "Details & controls") to open a full printer modal: image, live telemetry (nozzle/bed/chamber temps, AMS filament colors + remaining, chamber light, firmware-update + alert badges), the controls (now inside the modal), a link-to-machine picker, and that printer's recent prints. Click any print: there or in the history list, for a detail card with a large cover you can click to zoom full-screen. The temperature controls show the live actual → target and pre-fill from the current target.
- Print queue gains priority and auto-reprint-on-fail. Mark a job High or Urgent and it jumps ahead of Normal jobs when a printer frees up; set an auto-retry count and a failed print re-queues and re-sends itself (pool jobs re-drip to any free printer, device jobs retry on the same one) instead of just stopping.
- Duplicate a print-update rule: a copy button on each rule clones it (cadence, message, hooks and all), so you can spin up a per-printer variant in seconds instead of rebuilding it.
- Print-update rules gain a "Test fire now" button (posts a real update from the printer's current live telemetry, so you can verify a rule end-to-end without waiting), plus pre/post hooks: run printer controls around the post (e.g. chamber light on → settle → shoot the photo → light off). Hooks run non-blocking, so the delays never hold up telemetry.
- Each Bambu printer now has a transport mode in its LAN-access panel: All cloud (status/control/history via Bambu's cloud), Prefer LAN (status + control + file-push over your bridge, cloud fills in print-history names), or LAN only (everything local, cloud off: no internet needed, but you lose cloud-only history names/covers). Cobblr pulls live telemetry over LAN for the LAN modes, so status keeps flowing even with the printer off the cloud.
- Scanning is now type-aware. Cobblr recognises what kind of code you scanned and routes it to the right place instead of running everything through the product-barcode databases: a UPC/EAN goes to the barcode chain, an ISBN is looked up as a book (Open Library: title, author, cover), a real Amazon ASIN tries the product page, an Amazon FNSKU (the `X00…` warehouse labels) is recognised as un-lookupable and sent straight to "name it" (no more endless "rate-limited, retrying"), and a URL goes to the maker-page resolver. The upshot: non-barcodes no longer waste a barcode lookup or get falsely stuck as rate-limited, and books/Amazon items identify better.
- When you scan a barcode with the camera, Cobblr now double-checks the result against your photo. After the barcode resolves to a product name, a vision model glances at the picture you captured and asks "does this actually look like that?", and if it clearly doesn't (a store-local or reused barcode resolving to an unrelated product), the item is flagged "⚠ this photo doesn't look like *X*, the barcode may be wrong" with its confidence dropped, so you catch the mismatch instead of filing the wrong thing. Runs in the background (your named item appears instantly) and only fires when there's a photo to compare. A fix you make then flows to the shared Barcode Intelligence DB so the next scan of that code is right.

### Improvements
- The live pump now also records a print it finds already finished when it connects, so a print that completed while the pump was offline (or before this tracking existed) still lands in Print history, not just prints it watched start-to-finish.
- Experimental cloud control for Bambu. From a Bambu's fleet card you can now toggle the chamber light or nudge Z over the cloud connection: no LAN setup. It rides the same MQTT the pump already holds (the broker the Bambu app uses). It's a safe, visible way to test whether the printer accepts our commands; if it does, full pause/resume/stop over cloud follows.
- Cobblr now captures the full Bambu data: the complete live MQTT report (temps, AMS, layers, fans, errors, the lot) and the cloud print-history (model name, cover image, weight, time), stored raw. The groundwork for richer print history and correct print names.
- Generic printer controls. Every printer's fleet card now has a Controls panel that shows exactly what *that* printer can do: declared by its driver. Bambu (cloud) gets pause/resume/stop, jog (X/Y/Z), chamber light, and nozzle/bed temperature; the declarative managers (OctoPrint/Klipper/Duet) get whatever their manifest declares, including custom commands. Only supported controls appear.
- Print history now shows Bambu prints by their real model name with a thumbnail: "Cherry Blossom Crossbody Bag" instead of the cryptic profile name, plus accurate weight and print time, pulled from Bambu's cloud history. Failed/successful prints, per-printer rollups, and filament totals all include them.
- Prints you start outside Cobblr now show up in Print history too. Cloud Bambu is monitor-only, so prints are kicked off from Bambu Studio: the pump now records each one when it finishes (completed/failed), and history merges them with the prints Cobblr sent.
- Tidied the Modules list. The built-in wasm demo modules (hello-world examples + a sandbox URL archiver) were only ever there to exercise the platform's sandbox feature. They no longer appear in real workspaces (they load only when you're developing Cobblr itself). And Maintenance is now an optional module you can switch off if you don't use it; it was previously pinned on with no way to disable it.
- Scanning the same barcode again no longer piles up duplicate rows in the Scan Inbox. It bumps the quantity on the existing pending entry (the way companion app does it), shown as a ×N badge on the card. Scan a thing five times → one row, ×5. (Scoped per scan session + area, and only while the entry is still pending, a re-scan after you've filed it starts fresh.) Plus, a phantom row with a spinner now appears at the top of the inbox the instant you scan, so you know it registered while the few-second lookup runs, then swaps in for the real row.
- You can now adjust a scanned item's quantity right in the inbox: the ×N badge on a card is a − / + stepper that updates the count in place (handy when a repeat-scan over-counted), keeping the item pending. No need to open the commit form to change a quantity.
- Discarding a scanned item from the inbox is now a single tap on the ✕: no confirmation dialog (it's not a big deal to remove a scan). And nothing's truly lost: a Recently deleted section at the bottom of the Scan Inbox lists what you discarded, each with a Restore button that puts it right back, enriched data and all.
- When you scan a maker's product QR (e.g. a Polar Filament spool), Cobblr fetches the maker's data and fills in the real product, and that vendor list is now data you can edit, not code. A new Scan Resolvers page in the operator console lets you add a maker by describing how to read its URL (match → fetch → map fields), test it against a real URL, and edit or disable built-ins, no deploy. Adding the next maker is a list entry, not a code change. (Replaces the old single-vendor maker-scan module; the built-in Polar resolver behaves identically.)

### Fixes
- Uninstalling a bundle now cleans up after itself properly. The tables a bundle added (e.g. a Yarn, Hooks, or Designs list) are removed, and any module it switched on is switched back off: but only when nothing else still needs it. If another installed bundle shares that module, or you turned it on yourself, it stays. The Remove dialog now tells you exactly what will go ("deletes Yarn (12 items), Hooks and turns off Inventory") before you confirm. Previously the bundle's lists and modules were left behind, lingering in your sidebar.
- Groundwork for the premium Discord-bot print-updates tier: print-update channels can now be a managed-bot destination (delivered by Cobblr's hosted layer), alongside the free incoming-webhook kind.
- The edge-bridge setup now names the bridge it's reporting on ("the garage bridge is online ✓" vs a vague "your bridge"), since you can run several. The Bridge-id field is clearer too: blank = your main bridge (installed without a BRIDGE_ID), and any additional bridge must be named so it gets its own channel.
- The LAN printer camera now shows the last frame instantly when you open a printer, instead of a few seconds of "connecting": the live feed swaps in as soon as a fresh frame loads.
- Two digifab fixes: a Bambu cloud connection no longer reads "monitor only": cloud control (chamber light, pause/resume/stop) works, so it now shows "control" with a tooltip on what still needs LAN. And the "link to machine" picker now finds machines across all instances (your printers under "3D Printers", lasers under "Laser cutters", …) instead of only the default collection, no more spurious "no matches".
- Printer-modal cleanup: the machine link is now a compact "Linked to X · change" line (no more full-width dropdown for a one-and-done), the empty photo placeholder is dropped when there's no machine image, the AMS chip hides a meaningless "0%" external-spool reading, duplicated print sub-labels are suppressed, and the "needs LAN control" note flips to "Commands route over your LAN bridge" once LAN is on.
- Print-history sub-labels (the slicer profile, e.g. "成年人版 0.2mm 层高, 3 层墙, 15% 填充") now render in English: "Adult 0.2mm layer, 3 walls, 15% infill". Bambu only translates the model title, so Cobblr maps the common Chinese slicer terms itself.
- Modals no longer close when you click outside them: a stray outside-click can no longer discard a half-filled form (Esc, the ✕, and Cancel still close). And when you start a 3D-printer and jump to "Install the edge connector," the name you typed now carries into the connector setup.
- Filing a scanned item into a category whose module isn't set up yet no longer dead-ends on a cryptic "Target create returned 409". Committing a scan now enables the target module automatically (e.g. Inventory) and files the item: the capture-first "scan first, structure later" promise. And if a commit can't go through, you now see the real reason (e.g. "Enable Inventory in Configuration → Modules") instead of a bare status code.
- Hands-free barcode scanning now works with Bluetooth scanners. On the Scan Inbox page a HID scanner types the code "behind the scenes" and auto-ingests it, but the detector judged a scan by the gap between individual keystrokes, which is too strict for Bluetooth's jitter (USB dongles are steadier), so BT scans were silently dropped unless you first opened the UPC field. It now judges the whole code's average speed instead, tolerant of Bluetooth's variable timing, still ignores human typing, so hands-free scanning works the same whether your scanner is USB or Bluetooth.

## 2026-06-20

### Features
- Added a guided edge bridge setup in Print Manager → Add a connection → "Edge adapter (your bridge)": it shows the one command to run the bridge on a box at your site, watches live for it to dial in ("Waiting…" → "Bridge online ✓"), and creates the tunnel connection once it's connected. The way to reach a LAN machine from a hosted Cobblr.
- Reach a LAN machine from a hosted Cobblr. A hosted instance (strict egress) can't dial your home network directly, so now an on-site edge bridge dials OUT to Cobblr and holds a tunnel open; Cobblr routes printer commands down it. Create an `edge_adapter` connection with a `cobblr-edge://` address and the bridge handles the rest. No inbound firewall hole, no SSRF surface (the cloud never fetches a private IP). The tunnel now powers connection-test and device-listing too, not just job send/poll.
- Read QR labels printed by another app. Configuration → External QR rules lets you teach the scanner a foreign label format (a companion app URL, a bare Homebox number, anything) and map it to the matching item here, so labels you already printed keep working without a reprint. Scan one and it opens the item exactly like a native Cobblr label; paste a sample into the built-in tester to check a rule before you trust it. Fully opt-in: with no rules, the scanner behaves exactly as before.

### Improvements
- Bambu connections now show which way they work: a "monitor only" / "control" badge with the reason (hover it), and when you add a Bambu you choose up front (Cloud account (live status, no remote control) Bambu blocks it) or LAN via your edge bridge (full control: send / pause / cancel).
- Cloud Bambu printers now show real-time status on the fleet: live nozzle/bed temps, print state, and the printer's own progress (layer + time left), streamed from Bambu's cloud MQTT instead of the slow HTTP poll. Works even for a print you started straight from Bambu Studio.
- When you add a Bambu printer and pick which one it is, the printers now show as a clickable card stack (name, model, online/offline) instead of a dropdown, and a single printer is pre-selected. Matches the look of the connect wizard.
- Adding another Bambu printer no longer asks you to sign in again. When you pick Bambu Lab, Cobblr reuses the Bambu account(s) you've already connected and lists the printers on them you haven't added yet: pick one and it links. You can connect several Bambu accounts at once (a connection is one account); when you have more than one, a small Bambu account picker lets you choose which, and + Connect another Bambu account adds a new login. Signing into the same account again refreshes that connection in place (no duplicate) so its machine links and fleet view stay intact.
- Adding a machine to a bridge you already run is now obvious: if your edge bridge is online, the setup skips the install steps and goes straight to "add a machine", so you can plug a Bambu (LAN), Prusa, Klipper, etc. into your existing bridge without reinstalling. Also: in the pool machine picker, the printer name now gets priority over the connection it's on (no more "Thum…").
- Known machines now get the right product photo. Cobblr checks a curated image catalog (the `CobblrHQ/printer-images` manifest) before its live web-image search, so a Prusa Mini stops showing up as an MK4. Fix or add a model's photo in that repo and it updates within minutes: no Cobblr redeploy.
- Hooking up a non-Bambu printer is now as slick as the Bambu wizard. Pick RepRapFirmware (Duet), Klipper, Prusa, or Marlin/OctoPrint when adding a 3D printer and a Connect directly option lets you enter the printer's address (and API key if its firmware needs one) right in the dialog. Cobblr installs the right driver, creates the connection, tests it, and links the printer. No trip to the Print Manager, no pasted JSON. The Print Manager → Drivers dialog also gained a one-click catalog for installing those drivers on their own.
- A Print history section on Digital Fabrication: at-a-glance stats (prints, success rate, filament used, print time) over a 7 / 30 / 90-day window, a per-printer rollup, and your recent prints: all from the jobs Cobblr already tracked, no new setup.
- A print-manager connection's printers now expand inline as a children list under the connection row. Click the chevron (or the row) instead of opening a separate modal. Fewer clicks, nothing pops over the page; the link-to-machine pickers are right there in place.
- Pause / resume / cancel a running print now works on OctoPrint, Klipper (Moonraker), and Duet: the live-control buttons that used to be greyed out for those managers now act. Support is declared per driver (a manifest's `commands`), so any declarative manager can add control without a code change.
- The Machines page now remembers whether you were on Items or Fleet across a refresh (a new persisted-view helper), and the fleet's live "time left" reads as 13h 2m instead of 782m.
- The edge bridge is now install-once, add-machines-anytime. Name it and Install the bridge with just a token: the box dials in and stays open. Then add machines to it whenever (in the setup dialog or later); each rides the tunnel and the running bridge configures it on the fly, no restart, no regenerated command. Naming the bridge is enough to install it; machines are step two, exactly as you'd expect.
- Installing the edge connector is a smooth in-flow step now: no trip to another page. Add a printer that's on your network from a hosted Cobblr, pick "Via an edge bridge", and the dialog: asks what it's bridging (Klipper / Prusa / Duet / Bambu-LAN / LightBurn, pre-selected from the printer kind, with the right fields), generates a least-privilege token for you (new `devices:edge` scope that can *only* run the bridge), and bakes both into a ready-to-run command. It then watches for the bridge to dial in and lets you pick the machine.
- The edge-bridge install step now offers a docker compose snippet alongside the `docker run` command: toggle between them and copy whichever fits how you run things.
- One edge bridge can now front all the machines on your site over the tunnel. The cloud routes each call to the right machine by its instance id (`/voron/devices`, `/mk4/status/…`), so a single bridge with several configured machines: Klipper here, a Prusa there, a Duet too, all reach a hosted Cobblr through one dial-out connection. Add a machine = add an instance to the bridge config + a connection in Cobblr.
- The edge-bridge setup now configures one bridge for all the machines at your site. It clearly frames the first machine, and + Add another machine lets you put several on the same bridge (each with its own driver + IP). It generates one run command and creates a connection per machine. Plus: the multi-step connect modals now have a clear ← Back to the previous step, and Cancel exits the whole dialog (it used to quietly go back, which was confusing).
- Share machines on your edge bridge with another Cobblr workspace: pick which printers, choose read-only (watch) or read + write (control), and send a one-time link. Their workspace never receives your printer's credentials, and you can revoke access any time.
- External QR rules got simpler to set up and now cover every kind of label. Define a foreign system once: its base URL plus a list of `/segment/ → kind` children (e.g. `/printers/ → Machines`, `/parts/ → Parts`), and one rule handles a whole host: the segment picks the kind, the trailing id resolves the item. Works for machines, parts, assets, and locations alike, and the seeding backfill now covers all four.
- Groundwork for reading QR labels printed by other apps. The scanner can now be taught (per workspace) how to map a foreign QR code (a companion app URL, a bare Homebox number, …) to the matching Cobblr item, so labels you already printed keep working without a reprint. When a rule matches, the scan behaves exactly like a native one (opens the item). Opt-in and invisible until you set up a rule. The point-and-click setup lands next; today it's configurable via the API.
- The live Fleet view (machine status, temps, progress, in-flight jobs) is now a tab right inside your 3D Printers section. No more hunting for it under Configuration → Print Manager. It appears whenever the Print Manager is enabled, alongside the printer list. A linked printer's detail view also shows a live status chip (linked / connected / idle / printing % / needs-clearing / offline) so you can see state at a glance.
- The "that's a LAN address" warning in the direct-connect printer flow now only shows on a hosted Cobblr (cobblr.me / managed), which can't reach a device on your network. A self-hosted Cobblr on the same LAN no longer shows it: direct connection just works. Driven by a new `hosted` flag on `/auth/config`.
- A printer's photo is now editable from its detail modal. Hover the image and click Change to upload your own, Auto to re-fetch a product photo, or Remove. No more being stuck with whatever auto-loaded.
- The auto-fetched printer photo now appears live in the open detail modal: no need to close or refresh. When you open a printer with no image, Cobblr finds its product photo in the background and the thumbnail updates in place as soon as it lands.

### Fixes
- The Bambu live pump now derives the cloud MQTT username from the account token when the stored one is blank, so real-time telemetry starts even for connections saved before this, plus clearer pump logging.
- The Digital Fabrication entry in the navbar now opens its page instead of bouncing back to the workspace home: the page is routed at `/digifab` to match the nav link.
- Fixed an edge-bridge printer getting stuck on "loading…" with a dead Link button. The machine's host/key wasn't reaching the bridge (a config-shape bug), so a PrusaLink/Duet printer never appeared. Also: a direct driver (PrusaLink, Duet, LAN Bambu) IS the one printer, so its detail no longer shows a pointless "which printer" dropdown. It links directly. The dropdown now only appears for a true multi-printer farm manager (FDM Monster / OctoPrint).
- Connecting a printer by IP no longer creates a broken connection. Typing a bare address like `192.168.1.50` (no `http://`) now gets normalized to a proper URL instead of being saved schemeless, which previously surfaced later as "unreachable, invalid URL" in the fleet. An address that still can't be parsed is rejected up front with a clear message.

## 2026-06-19

### Features
- Connected printers now get a product photo automatically: you do nothing. When you add or open a 3D printer, Cobblr searches the web for its model (e.g. "Bambu Lab A1 3D printer"), fetches the best match into your file store, and sets it as the printer's image. Reuses the same image pipeline that gives a scanned product its photo; the image lives in your workspace, and any photo you upload yourself always wins.
- You can now connect a 3D printer to its print manager right from the printer flow: the "New 3D printer" modal has a Print Manager section (turn it on with one tap if it's off, then link to FDM Monster, OctoPrint, or Bambu), and the printer's own page has the connect panel. Workspaces that installed a machine bundle before this feature shipped get Print Manager turned on automatically: no reinstall.
- Connect a Bambu Lab printer by signing in with your Bambu account: no IP, no access code to copy. Add a connection → Bambu Lab, log in (region + email + password, with 2FA if you use it), and Cobblr discovers the printers on your account and pulls each one's details automatically. Cloud mode gives you live status in the fleet cockpit; the wizard tells you up front what each mode can and can't do (remote control needs LAN + Developer Mode, coming next).
- A machine type with its own tab (3D Printers, Laser Cutters, CNC Machines) now opens the full machine page. Set every field, edit, attach files, and link the machine straight to its print manager (FDM Monster/OctoPrint) from its own page, instead of a name-only stub. Installing one of those bundles also offers a checked-by-default "Connect to your machines" option that brings the Print Manager along.
- Setting up a tracker is smoother. The "what do you want to keep track of?" search now tolerates typos: *"pprint"* still finds 3D Printers, *"machne"* finds Machines. And picking a ready-made tracker no longer pops a second *"enable this module?"* dialog; Cobblr enables what the tracker needs and installs it in one step.
- Forward receipts by email. Every workspace you're in now has its own `receipts+…@` address (shown on the Scan page. Tap Copy). Forward a receipt there and its attachment runs through the same parser as an upload, landing as line items in that workspace's scan inbox, no app, no typing. The address itself names the workspace (signed, so it can't be typo'd or forged into someone else's), so multi-workspace folks get one address each and they all arrive in one inbox on our side. Operator-configured (needs the mail Worker set up, like reply-by-email); when it isn't, the address just doesn't show.
- Receipts now parse without AI whenever they can. Upload a CSV export or a text PDF with a line-item table and Cobblr pulls out the items deterministically: header-mapped columns for CSV, table extraction for PDFs, no model call, works even with no AI provider configured. A messy PDF falls back to a single AI text read, and a photo of a receipt still uses an AI vision read. Same one-row-per-line-item inbox triage either way; the inbox remembers how each line was read.
- A scanned or forwarded receipt can now become one purchase order instead of a pile of unrelated parts. With the Purchases module on, a receipt in your scan inbox shows a "Confirm as purchase order" banner: one tap creates a purchase order (vendor, date, total) and files every line as an order item linked to its part. You can still fix or discard individual lines first; it rolls up whatever's left. Without Purchases, the lines just confirm into parts as before.
- Public surfaces (wall panels) now have a per-surface auto-refresh setting and an E-paper mode. Pick how often a published screen re-fetches (every 30s up to hourly, or Off for a static panel) and flip on E-paper mode to force a light, high-contrast (black-on-white) layout with auto-refresh off, so a reflective panel in a cabinet doesn't ghost on a timer. Works for view, board, and whole-App surfaces.

### Improvements
- Connecting a Bambu printer now lives right in the New 3D printer flow: pick what kind of printer it is (Bambu, Klipper, Prusa, …) and how to connect: Directly through Bambu API or through a manager (FDM Monster / OctoPrint). Choosing Bambu-direct signs you into your Bambu account once, discovers every printer on it, and lets you pick which one this is, with the name and model pre-filled and the printer linked the moment you create it. One login covers your whole fleet.
- After a one-click bundle update, the dashboard row keeps a "See details" link (and shows the version you updated to), so you can still open the bundle and read what changed. The 3D Printers / Laser Cutters / CNC Machines bundles now carry that "what's new" note too.
- Creating a location while filling out a form no longer pops a second modal: "+ new location" now expands as an accordion right inside the form you're in. Kind is a two-button pick (Container / Area) instead of a dropdown, and a container you name "Bin" (or "Shelf", "Drawer") gets a globally-unique number appended automatically, "Bin 17", so every container has a unique label/scan number.
- The machine / 3D-printer detail modal opens without scrolling: a tighter header (photo + actions + Kind share the top row) and small add-pills: Tag · File · Link · Note. That only grow into full fields once you use them, instead of a wall of empty attachment boxes. Known printer models also show a product photo in place of the name-initial placeholder (drop images in `web/public/printers/`).
- A 3D printer's detail form now shows only the fields its kind needs. A Bambu (or Prusa) (a closed product where the vendor owns the hardware) hides the DIY build-detail block (hotend, extruder, mainboard, firmware, bed size, local IP); a Klipper / RepRap / Marlin printer you configured yourself still shows them. Quantity is gone from individual machines too (a printer is one unit, not a stock count). Existing Bambu printers clean up automatically; a Kind selector lets you adjust.
- The New 3D printer "what kind of printer is this?" picker now includes RepRapFirmware (Duet) alongside Bambu, Klipper, Prusa, and Marlin, so Duet owners pick their control ecosystem and connect through Duet Web Control.
- A machine's State is now a dropdown (functional / needs maintenance / broken / building / built / kit / rebuilding / loaned / sold / decommissioned) instead of a free-text box, with any existing value preserved. Also fixed the printer thumbnail: a broken image now falls back to the name-initial letter (no broken-image icon), and the auto-fetched product photo now lands correctly on printers that live in a named tab (e.g. 3D Printers).

## 2026-06-18

### Features
- Plug in a barcode scanner and just scan. USB or Bluetooth barcode scanners (1D laser or 2D imager) now work hands-free on the Scan page: point, pull the trigger, and the item lands in your inbox automatically. No need to open the UPC box or click into a field first. Typing into a real field still works normally, so the scanner never gets in the way.
- A brand-new workspace now starts with capture, not setup. Instead of a blank builder asking you to define modules and fields, the first screen asks "What do you want to keep track of?": just scan something or write something down ("3 skeins of blue worsted wool", "my Ender 3 printer"). Cobblr reads each thing against its ready-made trackers and, once a few cluster, offers to build the right one in a tap: "These look like yarn, make a Yarn tracker (3)" installs the tracker and files everything you captured into it, fields and all. No structure to design up front, do first, organise after.
- Track a consumable (a filament spool, a yarn cone, a roll of anything) properly. Set its capacity (the new 1kg), and Cobblr keeps a consumption ledger on the item: what drew it down and how much, each with its source, so you can see *"started at 1kg → these prints used this much → here's what's left."* A print that declares its filament writes a ledger row automatically (attributed to the print). The part page shows a remaining-vs-capacity gauge + the full history.
- Building a print farm from scratch? Digital Fabrication's new Add several button stands up a whole fleet at once: paste a list of printer URLs (one per line: `url`, or `name, url`, or `name, url, apikey`), pick a default firmware, and Cobblr makes one direct connection per printer, installs the drivers it needs, and (optionally) groups them all into a pool. Hit Detect firmware and it probes each URL to guess OctoPrint vs Klipper/Moonraker vs PrusaLink vs Duet for you. Tick test each to see which came up reachable. (Migrating an *existing* FDM Monster farm? Use Import farm instead.)
- The print farm got a real cockpit: live camera feeds, temperatures, and pause/resume. Each printer on the Fleet now shows its live nozzle and bed temperature, and you can give it a camera URL (your OctoPrint/Klipper webcam stream, or any MJPEG/HLS feed). Cobblr embeds the live feed right on the printer's card, so you can watch every machine from one screen. And a running print now has Pause and Resume controls (where the machine's manager supports it). Cobblr still never touches the camera or the hardware directly; it embeds the stream the manager already serves and asks the manager to pause; coordinate-not-control all the way down.
- A finished print no longer closes your task until you confirm it came out good. A printer reporting "completed" only means the gcode finished, not that a good part is on the bed (it could be spaghetti). So Cobblr now treats the after-effects by how much it hurts to get them wrong: your filament count and machine hours still update the moment a print finishes (cheap to undo), but closing the to-do that print was for now waits for your verdict. When you clear the bed, you tap Came out good: which closes the linked task, or Scrapped: which puts the filament and the machine usage back and leaves the task open. In the normal good case it's one tap you were already making; the one thing that used to silently go wrong on a failed print (a task marked done that wasn't) no longer can.
- Drive a printer at home from a Cobblr in the cloud: no port-forwarding. The print farm now supports the device tunnel: an edge agent on your network dials *out* to Cobblr and holds the connection open, so a hosted Cobblr can list, upload, print, pause, and track a printer behind home NAT exactly as if it were on the same LAN. Point a Digital Fabrication edge-adapter connection at `cobblr-edge://` and it routes through the tunnel instead of a direct URL. Two edge agents ship: the standalone edge-bridge (Docker/Pi) and a new OctoPrint plugin (`OctoPrint-Cobblr`) that also posts rich print updates, with a live webcam snapshot, straight to Discord. (It reuses the same proven dial-out path as Cobblr's local-AI relay. The cloud relay is hosted-only; self-hosted Cobblr on your LAN doesn't need any of this.)
- Print farm safety. The pool queue no longer auto-starts the next print on a printer that just finished: a finished (or failed) print now flags the printer "needs clearing" on the Fleet view, and the auto-assigner skips it until you tap Cleared, ready. No more crashing a fresh print onto a bed that still holds the last part. Two more hardening fixes ride along: a transient network blip while polling no longer flips a healthy in-progress print to "failed" (it retries a few times first), and a pool job whose send fails now shows up as failed with the reason instead of silently sitting "waiting" forever.
- Digital Fabrication now opens on a live Fleet view: every machine across all your connections on one floor, each with its current state (printing / idle / error / offline) and, when there's a Cobblr job on it, the file and a progress bar. A header counts the fleet at a glance ("12 machines · 4 printing · 1 error"). It refreshes itself, and an unreachable manager just flags its own row instead of blanking the rest. It reads what your machine managers (FDM Monster, OctoPrint, …) report. It never drives the hardware.
- Machines now accrue usage as your prints run. Link a print job to a machine, and each completed print bumps that machine's lifetime print count (and hours, when the manager reports a duration). It's the foundation for maintenance-by-usage: pair it with a maintenance threshold to get reminded "nozzle due at 500 prints" instead of guessing. Runs through a wire on print completion, so it's tunable like anything else.
- A print job can now declare the filament it consumes (a part + grams), and when the print finishes Cobblr deducts that material from your stock automatically. Your farm finally knows your filament. No more guessing whether you can start the next plate. (Set it on a new job under "Filament"; deduction runs through a wire on completion, so you can tune or disable it like any wire.)
- Digital Fabrication can now run a cross-machine queue. Make a pool (a set of machines, even across different connections) then queue jobs onto the pool instead of a single printer. Cobblr drips each job onto the next *free* machine as printers finish, so you can throw a batch at your farm and walk away. It's how you run a pile of individual printers (OctoPrint, Klipper, …) as one farm without FDM Monster in the middle. Pure coordinate-not-control: Cobblr only picks which printer gets the file.
- Live print updates in Discord, with the webcam snapshot, the ETA, and one post instead of two plugins. As a job runs, the farm posts to Discord (or in-app, or email): started → 25 / 50 / 75 % → finished / failed, as a rich, colour-coded card with Progress, Remaining, Elapsed, and Filament, the live webcam snapshot embedded right in the post, and a Live view link to the feed, the OctoEverywhere experience, in one message. Set it up once at Me → Notification channels: add `digifab.print.*` (or `*`) on the Discord channel with your server's webhook URL. The snapshot works wherever Cobblr can reach the camera (a self-hosted instance on the same network as the printer); everything else (the card, ETA, links) works everywhere. A failed print comes through as a red, high-priority alert.
- The print farm scales to a real fleet: searchable pickers + bulk actions. Every New-Job dropdown (connection, filament spool, pool, machine, printer) and the printer→machine link picker is now a type-to-search box instead of a scroll-through list, so picking the right spool out of a full inventory, or the right printer out of fifty, is a few keystrokes. The print queue got checkboxes: select a batch (or select-all) and Send / Cancel / Remove them in one click instead of confirming fifty times, a mixed selection only acts on the rows each action applies to. And building a pool is no longer one-dropdown-click-per-machine: + add machines… opens a searchable checklist so you add the whole set at once.
- Print queue: live, cancellable, and no longer capped. The queue now updates on its own while anything is running (no more stale snapshot: pool auto-assignment and print progress refresh live). Every job gets Cancel (tells the printer to stop where the manager supports it; otherwise marks it cancelled so Cobblr stops tracking it) and Remove from queue (a queued/finished job, an active print must be cancelled first so you can't lose track of it). And the queue no longer silently hides everything past 200 jobs: it pages, with a Load older jobs button and a status filter behind the API.
- Stuck print jobs can be re-pointed at a printer: no delete-and-recreate. When a job's target matches no printer (or several), it lands in *awaiting assignment* instead of guessing. Now each such job in the queue has a Pick printer button: choose one from a searchable list and it's sent straight there, reusing the file Cobblr already uploaded, so there's no re-upload and no rebuilding the job from scratch. Picking a printer that isn't on that connection is refused, so a stray pick can't misroute the print.
- See your printer's webcam from anywhere: opt-in snapshot relay (off by default). A printer's camera is usually a LAN-only address, so it only shows in the Fleet when you're on the same network. Turn on Relay snapshots to cloud (the camera control on a Fleet card) and the edge agent pushes a frame every few seconds up to Cobblr, which serves the latest back, so you get a near-live thumbnail of the printer from your phone, anywhere, without exposing your camera or streaming full video. It's off until you switch it on (it uses upload bandwidth), and the tunnel itself still carries only stats, not video. This is the deliberate, cheap middle ground for remote viewing. Paired with the OctoPrint plugin's matching "relay snapshots" toggle.
- Migrating off FDM Monster? Digital Fabrication now has an Import farm button. Point it at your FDM Monster and bring its printers in two ways, your choice: connect to each printer directly: Cobblr recreates every printer as its own connection of the *matching* type (OctoPrint, Klipper/Moonraker, PrusaLink, FluidNC: per printer, not all-OctoPrint), installs the drivers it needs, pools them, and drops FDM Monster from the path; or keep FDM Monster and mirror its printers into a Cobblr pool. Either way the Fleet reads the imported machines as one farm.
- Upload a receipt and Cobblr reads it for you. On the Scan page, hit Receipt, pick a receipt PDF or a photo of one, and AI pulls out the vendor, date, and every line item. Each lands in your scan inbox as its own row, ready to confirm into a part the same way a barcode or photo scan does. No more typing a receipt in by hand.
- Spoolman users: Cobblr now talks to it. Connect your Spoolman (Inventory → Spoolman) and Sync: each spool comes in as an item with its measured remaining weight, and Cobblr treats Spoolman as the source of truth: it mirrors the number and won't deduct that spool itself (so a print counted by Moonraker → Spoolman isn't double-counted). Don't use Spoolman? Nothing changes. Cobblr's own consumption ledger keeps tracking. The right tracker for your setup, automatically.

### Improvements
- Capture-first onboarding now works without AI. When a workspace has no AI provider (free tier, self-host, or offline), the matcher falls back to a deterministic keyword + field-overlap pass, so writing "3 skeins of blue worsted merino wool" still gets identified as Yarn (fiber Wool, weight Worsted, qty 3) and offers to build the tracker, at zero AI cost. Connecting an AI provider only sharpens the identification + field-fill; capture-first never goes dark.
- The Configuration page is no longer a wall. It now opens to the ~11 settings you actually use day-to-day, grouped and fully visible; the other two dozen (tokens, integrations, diagnostics, …) tuck behind a single Show advanced settings button. Search still finds every setting regardless. And Simple mode now does something visible: turning it on drops you straight onto your clean workspace home, and if you come back to Configuration it shows a calm one-card state instead of the control room, with one click to turn it back off.
- The print-farm cockpit now shows chamber temperature and the current job stage ("Preheating bed", "Calibrating extrusion", …) on each printer card, so you can see *why* a printer isn't printing yet, not just that it's busy. Surfaced by the edge-bridge's Bambu and new Klipper/Moonraker drivers.
- The Fleet floor now groups machines by pool: so a pool of individual printers (one OctoPrint here, a Klipper there) reads as one farm, with a header and a machine count, even across different connections. Machines that aren't in a pool still show under their connection. The payoff of running your printers as a Cobblr farm instead of through FDM Monster.
- Opening Cobblr on a new device (or any time there's no remembered workspace) now lands you on the top of your workspace switcher (your first owned workspace) instead of your oldest membership, which could be a shared workspace sitting low in the list. (A pin-your-own default workspace is a planned follow-up.)
- Managed apps (like Cobblr for Yarn) now show their tabs in a curated order (the headline tracker first) instead of alphabetically.
- The + New thing dialog now explains its two choices far more clearly.
